# WikiStow protocol review log

This log records the focused security review of [`protocol.md`](protocol.md). `wikistow.md` L333 and L357 require that review before the protocol is final, and before any production rollout. For Milestone 2 it is a series of adversarial review rounds, each with a different brief. Every finding is logged here with its outcome.

## How rounds run

1. **Each round runs three briefs** against the current text of `protocol.md`, together with the code it describes.

   | Brief | Prefix | Focus |
   |---|---|---|
   | server | `S<round>-n` | **Malicious server or operator.** Forks, rollback, omission, key substitution, reordering, relabelling, withheld bodies, inserted grant rows, replayed records, and the M1 backfill. |
   | member | `M-n` through round 6, then `M<round>-n` | **Malicious or removed member, or a compromised agent host.** Escalation through grants, groups, ownership and agent grants. What a removed member keeps. An agent outliving its human's access. Abuse of enrollment and lookup codes. A non-owner endorsing a forged log. |
   | encoding | `Fn` (High and Medium), `Ln` (Low); from round 2 logged as `E<round>-Fn` and `E<round>-Ln` so IDs stay unique. Rounds 7 and 8 used `E<round>-Mn` (Medium) and `E<round>-In` (Info), logged as given | **Encoding pitfalls.** Domain separation, length prefixes, field order, number and string encoding (including lone surrogates), base64 strictness, signature and hash malleability, and the fact that sealed boxes don't authenticate their sender. |

   Round 1 ran the three briefs in parallel, as three independent reviewers. Round 2 and later rounds repeat all three, and may add a new brief a round suggests.
2. **Reviewers** are independent subagents. Each gets only its brief, `protocol.md`, and the code paths it names. A reviewer reports findings with:
   - a severity;
   - a concrete attack or failure;
   - the section it breaks.
3. **Triage.** Each finding keeps the ID its reviewer gave it, with the prefix above. The **Round** column says which round found it. Each gets an outcome:
   - **fixed**, with the protocol.md section or commit;
   - **accepted**, recorded as a residual risk or open question in protocol.md;
   - **rejected**, with the reason.

   A finding can be partly fixed and partly accepted; the outcome says which part is which.
4. **Stopping rule.** Rounds continue until one finds nothing high or critical. Then the identifiers in protocol.md §3.5 are frozen, and protocol.md's status changes to frozen.
5. **Later changes.** After freezing, any change to a frozen format needs a new domain version and a new entry in this log.

## Severity

| Severity | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Critical | Plaintext, a content key or a secret key reaches someone outside the intended audience, or an attacker gains authority over a space, without any user mistake. |
| High | Same as critical, but needing an unusual precondition, or a user mistake the interface invites. Also: an attack that goes undetected on a property protocol.md says is detected. |
| Medium | An integrity or availability break with no confidentiality loss, or a metadata leak beyond residual risk R3. |
| Low | A hardening, clarity or consistency issue. |
| Info | A note with no security effect. It doesn't count toward the stopping rule. |

| 6 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 5 revision (a983d69) | Yes: 3 High, 2 Medium, 10 Low (15 in all) | Most round 5 fixes held: agent labels, `grantedAgents`, own-entry scopes `S`, unverified members in previews, and single-entry `unresolvedAppends`. The High findings: the lost-race confirmation compared a 48-bit prefix with a partner who need not have verified the entry (S6-1); a batch counted as landed from its first entry, so a server could apply only a prefix (S6-2); the CLI printed server text next to the safety-code words, so terminal control sequences could forge them (E6-F1). S6-1 shares its cause with every revocation finding since round 3: an `agent-revoke` named one grant, so the human's client had to locate each grant it might have made. **`agent-revoke` now names the agent** (§6.4, §6.5): it ends every grant for the agent in the space, bars later ones, and carries the agent's keys and human so replay checks the derivation without a visible grant. Intents, `lostTo`, `confirmedAt`, contested and spent states and the out-of-band confirmation are removed; `grantedAgents` is written before each grant is appended (§10.4). A grant on a branch the human never sees is a fork (R2). Every finding is addressed by the round 6 revision. Round 7 repeats all three briefs. |
| 7 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 6 revision (a026e58) | Yes: 2 High, 7 Medium, 11 Low (20 in all) | Most round 6 fixes held: `agent-revoke` naming the agent, the bar on re-granting, the cascade only on lost access, agent naming in previews, and the CLI's enrollment checks. Both High findings treat a space mark as proof of what the log holds when the mark recorded only what was persisted. S7-1: round 6 kept a second "done" case (no access left and no visible grant) that a freeze at the mark the grant was signed over, or a hidden grant of the human's own, satisfies: **a regression of S4-1 introduced in round 6**. A space is now done only when its verified log holds an `agent-revoke` for the agent (§12.5). S7-2: the mark a success answer advanced was persisted by a best-effort contacts write the server could refuse, so a split batch went unnoticed on other devices; every append is now recorded with its full signed entries in a confirmed write before it is sent, and a change is done only after a verified log holds it and a confirmed write advances the mark (§6.8). The Mediums: former members could append `agent-revoke`s at will (now one per eligible agent, duplicates refused, and the cascade covers every agent a removed human granted or submitted); `removed` stopped the log at an unpinned former member's revoke (now it governs trust, not replay); another device couldn't complete a partial batch; an `agent-revoke` by a signer with no visible binding had no single verdict; the CLI's escaping left out decrypted content and `--json`. The member reviewer numbered its findings `M7-n` and the encoding reviewer `E7-Mn` and `E7-Ln`; they are logged as given. Every finding is addressed by the round 7 revision. Round 8 repeats all three briefs. |
| 8 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 7 revision (65d8eb7) | Yes: 1 High, 5 Medium, 10 Low (16 in all), and one informational note | Most round 7 fixes held: a space is done only on a verified log holding the `agent-revoke` plus a confirmed mark, write-ahead with full signed entries, former members' eligibility and caps, and the cascade decided on the state after the batch. The High finding (M8-1): a removal was never re-signed without asking the person again, so a member appending free `agent-revoke`s of made-up agents could make every removal of them lose its race; removals now re-sign silently unless an owner signed something in the race, and the server refuses revokes of unknown agents and repeated grants, rate-limits non-owner appends, and honours removal holds, with administrator suspension as the named fallback (§6.8). The Mediums: an honest refusal blocked every later append in the space, because no other entry could be signed at that `seq` (S8-2, E8-M1; now refused and abandoned items are closed and free their `seq`); a split batch had no exit (E8-M2; now changes and items, "missing", and a below-the-base case); `removed` still stopped replay at a removed owner's past entries (M8-2; now replay ignores it); a server could hide a member's own access to make them a former member (S8-1; now marks record access entries whose bodies are required). Every finding is addressed by the round 8 revision, except where its row says otherwise. Round 9 repeats all three briefs. |
| 9 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 8 revision (f338058) | Yes: 1 High, 7 Medium, 14 Low (22 in all) | Most round 8 fixes held: silent re-signing of removals over non-owner entries, the removal hold, the enrolled-agent exception, closed items freeing their `seq`, partial batches, and `accessSeqs` against S8-1 in principle. The High finding (S9-1) is older than round 8: marks for one space were merged by keeping the higher `seq`, so a device on another branch of a forked log replaced the mark that proved a change, and the change was silently undone for every later session; marks are now sets of checkpoints, reconciled and never maxed (§6.8, §10.4). Three Mediums (M9-1, S9-2, E9-F1) are one regression from round 8's S8-1 fix: recorded scopes and groups joined `S` and `G`, so with an honest server a member who lost one grant and kept another hit `missing-body`. Of the two options, removing **Recorded access** and accepting S8-1 in R9, or narrowing it, it was **narrowed**: it now requires only the recorded entries' own bodies, which §6.9 always delivers, adds nothing to `S`, `N` or `G`, and records every access-giving entry, owners' included (E9-F2). That removes a rule rather than adding one, keeps S8-1 fixed, and needs no new delivery or R3 change; withholding an entry that ends recorded access leaves the human on the member path, which fails safe. The other Mediums: re-signing at a freed `seq` could reproduce a closed item's identity (E9-F3), a partly applied closed item had no rule (E9-F4), and submissions were unbounded and recorded before the bounds (M9-2). Every finding is addressed by the round 9 revision, except the part of S9-3 accepted in R2. Round 10 repeats all three briefs. |
| 10 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 9 revision (e579402) | **No**: 10 Medium, 11 Low (21 in all) | **Meets the stopping rule.** Round 9's core held: checkpoints are dropped only by a log that holds them on one chain, narrowed **Recorded access** requires only bodies §6.9 always delivers, fresh salts keep item identities distinct, and the `agent-limit` cap and bounds-first ordering hold. The Mediums are interactions between round 9's mechanisms and honest operation, or bounded availability levers. Marks: a held log merely behind another device's checkpoint was reported as `log-rollback` (S10-2, E10-1), and nothing recovered a space from a real fork or rollback, so an honest backup restore blocked it for good (S10-5); now a held log is judged only at the seqs it holds, and the person can reset a mark explicitly, recorded and shown (§6.8 **Resetting a mark**, §10.3). Recorded access was judged entry by entry, so dormant memberships and group grants after a member left the group were never "visibly ended" (S10-3, M10-1, E10-2); replay now decides it from effective access, and the `log-fork` check is structural. Holds: the first item of a split removal released the hold, and enrolled-only revokes escaped the rate limit, a bounded reopening of M8-1 (S10-1, M10-2, M10-3); a hold now runs its 10 minutes across every item, and only revokes of submitted agents are exempt. A closed grant could land at a time the server picked while the interface implied it was cancelled (S10-4); it is now shown as "may still be applied". Every finding is addressed by the round 10 revision, except where its row says otherwise. Because the Medium fixes change text, a confirmation round repeats all three briefs against the round 10 revision before freezing. |
| 11 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 10 revision (e4702dc); the confirmation round | Yes: 1 High, 6 Medium, 13 Low (20 in all) | **Doesn't meet the stopping rule.** The confirmation round found 1 High in round 10's new **Resetting a mark** text. Round 10's other changes held: "behind" applies only to a held log, so a served rollback can't pass as one; the high-water rule closes S10-6; "visibly ended" by replay fails safe when an ending body is withheld; holds run 10 minutes across every item of a split; "may still be applied" is honest while the item is recorded. The High (S11-1, with M11-5 the same gap at Low): a reset after a rollback undid removals the person had completed, which had already left `unresolvedAppends`, without listing them, and R2 presented comparing heads as the safeguard though it can't catch a rollback shown to every owner or tell it from an honest restore. Now marks keep the person's landed removals in `removals`, and a reset lists every lost change, re-makes lost removals and unpublishes over the new head before it is done, and refuses meanwhile to seal to anyone they cover (§6.8 **Lost changes**); R2 is corrected. The Mediums: the reset adopted whatever log it fetched, not the head compared (S11-2); it failed and dropped items below the new base, which a server can still apply silently by re-serving the abandoned entries (S11-3, M11-1, E11-2); the desktop app accepted an older contacts record re-applying only what the session held (S11-4); merges resurrected recorded seqs a reset dropped (E11-1). Every finding is addressed by the round 11 revision, except where its row says otherwise. Round 12 is a confirmation round on these fixes; it led to removing the reset (see its row). |
| 12 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 11 revision (7ac91ba); a confirmation round | Yes: 2 High, 8 Medium, 9 Low (19 in all) | **Doesn't meet the stopping rule.** Nearly every finding is in the reset of rounds 10 and 11. The Highs: the block on sealing to the targets of re-made removals had nothing persisted behind it, so it lifted in other sessions and after a refusal (S12-1); and lost removals were judged entry by entry, so a restored owner could rewrite their own access before the remover reset, and the reset found nothing lost (M12-1). The Mediums: lost removals named by `seq` hit other entries on a regrown log (S12-2, E12-1); a stale desktop copy reopened and resent closed items (S12-3); re-made removals duplicated ones the log held, widened narrow ones and couldn't be declined (M12-2); the reset skipped the race display (M12-3); agents didn't apply the block (M12-4); held pre-restore logs and merges brought abandoned checkpoints back (E12-2); the reset's pending state wasn't stored (E12-3). **The project owner chose read-only recovery instead of patching the reset.** A space whose log is `log-fork` or `log-rollback` is read-only for the person: their clients read what they can already decrypt, and seal or append nothing there. A member is re-admitted only by an owner's entry naming them on the served log, which they confirm with that owner; an owner, including a sole owner, accepts the served log themself, told that every change after the restore point is undone and nothing is made again. Removals stay forbidden in a read-only space; a member asks an owner (§12.5 step 7). `removals`, `resets`, `droppedAccessSeqs`, **Lost changes**, **Items after a reset**, the reset merge rules and the CLI reset are deleted; marks keep only `abandoned` (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback** and **Abandoned checkpoints**, §10.2–10.4, §12.3, R1, R2, R9). Round 13 is a confirmation round on this simplification. **Superseded in round 14:** read-only became permanent and every way out of it was removed, so this round's outcomes for re-admission, self-acceptance, `owners` and `abandoned` no longer apply. |
| 13 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 12 revision (5a03558); a confirmation round | Yes: 4 High, 4 Medium, 10 Low (18 in all) | **Doesn't meet the stopping rule.** Read-only itself held: it follows from the persisted mark on every device and session, blocks every seal and append, and no reference to the reset is left. Every High is in the ways out of it, and has one cause: the server chose which owner entry or which owner status counted. Re-admission accepted any pinned owner's `grant` or `vouch` of the person outside `accessSeqs`, which never recorded `vouch`es, and the person's confirmation with that owner named no entry, so the server could show an old one while the owner truthfully said yes (S13-1, M13-2). The owner was judged on the served branch, so a removed owner, or a stolen superseded key, could re-admit a member on a fork below the removal, or make them an owner there and so offer them self-acceptance (S13-2, M13-1). **Re-admission is now an explicit `readmit` entry** (`personId`, `personKeyVersion`, `abandonedHash`, domain `readmit/v1`): the read-only person signs a `"readmit"` owner request carrying their abandoned set to the owner they choose; that owner's client, which must not itself be read-only and must have pinned the person, shows the set and appends the `readmit` on its own verified log; the person's client lifts read-only only for a `readmit` naming them whose hash matches their current set, from a pinned owner in the `owners` the person's mark records at their highest checkpoint, and the person confirms it is the owner they asked. Ordinary grants and vouches no longer re-admit. An owner accepts for themself only if that set holds them, after comparing the exact head with a co-owner if there is one (§3.5, §6.4, §6.5, §6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**, §12.5 step 7, R2). The Mediums: merges dropped another device's contrary checkpoint silently (M13-3); `abandoned` wasn't defined for a branch extending the new starting point (E13-F1); a held-log test needed unpersisted state (E13-F2); a former member could never leave read-only (S13-3). Every finding is addressed by the round 13 revision, except where its row says otherwise. Round 14 is a confirmation round on the `readmit` design. **Superseded in round 14:** read-only became permanent and every way out of it was removed, so this round's outcomes for re-admission, self-acceptance, `owners` and `abandoned` no longer apply. |
| 14 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 13 revision (1166014); a confirmation round | Yes: 6 High, 4 Medium, 8 Low (18 in all) | **Doesn't meet the stopping rule.** The `readmit` entry itself held: it can't be moved or replayed, `readmit/v1` is unambiguous, and its signer is checked against `owners`. Every High is in another way out of read-only. `owners` followed the higher `seq` when marks met across a fork, so a removed owner could pad their branch and become the person's recovery authority (M14-1); former-member acceptance didn't check who signed the entries that ended the person's access, then replaced `owners` with the fork's (S14-1, M14-2); owner self-acceptance looked for the co-owner to compare with on the served log, which a rollback or a removed owner's fork can empty (S14-2, M14-3); and the CLI's accept command and the co-owner comparison used a 48-bit `entryHash` prefix that a signer can grind (M14-4, E14-F1). The Mediums: a frozen owner's `readmit` could land the person on a removed owner's fork (S14-3); acceptance reset `accessSeqs` from access the server can hide (S14-4); an honest restore below an ownership change left read-only with no way out (E14-F2). **The project owner chose to remove in-place recovery rather than patch it.** A space whose log is `log-fork` or `log-rollback` is read-only for the person for good, recorded as `readOnly` on the mark, which merges as either side's and is never cleared; nothing lifts it, not even a later log that passes the mark. The `readmit` kind and `readmit/v1`, `"readmit"` owner requests, `owners` and `abandoned` on marks and their merge rules, **Abandoned checkpoints**, **The history coming back**, owner and former-member self-acceptance, the accept path out of §10.3, and the CLI's accept command are deleted. An owner, read-only or not, **re-homes** the space: a fresh genesis, copies of the chosen pages' current published versions and drafts sealed as §13 seals publications with provenance only in `wikistow.rehome` inside the payload, and sharing again by pins; nothing carries over, and nothing redirects (§13 **Re-homing**). In a read-only space an agent revocation stays pending, and the revoke may never land there (R9). Remaining out-of-band head comparisons use 22 characters of `entryHash` (§6.8 **Attack indicators**). The outcomes of rounds 12 and 13 for removed mechanisms are superseded. Every finding is fixed, most by removal. Round 15 is a confirmation round on this simplification. |
| 15 | 2026-10-04 | server, member, encoding | three independent subagents, one per brief, against the round 14 revision (a872f28); a confirmation round | Yes: 3 High, 7 Medium, 11 Low (21 in all) | **Doesn't meet the stopping rule.** Read-only held: no member triggers it alone, `readOnly` merges as OR and is never cleared, and round 14's removed machinery left no dangling references. Its member suggestions came from that log, so a restore or a removed owner's fork put removed people forward for re-sharing (E15-F1, M15-2, S15-1); the old space stayed open to members who accepted a restored log, who then sealed to people removed after the backup point, with no read-only owner able to remove them again (M15-1, S15-4); it copied current published versions by default, so a restore below an `unpublish` republished withdrawn content (M15-3, E15-F2, S15-2); "held before read-only" was undefined (S15-3); and copies kept no sign of who wrote them (M15-4). Fixes: an owner-signed `close` entry, which a read-only owner may sign because it only restricts, ends the log, and re-homing starts with it; re-homing takes no recipient from the old space, copies only versions the owner selects page by page, with the owner's own records of unpublishes and removals shown; copies are the re-homing owner's, shown as "copied from <old space> by <owner label>". Lows fixed as the reviewers suggested. Round 16 confirms. |

## Findings

| ID | Round | Severity | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1-1 | 1 | Critical | Rewrappers seal whatever the server's work list names; no audience check, no keyHash, key may come from the directory. | Fixed: §9.2 (normative rewrapper check for every item), §9.1 (`keyHash` in the operation), §9.3 check 6 (opening side), §9.6 (moves). |
| S1-2 | 1 | High | A freeze plus head rollback keeps a writer sealing to a revoked member, undetected. | Partly fixed: §5.8 (`authorizedSeq` never decreases along `basedOn`), §6.8 (`stale-log` and `log-rollback` reported as attacks), §6.7 (admission rule), §9.5 (guarantee restated as best effort). Signed owner head attestations deferred (Q3). The remaining freeze is accepted as R2. |
| S1-3 | 1 | High | The anchor can sit in a common prefix, so a removed owner can fork from before their removal. | Fixed: §6.7 (the anchor must be at or after the client's admission seq; it refuses to seal or sign until then). A fork that adds recipients to a space the client already belongs to is documented in R2, with out-of-band head comparison for compromise removals. |
| S1-4 | 1 | High | The reader epoch is unsigned server state, so rewrappers can be fed old-epoch publications. | Fixed: §6.4 and §6.5 (`unpublish` log kind), §15 (reader history defined from signed entries), §9.2 check 5, §5.8 (server refuses a publish below the latest unpublish). |
| S1-5 | 1 | High | Agent revocation depends on the server until a client appends `agent-revoke`, in spaces the server names. | Fixed: §12.5 (client flow, every space from the client's own marks, verified, "revocation incomplete" until done), §10.2 (`revokedAgents`), §9.5 (cascade). The window until the human's client runs is accepted as R9. |
| S1-6 | 1 | High | Readers never check write authority, and placements accept a backdated `authorizedSeq`. | Fixed: §5.7 (v2 records sign `authorizedSeq`), §5.8 (author authority and no backdating), §7.2 rule 6. Owner adoption of placements is rejected: it conflicts with one placement per node (§7.1). Backdating by a removed creator or publisher is accepted as R8 and Q13, with the "former member" mark. |
| S1-7 | 1 | Medium | The M1 backfill can be triggered, forked and misdirected by the server. | Fixed: §16.2 (no backfill with any mark; every pre-genesis record must be the client's own; tree review; legacy manifests; mark after confirmation; grant previews list the subtree), §5.7 (clients refuse v1 records not by the genesis owner). |
| S1-8 | 1 | Medium | A contacts record forked across devices goes undetected on desktop and CLI. | Partly fixed: §10.3 (marks store `{version, contactsHash}`; same version with another hash is `contacts-fork`; desktop and CLI verify the chain from their mark). Round 2 (S2-3) showed this only catches a fork one device straddles; a fork that keeps each device on its own branch is accepted as R1. |
| S1-9 | 1 | Medium | The binding map and conflict rules are underspecified. | Fixed: §6.5 (bindings keyed by `(id, keyVersion)`), §6.7 (binding rules for every kind), §8.3. |
| S1-10 | 1 | Medium | Partial-scope replay is undefined for historical checks, and unsigned trailing headers can be fabricated. | Fixed: §6.8 (verified head; pending entries never cited), §6.9 (historical checks use the state as of the cited seq; past ancestors delivered; unchecked items listed). |
| S1-11 | 1 | Medium | Move envelopes exist before the move is signed. | Fixed: §9.6 (confirm before sealing; envelopes cite the signed `move` entry; atomic append and release; server deletes them on abort). A malicious gainer opening keys after the owner confirmed is accepted as R10. |
| S1-12 | 1 | Low | Silent re-sign after `stale-head` can widen a grant. | Fixed: §6.8 (ask again if covered nodes or audience change). |
| S1-13 | 1 | Low | Enrollment expiry is enforced only by the server. | Fixed: §12.1 (CLI and human's client enforce `expiresAt` from their own clocks). |
| S1-14 | 1 | Low | The holders list for attachment reuse is too narrow. | Fixed: §14.2 rule 4 (union over every revision listing the copy). The list stays server-supplied (Q4). |
| S1-15 | 1 | Low | Contacts are sealed to agents the server lists. | Fixed: §10.1 and §10.2 (sealed only to agents in the signed `agents` list, enrollments verified, minus `revokedAgents`). |
| S1-16 | 1 | Low | Content-head rollback and forks are missing from the residual risks. | Accepted as R8, partly mitigated by §5.8 (authority and monotone `authorizedSeq`) and §9.2 check 6. |
| M-1 | 1 | Critical | Any editor can sign a second placement for an existing node and re-parent it into their scope. | Fixed: §7.1 (node ID derived from its one placement), §7.2 rule 0, §16.2 (legacy nodes fixed by the genesis manifest), Q9. |
| M-2 | 1 | Critical | The rewrapping client never checks the recipient belongs in each item's audience. | Fixed: §9.2 (normative rewrapper check), §9.1 (`keyHash`), §9.3 check 6, §9.6. |
| M-3 | 1 | High | The reader epoch and "older drafts aren't included" are server-enforced, yet §15 claimed cryptography enforces them. | Published part fixed: §6.4 (`unpublish`), §15, §9.2 check 5. Draft and metadata heads stay server-attested, limited by §9.2 check 6 to the head the rewrapper resolved; accepted as R8. |
| M-4 | 1 | High | Clients authenticate authors but never check their authority. | Fixed: §5.7, §5.8 (role check at `authorizedSeq`, agents intersected, "former member" mark), §9.3 check 5, §7.2. Backdating window accepted as R8. |
| M-5 | 1 | High | Revoking an agent, deleting an account or revoking an identity depends on the server; agent grants revive on re-grant. | Fixed: §12.5 (client revocation flow), §10.2 (`revokedAgents`), §9.5 (cascade revokes agent grants with their human), §8.4 (owners revoke deleted or replaced identities). The window until a client appends the entries is accepted as R9. |
| M-6 | 1 | High | An owner's vouches outlive their ownership, so a removed owner keeps fake bindings alive. | Fixed: §6.7 (voucher lifetime and the owner-remove flow), §6.4 and §6.5 (`vouch` kind). |
| M-7 | 1 | Medium | Binding rules are under-specified; implementations will diverge. | Fixed: §6.7 binding rules (one key per version for life; a higher version only after the lower one has no access, so revoke then re-grant; never down), §4.6, §8.3. In-place replacement waits for `key-update`. |
| M-8 | 1 | Medium | The server's checks for agents aren't specified to intersect with the human's access. | Fixed: §8.4 (normative `accessTo` for agents, used for every server authorization). |
| M-9 | 1 | Medium | A cancelled move has already handed its gainers their keys. | Fixed: §9.6 (confirm before sealing; envelopes bound to the move entry; deleted on abort). A malicious gainer after confirmation is accepted as R10. |
| M-10 | 1 | Medium | A removed owner can fork the space for members who haven't seen the removal. | Fixed: §6.7 (admission rule). A fork that only adds recipients is documented in R2. |
| M-11 | 1 | Low | Rekey scope and completion are ambiguous. | Fixed: §9.5 (group scopes, pending grantees, attachment rule for every write). |
| M-12 | 1 | Low | The attachment reuse rule needs a same-audience condition and `plainHash` checks. | Fixed: §14.2 rules 3 and 5, §5.3, §14.1. |
| M-13 | 1 | Low | Publishing a draft into another space bypasses the source's publish control. | Fixed: §13 (a draft source needs publish rights at the source, checked by client and server). |
| M-14 | 1 | Low | Agents learn all of their human's spaces from `spaceMarks`. | Fixed: §10.1 and §10.2 (`spaceMarks` moved to a private payload sealed only to the owner). |
| F1 | 1 | High | A placement isn't unique per node, so an editor plus the server can re-parent a node. | Fixed: §7.1 and §7.2 (derived node IDs, one placement per node, server refuses a second), §16.2, Q9. Derived IDs were chosen over a `placementHash` in v2 records because they also cover nodes with no record yet. |
| F2 | 1 | Medium | XChaCha20-Poly1305 isn't key-committing, so one signed revision can decrypt differently for different recipients. | Fixed: §3.1, §5.7 (`keyCommitment`, `key-commitment` error), §10.1 (contacts commit to both keys). |
| F3 | 1 | Medium | Author authority is never checked, and `authorizedSeq` can be backdated. | Fixed: §5.7, §5.8, §7.2 rule 6. Backdating window accepted as R8. |
| F4 | 1 | Medium | A `replacement` rotation statement proves nothing, yet was shown as intended. | Fixed: §4.6 (only an old-key-signed `routine` or `compromise` statement changes the wording). `replacement` stays in the enum as a label only, since the format isn't frozen. |
| F5 | 1 | Medium | `plainHash` is asserted by the writer and never checked. | Fixed: §5.3, §14.1 (every decryption checks it), §14.2 rule 5. |
| L1 | 1 | Low | Redacted trailing entries are never verified but can advance marks and `authorizedSeq`. | Fixed: §6.8 (verified head), §6.9. |
| L2 | 1 | Low | Hash fields have no canonical text form. | Fixed: §3.2 (`Hash32` for every hash field, compared as text). |
| L3 | 1 | Low | `\w` in regexes depends on the dialect. | Fixed: §3.2 and §12.4 (ASCII-only patterns). |
| L4 | 1 | Low | "Characters" is undefined for names. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 5 (scalar values, well-formed, no controls, no normalization), §5.5, §6.4. |
| L5 | 1 | Low | JSON doesn't fix the stored form of integers. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 6 (raw token check). |
| L6 | 1 | Low | `access-body/v1` is under-specified for a second implementation. | Fixed: §6.3 and §6.4 (one table per kind, exact JSON properties, salt length, cross-field rules). |
| L7 | 1 | Low | `Sealed` isn't length-delimited between nonce and ciphertext. | Fixed: §3.2 (24-byte nonce, ciphertext at least 16 bytes). |
| L8 | 1 | Low | Low-order and mixed-order keys are accepted. | Fixed: §4.1 (key acceptance), §3.1 (seal failures are `CryptoError`). |
| L9 | 1 | Low | Assorted: Argon2id parameters, passphrase bytes, `encodeField` limit, same-version contacts, v1 envelopes on new records, the §3.5 claim. | Fixed: §11.1 and §5.6 (RFC 9106 terms; passphrase bytes as built; new passphrases must be well-formed), §3.3 rule 2 and §16.5 (`encodeField` check in PR 2), §10.3 (`contacts-fork`), §5.2 and §5.7 (v1 envelopes only on v1 records), §3.5. NFC for passphrases is deferred to a future wrapper format. The BIP-39 digest remains open as known item 12. |
| S2-1 | 2 | High | An old key version keeps its authority after the person is re-granted at a new version: bindings stay live through owner vouchers, and every authority check is by ID, so a stolen old key can sign `agent-grant` or owner entries. | Fixed: §6.7 **Superseded versions** (`keyVersions`; a lower version has no authority from the seq that bound the higher one; old signatures stay valid), §6.5 rule 4 and §6.6 (signer must be at its current version, else `log-invalid`), §6.7 voucher lifetime (a binding is live only at the current version), §5.8 rule 2, §7.2 rule 4 and §9.3 check 6 (author, creator and granter key version current at `authorizedSeq`), §6.5 `agent-grant`, §6.6 (pin and binding compared per full reference; an older or newer pin refuses sealing to that ID, not the log). Backdated records signed with the old key stay possible, marked "with a replaced key" (§5.8 rule 5, R8). |
| S2-2 | 2 | High | The client can report agent revocation complete while a space it never scanned still seals to the agent, because its space list and `revokedAgents` come from a contacts record the server can roll back or fork. | Partly fixed: §10.4 **Mark before agent grant** (a space's mark is in a confirmed contacts version before any `agent-grant`), §12.5 steps 3–4 and 6 (the device's own marks are the space list; "revoked" only after a confirmed revoke in every space on it; others stay "revocation pending" and are retried every unlock; checked on every log verification), §10.2 (the human's clients never seal to an agent in `revokedAgents`, `revocation-pending`). A space hidden by a contacts rollback or fork is accepted: R1, R9. A renewable `notAfter` on `agent-grant` is deferred as Q14. |
| S2-3 | 2 | Medium | A contacts fork that keeps each device on its own branch is undetectable, so unpins and agent revocations don't propagate. | Accepted as R1 (now covering forks on every platform, with what doesn't propagate listed), §10.3 **What marks can't catch**, Q5. Partial mitigation: each device shows the record's version and `contactsHash` prefix for comparison. S1-8 reclassified as partly fixed. |
| S2-4 | 2 | Low | A partial-scope member can be made to sign on top of a fabricated pending entry. | Fixed: §6.8 (a client never appends while it holds pending entries; it waits or reports `stale-log`). The availability cost is stated; revocations held up this way stay "revocation pending". |
| S2-5 | 2 | Low | Enrollment expiry rests on the server-supplied `expiresAt`. | Fixed: §12.1 (CLI deadline is the earlier of `expiresAt` and its own request time plus 10 minutes; the human's client bounds claim to approval the same way). |
| S2-6 | 2 | Low | §13 says destination records are both v1 and v2. | Fixed: §13 (v2; `sourceRecordHash` over the source record's own signed bytes, v1 or v2 by `protocol`). Same as M-25 item 1 and E2-L5. |
| S2-7 | 2 | Low | The claim that the rekey closes backdating for drafts and folder names is false. | Fixed: R8, Q13 and §9.5 corrected (backdating is open for every record kind). Added: heads marked "by a former member" or "with a replaced key" need the person's confirmation before being built on or re-saved (§5.8 rule 5). Per-subject head marks are noted in Q13 as the way to close it. Same as M-16. |
| S2-8 | 2 | Low | §5.8 rule 3's predecessor fetch is unspecified when the predecessor is unavailable. | Fixed: §5.8 rule 3 (the server serves `basedOn` records without payload to everyone who can fetch the successor; a missing or unverifiable predecessor leaves the record unchecked, never valid), §6.9 unchecked case 4, R3. |
| M-15 | 2 | High | Agent revocation can report success while a space still seals to the revoked agent; a browser contacts rollback can also un-revoke an agent. | Fixed with S2-2: §10.4 mark before agent grant, §12.5 steps 3, 4 and 6, §10.2. The rollback and fork consequences are named in R1 and R9. |
| M-16 | 2 | Medium | The rekey doesn't close draft and folder-name backdating, because `basedOn` is attacker-chosen. | Fixed with S2-7: R8, Q13, §9.5 corrected; §5.8 rule 5 (confirmation before building on or re-saving a marked head). Backdating itself stays accepted (R8). |
| M-17 | 2 | Medium | `owner-remove` leaves the removed owner's grants, group memberships and agents in force. | Fixed: §6.7 **Removing an owner** (removal from the space is the default: the batch revokes the target's grants and memberships and their agent grants; demotion is an explicit choice showing remaining access), §6.5 (`owner-remove` ends ownership only), §6.8 (server refuses a batch that leaves access without `demotionConfirmed`), §9.5 (items the target can still open aren't rekeyed). A cascade in replay was rejected: demotion is legitimate, and explicit entries keep each access change visible with its own rekey scope. |
| M-18 | 2 | Medium | One bad binding by an owner bricks the log for everyone who pinned the target, and locks the target out. | Partly fixed: §6.6 (pin and binding disagreement affects that ID only: no sealing to it, signatures through the pin, signer named), §6.7 rule 1 (a conflicting binding, or one whose keys fail §4.1, makes that `(id, keyVersion)` conflicted instead of the log invalid), §4.4 (unresolved references), §8.3. The target's lock-out at that key version is accepted until key rotation (Q7): re-binding the same version would make every signature's key depend on its seq. |
| M-19 | 2 | Medium | The admission rule blocks agents of non-owners from sealing indefinitely. | Fixed: §6.7 (an agent's grants are endorsed by its pinned human's signature; its anchor must still be at or after its human's admission seq; the block for humans is stated as space-wide, with the reason), §2.3. |
| M-20 | 2 | Low | Clients enforce enrollment expiry against the server-supplied `expiresAt`. | Fixed with S2-5: §12.1, and §12.2 step 1 (fresh keys per request, never reused after expiry). |
| M-21 | 2 | Low | "Pinned person" versus "pinned key" for vouches and grants is undefined across key versions. | Fixed: §6.7 (every pinned check before signing a binding or vouch compares the full identity reference; a pin at another version doesn't count), §6.6. |
| M-22 | 2 | Low | The cascade and owner-remove vouch rules are batch rules replay can't check; a skipped cascade lets agent grants revive. | Fixed: §6.5 **Agent grants follow their human** (replay makes an agent grant permanently inactive once its human's access falls short; inactive grants count nowhere), §8.2, §8.4 `accessTo`, §9.5 item 4. The vouch batch rule fails closed without a replay check (§6.7). |
| M-23 | 2 | Low | Group entries' bodies are both "by group" and "required for everyone". | Fixed: §6.9 (the `scopeNodeId = null` bullet excludes entries with a `groupSeq`). |
| M-24 | 2 | Low | Opener check 6 (now check 7) can run at an envelope `authorizedSeq` older than the record's. | Fixed: §9.3 check 3 (envelope `authorizedSeq` at or above the record's; equal when the granter is entitled only as author). Same as E2-L6 (d). |
| M-25 | 2 | Low | Contradictions: §13 record version; agents and contacts routes; "full history" for editors. | Fixed: §13 (v2, `sourceRecordHash` by `protocol`), §12.4 (read-only routes agents may call; contacts writes and appends stay human-only apart from `unpublish`), §15 (editors keep only the history they hold keys for; check 5 has no editor exemption). |
| E2-F1 | 2 | High | Log-less spaces have no client sealing rule, so the server chooses recipients from the person's contacts; a member can be downgraded to a "legacy" view of a shared space. | Fixed: §16.4 **Log-less spaces** (log-less after a mark, or with any post-genesis artefact, is `log-rollback`; only the Milestone 1 owner, by the backfill ownership check, treats it as legacy, everyone else gets `log-missing`; sealing there is exactly `{self}`, never to agents, and offered as part of the backfill), §5.7, §2.3, §16.5 (separate legacy sealing function). |
| E2-L1 | 2 | Low | The served legacy manifests are "sorted" without saying how; base64url text order isn't byte order. | Fixed: §16.2 (served bytewise over decoded values; clients ignore the order, decode, refuse duplicates and sort the bytes themselves), §16.5 vector. |
| E2-L2 | 2 | Low | Duplicate JSON member names aren't specified. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 7 (malformed in every signed, hashed or decrypted object), §3.2, §16.5. |
| E2-L3 | 2 | Low | "Anchored, no flags" doesn't fix what `$` means across regex dialects. | Fixed: §3.2 (patterns match the whole string; dialect equivalents named; trailing `\n` negative vector). |
| E2-L4 | 2 | Low | The signature vectors should include a mixed-order `R`. | Fixed: §3.1 (mixed-order `R` named, libsodium refuses it), §16.5 vectors, plus a mixed-order public key for §4.1. |
| E2-L5 | 2 | Low | §13 contradicts itself on the destination record version. | Fixed with S2-6 and M-25: §13. |
| E2-L6 | 2 | Low | Version and field selection for v2 envelopes and agent references is loose. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 8 (strict parsers; no `version` is v1, `2` is v2, anything else fails closed; same for record `protocol`), §1, §9.3 (envelope `spaceId` comes from the record, not a JSON property; check 3 for `authorizedSeq`), §4.2 and §6.4 (agent references have `keyVersion = 1`), §4.4 (`keyHash` uses the reference's own `keyVersion`). |
| S3-1 | 3 | High | A freeze of the log at the pre-grant contacts mark hides an `agent-grant` from a later revocation, which reports "revoked" while every other member keeps sealing to the agent. | Fixed: §10.4 **Intent before agent grant** (the client signs the grant first and records an intent `{spaceId, agentId, seq, entryHash}` in a confirmed contacts version before appending; re-signs and rewrites it after a lost race; a verified log below an intent's `seq` leaves the space "revocation pending" with `stale-log`; a different entry at that `seq` spends it; settled intents are dropped), §10.2 `agentGrantIntents`, §12.2 step 5, §12.5 steps 3, 4 and 6, R9 (claims corrected: a freeze could hide the grant before; only a contacts rollback or fork can now). *Round 4 correction (M-38, E4-F2, S4-3):* the "only a contacts rollback or fork" claim was false. A log fork at the intent's `seq` also spent the intent, so the "spent" rule is narrowed (§10.4) and the claim corrected. |
| S3-2 | 3 | High | The server can shrink a non-owner's view of their own access by withholding undetectable bodies, which redacts their own `agent-grant`, so revocation reports "revoked". | Fixed: §6.9 (every entry whose `signerId` is the caller is a required body; for a human, agent grants and revokes for its agents; the direct-grant case listed among undetectable withholdings; the "never adds a recipient" justification corrected), §12.5 step 4 (an own `agent-grant` header without a body is `missing-body`, "revocation pending", never "revoked"), §12.5 step 3 (revocation revokes lapsed grants too, since a hidden access path can make a grant look lapsed to the human alone). Round 3 accepted one remainder in R9 **Spaces it has left**: a server that hides all of the human's remaining access from one device. *Round 4 correction (S4-1):* that remainder wasn't a corner case. Combined with M-33's "no access left" completion it reopened this finding for a single-path member or a demoted owner. The completion is removed, a former member can still revoke their own agents' grants (§6.8 **Former members**), and the scopes of the caller's own entries are roots, so every access path keeping their agent grant in force is a required body (§6.9). No remainder is accepted now. |
| S3-3 | 3 | Medium | Any member can permanently conflict another person's agent with an unresolved `agent-grant`, and log validity depends on the directory. | Fixed: §6.4 (the `agent-grant` body carries the agent's two public keys), §6.5 (replay checks §4.1, `keyHash` and the ID's derivation from the signer from the body alone; any failure is `log-invalid`; the "unresolved but counts" clause is gone), §4.4, §6.6, §6.7 rule 1 (agents can't be conflicted). |
| S3-4 | 3 | Low | §4.1 contradicts §6.7 rule 1 on bad keys, and the conflict state depends on what the directory serves. | Fixed: §4.1 (a refused key leaves the reference unresolved, never live, never `log-invalid` or conflicted), §4.4, §6.5 rule 6 and §6.7 (conflict is defined on `keyHash` alone; no structural verdict depends on resolution). Same as M-31 and E3-L1. |
| S3-5 | 3 | Low | A colluding non-owner's covering signature endorses a fabricated pending header. | Fixed: §6.8 **Verified head** (only an entry signed by an owner, the client itself, or an agent's human counts as covering), §6.9. Same as M-28. |
| S3-6 | 3 | Low | An empty space satisfies the legacy ownership check vacuously. | Fixed: §16.2 and §16.4 rule 2 (the space-metadata chain must be non-empty and rooted in a v1 record the client authored; an empty listing fails). |
| S3-7 | 3 | Low | A client isn't told when its own ID is bound to a foreign key. | Fixed: §6.6 (reported as an attack naming the signing owner; the client refuses to sign in that space). |
| S3-8 | 3 | Low | R2 names only removed owners, but any superseded or revoked key can fork before its supersession. | Fixed: R2 (widened to any member removed or re-keyed after a compromise; a rotated owner's old key anchors on such a branch), §6.7 **Superseded versions** ("on the log the client follows"). |
| M-26 | 3 | Medium | "Active" has two meanings, so revocation, vouchers and admission can skip pending agent grants. | Fixed: §6.5 (the replay state is renamed **lapsed**, `lapsedSeq`; **in force** means unrevoked and not lapsed, pending or active; "active" is only the §9 state), §6.7, §8.2, §8.4, §9.5, §12.5 (revocation covers every grant, pending, active or lapsed), §16.5. CONTEXT.md's "Pending access" already uses the §9 meaning and is unchanged. |
| M-27 | 3 | Medium | Owner removal leaves memberships in groups with no grants yet; a later grant to that group lets the removed owner back in. | Fixed: §6.7 **Removing an owner** (the batch removes every membership), §6.8 (the server refuses a batch that leaves any access-giving entry or membership without `demotionConfirmed`), §6.7 **Grant previews list group members** (marking members removed as owners or revoked since joining). |
| M-28 | 3 | Low | Any colluding non-owner signer can "cover" a made-up trailing header. | Fixed with S3-5: §6.8, §6.9. |
| M-29 | 3 | Low | One owner can bind any higher key version: the person is locked out, and people who pinned them accept the new key's signatures as theirs. | Partly fixed: §6.6 (for a client with an older pin, signatures at the newer version are attributed to "an unverified new key" of the person, never plainly to them). The lock-out is accepted and named in §6.7 rule 2 and Q7. Rejected: limiting increases to exactly `current + 1`, because a person can rotate more than once between two grants in a space, and the honest re-grant would then be impossible. |
| M-30 | 3 | Low | Whether a binding is conflicted depends on the view; a partial-scope member can treat a conflicting binding as live. | Partly fixed: §6.7 rule 1 ("for any client that sees both bindings"; owners' clients refuse to sign a conflicting binding), §6.8 (the server refuses a write whose audience includes a member conflicted in its full view), §6.9 (binding rules over delivered bodies only; unchecked case 5). Accepted: a partial-scope member can still miss a conflict made by a misbehaving owner, who could grant someone openly anyway. Same as E3-L2. |
| M-31 | 3 | Low | §4.1 still makes a refused binding key `log-invalid`. | Fixed with S3-4: §4.1. |
| M-32 | 3 | Low | The agent-revoke cascade is worded loosely: lapsed grants block every later batch, moves can't carry agent-revokes, and order within a batch matters. | Fixed: §6.8 (the server requires a revoke only for grants this batch makes lapse; **Order within a batch**: grants before revokes), §6.5 (lapse is checked after each entry), §9.5 item 4, §9.6 steps 2 and 4 (the move request carries the cascade's agent-revokes). |
| M-33 | 3 | Low | "Revocation pending" can stay up for ever, both in spaces the human has left and where only an owner could help. | Fixed: §12.5 step 7 (a request the server shows to owners, who append an ordinary `agent-revoke`; any owner entry also covers pending entries and fills an intent's `seq`), §6.8. The request carries no authority and the server can drop it (R9). *Round 4 correction (S4-1, M-39):* the first part of the round 3 fix, counting a space done when the human had no access left, could never fire with an honest server and fired only under attack. It is removed. Spaces the human has left are now handled by letting a former member fetch the log and revoke their own agents' grants (§6.8 **Former members**, §12.5 steps 3 and 4). The request is now signed (M-42). |
| M-34 | 3 | Low | Agents meeting a marked head have no defined behaviour, so an automated agent can launder a backdated head. | Fixed: §5.8 rule 5 (an agent refuses to build on, re-save or publish a marked head, with no override; only a human's client confirms). |
| M-35 | 3 | Low | Re-signing an owner-removal batch after `stale-head` happens silently. | Fixed: §6.8 **Losing a race** (a batch that removes access is never re-signed silently; the client shows every entry appended since the confirmed head, marking those by the target). |
| M-36 | 3 | Low | Which bodies an agent gets is undefined for a `null`-scope agent grant whose human isn't space-wide. | Fixed: §6.9 (an agent's scope roots are the intersection of its agent grants' scopes with its human's roots; the server delivers the bodies the human's roots depend on, which the agent can't check for omission). |
| E3-F1 | 3 | Medium | A replaced key keeps full authority in a space until the re-grant, even for clients that have pinned the newer key, and can vouch for new recipients. | Fixed with the stricter rule: §6.6 (a client that has pinned a newer version than the space binds refuses to seal anywhere in the space, `identity-key-changed`, and signs there only the revoke and re-grant), §6.7 **Superseded versions**, Q7 (rotation should ship with `key-update`, since a sole owner can't re-grant themself). Latent in Milestone 2: every human is at key version 1. |
| E3-L1 | 3 | Low | §4.1 contradicts §6.5 and §6.7 on bad keys, and log validity depends on directory answers. | Fixed with S3-4 and S3-3: §4.1, §4.4, §6.5 (agent derivation checked from the body), §6.7. |
| E3-L2 | 3 | Low | `keyVersions` and the binding rules are computed from delivered bodies only, so partial-scope members disagree with everyone else. | Fixed with M-30: §6.9 (stated, unchecked case 5), §6.7 rule 1 reworded, owners' clients refuse to sign a conflicting binding. The disagreement itself is accepted for partial-scope members. |
| E3-L3 | 3 | Low | A duplicate-detecting JSON parser built with plain assignment turns a `__proto__` member into a prototype. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 7 (own data properties only; `__proto__` is an ordinary name, kept in payloads and `malformed` in signed objects), §16.5 vector. |
| E3-L4 | 3 | Low | How decrypted payload bytes are decoded isn't specified, and the as-built decoder is lenient. | Fixed: §3.2 **Decoding payloads** (fatal UTF-8, no BOM, `malformed` otherwise), §16.5 (`parseJson` change and both vectors). |
| E3-L5 | 3 | Low | X25519 public keys have more than one encoding, so one key has several `keyHash`es, fingerprints and agent IDs. | Fixed: §4.1 (refuse the top bit set or a value at or above 2^255 − 19, with `bad-key`), §16.5 vectors. |
| E3-L6 | 3 | Low | Clients can't check that the legacy placements manifest has one entry per node. | Fixed: §16.2 and §3.5 (`legacy-placements/v1` adds `nodeIds`, the sorted legacy node IDs; duplicates or a count mismatch are `malformed`; served with the manifest), R3 (members learn the legacy node IDs), §16.5 vector. *Round 4 correction (E4-F1):* two independent lists didn't bind an ID to its hash, so a padded ID list still allowed two placements for one node. | S4-1 | 4 | High | The "no access left" completion of an agent revocation fires only when the server hides the human's access, so S3-2 is back for a single-path member or a demoted owner: the client reports "revoked" while everyone else seals to the agent. | Fixed: §12.5 step 4, R9 and §6.9 (the completion is deleted; a space is done only when every `agent-grant` for the agent in the verified log is revoked, with admission endorsed and no contested intent), §6.8 **Former members** and §6.5 (a human with no access who signed an `agent-grant` may fetch the log, up to the head while any of their agent grants is unrevoked and otherwise up to the later of their last access and their last revoked grant, and may append `agent-revoke`s of their own agents' grants; the server's "active access" rule has an exception for this entry only), §6.9 (with S4-2, every access path keeping the human's agent grant in force is a required body), §6.7 (no admission seq without access), R3. | S4-2 | 4 | Medium | Self-signed bodies are replayed, but the bodies their access checks need aren't required, so an honest member can get `log-invalid`, or implementations split on "unchecked". | Fixed: §6.9 (scope roots include the `scopeNodeId` of every non-null-scope entry the caller signed: `agent-grant`, `agent-revoke`, `unpublish` for a human, `unpublish` for an agent; **Historical checks** corrected and the `unpublish` rule added to it; with an honest server a self-signed entry always has the bodies its check needs), R3 (grant bodies at nodes where the caller once acted). |
| S4-3 | 4 | Medium | The revoked agent can sign the competing entry at its own grant's intent `seq` on a log fork, so the intent counts as spent; the "only a contacts rollback or fork" claim is false. | Fixed with M-38: §10.4 (an intent is spent only with the client's own record of a lost race, `lostTo`, and the competing or a later entry signed by an owner other than the human; otherwise it is contested, `log-fork` is reported when no lost race is recorded, and the space stays "revocation pending"; step 5 retries the post-append mark write at every unlock until confirmed; a mark settles an intent only from a log holding the intended grant), §10.2, R2, R9, §12.5 step 3. |
| S4-4 | 4 | Low | Admission, the newer-pin block and the own-ID false-binding block also stop the human revoking their own agent. | Fixed: §6.6 **Signing blocks never stop an agent revocation**, §6.6 bullets 3 and 4, §6.7 admission (the client may sign `agent-revoke`s of its own agents' grants under every signing block, never over pending entries), §12.5 step 4 (the space counts as done only with admission endorsed). |
| S4-5 | 4 | Low | Space marks have no retention rule, but settled intents rely on them, so a single `not-found` could prune a space from revocation. | Fixed: §10.2 (a client never removes a space mark). |
| S4-6 | 4 | Low | An append answered with an error can still land later, and nothing tells the client to look for it. | Fixed: §6.8 **Unknown outcomes** (treated as possibly applied; looked for at the next verification; the person is told, and offered the revoke for a grant, membership or agent grant). |
| S4-7 | 4 | Low | A removal batch is re-signed after a lost race rather than recomputed, so access the target added in the race survives. | Fixed: §6.8 **Losing a race** (the batch is recomputed from the new state and the difference shown before signing). |
| M-37 | 4 | Critical | Pins carry no name, so the server and any member the sharer has pinned can redirect a grant or `owner-add` to that member by relabelling directory entries. | Fixed: §10.2 (each contact stores the `label`, the name and email the person confirmed while verifying, inside the signed, encrypted payload), §11.3 (the label is confirmed during the comparison), §11.5 (pinned identities are named only by their labels at every decision point, directory text is marked as from the server, and a lookup or directory answer that disagrees with stored labels stops with `contact-label-mismatch` and offers a fresh comparison; vouched co-members are named by server text plus the vouching owner's label, which is attribution only and never affects sealing), §6.7 (previews use labels; owners are offered to unpin someone they remove), §10.4 (merge rule for labels), §2.3, §16.5, CONTEXT.md. | M-38 | 4 | Medium | An access-log fork at an intent's `seq` marks the intent spent and hides the grant from revocation; the "only R1" claim is false. | Fixed: §10.4 (spent needs a recorded lost race and the competing or a later entry signed by another owner; otherwise contested, the space stays "revocation pending", and the owner request names the intent's `entryHash`; a client that finds its own entry at the `seq` after `stale-head` treats it as appended), §6.8 **Losing a race**, §12.5 steps 3, 4 and 7, R2, R9. | M-39 | 4 | Low | With an honest server the "no access left" completion can't be reached, so spaces the human has left stay "revocation pending" for ever. | Fixed with S4-1: §6.8 **Former members** (former members fetch the log up to a defined point and revoke; a `not-found` after the space is done doesn't reopen it), §12.5 step 4. *Round 5 correction (M-45, S5-3):* the route served only humans who had signed an `agent-grant`, so spaces left without one stayed pending; a space is now on an agent's revocation list only if the human's records name the agent there, and the "doesn't reopen" sentence is removed. |
| M-40 | 4 | Low | The newer-pin block also fires where the old key version has no access left, and the only way out is to re-grant someone the owners removed. | Fixed: §6.6 (the space-wide block applies only while an access-giving entry for the older version is in force; otherwise it is `identity-key-changed` for that ID only), Q7. |
| M-41 | 4 | Low | Cross-space publication carries a marked head into another space as unmarked content, and the agent rule doesn't cover it. | Fixed: §13 step 1 (the source must pass §5.8; `authority-invalid` or unchecked sources are refused; a marked source needs the human's confirmation and an agent refuses it), §5.8 rule 5. |
| M-42 | 4 | Low | Revocation requests are unsigned, so anyone can get owners to revoke someone else's agent. | Fixed: §12.5 step 7 and §3.5 (`owner-request/v1`, signed by the requesting human; the owner's client acts only if it verifies under the key of the human the agent derives from, shown by that human's label; replays do no harm). |
| M-43 | 4 | Low | A non-owner can keep partial-scope members' logs pending indefinitely, which blocks their unpublish and other appends. | Partly fixed: §6.8 (the abuse is named next to the availability cost; it is attributable, and owners can remove the member), §12.5 step 7 (`owner-request/v1` also carries `unpublish`, which an owner signs as an ordinary entry). | E4-F1 | 4 | Medium | `nodeIds` doesn't bind IDs to hashes, so a padded manifest still carries two placements for one node; `nodeIds` parsing and empty lists are unspecified. | Fixed: §16.2 (the manifest is a list of `(nodeId, legacyNodeHash)` pairs sorted by node ID; `nodeHashes` is in that order; a placement is valid only if its node's pair holds its hash; served as `legacyNodes` pairs, which clients check, sort and join themselves, so no joined string is ever parsed), §7.2 rule 0, §3.5, §16.5 vectors (padded made-up ID, `count = 0`, out-of-order). |
| E4-F2 | 4 | Medium | A forking server can make an agent-grant intent look spent, by answering `stale-head` and showing the human another member's entry at that `seq`. | Fixed with M-38: §10.4 (a lost race spends an intent only after an owner other than the human signs over the competing entry), R2, R9. |
| E4-L1 | 4 | Low | §3.2's Python claim is wrong, and payloads can still split implementations at the JSON layer (lone-surrogate escapes, non-integer numerals). | Fixed: §3.2 (decoders decode strict UTF-8 themselves before parsing, with the Python form; after parsing, every payload string must be well-formed and the payload integer fields follow §3.3 rule 6), §16.5 (vectors for UTF-16LE, `"\ud800"` and `"size": 1.0`). |
| E4-L2 | 4 | Low | §6.5 rule 6 says §4.1 never affects replay, but the `agent-grant` rule makes it `log-invalid`; replay validity of group names is unstated. | Fixed: §6.5 rule 6 (only directory-resolved references are exempt; `agent-grant` body keys are checked by its kind rule), §6.5 `group-create` (a name breaking §3.3 rules 1 or 5, checked on the parsed string, is `log-invalid`). |
| E4-L3 | 4 | Low | `AttachmentRef.blobHash` is plain `Base64` as built, though §3.2 requires `Hash32`. | Fixed: §16.5 (`blobHash` and `plainHash` use `Hash32`). |
| S5-1 | 5 | High | A person's own agents are named only by server text, so the server can swap two agents' names and have the human revoke the wrong agent, or sign an `agent-grant` for the wrong one. | Fixed: §10.2 (each `agents` entry is `{enrollment, label: {name, enrolledAt}}`; the name is typed by the person while comparing the safety code at approval, never filled in from server or CLI text, and `enrolledAt` comes from the approving client's clock; both are signed and encrypted with the payload; names are distinct among all the human's agents), §10.4 (enrolling an agent whose name another entry has raises `contacts-conflict` and offers a rename; a rename change), §11.5 (the human's own agents are named only by label, `enrolledAt` and a short ID at the agent list, revocation, `agent-grant` previews at approval and for later scope additions, the "revocation pending" list, owner requests the client sends, and attribution; other people's agents are server text plus "agent of" their human's label, and no decision uses that name; any server text about an agent is marked as from the server), §6.7 and §9.6 (previews), §12.2 step 5, §12.3 (`wikistow whoami` prints the label and ID), §12.5 step 4, §2.3, §16.5, CONTEXT.md. |
| S5-2 | 5 | Medium | Roots from the caller's own entries pull in every descendant, so a member keeps learning a folder's sharing for ever after losing it, and the text and the mechanism disagree. | Fixed with M-44: §6.9 (own-entry scopes `S` add only their node and its current and past ancestors to `N`, never descendants; grant roots keep the full rule), R3, §6.8 **Former members**, §16.5. |
| S5-3 | 5 | Low | Every space the human left without granting an agent stays "revocation pending" for every revocation, for ever; "done doesn't reopen" contradicts "no storage". | Fixed with M-45: §10.2 (`grantedAgents`), §12.5 steps 3 and 4 (only spaces whose records name the agent are on the list; done is recomputed, and the "doesn't reopen" sentence is removed), R9. |
| S5-4 | 5 | Low | Intents that can never resolve: retries that re-sign, crashes before any answer, and spaces with one owner. | Fixed with M-47: §6.8 **Retrying** (a retry after an unknown outcome resends the identical signed entries, never re-signs at that `seq` while the device holds them), §10.4 step 4 (`lostTo` is also recorded when an unknown-outcome append finds another entry at its `seq`, unless the device saw it succeed), §10.4 **Confirming a lost race** (the human confirms the competing entry by an out-of-band comparison with a pinned owner, or with any pinned member in a sole-owner space; recorded as `confirmedAt`, which spends the intent with a matching `lostTo`), §10.2, R2, R9 (sole-owner limitation), §12.5 step 7. |
| S5-5 | 5 | Low | A "possibly applied" append is checked only once, but the server can land it whenever it chooses. | Fixed: §6.8 **Unknown outcomes** (the append stays possibly applied until a verified log holds its first entry or a different entry at that `seq`; it is recorded in the private payload's `unresolvedAppends` so it outlives the session, and rechecked at every verification and before each write in that space), §10.2, §10.4. |
| S5-6 | 5 | Low | Labels can still come from the server: without a lookup, and when re-comparing after a mismatch. | Fixed with M-50: §11.3 step 2 (the label email is always typed by the person, including when verifying an admitting owner or an owner named in `log-unendorsed`; directory text may be shown beside the field, marked, but is never copied in), §11.5 (a relabel after a mismatch is typed the same way, old and new labels are shown side by side, and the new email is read aloud during the comparison). |
| S5-7 | 5 | Low | A grant to a group is a sharing decision made on server text, though §11.5 says none exists. | Fixed with M-46: §6.7 **Previews that add recipients don't use server names** (unpinned members listed first as unverified, by values the server can't choose, with an offer to verify), §11.5 (the "never affects who is sealed to" claim is corrected). |
| S5-8 | 5 | Low | Legacy placement verdicts disagree between §7.2 and §16.2, and "refuses both" lets an invalid placement disable a valid one. | Fixed: §7.2 rule 0 (a placement with no pair is `placement-invalid` for that placement only; a second placement failing rule 0 or its signature is ignored and reported; "refuses both" applies only when both pass, and is reported as an attack), §16.2 (the unpaired placement is no longer a manifest defect). |
| S5-9 | 5 | Low | R9 omits a fork at the human's own `agent-revoke`. | Fixed: R9 (**A fork at the revoke itself**: "revoked" assumes no fork at the revoke; it costs a current member a freeze, costs nothing for a former member whose grants have already lapsed, and is detectable only by later fork signals or out-of-band comparison until Q3). |

| M-45 | 5 | Medium | The former-member route serves only people who signed an `agent-grant`, so every other space the human has left stays "revocation pending" for ever; "done doesn't reopen" contradicts "no storage". | Fixed with the reviewer's second option: §10.2 (each space mark carries `grantedAgents`, the agents the human has signed an `agent-grant` for there, from verified logs and from intents as they settle; never cleared), §10.4 (step 5 adds the agent to the mark; an intent settles only on a mark naming its agent; merges take the union), §12.5 step 3 (a space is on an agent's list only if its mark names the agent or it holds an intent for it; other spaces may be checked, and join the list if a grant is found, but the server never takes one off), step 4 (done isn't stored and the "doesn't reopen" sentence is removed; an honest former-member route keeps a done space checkable), step 6, R9, CONTEXT.md. Owner-attested emails in binding bodies were not added: the first option removes server names from these decisions without new body fields. |

| M-49 | 5 | Low | Former members get the log but not the tree, so they can't compute `N` or the historical checks. | Fixed: §6.8 **Former members** (the route also serves the placements of every node in their `N`, computed with the M-44 rule, and the legacy manifest), §6.9 **Historical checks**, §16.5. |
| M-50 | 5 | Low | The label name is pre-filled from the directory, and names are never checked for duplicates or required to show with the email. | Fixed: §11.3 step 2 (the name starts empty or from the typed email's local part, never from directory text; a name another pin's label has stops for confirmation), §10.4 (a pin or relabel whose name another label has raises `contacts-conflict`), §11.5 (decision points show label name and email together). |
| M-51 | 5 | Low | Labels sit in the shared payload, so every enrolled agent holds the human's labelled address book. | Fixed: §10.2 (contact labels move to the private payload as `labels`, keyed by the pin's `{id, keyHash}`), §10.4, §11.3 step 4, CONTEXT.md.
