| M-52 | 5 | Low | An editor without publish rights at the source can publish a withdrawn version into another space. | Fixed: §13 step 1 and **Server checks** (a published version outside the page's current reader history, or any version of a currently unpublished page, needs publish rights at the source page, like a draft). |
| E5-L1 | 5 | Low | The group-name replay rule lets the server turn a lone-surrogate substitution into `log-invalid` blamed on an owner, and serde reaches a different verdict. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 1 (well-formedness is a parse-time property of every signed or hashed JSON object; an ill-formed string makes it `malformed` before any hash or signature check; a `malformed` body is handled as undelivered, then `missing-body`, never `log-invalid`), §6.5 `group-create` (only a well-formed name breaking the length or control-character rule is `log-invalid`), §16.5 vector. |
| E5-L2 | 5 | Low | The JSON grammar for payloads isn't pinned; `NaN`, `Infinity` and out-of-range numerals split implementations, and the integer field list is incomplete. | Fixed: §3.2 (RFC 8259 JSON; Python uses a raising `parse_constant`; every number token must be finite as a double; the integer rule covers every field this document types as an integer, naming the keyring's `public.keyVersion` and `wikistow.publication.format`; vectors with `NaN` and `1e400` in `extensions`), §16.5. |
| E5-L3 | 5 | Low | `issuedAt` has no shape, §3.3 rules 1, 6 and 8 don't name owner requests, and per-action null patterns aren't validity rules. | Fixed: §3.2 **Timestamps** (one `Timestamp` pattern for every timestamp field), §3.3 rules 1, 6 and 8 (owner requests named), §12.5 step 7 (any other combination of `action` and null fields is `malformed`; `subjectId` matches the agent-ID shape or the ID regex by action), §16.5 vectors. |
| E5-L4 | 5 | Low | The label email's stored form and comparison rule differ between sections and from the server's normalization. | Fixed: §10.2 (`email` matches the contracts `Email` shape and is stored trimmed with ASCII letters lowercased; `name` follows rule 5; a label breaking either is treated as missing, so the pin is an unlabelled contact), §11.5 (every comparison is exact on the normalized form), §16.5 vectors for whitespace and case. |
| E5-L5 | 5 | Low | A former member's `agent-revoke` signed through a binding that is no longer live stops every co-member who hasn't pinned them at `log-unendorsed`. | Fixed: §6.6 (an `agent-revoke` signed by the revoked grant's human is accepted through their binding even when no voucher counts, if it isn't conflicted, is at their current version and resolves; it only removes access), §12.5 step 7 (owner requests from that human likewise), §16.5. |
| E5-L6 | 5 | Low | The identity of an agent-grant intent for merging isn't defined, and `lostTo` isn't keyed. | Fixed: §10.2 (an intent is identified by `(spaceId, entryHash)`; duplicates, or a disagreeing `agentId` or `seq`, are `malformed`; `lostTo` and `confirmedAt` are required and may be `null`), §10.4 merge table. |
| S6-1 | 6 | High | The lost-race confirmation compares only a 48-bit `entryHash` prefix, and the partner need not have verified the entry, so a fork passes it and a revoked agent stays a recipient everywhere but on the human's branch. | Fixed by removal: §6.4 and §6.5 (`agent-revoke` names the agent, with `humanId` and the agent's keys so replay checks the derivation from the log alone; scope `null`, so every member gets the body; it ends every grant for the agent, pending, active or lapsed, and later `agent-grant`s for it are `log-invalid`), §10.4 (intents, `lostTo`, `confirmedAt`, contested, spent and settled states, and **Confirming a lost race** removed; the client records the agent in the mark's `grantedAgents`, with the mark at or above the head it grants from, in a confirmed contacts version before appending), §10.2, §12.5 steps 3, 4, 6 and 7 (one revoke per space; done when the verified log holds an `agent-revoke` for the agent, or the human has no access and the log holds no grant for it; owner requests drop `entryHash`), §6.8 **Former members** (any former member, capped at the end of their access), §6.9, §8.5, §9.5 (rekey scope; the cascade revokes the agent only when its human is left with no access-giving entry), §9.6, §3.5, R2, R9, R3, §16.5, §18, CONTEXT.md. Accepted: a grant on a branch the human never sees is a fork (R2); a device that got a success answer for the grant reports it as `log-fork` (§6.8). Consequence: an agent revoked in a space, including by the cascade when its human is removed, can't be granted there again; a returning human grants a newly enrolled agent (§9.5). |
| S6-2 | 6 | High | `unresolvedAppends` records only a batch's first entry, and the batch counts as landed when that entry appears, so a server that applies only a prefix keeps a removed owner as a recipient while the remover is told the removal landed. | Fixed: §6.8 **Unknown outcomes** (every entry is recorded; landed means every entry is present in order; failed means a different entry at the first `seq`; partial means a strict prefix, reported as a possible attack and completed, by resending the held remainder or recomputing it under **Losing a race**, before the change is reported done), **A success answer** (the mark advances to the batch's last entry, so a missing entry is `log-rollback` or `log-fork`), **Order within a batch** (a suffix can be dropped), §10.2, §10.4 merge table, §16.5. |
| S6-3 | 6 | Medium | A merge erases the "this device saw the append succeed" alarm, because the success answer isn't recorded in the intent. | Fixed by removal with S6-1: intents and `lostTo` are gone. A success answer is now persisted as the space mark, advanced to the batch's last entry (§6.8 **A success answer**); marks merge by the higher `seq` and raise `log-fork` on a different hash at the same `seq` (§10.4). |
| S6-4 | 6 | Low | The confirmation shows the log head but doesn't require it to match. | Fixed by removal with S6-1. |
| S6-5 | 6 | Low | §6.7 and §11.5 contradict on how other people's agents appear in recipient-adding previews, and group-grant previews omit agents whose access grows. | Fixed: §6.7 **Previews that add recipients don't use server names** (every gaining agent of another person, including through a group grant, is listed as "agent `<short ID>` of" its human, named by stored label or the unverified-member form, with no server text), §9.6 step 1, §11.5. |
| M-53 | 6 | Medium | In a sole-owner space, any pinned member can confirm a lost race, including the member who signed the competing entry, and so hide the human's agent grant from revocation. | Fixed by removal with S6-1: no confirmation remains, and nothing another member says changes whether a space is done. A sole owner who can't append in a space has nobody to ask (R9). |
| M-54 | 6 | Low | Confirmation clears intents whose own records prove a fork, and the success answer that should make it `log-fork` isn't persisted. | Fixed by removal with S6-1; the success answer is persisted as the advanced mark (S6-3). |
| M-55 | 6 | Low | `wikistow whoami` is offered as the way to identify a device before revoking, but a compromised host controls its output and holds every agent label. | Fixed: §12.3 (`whoami` identifies only a trustworthy device; when compromise is suspected the interface and the CLI's help say to identify the agent by the label typed at approval, and if in doubt to revoke every agent whose host the person can't vouch for), §11.5. |
| M-56 | 6 | Low | Agents still read the human's pins and `removed`, and may look up every identity the contacts record references, so moving labels doesn't hide the verified-contacts list. | Partly fixed: §12.4 (agents look up only identities their spaces' logs reference; pinned keys come from the record itself, and the server refuses other lookups). Accepted, with §10.2 corrected: pins and `removed` stay in the shared payload, because an agent needs them to anchor logs and to distrust removed keys, so a compromised host learns those IDs and keys, but not their labels or directory names. |
| M-57 | 6 | Low | How other people's agents appear in previews that add recipients is unspecified. | Fixed with S6-5. |
| E6-F1 | 6 | High | The CLI prints server-chosen text (the lookup code, the claimer's name and email) next to the safety-code words, so terminal control sequences can draw the browser's words and hide the real ones, defeating enrollment's only protection against substituted keys. | Fixed: §12.2 step 1 (the CLI MUST check `lookupCode`, `enrollmentId` and `expiresAt` against their exact patterns and refuse the response otherwise), step 3 (server text only marked and escaped, words printed last from local computation), step 4 (the prompt follows the words directly and repeats them), §12.3 **Printing server text** (every server string the CLI prints, including errors, has C0, C1, DEL, U+2028, U+2029 and bidi controls replaced by visible escapes), §11.5 (no server text beside the words in the browser), §16.5 vectors. |
| E6-L1 | 6 | Low | `Timestamp` accepts impossible dates and far-future times, so the 7-day check on unpublish requests differs between implementations and can be bypassed. | Fixed: §3.2 (a real instant from 2000 to 9999, no `24:00` or leap second, checked by re-serializing), §12.5 step 7 (an `issuedAt` more than 10 minutes ahead is refused), §16.5 vectors. |
| E6-L2 | 6 | Low | Within contacts payloads, which defects break the whole record and which one entry is contradictory, duplicates are partly unspecified, and nothing says what a client does when its own record is `contacts-invalid`. | Fixed: §10.2 (**Duplicates**: an identity for every list and a verdict for each; **What breaks the whole payload**: text-level and structural defects, a bad `Timestamp` such as `enrolledAt`, and a missing required property are `malformed`; a well-formed value breaking a label rule degrades one entry), §10.3 **An invalid own record** (the client stops, never writes a version that drops `removed`, `revokedAgents`, `spaceMarks` or `grantedAgents`, and seeds any fresh record from everything it can still read). The merge `malformed` case went with intents. |
| E6-L3 | 6 | Low | The label email's accept pattern lives in a library, "trimmed" isn't defined, and an unnormalized stored email is unspecified. | Fixed: §3.2 **Emails** (the pattern written out; normalization removes only space, tab, LF and CR and lowercases ASCII; other characters such as U+FEFF stay in and cause a mismatch; PR 2 pins zod or tests against the pattern), §10.2 (a stored email not equal to its normalization breaks the label rule), §16.5 vector. |
| E6-L4 | 6 | Low | A `malformed` body caused by the signer's own committed values is blamed on the server as undelivered. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 1 (text-level defects are handled as undelivered; for field-value defects the client checks `bodyHash` over the parsed values, and a match is `log-invalid` naming the signer), §16.5 vector. |
| S7-1 | 7 | High | The second "done" case of §12.5 step 4 (no access left and no `agent-grant` visible) fires on a log frozen at the mark the grant was signed over, with the human's own access hidden, or after a lost race re-signed the grant over a later head, so the agent is reported revoked while its grant stays in force for everyone else. A regression of S4-1, introduced in round 6. | Fixed by removing the case: §12.5 step 4 (a space is done when, and only when, its verified log holds an `agent-revoke` for the agent and a confirmed contacts version holds the mark at or above it; no view of the human's own access counts), step 3 (the client always attempts the revoke when the log lacks it, including as a former member, and asks an owner if the server refuses), R9 (**Spaces it can't reach**, **Spaces it has left**), §6.8 **Former members** (a former member's log never makes a revocation done by itself; the false "can't hide a later grant" sentence removed), §6.9 (the "never invisible to it" claim replaced), §10.4 (the false claim that the mark at `v` covers the grant removed), §9.5 item 4 and §6.8 (the cascade revokes every agent a removed human granted or submitted, so a removal batch normally completes the revocation itself), R2. Checked that no other path counts a space done without the entry: §6.6 signing blocks, §6.7 admission and the former-member route all defer to step 4. |
| S7-2 | 7 | High | A success answer advanced only an in-memory mark; the contacts write that persisted it was best effort and the server could refuse it, so a split batch went unnoticed on the person's other devices and later sessions, and a removed owner stayed a recipient. | Fixed: §6.8 **Write-ahead** (every append, re-signed or resent, is recorded with its full signed entries in `unresolvedAppends` in a confirmed write before it is sent; no send without confirmation), **A success answer** (in-memory only; never resolves the append on its own), **Resolving an append**, **Reporting a change done** (done only when a verified log holds every entry and a confirmed write has removed the batch and advanced the mark; otherwise **unconfirmed**, retried at every unlock, never done), §10.4 **Confirmed writes** (defined once, and the writes that must be confirmed), **When to write**, **Recording an agent grant** (one confirmed write holds the mark, `grantedAgents` and the signed grants), §10.2, §10.3 (`unresolvedAppends` never dropped), §12.2 step 5, §12.5 steps 2 and 4, R1, R2, §16.5. |
| S7-3 | 7 | Low | Former members can append unlimited `agent-revoke`s for never-granted agents, each bumping the whole space's permission version. | Fixed with M7-1 and M7-3: §6.5 and §6.8 (the server refuses a second `agent-revoke` for an agent from anyone, and from a caller without access accepts one only for an eligible agent), §8.5 (an `agent-revoke` bumps permission versions only when it ends a grant in force at `seq − 1`, and then only over the rekey scope). |
| S7-4 | 7 | Low | §6.8 decides the cascade per entry and §9.5 per batch, so two servers accept different batches, and the batch order is only a convention. | Fixed: §6.8 server rules and **Order within a batch** (clients MUST put access-giving entries before access-removing ones, then `vouch`es and cascade revokes; the server refuses another order; the cascade is decided only by the state after the batch's last entry), §9.5 item 4 (the same rule). |
| S7-5 | 7 | Low | "Every string from the server" doesn't clearly cover decrypted content the CLI prints, such as page titles holding OSC 52. | Fixed with E7-M3: §12.3 (every foreign string, decrypted content included; raw output only when stdout isn't a terminal). |
| M7-1 | 7 | Medium | A former member can lift their own log cap at will with a duplicate or made-up `agent-revoke`, learning the head from the fetch response or a `stale-head` answer, and a dormant group membership keeps a former member's view uncapped without any append. | Fixed: §6.8 server rules (the server records agents **submitted** in `agent-grant`s by callers with active access; refuses duplicate `agent-revoke`s from anyone; from a caller without access accepts one only for an **eligible** agent, submitted there and not yet revoked), **Former members** (uncapped only while an eligible agent remains; otherwise capped at the later of the end of access and the first `agent-revoke` of each of their agents; the head in a fetch or `stale-head` answer to them is the cap; **What a former member learns is bounded**), §9.5 item 4 (the cascade fires when a human is left with no effective access at all, so a dormant membership no longer escapes it, and covers submitted agents too), §6.5, R3, §16.5. |
| M7-2 | 7 | Medium | The dead-binding exception contradicts the `removed` rule, so an owner who unpinned a former member stops at `log-unendorsed` at that person's next `agent-revoke`, and is locked out of appending in the space. | Fixed: §6.6 **Removed keys** (`removed` governs trust, not replay: a removed key is never a recipient or a voucher for this client, but replay still verifies its `agent-grant`, `agent-revoke` and `unpublish` entries; its agents are never live for this client unless pinned; owner-only kinds by a removed key still stop at `log-unendorsed`), §6.7 **Voucher lifetime** and **Unpinning after a removal**, §10.2. |
| M7-3 | 7 | Medium | Former members, whom owners can't remove again, can keep partial-scope members' logs pending and bump the whole space's permission versions as often as they like. | Fixed: §6.8 (at most one `agent-revoke` per eligible agent, and an honest removal leaves none; the M-43 rationale now covers former members), §8.5 (no bump for an `agent-revoke` that ends no grant in force). |
| M7-4 | 7 | Medium | The second "done" case ignores the client's own unresolved `agent-grant`, which the server can land after "revoked" is reported; R9 contradicts the case. | Fixed with S7-1: the case is removed, so a held grant can't be outrun; the revoke always ends it, or bars it if it lands later. §12.5 step 3 (always attempt the revoke), R9. |
| M7-5 | 7 | Low | "The mark at `v` keeps the log from being cut below the grant" is false (the grant is at `v + 1`), and re-signed grants aren't recorded again. | Fixed: §10.4 (the claim removed; the mark plays no part in deciding a revocation is done; every re-signed grant is recorded before it is sent, through **Write-ahead**). |
| M7-6 | 7 | Low | Scope roots `R` don't exclude revoked grants or past memberships, so implementations differ on which bodies are served and required. | Fixed: §6.9 (`R` counts unrevoked grants, pending or active, to the caller or to undeleted groups it currently belongs to, at the head served). |
| M7-7 | 7 | Low | A human-signed `agent-grant` for an agent the human never enrolled is added to `grantedAgents` silently, never flagged or revoked. | Fixed: §12.5 step 6 (after re-reading its contacts, the client reports it as an attack, adds the agent to `revokedAgents` in a confirmed write so it is revoked wherever the grant is found, and tells the person to treat the identity key as compromised; it notes a contacts fork could hide a real agent). Key replacement waits for rotation (Q7). |
| E7-M1 | 7 | Medium | `unresolvedAppends` holds only hashes, so a reloaded browser or another device can't complete a partial batch, and a dropped `revoke` keeps a recipient. | Fixed: §10.2 (each item holds the batch's full signed entries in wire form), §6.8 **Who completes a batch** (any of the person's clients that can read the private payload resolves and completes any batch it finds, resending the missing entries while they chain, or recomputing them from what the entries say; the CLI completes only its own `unpublish`es), **Write-ahead**. |
| E7-M2 | 7 | Medium | An `agent-revoke` signed by a human with no visible binding is `log-invalid`, pending or valid depending on scope and pins. | Fixed: §6.6 **No binding at all** (`log-invalid` only for a space-wide client; for a partial-scope client the signer is unresolved and the entry pending) and **Signers of `agent-revoke`** (a visible binding at `keyVersions[humanId]`, live or not; else a pin at exactly `signerKeyVersion`, with rule 4's version check skipped because it can't be computed, which can only remove a recipient; else pending; the agent ID always derives from `humanId` and the body's keys), §6.9, §12.5 step 7, §16.5 vectors. The reviewer suggested never using a pin here; the pin is kept because a pinned key is the strongest key the client has, and the only effect of the skipped check is a revoke a full-view client would refuse. |
| E7-M3 | 7 | Medium | The CLI's escaping leaves out decrypted, member-written text and `--json` output, works on code points where terminals act on bytes, and has no exact escape form. | Fixed: §12.3 **Printing text the CLI didn't write** (every foreign string, on every output path including errors and exception text; the exact set `D`; the escape form `\u{hex}` in lowercase and `\\` for a backslash; every non-ASCII code point escaped when the output encoding isn't UTF-8, and always for server text during enrollment; `--json` written as printable ASCII with `\uXXXX` escapes and no raw control character but the serializer's line feeds, terminal or not; raw output only when stdout isn't a terminal), §16.5 vectors. |
| E7-L1 | 7 | Low | Boolean `0`/`1` and an object-of-nulls `IdentityRef` encode identically, so the server can get `log-invalid` blamed on the signer; headers aren't covered by rule 1. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 1 (a JSON value of the wrong type for its field is text-level; field-value defects are well-typed values; headers split the same way, with the signature in place of `bodyHash`), §16.5 vectors. |
| E7-L2 | 7 | Low | An empty prefix counts as "partial", so every refused append raises an attack alarm, and the shape of `entries` is unchecked. | Fixed: §6.8 **Resolving an append** (partial needs at least one landed entry; a log ending at the batch's base is **still open**, never an attack), §10.2 (`entries` non-empty, consecutive and chained, or `malformed`; two items with one identity are `malformed`), §10.4 merge table (a pending item meeting the same identity with different entries is `contacts-conflict`). |
| E7-L3 | 7 | Low | The delivered token and the human's identity aren't validated, and the key-file format isn't constrained. | Fixed: §12.2 step 3 (the human's `PublicIdentity` is checked as `pinVerified` does), step 6 (`credentialId` and the opened token checked against their patterns and each other; the key file written with a structured serializer). |
| E7-L4 | 7 | Low | Signed names may hold bidi and line-separator controls that the CLI treats as dangerous. | Fixed: §3.3 rule 5 (writers refuse U+2028, U+2029 and the bidi controls in group names, node names and labels; interfaces render names in bidi isolation for Milestone 1 data). |
| E7-L5 | 7 | Low | "The agent-ID shape" has no pattern. | Fixed: §4.2 (`AgentId = /^agent-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{42}[AEIMQUYcgkosw048]$/`, used by every agent-ID field), §12.5 step 7, §16.5 vector. |
| M8-1 | 8 | High | A batch that removes access is never re-signed silently, so a member who appends free entries without pause (`agent-revoke`s of made-up agents, repeated `agent-grant`s) makes every removal of them lose its race, while every member keeps sealing to them. | Fixed with all four of the reviewer's fixes: §6.8 **Losing a race** (a removal is recomputed, cascade included, and re-signed silently when no entry appended since the confirmed head is owner-signed; the raced entries are shown afterwards, the target's marked; owner-signed entries still need a new confirmation; a `cascade-incomplete` refusal is handled the same way); §6.8 server rules and §6.5 (the server refuses an `agent-revoke` for an agent with no `agent-grant` in the log, no submission and no enrollment under `humanId`'s account, and an `agent-grant` identical to one in force); §6.8 **Bounding appends** (a per-signer, per-space rate limit on non-owner appends, and an owner's 10-minute removal hold during which the server refuses the target's appends, both stated as availability only); administrator suspension named as the fallback (§6.8, §6.7 **Removing an owner**, R7). The enrollment exception, not in the reviewer's fix, keeps a human able to revoke an agent whose grant never reached the server. Each use costs an approved enrollment, it is subject to the rate limit, and a target under a hold can't use it. |
| M8-2 | 8 | Medium | `removed` still stops replay at a removed owner's historical owner-only entries and at entries vouched for only by a removed key, so the recommended unpin locks the remover out of their own space, and an unpinned member's agent can do it with one `unpublish`. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.6 **Removed keys** (replay, signer resolution and signer liveness ignore `removed` for every kind and `seq`; it applies only to sealing, the anchor, and signing and previews; a removed key that is still an owner at the verified head is a space-wide sealing and signing block, exempting `agent-revoke`s of the client's own agents and a batch that removes that owner), §6.7 **Voucher lifetime** (removed vouchers stop counting only for recipient checks) and **Unpinning after a removal**, §10.2, §16.5. |
| M8-3 | 8 | Low | Still-open batches are resent by any device at any later unlock without rechecking `removed` or `revokedAgents`, and can't be abandoned. | Fixed: §6.8 **Recheck before sending** (an item binding a key in `removed`, or granting an agent in `revokedAgents`, is never sent and is closed as `abandoned`), **Closing an item** (the person can abandon any open item; a closed item that lands later is reported, with an offer to revoke), §10.2 (`state`). |
| M8-4 | 8 | Low | "Their agents" in the former-member cap is undefined, owner requests for never-granted agents move the cap, and the request limit is keyed on a free `subjectId`. | Fixed with all three fixes: §6.8 **Former members** (the cap counts only agents with an `agent-grant` signed by them in the log or submitted by them there), §12.5 step 7 (an owner's client declines an `"agent-revoke"` request for an agent with neither, and says why; the server accepts such requests from a caller without access only for agents submitted by them). A human whose grant never reached the server now stays "revocation pending" there for good; with an honest server that grant can't land after the removal (§6.8, R9). |
| M8-5 | 8 | Low | When a submission is recorded, what a cascade refusal returns, and whether submitted agents' keys outlive the account are unspecified. | Fixed: §6.8 server rules (a submission is recorded for an `agent-grant` in a request that passed the head check, from its signer with active access, naming an approved enrollment, whatever `applyEntry` says; the server keeps and serves submitted agents' public keys for the life of the space), **Refusals** (`cascade-incomplete` lists the missing agents with their keys), **Losing a race** (the client adds those revokes and re-signs silently), §9.5 item 4. |
| S8-1 | 8 | Medium | A withheld own-access body makes a current member look like a former member, so the fork at their `agent-revoke` that R9 accepts costs the server nothing, and R9 overstates that cost. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Recorded access** (every mark write records `accessSeqs`, the entries making up the human's access; on later logs their bodies are required, their scopes join `S` and their groups `G`, so the entries that could end them are required too; the former-member path needs every recorded entry visibly ended), §6.9 (the server delivers every human's past-access bodies), §10.2, §10.4 (union on merge), §12.5 step 4, R9 corrected. Access given after the last mark write can still be hidden until the next one (R9). |
| S8-2 | 8 | Medium | After any refused append, write-ahead plus "never re-sign at the same seq" leaves the person unable to append in that space, which blocks removals and agent revocations, even with an honest server. | Fixed: §6.8 **Refusals** (`stale-head` or `append-refused` with a reason code; an honest server discards what it refuses), **Resolving an append** (a refused item whose verified log still ends at its base is closed as `refused` with the server's reason), **Closing an item** and **Retrying** (a closed item doesn't hold its `seq`; a new batch, recorded in the closing write, may take it, and both are resolved; two batches by one signer at one `seq` are a fork the server could build anyway, R2), **Recheck before sending** (an item granting a revoked agent is abandoned, so the `agent-revoke` takes its `seq`), §6.6, §12.5 step 3. |
| S8-3 | 8 | Low | Nothing requires the human's own agents to honour `revokedAgents`. | Fixed: §10.2 (an agent's client never seals to an agent in its human's `revokedAgents`, and refuses such a write with `revocation-pending`), §12.3, R9, §16.5. |
| S8-4 | 8 | Low | The batch-order rule forbids the revoke-then-re-grant a key-version change needs, so re-granting a rotated member forces the cascade and bars their agents for good. | Accepted for Milestone 2, where every human is at key version 1: Q7 records that rotation needs `key-update` or an order exemption for entries binding a strictly higher version whose lower-version access the same batch ends. |
| E8-M1 | 8 | Medium | After any refusal other than a lost race, the client can never sign another entry at that `seq`, so a person being removed, or the client's own refused grant, blocks a removal or agent revocation indefinitely. | Fixed with S8-2, plus the cascade-refusal answer of M8-5. |
| E8-M2 | 8 | Medium | A partially landed batch has no exit, "missing" isn't defined, two devices completing one batch report a failure, and a log below an item's base matches no case. | Fixed: §6.8 **Changes and items** (`changeId` ties every item of a change), **Resolving an append** (a recorded remainder replaces its partial item in one confirmed write, and a partial item whose remainder is recorded is never completed again; a failed item whose change landed, or has nothing missing, is removed without a report; a fifth case, below the base, is `log-rollback`), **Missing entries** (by `entryHash` when resending, by effect when recomputing), **Write-ahead** (every write-ahead records the mark at or above the base), **Reporting a change done**, §10.2, §10.4, §16.5 vector. |
| E8-L1 | 8 | Low | The ordered `agent-revoke` signer rules have no verdict when rule 1's binding is conflicted, unresolvable or older than the signer, or when a pinned signature fails. | Fixed: §6.6 **Signers of `agent-revoke`** (a conflicted or unresolvable binding falls through to the pin, then to rule 3; for a space-wide client rule 3 is `log-unendorsed` naming `humanId`, never `log-invalid`; a partial-scope client seeing only lower versions treats the entry as having no visible binding; a bad signature under the pin is refetched, then `log-invalid` naming no signer), §16.5 vectors. |
| E8-L2 | 8 | Low | The group-name replay rule doesn't say whether the bidi and line-separator refusal applies, and readers of v2 node metadata are unspecified. | Fixed: §6.5 `group-create` (the full rule 5 set; a well-formed name holding any of it is `log-invalid`), §3.3 rule 5 (node-metadata readers, Milestone 1 or 2, show such a name isolated and escaped, never `malformed`), §16.5 vectors. |
| E8-L3 | 8 | Low | "A human ID" is undefined now that `AgentId` exists, and the defect class of a bad agent ID is unstated. | Fixed: §4.2 (`HumanId`, an ID not starting with `agent-`, used by every human-ID field; an `agent-`-prefixed value that isn't an `AgentId` is invalid in every ID field), §3.3 rule 1 (a field-value defect), §6.5, §16.5 vector verdicts. |
| E8-L4 | 8 | Low | The wrong-type rule leaves `null` in a non-nullable field ambiguous, and blames the server for bytes only the signer could make. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §3.3 rule 1 (only wrong-typed values whose canonical encoding equals a well-typed one's are text-level; every other wrong-typed value is a field-value defect, judged by `bodyHash` or the signature), §16.5 vector. |
| E8-L5 | 8 | Low | Enrollment acceptance doesn't require the agent's `keyVersion` to be 1, which can set off the space-wide newer-pin block. | Fixed: §4.5 (acceptance requires `agent.keyVersion` 1), §16.5 (`pinEnrolledAgent` in PR 2, vector). |
| E8-I1 | 8 | Info | The locale test doesn't say whether `utf8` or an empty variable count, and §18's round table stops at round 6. | Fixed: §12.3 (the first non-empty variable; the codeset compared case-insensitively with `-` removed; a `LANG=C.utf8` vector), §18 (rows for rounds 7 and 8). |
| S9-1 | 9 | High | Marks for one space are merged by keeping the higher `seq`, so a device on another branch of a forked log replaces the mark that proves a change (an owner's removal of a member), and every later session of the person follows the branch without it, with nothing reported. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **High-water marks** (a mark is a set of checkpoints; a log passes it only if it holds every checkpoint) and **Marks are reconciled, never maxed** (whenever two marks meet, in memory, a local file, a record just read, or a merge, the client keeps the union and checks it at once against every verified log it holds; a checkpoint is dropped only by a verified log that holds it below a higher one; otherwise `log-fork` or `log-rollback`, with every checkpoint kept), §10.2 (`checkpoints`), §10.4 merge row, §6.8 **Reporting a change done**, §12.5 step 4, R2 bullet 2, §16.5 vector. A recorded seq that names no access-giving entry is `log-fork` (S9-6). |
| S9-2 | 9 | Medium | **Recorded access** makes the client require bodies (recorded scopes in `S`, recorded groups in `G`) that §6.9 never tells the server to deliver, so an honest server gets `missing-body`, and a server that delivers them leaks beyond R3. | Fixed by narrowing, with M9-1 and E9-F1: §6.8 **Recorded access** (the client requires only the recorded entries' own bodies, which §6.9 always delivers; recorded entries don't join `S`, `N` or `G`; an entry ending recorded access that the server withholds leaves the access in force for the client, so it stays on the member path and the scope that access makes relevant requires the withheld body), §6.9 past access (the client can't tell when any unrecorded one is missing), §16.5 vector. No delivery or R3 change is needed. The reviewer's alternative, delivering revokes at recorded scopes and removals in recorded groups, wasn't needed. |
| S9-3 | 9 | Low | Freeing a refused item's `seq` lets a server fork a space where only one person signs, which R2 said it couldn't do. | Accepted and corrected: R2 bullet 3 and §6.8 **Retrying** (the "nothing new" claim holds only where someone else appends; in a space one person appends to, one refusal the server chooses is enough to fork it). The reviewer's mitigation of keeping a refused item as "possibly applied elsewhere" isn't added; detection rests on R2's other signals. |
| S9-4 | 9 | Low | **Missing entries** has no effect rule for `group-create`, `group-delete`, `move`, or later entries naming a group the change created, and the late-landing offer leaves out `owner-add` and `move`. | Fixed: §6.8 **Missing entries** (`group-delete`'s effect is the group deleted; a `move`'s is the node's parent equal to `toParentId`; a `group-create` is missing unless its own entry is in the log by `entryHash`, and later entries naming that group are recomputed against the group the change created), **Resolving an append** (landed: an offer to undo every entry that gives access or moves a node, `owner-add` and `move` included). |
| S9-5 | 9 | Low | Whether a request refused by a removal hold or the rate limit still records submissions is unspecified. | Fixed with M9-2: §6.8 server rules (the server applies the bounds first; a request they refuse records nothing, not even a submission). |
| S9-6 | 9 | Low | A recorded seq that isn't the human's access entry has no verdict. | Fixed: §6.8 **Recorded access** (such a seq is `log-fork`), §16.5 vector. |
| S10-1 | 10 | Medium | A split removal ends the removal hold with its first item, so the rest runs unheld and the target can't be held again for an hour; splits beyond two items are unspecified. | Fixed with M10-3: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (a hold lasts 10 minutes from placement whatever the holder appends, and the client releases it once the change is done or every item is closed, so one hold covers every item of a split or re-signed removal; a split takes as many items as it needs, each within the bound and in batch order, with any `owner-remove` last), §6.8 server rules (a removed owner's bindings need another counting voucher after the batch, so `vouch`es may land in an earlier item), §6.7. The reviewer's renewal or extension isn't added: it would let a co-owner hold another owner indefinitely, and an honest split lands well within 10 minutes; a hold that expires first falls to the named fallbacks. |
| S10-2 | 10 | Medium | Reconciling marks checks a merged checkpoint against an older held log, so routine two-device use reports `log-rollback` as a possible attack. | Fixed with E10-1: §6.8 **Marks are reconciled, never maxed** (a held log is judged only at the checkpoints it holds; a different `entryHash` there is `log-fork`; a held log that ends below a checkpoint is only behind, reported as nothing, and the client fetches before relying on it; `log-rollback` is a verdict only on a log served after the client held the checkpoint), **Resolving an append** (resolved only by a log fetched after the item was read), §10.4 merge row, §16.5 vector. |
| S10-3 | 10 | Medium | "Visibly ended" is per entry, but access is effective, so a dormant membership, or a group grant after the human left the group, leaves the human neither a member nor a former member, and revocation never completes. | Fixed with M10-1 and E10-2: §6.8 **Recorded access** (recorded access is visibly ended when replay, which applies every recorded entry, gives the human no effective access at any node; withholding an ending entry still fails safe), §6.8 **Former members**, §6.6, §12.5 step 4, R9, §16.5 vectors for both cases. |
| S10-4 | 10 | Medium | A refused or abandoned grant can still land at a time the server picks, and nothing tells the person it isn't cancelled. | Fixed with the reviewer's first option: §6.8 **Closing an item** (closing doesn't cancel: a closed item that gives access is shown as "may still be applied" until another entry takes its `seq`, and an `agent-grant` can be settled at once by revoking the agent there, which bars it there for good), R2 bullet 3, §16.5. A void entry kind isn't added: for owner grants, the next entry anyone appends settles it, and a landing is reported with the offer to undo it. |
| S10-5 | 10 | Medium | No recovery from a detected fork or rollback: a malicious race or an honest backup restore locks a space for good, and implementations will improvise an override. | Fixed: §6.8 **Resetting a mark** (only the person resets, after comparing heads with an owner or deciding to trust the served log; the log is still verified apart from the mark; checkpoints become its head, recorded seqs it doesn't hold are dropped, `grantedAgents` is kept, and unresolved items it lacks are failed so the person is asked again; the reset is recorded with the abandoned checkpoints and shown on every device; a side lacking a reset contributes nothing it excludes in a merge), §10.2 (`resets`), §10.3 **Accepting an older record** for `contacts-rollback`, §10.4, R2 (a reset trusts the person), §16.5 vector. An owner-signed head statement, the reviewer's other option, is part of deferred Q3. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| S10-6 | 10 | Low | A log passes by merely holding a checkpoint, so a pending tail can lower the mark below it. | Fixed: §6.8 **High-water marks** (a log passes only if the verified head is at or above every checkpoint; holding one only in pending entries is `stale-log`; a mark never goes down), §16.5 vector. |
| S10-7 | 10 | Low | A server refusal code outside the pattern, stored verbatim, makes the person's own private payload `malformed`. | Fixed with E10-3: §6.8 **Refusals** (a missing or non-matching code is recorded as `unrecognized`; an unparseable `missingAgents` is a refusal with no list), §10.2 (writers check the whole private payload against the reading rules before writing), §16.5 vector. |
| S10-8 | 10 | Low | `missingAgents` is unfiltered and unbounded. | Fixed in part: §6.8 **Losing a race** (listed agents that fail the derivation check, are already revoked or in the batch, or repeat are ignored and reported; a list for a batch that leaves nobody without access is ignored). A server listing made-up agents that pass the derivation check only lengthens the batch, which splits; that is a malicious server delaying an append, accepted under R2. |
| S10-9 | 10 | Low | "At most once per session" makes the read-only mark write, and with it recorded access, optional. | Fixed: §10.4 **When to write** (once per session, promptly after the first verification that raises a mark or adds to `accessSeqs`, retried at the next unlock if it fails). |
| S10-10 | 10 | Low | The hold `DELETE` route names neither the target nor who may release it. | Fixed: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (`DELETE /api/spaces/:id/removal-holds/:targetId`, holder only), §16.5. |
| S10-11 | 10 | Low | E9-L5's "recorded seqs above the mark are `log-fork`" is missing from the text. | Fixed: §6.8 **Recorded access** (a recorded seq above the mark's highest checkpoint is `log-fork`). |
| M10-1 | 10 | Medium | Recorded access records access-giving entries but triggers on effective access, so a dormant membership strands the human; the group-grant ending rule and the "gave access" `log-fork` test are ambiguous; "admission endorsed" is undefined with no admission seq. | Fixed with S10-3: §6.8 **Recorded access** (the `log-fork` check is structural: a `genesis` or `owner-add` of the human, a `group-member-add` of the human, a `grant` to the human, or a `grant` to a group the human was a member of while it was in force; whether it gave effective access is never asked), §12.5 step 4 (a human with no admission seq never counts as endorsed), §16.5 vectors. |
| M10-2 | 10 | Medium | The `agent-revoke` rate-limit exemption covers revokes of enrolled-only agents, so an unheld accomplice wins every race against a removal, one enrollment per race; the interface points at the target; owners' agents escape the limit. | Fixed with the reviewer's fixes: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (only revokes of agents recorded as submitted in the space are exempt, at most `agent-limit` unrevoked per human; every other `agent-revoke` counts; every agent's appends count, an owner's included; when a removal keeps losing, the interface names the racing signers as it happens and offers to hold, remove or suspend them), §16.5. |
| M10-3 | 10 | Medium | The hold ends when the first split item lands, the target can force the split, and a refused hold has no rule. | Fixed with S10-1: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (one hold across the change; `agent-limit` is at most half of `too-many-entries`, so a non-owner can't force a split of their own removal; a refused hold still sends the removal and tells the person why). |
| M10-4 | 10 | Low | A stale held log raises false `log-rollback` alarms, and a pending tail can drop a checkpoint. | Fixed with S10-2 and S10-6. |
| M10-5 | 10 | Low | Current members can open unlimited owner requests, burying real ones. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §12.5 step 7 (from any caller, an `"agent-revoke"` request only for the requester's own granted or submitted agent, an `"unpublish"` request only for a page they can publish at, and a few open requests per requester and space), §16.5. |
| E10-1 | 10 | Medium | A held log merely older than another device's checkpoint is reported as `log-rollback`, and the mark can be lowered to a verified head below a checkpoint. | Fixed with S10-2 and S10-6, including the reviewer's vector (a held log at seq 10 meeting a mark `{12}`: no report, a fetch, a mark of `{12}` or higher; §16.5). |
| E10-2 | 10 | Medium | What is recorded, the "gave access" verdict and the ending rules disagree for group paths: false `log-fork`, and the former-member path never taken after routine group removals. | Fixed with S10-3 and M10-1: the verdict is structural, and "visibly ended" is decided by effective access. Memberships in groups with no grant stay recorded and give nothing, so a withheld `group-delete` of such a group doesn't matter and §6.9 needs no new delivery. §16.5 vectors for the three honest cases. |
| E10-3 | 10 | Low | A non-matching refusal code stored verbatim breaks the private payload; writers needn't check what they write; `missingAgents` failures have no rule. | Fixed with S10-7 and S10-8. |
| E10-4 | 10 | Low | Element types, empty `checkpoints`, genesis items, stored-entry strictness and merges of `confirmedSeq` and `refusal` are unspecified. | Fixed with the reviewer's fixes: §10.2 (every seq an integer ≥ 1; an empty `checkpoints` or `abandoned` is `malformed`; `confirmedSeq` at most the first entry's `seq` − 1; stored entries parsed under §3.3 and §6.1–6.4 in full; checkpoints sorted in ASCII text order), §6.8 **Write-ahead** (a genesis batch records no mark and has `confirmedSeq` 0) and **Resolving an append** (a genesis item is still open while the space has no log), §10.4 (entries compared by `entryHash`; the lower `confirmedSeq` wins; two `refused` copies keep the newer record's code), §16.5 vectors. |
| E10-5 | 10 | Low | §16.5 still calls every wrong-typed value text-level, contradicting §3.3 rule 1. | Fixed: §16.5 (text-level only when the canonical encoding equals a well-typed one's; otherwise a field-value defect). |
| S11-1 | 11 | High | A reset after a rollback silently undoes the person's completed removals, which have left `unresolvedAppends`; on desktop, accepting an older contacts record loses revocations made since; comparing heads with an owner, which R2 presents as the safeguard, can't catch a rollback shown to every owner. | Fixed: §6.8 **Resetting a mark** and **Lost changes** (before the person confirms, the client lists every change of theirs the new log lacks, removals first, by stored labels, from the mark's new `removals`, the open items below the new base, and any held log of the abandoned range; each lost removal or `unpublish` is recomputed from the new state and re-signed over the new head as a new item of its change, recorded in the reset's confirmed write; the reset is reported done only once those changes are done, and meanwhile the person's clients refuse writes sealing to anyone they cover, `revocation-pending`; other lost changes are listed to make again or drop; other people's lost removals are listed from a held log), §6.8 **Reporting a change done** and §10.2 (`removals`: every landed entry of the person's that removes access or unpublishes, never removed, read like stored entries), §10.3 (S11-4), §10.4, R1, R2 (comparing heads catches a fork between people, never a rollback shown to all; other people's removals return until their signers reset; operator documentation names the restore consequence), §16.5 vector. The reviewer's "offer every key in `removed` that has access" isn't added: this person's clients never seal to a removed key anyway. Not fully closed: a member removed by **someone else** above the new head is back in this person's audiences until the remover resets or another owner removes them again (R2). **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| S11-2 | 11 | Medium | The reset refetches and adopts whatever verifies, not the head the person compared, so a server can swap branches at reset. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Resetting a mark** (the interface shows the head the reset would adopt, by `seq` and 8 characters of `entryHash`, which is what the person compares; at confirmation the client fetches again and stops if the verified head differs, showing the new one to compare), §16.5 vector. Every reset's adopted head is shown on every device. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| S11-3 | 11 | Medium | A reset fails and removes items below the new base, but in a prefix rollback the server can re-serve the abandoned entries and land a refused grant with no report. | Fixed with M11-1 and E11-2: §6.8 **Items after a reset** (an item below the new base isn't failed; an open one is closed as `abandoned` and the person asked again; it stays recorded and "may still be applied" until a verified log fails it or it lands and is reported, with the settle-by-revoke offer for an `agent-grant`), **Resolving an append** (failed now also covers a different entry at the item's base `seq`, so the item settles once the new branch passes its base), §16.5 vector. The reviewer's "a log holding an abandoned checkpoint is an attack" isn't added: re-serving the abandoned branch is now reported through the item landing, and an honest server restoring a newer backup would trigger it falsely. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| S11-4 | 11 | Medium | Accepting an older contacts record re-applies only what a desktop session holds, so after a restart unpins, revocations, `grantedAgents` and unresolved items are lost with no warning. | Fixed: §10.3 **Accepting an older record** (the desktop app's local file keeps both payloads of the newest record it accepted or wrote; acceptance re-applies them as after a write conflict; with no such copy, the interface says plainly that unpins, revocations and pending appends since the backup may be lost and asks the person to re-check contacts and revoke again; the CLI accepts only through a command the person runs, and keeps honouring the `removed` and `revokedAgents` it has read). The browser stays under R1. *Round 12:* still holds, except that items come back only closed, and a space whose log fails a carried-back mark is read-only rather than reset (S12-3). |
| S11-5 | 11 | Low | The CLI can't read the person's resets, so after an honest restore its space stays `log-rollback` for good. | Fixed: §12.3 (the CLI resets its own mark only through a command the person runs on its host, under §6.8's rules, with its own landed `unpublish`es as `removals`), §6.8. A compromised host controls the CLI anyway (R4). *Round 12:* the CLI reset is removed; the CLI accepts a new starting point only through a command its person runs, after their own client is re-admitted (§12.3). |
| S11-6 | 11 | Low | The merge exclusion assumes a side lacking a reset predates it; whether local files store `resets` is unspecified. | Fixed with E11-4: §6.8 (a side lacking a reset contributes no checkpoint it abandoned and no seq it dropped, and no checkpoint above its new head unless a held verified log has it on the new head's chain; recorded seqs above the new head are recorded again by the next mark write if in force), §6.8 **High-water marks** (local files hold each mark whole, `resets` included). **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| M11-1 | 11 | Medium | A reset fails a closed below-the-base grant and removes it, so a server that re-serves the abandoned entries lands it with no report or undo offer. | Fixed with S11-3, including the reviewer's vector. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| M11-2 | 11 | Low | An owner target can force a split of their own removal longer than the hold. | Fixed with the reviewer's second option: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (says so, and names suspension as the remedy). The first option, deferring the server's demotion and voucher checks to the change's last item, adds a request flag and server state for a case a malicious owner can meet more simply by removing the remover. |
| M11-3 | 11 | Low | An owner can hold themself to block every other owner's hold, and re-asking for a hold on a rebuilt batch is unspecified. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (no hold on oneself; before each rebuilt batch a client that doesn't hold the target asks again; the remover is shown who holds it). |
| M11-4 | 11 | Low | "Open" owner requests are undefined, stale `unpublish` requests can fill the cap ahead of `agent-revoke` requests, and dropped requests are silent. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §12.5 step 7 (a request closes when the log holds the requested revoke or unpublish, or an owner declines it; `"agent-revoke"` requests for submitted, unrevoked agents don't count toward the cap; the rest are refused with a reason code the client shows). |
| M11-5 | 11 | Low | After a reset nothing lists the removals it undid. | Fixed with S11-1. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-1 | 11 | Medium | Recorded seqs a reset dropped at or below its new head come back from a side lacking the reset, giving `log-fork` or `missing-body` after an honest restore. | Fixed with the reviewer's first option: §10.2 (`resets[].droppedAccessSeqs`, a set, possibly empty, of seqs ≥ 1), §6.8 (a side lacking the reset contributes none of them), §16.5 vector extended. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-2 | 11 | Medium | Reset step 3 fails below-the-base items, which aren't dead, so a closed grant can land silently. | Fixed with S11-3. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-3 | 11 | Low | Whether a reset runs **Recorded access** is undefined: strict blocks recovery, lax lets a withheld body strip recorded access; a log that passes has no rule. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Resetting a mark** (verification skips only the checkpoints and the structural test; every recorded seq the log holds needs its body, `missing-body` stops the reset; a seq is dropped only above the new head or when its delivered body fails the structural test; a log that passes needs no reset). **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-4 | 11 | Low | The merge exclusion drops legitimate checkpoints and recorded seqs above a reset's head from devices that verified the new branch first; local-file contents are unspecified. | Fixed with S11-6. A re-derivation rule isn't added: every mark write already records the access in force at that mark (§6.8 **Recorded access**). **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-5 | 11 | Low | "The abandoned checkpoints" is undefined, and under one reading can be empty, which is `malformed`. | Fixed: §6.8 and §10.2 (every checkpoint of the mark before the reset, non-empty by construction; a log that passes needs no reset, E11-3). **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-6 | 11 | Low | Genesis items can't be closed as `refused`, "missing" is undefined for them, and another device can sign a second genesis. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Closing an item** (`refused` for a genesis item when the space still has no log), **Missing entries** (a `genesis` is missing unless its own entry is in the log; a different one at seq 1 is a reported failure), §16.2 (an unresolved genesis item bars backfill; the client completes it, never a second genesis), §16.5 vector. §16.4 rule 1 is unchanged: a log-less answer with only an open genesis item is what an honest server shows before the genesis lands. |
| E11-7 | 11 | Low | `resets[].at` is missing from §3.2's list of timestamp fields. | Fixed: §3.2, §16.5 vector. **Superseded in round 12:** the reset is removed; a space whose log fails the mark is read-only until an owner re-admits the person, or an owner accepts the served log themself (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| E11-8 | 11 | Low | A held log holding a higher checkpoint only in pending entries matches no reconciliation case. | Fixed: §6.8 **Marks are reconciled, never maxed** (it is behind), §16.5 vector. |
| E11-9 | 11 | Low | Parsing stored entries "under §6.4 in full" includes log-dependent rules, so implementations disagree on whether the person's own payload is `malformed`. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §10.2 (only rules that need no log at read time; log-dependent rules are checked on resend or comparison, like the signature), §16.5 vector. |
| S12-1 | 12 | High | The seal block for re-made removals has no persisted source; a refused re-made item is silently removed, and other sessions seal to the removed member. | Fixed by removal: re-made removals and their block are gone. A space whose log fails the mark is read-only, and that follows from the persisted mark on every device and session, so no client of the person seals there until a log passes the mark or the person is re-admitted (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). |
| S12-2 | 12 | Medium | Lost removals are judged and re-made by `grantSeq` and `groupSeq`, which name other entries on a regrown or forked log. | Fixed by removal: nothing is re-made and `removals` is gone. After re-admission a removal is made again only by a person, as a new change against the new log. |
| S12-3 | 12 | Medium | Accepting an older contacts record reopens items closed on another device and resends them automatically, with no warning. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §10.3 (items come back only closed, as `abandoned`, so a landing is reported and nothing is resent; the interface always warns that changes since the copy's version may be lost; a space whose log fails a carried-back mark is read-only), §16.5 vector. R1 isn't extended: it already covers a device lacking items, and acceptance no longer resends. |
| S12-4 | 12 | Low | The person's lost removals they can no longer sign are neither re-made nor blocked, contrary to R2. | Fixed by removal: R2 no longer claims removals are re-made, and says every change after the restore point is undone on an accepted log. |
| M12-1 | 12 | High | Lost removals are judged per entry, so a restored owner who rewrites their own access before the remover resets makes the reset find nothing lost and keep sealing to them. | Fixed by removal: there is no lost-change test to fool. The remover's clients are read-only on the restored log, so they seal nothing there, until an owner re-admits them or, as an owner, they accept the log themself, told that every change after the restore point, their removals included, is undone and nothing is made again (§6.8 **Recovering from a rollback**). Whoever accepts the branch must remove the target again (R2). |
| M12-2 | 12 | Medium | A reset re-makes removals the new log already holds, widens narrow removals into full ones, and can't decline them. | Fixed by removal. |
| M12-3 | 12 | Medium | Re-made removals take the new head as `confirmedSeq`, skipping the **Losing a race** display of the target's and owners' entries. | Fixed by removal: recovery signs nothing. A later removal is an ordinary change under **Losing a race**, and an owner who accepts the log is told to re-check members. |
| M12-4 | 12 | Medium | The remover's agents don't apply the re-made removal block, and keep sealing to the removed member. | Fixed in part by removal: the human's own clients are read-only on the restored log, and the CLI, whose mark the log fails, is read-only too until its person runs the accept command (§12.3). Accepted under R2 (**Freeze**): an agent whose mark is below the removal follows the restored log like any writer holding no mark past it. |
| M12-5 | 12 | Low | A reset stops unless the refetched head equals the one shown, so routine appends during the comparison block recovery; holds in a blocked space are unspecified. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Recovering from a rollback** (confirmation goes on if the refetched verified chain holds the head shown, by `seq` and `entryHash`, and shows entries above it). A read-only person appends nothing and places no hold; an owner accepts first. |
| M12-6 | 12 | Low | **Items after a reset** and **Lost changes** disagree on open removal items below the new base. | Fixed by removal: re-admission closes every open item whose base the new log doesn't hold, and re-makes nothing. |
| M12-7 | 12 | Low | A declined owner request can be resubmitted at once, without limit. | Fixed in part: §12.5 step 7 (after a decline the server refuses the same requester, space, action, subject and `revisionId` for a cooldown, an installation setting). Withdrawal isn't added: it is availability only, and a request closes once its action is in the log. |
| E12-1 | 12 | Medium | `removals` entries name targets by `seq`, so after a regrown restore a reset revokes an innocent grant and reports done. | Fixed by removal, with S12-2. |
| E12-2 | 12 | Medium | A held pre-restore log raises the mark back to the abandoned head, and "on the chain of the new head" admits old-branch checkpoints, so every device is blocked again. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix, recast for re-admission: §6.8 **Abandoned checkpoints** (an abandoned checkpoint never re-enters, from any source; a side that predates a re-admission contributes only `abandoned` and `grantedAgents`; a held log that holds an abandoned checkpoint, or was verified before the client read the current `abandoned`, never judges or changes a mark), §16.5 vector with the other device's local file holding the old log. |
| E12-3 | 12 | Medium | The reset's pending state (re-made removals, the refusal, done, dropped changes) isn't stored, so other sessions seal to the target. | Fixed by removal, with S12-1. |
| E12-4 | 12 | Low | `removals` stores no `changeId`, and removals landing outside a "done" write aren't journaled. | Fixed by removal. |
| E12-5 | 12 | Low | The stored-entry parse list omits context-free checks; `removals` entries have no failure outcome; the CLI's signer contradicts the owner rule. | Fixed: §10.2 (the context-free checks add `protocol`, `installationId`, the `kind` enum, `bodyHash` and the per-kind header nulls; the signature is checked on resend or comparison, never at read time). The `removals` and CLI parts are fixed by removal. |
| E12-6 | 12 | Low | `expiresAt` is missing from §3.2's timestamp list, and the human's client needn't validate it, so a malformed value disables its own deadline. | Fixed: §3.2, §12.2 (the human's client refuses a claim response whose `expiresAt` fails `Timestamp`), §16.5 vector. |
| E12-7 | 12 | Low | Two resets to divergent heads merge with the lower one silently winning, with no `log-fork`. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix, recast: §6.8 **Abandoned checkpoints** (if each side lacks one of the other's abandoned checkpoints, both contribute and the space is read-only, `log-fork`), §16.5 vector. |
| E12-8 | 12 | Low | **Items after a reset** and **Lost changes** disagree, and open items failing at a forked base are missed. | Fixed by removal: re-admission closes every open item whose base the new log doesn't hold, whether below it or forked under it. |
| S13-1 | 13 | High | Re-admission accepts any pinned owner's `vouch` of the person outside `accessSeqs`, which never records `vouch`es, and the confirmation names no entry, so the server shows an old one and the honest owner's "yes" re-admits the person onto a rollback that lacks a removal. | Fixed by redesign: §6.8 **Recovering from a rollback** (re-admission needs a `readmit` whose `abandonedHash` commits to the person's current abandoned set, sent in their signed request; ordinary grants and `vouch`es never re-admit; a stale `readmit` can't match a later set), §6.4, §6.5, §3.5 (`readmit/v1`), §12.5 step 7, R2. The `accessSeqs` filter is dropped from recovery (E13-L3). |
| S13-2 | 13 | High | Both recovery paths judge "owner" on the served branch, so a removed owner or a stolen superseded key re-admits a member on a fork below the removal, or makes them an owner there and so offers them self-acceptance. | Fixed with the user's design: each space mark records `owners`, the `{id, keyVersion}` of every owner replay gives at the mark's highest checkpoint, set by every write that advances the mark and at a new starting point, and following the highest checkpoint when marks merge (the references both sets hold when two checkpoints tie at that `seq`) (§10.2, §10.4). §6.8: the person asks an owner in `owners` and confirms the `readmit`'s signer is that owner; a signer whose `id` and `keyVersion` aren't in `owners` is refused, whether or not the client holds a log of the abandoned branch, so a browser refuses a removed owner or a superseded key too; self-acceptance is offered only if the person is in `owners`, shows the entry that made them an owner on the served log, and needs the exact head compared with a pinned co-owner in `owners`, binding the new checkpoint to that head. R2 keeps only what no mark can see: a removal above the person's highest checkpoint, which a freeze hid, and a tie whose branches share no owner. |
| S13-3 | 13 | Medium | A former member whose recorded access is visibly ended can never leave read-only after a restore, so their revocation there stays pending for good. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Recovering from a rollback** (a person whose recorded access is visibly ended on the served log, with the recorded-seq checks passing, may accept it themself; they seal nothing and append only `agent-revoke`s), §12.5 step 4, R9, §16.5 vector. |
| S13-4 | 13 | Low | Whether a `log-fork` found through a record's, placement's or envelope's `authorizedHash` makes the space read-only is unspecified. | Fixed: §6.8 **Recovering from a rollback** (it is reported, but doesn't make the space read-only: the client's own log passes its mark and nothing persisted would hold the block), R2, §16.5 vector. |
| S13-5 | 13 | Low | The CLI accept command compares heads by eye, which a moved log or a branch with a removed owner's entries defeats. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §12.3 (the command takes the `seq` and 8 characters of the `entryHash` of the head the human's client shows, and goes on only if the CLI's verified chain holds that entry), §6.8, §16.5 vector. |
| S13-6 | 13 | Low | An owner's client refuses to vouch for a person it hasn't pinned, but recovery told such people to ask for re-admission without saying so. | Fixed: §6.8 step 2 (the owner's client never signs a `readmit` for an unpinned person, and offers a safety-code comparison first). |
| M13-1 | 13 | High | A removed owner can re-admit, on a fork below their removal, the members who removed them; the confirmation goes to the attacker. | Fixed, with S13-2, by the reviewer's `owners` field on each space mark (§10.2): the person chooses whom to ask, only that owner's `readmit` for their current set counts, and its signer must be in `owners`, the owners at the person's highest checkpoint, which a removed owner isn't, on any client and whatever log it holds (§6.8, step 3; R2). |
| M13-2 | 13 | High | Re-admission accepts a stale owner entry, such as an earlier re-admission's `vouch`, and the confirmation can't tell it from the fresh one. | Fixed by redesign, with S13-1: a `readmit` commits to the person's current abandoned set, so an earlier incident's entry never matches, and the server can't move one to another branch; §16.5 vector. |
| M13-3 | 13 | Medium | A merge treats a device lacking the newest `abandoned` as older, and silently drops its checkpoint, even when that device followed another branch past the abandoned one. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Abandoned checkpoints** (a predating side's checkpoint is dropped only if already abandoned, goes to `abandoned` if a held log shows it on the abandoned branch, and otherwise joins the union, so a log that lacks it is `log-fork` and the space is read-only on every device until the person accepts a new starting point with it in the set; the interface shows it as another device's head), §10.4, §16.5 vector. |
| M13-4 | 13 | Low | One owner's decline closes an `"agent-revoke"` request for every owner and starts the cooldown; declines are unspecified. | Fixed: §12.5 step 7 (a decline isn't signed, carries a reason shown as server text, and closes the request only for the declining owner; the cooldown starts once every owner shown it declines; `"agent-revoke"` requests for the requester's submitted agents have no cooldown; an owner's client offers to decline one only when its checks fail). |
| E13-F1 | 13 | Medium | Every old checkpoint goes into `abandoned`, even one on the new chain, and a log of the abandoned branch extending the new starting point has no verdict. | Fixed with the reviewer's fixes: §6.8 (only old checkpoints the new chain doesn't hold go to `abandoned`, and one in both lists is `malformed`, §10.2; a held log holding an abandoned checkpoint belongs to the abandoned branch, and its entries not on the new log at their seq, above the new starting point included, are listed as lost; a served log that passes the mark and holds an abandoned checkpoint is **the history coming back**: accepted, the checkpoint leaves `abandoned`, landed items reported; the "can't land" sentence is replaced by "may still be applied"), §16.5 vectors. |
| E13-F2 | 13 | Medium | Classing a held log as abandoned because it was verified before `abandoned` was read needs state nothing persists. | Fixed by dropping that test: §6.8 **Abandoned checkpoints** (a held log belongs to the abandoned branch only if it holds an abandoned checkpoint; a stale log ending below one is judged as usual, and its checkpoint, if not provably abandoned, joins the union, so the space is read-only once and the next request's set holds it, which ends the cycle), §16.5 vector. |
| E13-L1 | 13 | Low | §10.3's must-not-drop list omits `abandoned`. | Fixed: §10.3 (`spaceMarks` and every field of them, `abandoned` included). |
| E13-L2 | 13 | Low | "A mark never goes down" is false after recovery, the exclusion rules name only re-admission, and §10.3 names only an owner's re-admission. | Fixed: §6.8, §10.2–10.4 (one term, "accepting a new starting point", for re-admission and both self-acceptances; "never goes down" qualified). |
| E13-L3 | 13 | Low | Refusing re-admitting entries at a seq in `accessSeqs` refuses honest fresh entries at reused seqs. | Fixed by removal: re-admission no longer looks at `accessSeqs` (S13-1). |
| E13-L4 | 13 | Low | The CLI's accept command doesn't say what it records or which items it closes. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §12.3 (the same steps against its local file: abandoned checkpoints, `accessSeqs`, and open `unpublish` items whose base the new log lacks closed). |
| E13-L5 | 13 | Low | After a restore, a former member's revocation can never be done. | Fixed with S13-3. |
| E13-L6 | 13 | Low | "What the person can already decrypt" in a read-only space is ambiguous. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 (content whose keys the person held before is shown as usual; anything newer is marked "on a log this device hasn't accepted"). |
| S14-1 | 14 | High | A removed owner's fork can make a current member a former member, who then accepts it, so an agent revocation is reported done on the wrong branch. | Fixed by removal: former-member acceptance is gone. A space whose log is `log-fork` or `log-rollback` is read-only for good, and an agent revocation there stays pending (§6.8 **Read-only spaces**, §12.5 step 4, R9). |
| S14-2 | 14 | High | Owner self-acceptance looks for the co-owner to compare with on the served log, so the server avoids the comparison by choosing the restore point. | Fixed by removal: owner self-acceptance is gone (§6.8, R2). |
| S14-3 | 14 | Medium | A `readmit` from an owner frozen below a removal can land the person on the removed owner's fork, and the confirmation doesn't compare owners. | Fixed by removal: the `readmit` kind and `readmit/v1` are gone (§3.5, §6.4, §6.5). |
| S14-4 | 14 | Medium | Accepting a new starting point resets `accessSeqs` from access the server can hide, reopening S8-1 once per incident. | Fixed by removal: nothing resets `accessSeqs`, and a seq never leaves it (§10.2). |
| S14-5 | 14 | Low | When each side of a merge predates the other's acceptance, the merged `accessSeqs` is undefined. | Fixed by removal: `abandoned` and predating sides are gone; `accessSeqs` merges as the union (§10.4). |
| S14-6 | 14 | Low | Nothing binds which owner the person asked for re-admission. | Fixed by removal: re-admission requests are gone (§12.5 step 7). |
| M14-1 | 14 | High | `owners` follows the higher `seq` when marks meet across a fork, so a removed owner who pads their branch becomes the person's recovery authority. | Fixed by removal: marks hold no `owners`, and a fork found when marks meet makes the space read-only (§6.8 **Marks are reconciled, never maxed**, §10.2, §10.4). |
| M14-2 | 14 | High | Former-member acceptance checks no signer of the ending entries against `owners`, then replaces `owners` with the fork's. | Fixed by removal with S14-1. |
| M14-3 | 14 | High | A removed owner's fork can empty the served log of co-owners, so owner self-acceptance goes ahead on the warning alone. | Fixed by removal with S14-2. |
| M14-4 | 14 | Medium | Round 13's head comparisons use a 48-bit `entryHash` prefix, which a signer can grind. | Fixed: the CLI's accept command and the co-owner comparison are removed, and every remaining out-of-band head comparison shows the `seq` and 22 characters (132 bits) of the `entryHash` (§6.8 **Attack indicators**, §16.5). |
| M14-5 | 14 | Low | The person's client doesn't check that the `readmit` signer is the owner they asked. | Fixed by removal with S14-6. |
| M14-6 | 14 | Low | The CLI's accept command doesn't set `owners`. | Fixed by removal: the accept command and `owners` are gone (§12.3). |
| E14-F1 | 14 | High | The CLI's accept command and the co-owner comparison rest on a 48-bit prefix. | Fixed with M14-4. |
| E14-F2 | 14 | Medium | An honest restore below an ownership change leaves a space read-only with no way out. | Fixed by removal, along the reviewer's option (b): any restore below a mark leaves the space read-only for good, and the way forward is re-homing, which needs an owner on the served log, such as the owner before the handover. An owner the restored log doesn't show can still publish what section 13 allows them into a space of their own (§13 **Re-homing**, R2). |
| E14-L1 | 14 | Low | The CLI's accept command never sets `owners`. | Fixed by removal with M14-6. |
| E14-L2 | 14 | Low | §6.8 and §10.4 disagree on a predating side's `owners`. | Fixed by removal with M14-1. |
| E14-L3 | 14 | Low | `readmit/v1` has two possible sort orders, no known-answer vector, and missing negative vectors. | Fixed by removal: `readmit/v1` and `"readmit"` requests are gone. |
| E14-L4 | 14 | Low | Dangling text: §16.5's owner-request and server bullets, §3.2's hash list and `owners` IDs. | Fixed by removal: `abandonedHash` and `owners` are gone, and §16.5's bullets now describe only revocation and unpublish requests, as they always did. |
| S15-1 | 15 | Medium | Re-homing suggests the old space's members from the rejected served log, so people removed since the restore point appear. | Fixed with E15-F1: no suggestions come from the old space (§13 **Re-homing** step 4). |
| S15-2 | 15 | Medium | Ownership, publish rights and unpublished status for re-homing and publication from a read-only space come from the rejected log, so an undone `unpublish` or demotion goes unseen. | Fixed with M15-3: nothing is copied by default; the owner selects each version, with the owner's own records of unpublishes shown and the served log's publish state labelled as rejected. R2 now says plainly that these checks use the rejected log, and the "only decides drafts" justification is gone (§13 **Re-homing** steps 1 and 3, R2). |
| S15-3 | 15 | Medium | Nothing defines "held before the space became read-only", so injected content on the rejected branch may be shown, and re-homed, unmarked. | Fixed with the reviewer's test: a record, placement or envelope is unmarked only if its `authorizedHash` matches the entry at its `authorizedSeq` in a held log that passed the mark before read-only, and "by a former member" is judged against that log too; everything else is marked, which in the browser after a reload is everything. Re-homing shows each version's author and `authorizedSeq` (§6.8 **Read-only spaces**, §13 **Re-homing** step 3). |
| S15-4 | 15 | Medium | After an honest restore, members who accept the restored log keep writing to removed people and revoked agents, and no read-only owner can remove them again. | Fixed with M15-1: `close` (§6.4, §6.8 **Closed spaces**); R2 states the confidentiality cost and the operator guidance to close and re-home restored spaces. |
| S15-5 | 15 | Low | Whether a rejected log resolves items is unclear, and the undo and settle offers need an append a read-only client can't make. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: a log failing the mark never resolves or removes an item, so items that give access stay "may still be applied"; the offers become closing the space (an owner) or an `"agent-revoke"` owner request (§6.8 **Read-only spaces**). |
| S15-6 | 15 | Low | `wikistow.rehome` proves nothing, and nothing forbids resolving it to the old space's name or presenting a space as another's continuation. | Fixed: the copy label names the copy's signer by label, the old space by name only if the viewer can open it themself; the interface never follows `wikistow.rehome` or calls a space another's continuation, always shows owners by label, and labels the old space "re-homed" only from copies the viewer signed (§13 **Nothing redirects**). |
| M15-1 | 15 | High | A removal undone by a restore can't be made again, and the old space stays open to writers who seal to the removed person. | Fixed: a new owner-signed `close {}` entry, never redacted, bumping the whole space; replay makes every later entry `log-invalid`, the server refuses every later append and content write, and clients refuse all sealing, saving, publishing and rewrapping there. An owner may sign it while read-only, over the served log, since it can only restrict; re-homing step 1 is closing the old space. R2 and R9 state what it can't do: recall sealed keys, reach members who don't fetch the log, or survive a freeze (§3.5, §6.4, §6.5, §6.8 **Closed spaces**, §8.5, §12.5 step 4, §13 **Re-homing**, R2, R9, §16.5). |
| M15-2 | 15 | High | Re-homing suggests the old space's members from the served log, inviting re-sharing with people removed after the restore point or added on a fork. | Fixed: re-homing takes no recipients from the old space.
