| M15-3 | 15 | Medium | A restore that undid an `unpublish` makes re-homing publish the withdrawn version to the new space's readers. | Fixed: re-homing copies only versions the owner selects per page, as draft or published; for each page the interface shows whether the owner's own records indicate an unpublish or removal, the served log's publish state is labelled as from a rejected log, and nothing is copied automatically (§13 **Re-homing** step 3, R2, §16.5). |
| M15-4 | 15 | Medium | Authorship checks on re-homed copies use the served log, so a removed member's content enters the new space as the owner's own unmarked record. | Fixed: copies are authored and signed by the re-homing owner, carry `wikistow.rehome` inside the payload, and are shown as "copied from <old space> by <owner label>", never as authored by the original author; the owner selects each version with its author and `authorizedSeq` shown, and "by a former member" is also judged against a held log that passed the mark (§6.8 **Read-only spaces**, §13 **Re-homing** step 3, **Nothing redirects**). |
| M15-5 | 15 | Low | `wikistow.rehome` is a self-claim, so anyone can stage a convincing fake successor space. | Fixed with S15-6: the label names the copy's signer, and the interface never presents a space as another's continuation (§13 **Nothing redirects**). |
| M15-6 | 15 | Low | Re-homing reuses Sealing step 3, whose attachment AD hard-codes `"published"`. | Fixed with E15-L4. |
| E15-F1 | 15 | High | Re-homing's re-share suggestions come from the served log, so a rollback or a removed owner's fork puts removed people back, contradicting R2. | Fixed with M15-2; R2 now says re-homing takes no recipient from the old log, and that a removed person the owner still pins can be shared with again only by the owner's own mistake. |
| E15-F2 | 15 | Medium | Re-homing's "current published version … isn't unpublished" is judged on the served log, so withdrawn content is republished. | Fixed with M15-3. |
| E15-L1 | 15 | Low | `readOnly` can be lost before its write is confirmed, and its precedence over advancing the mark is unstated. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: the desktop app and the CLI write `readOnly` to the local file before anything else; a browser session that ends before the write is confirmed loses it (R1); a mark with `readOnly` changes its checkpoints only by union (§6.8 **High-water marks**, **Marks are reconciled, never maxed**, **Read-only spaces**). |
| E15-L2 | 15 | Low | A merge that brings in `readOnly` doesn't close the receiving side's open items, and nothing re-checks `readOnly` before sending. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: every write whose merged mark has `readOnly` closes the space's open items, apart from a `close`, and a client re-checks `readOnly` in the confirmed record before sending (§6.8 **Read-only spaces**, §10.4, §16.5). |
| E15-L3 | 15 | Low | 22 characters resist a second preimage but only about 2^66 for a collision by a signer on both branches, and heads at different seqs have no defined comparison. | Fixed: comparisons show 32 characters (192 bits, about 2^96 for a collision), at one `seq`, the lower of the two heads; each interface shows the entry it holds at a `seq` the person enters (§6.8 **Attack indicators**, §16.5). |
| E15-L4 | 15 | Low | A re-homed draft copy's attachments get the `"published"` AD. | Fixed: re-homing reseals under the new space, the new page and the copy's own audience, with `plainHash` recomputed for them (§13 **Re-homing** step 3, §16.5). |
| E15-L5 | 15 | Low | `wikistow.rehome` has no carry-forward or strip rule, and the "re-homed" label can be spoofed. | Fixed: clients drop `wikistow.rehome` from every later record and from publications; the copy label is shown only on a `basedOn: null` record that carries it, naming its signer; the old space's "re-homed" label comes only from copies the viewer signed; re-homing replaces an earlier `wikistow.rehome` (§13 **Re-homing**, **Nothing redirects**). |
| E15-L6 | 15 | Low | Cross-space publication from a read-only space skips the "hasn't accepted" confirmation. | Fixed: Sealing step 1 adds "on a log this device hasn't accepted": the human confirms, an agent's client refuses (§13 **Sealing**, §16.5). |
| E15-L7 | 15 | Low | §16.5 has a stale vector for merged fork marks and lacks vectors for the new rules. | Fixed: the merged-fork vector now sets `readOnly` and closes open items, and §16.5 adds a missing and non-boolean `readOnly`, `wikistow.rehome.format` as `1.0` and `1e0`, both provenance extensions on a copy, the merge race, the different-seq comparison, and `close` and re-homing vectors. |
| M9-1 | 9 | Medium | Recorded scopes and groups join `S` and `G` on the client only, so an honest server's log is `missing-body` for any member who lost one access path and kept another, and for many former members. | Fixed with S9-2. |
| M9-2 | 9 | Medium | Unbounded, invisible submissions from refused `agent-grant`s inflate the required cascade past any size limit, and if recorded before the hold is checked they defeat the hold; nothing lets the client split the cascade. | Fixed with the reviewer's fixes: §6.8 server rules (bounds first; a refused request records nothing), **Bounding appends** (a **Size** bound: a maximum number of entries per append, `too-many-entries`, and a cap on agents a human has submitted in a space and not yet revoked, `agent-limit`, both installation settings; a removal still too long is split, with the cascade's `agent-revoke`s and the `vouch`es appended first as earlier items of the same change, under the hold, needing no new confirmation), §6.8 **Refusals**, §16.5. A per-account enrollment cap isn't added: the per-space cap bounds the cascade. |
| M9-3 | 9 | Low | For moves, §6.8's silent re-sign and §9.6's "ask if gainers or losers change" disagree: one seals to unconfirmed gainers, the other leaves moves open to the M8-1 lever. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §9.6 step 4 (ask again only if an owner signed in the race or the set of humans gaining or losing changed; otherwise re-sign silently, sealing to an added agent only when its human is a confirmed gainer of the same audience class), §6.8 **Losing a race** (moves follow §9.6 step 4). |
| M9-4 | 9 | Low | The recheck misses a `vouch` for, and a group grant reaching, a key the person has since put in `removed`. | Fixed with E9-L1: §6.8 **Recheck before sending** (an item vouching for a binding of a removed key is abandoned; an item granting to a group with a member in `removed` isn't sent until the person confirms it again). |
| M9-5 | 9 | Low | "The head the person confirmed" isn't stored and counts the person's own landed entries, so devices completing a removal disagree on whether to ask again. | Fixed: §10.2 (`confirmedSeq` on every item, carried unchanged by every item of the change until the person confirms again), §6.8 **Losing a race** (entries of the same change don't count as owner-signed; a batch of only `agent-revoke`s is always re-signed silently). |
| E9-F1 | 9 | Medium | Recorded scopes and groups join `S`/`G`, so an honest server's log is `missing-body` after any partial revoke or group removal. | Fixed with S9-2. |
| E9-F2 | 9 | Medium | `accessSeqs` records only an owner's admission entry, so a demoted owner's grants and memberships can be hidden to pass them off as a former member (S8-1 reopened). | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Recorded access** (`accessSeqs` records every entry in force giving the human access or ownership: `genesis` or `owner-add`, unrevoked grants to them, their memberships in groups not deleted, and unrevoked grants to those groups, for owners too), §10.2, §16.5 demotion vector. |
| E9-F3 | 9 | Medium | Re-signing at a freed `seq` with the same bodies reproduces the closed item's identity, making the private payload `malformed` or the merge a `contacts-conflict`. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Retrying** (every entry of a new item has a fresh `salt`, even one repeating a closed item's entry at the same `seq`, so no two items share an identity, and the writer checks that before the write), §16.5 vector. |
| E9-F4 | 9 | Medium | "Partial" is undefined for closed items: whether to complete the change the person dropped, and no offer to revoke the landed gives. | Fixed with the reviewer's fix: §6.8 **Resolving an append** (partial: for a closed item the rest is never sent or recomputed; the landed entries are treated as landed, with the offer to undo them; the item is removed once the person decides), §16.5 vector. |
| E9-L1 | 9 | Low | "Binds" is undefined for `vouch`, and automatic abandonment drops removals silently. | Fixed: §6.8 **Recheck before sending** (a `vouch` for a binding of a removed key counts; abandoning tells the person, and the item's entries that remove access are recomputed as a new item of the same change). |
| E9-L2 | 9 | Low | The failed case short-circuits on another item of the change landing, though items of one change can differ. | Fixed: §6.8 **Resolving an append** (failed: decided only by nothing of the change being missing, judged against the change's newest item). |
| E9-L3 | 9 | Low | The rate limit has no `agent-revoke` exemption, so a sibling agent can keep its human's revocation `rate-limited`. | Fixed: §6.8 **Bounding appends** (an append of only `agent-revoke`s doesn't count toward the rate limit and is never refused by it). |
| E9-L4 | 9 | Low | Refusal reason codes, the `cascade-incomplete` list's wire shape and the hold route aren't specified. | Fixed: §6.8 **Refusals** (the codes clients act on; any other is server text; `missingAgents` as strict `{agentId, humanId, agentBoxPublicKey, agentSignPublicKey}` objects), **Bounding appends** (`POST` and `DELETE /api/spaces/:id/removal-holds`), §16.5. Step 2 and step 3 failures get no individual codes: clients only show them. |
| E9-L5 | 9 | Low | Gaps in the private payload: `refusal` on merge, nested-list rules, embedded-entry strictness, an empty remainder, bad signatures on resend. | Fixed: §10.4 (`abandoned` over `refused` sets `refusal` to `null`), §10.2 (**Nested lists**: sets, sorted ascending without duplicates by writers, duplicates read as one; embedded entries strict under §3.3 rules 6 and 8 with `signerId` the owner; a bad signature on resend closes the item as `abandoned` and is reported), §6.8 **Resolving an append** (an empty remainder records no new item). Recorded seqs above the mark, or naming no access, are `log-fork` (S9-6). |
| E9-L6 | 9 | Low | A refused removal awaiting reconfirmation isn't persisted, so ending the session forgets it. | Fixed: §6.8 **Resolving an append** (a failed removal stays recorded, and on screen, until the person makes it again or dismisses it, so every device and later session asks again). No new item state is needed: a failed open item doesn't hold its `seq`. |
| E9-L7 | 9 | Low | §4.2 and §4.5 are headed "as built" but state Milestone 2 checks; "Info" isn't a defined severity; the prefix table doesn't match the IDs in use. | Fixed: §4.2 (the full `AgentId` pattern is a Milestone 2 change; `isAgentId` tests only the prefix today), §4.5 (the `keyVersion` check is a Milestone 2 change), this log's **Severity** table (an Info row) and prefix table. |
| S16-1 | 16 | Medium | A read-only owner's `close` is signed over a log prefix the server picks; the server shows that closed branch to chosen members, where it finishes their agent revocations and settles their removals, while everyone else stays on a branch without it, and the closed branch hides itself because nothing more is written there. | Accepted (R2 **What a `close` doesn't settle**, R9 **A fork at the grant or at the revoke**). §6.8's "gives nothing to anyone" corrected in wording: a `close` gives no one access, but is an owner's signature over a server-chosen prefix. Detectable only by out-of-band head comparison. |
| S16-2 | 16 | Low | R9, §6.9, §10.4 and §16.5 say only an `agent-revoke` makes a space done, while §12.5 step 4 also counts a `close`. | Wording fixed: R9 (two places), §6.9, §10.4 and §16.5 now agree with §12.5 step 4 (an `agent-revoke` or a `close`). The risk of counting a `close` is accepted with S16-1. |
| S16-3 | 16 | Low | A read-only `close` item's outcome below its base, and when the signer is no longer an owner at the new head, is undefined. | Accepted (R2 **What a `close` doesn't settle**, **A restore can leave no one able to close** and **Cases left to implementations**). |
| S16-4 | 16 | Low | Re-homing's cautions draw on records nothing persists, so in the browser they are almost always empty. | Accepted (R2 **What a `close` doesn't settle**, **Re-homing cautions only from what the device still holds**). |
| S16-5 | 16 | Low | Whether owners can still delete nodes or the space after a `close` is undefined. | Accepted as server policy (R2 **Cases left to implementations**, Q9). Availability only. |
| S16-6 | 16 | Low | Which log a read-only client takes the entry from for a head comparison is undefined. | Accepted with E16-6 (R2 **Head comparisons in a read-only space**). |
| M16-1 | 16 | Medium | After a restore, a removed owner made an owner again can hold and remove the read-only owners before their `close` lands, and a restore that predates every current owner leaves nobody able to close. | Accepted (R2 **A restore can leave no one able to close**): members who accept the restored log keep writing until an administrator suspends that person (R7). |
| M16-2 | 16 | Low | A `close` fixes membership, so it can pre-empt a removal, and settling the removal's item drops the re-homing caution for that person. | Accepted (R2 **Membership is fixed at the `close`**). Nothing is sealed after the `close` (R6). |
| M16-3 | 16 | Low | A read-only owner's `close` lands on the branch the server chose but is shown as closing the space, and quietly ends co-owners' pending removals there. | Accepted with S16-1 (R2 **A read-only owner's `close` lands where the server chose**); §6.8's justification corrected in wording. |
| E16-1 | 16 | Medium | The §16.5 vector "a `close` body with a field (`log-invalid`)" contradicts §3.3 rule 1 and §6.3: an extra member is a text-level defect, so a server could make an honest owner's `close` blame its signer. | Wording fixed: the vector is now "a `close` body with an extra member and a matching `bodyHash` (`malformed`, handled as undelivered, then `missing-body`, never `log-invalid`; section 3.3, rule 1)", which agrees with the text. The 15-byte-salt case is already a vector. |
| E16-2 | 16 | Low | R9 and §16.5 contradict §12.5 step 4 on whether a `close` finishes an agent revocation. | Wording fixed with S16-2. |
| E16-3 | 16 | Low | A `close` item's lifecycle below its base, after a merge brings `readOnly`, and in a mixed batch is unspecified. | Accepted (R2 **Cases left to implementations**). |
| E16-4 | 16 | Low | §6.6's signing blocks don't list `close`. | Accepted as written (R2 **Cases left to implementations**): no exemption is listed, so an owner under a signing block can't close until it clears. |
| E16-5 | 16 | Low | The route and page mapping for re-homed published copies are undefined. | Accepted (R2 **Cases left to implementations**): left to PR 2; no choice seals to anyone the owner didn't choose. |
| E16-6 | 16 | Low | Which entry a read-only interface shows for a 32-character head comparison is undefined. | Accepted with S16-6 (R2 **Head comparisons in a read-only space**). |

## Outcome

The review ran **16 rounds**, each with three independent adversarial briefs (server, member, encoding). Across all rounds it found:

| Severity | Findings |
|---|---|
| Critical | 4 |
| High | 41 |
| Medium | 94 |
| Low | 205 |
| Info | 1 |

That is 344 findings, plus one informational note. Every finding above has an outcome. Round 10 found nothing High or Critical, but its fixes added a reset that round 11 found a High in, so review went on. Round 16 is the first confirmation round with nothing High or Critical, and it meets the stopping rule. Its findings were fixed in wording or accepted as residual risks, with no new mechanism added, because new mechanisms added after a clean round had reopened High findings before.

the project owner made two scope decisions during the review:

1. **Read-only recovery** (round 12). The self-service reset after a rollback was removed. A space whose log fails its mark became read-only until an owner re-admitted the person.
2. **Re-homing with close** (rounds 14 and 15). In-place recovery was removed. Read-only is permanent, and an owner re-homes selected versions into a new space, starting with an owner-signed `close` of the old one.

This review counts as the protocol's focused security review that the Milestone 2 plan and `wikistow.md` L333 and L357 require. protocol.md is **frozen for Milestone 2** (§1, §18). Open questions and limitations remain in protocol.md §17. Any change after the freeze needs a new review round logged here, and a changed format also needs a new domain version (rule 5 above).

That outcome is for the access-log design. the project owner replaced it on 2026-10-05, before any of it shipped; see **Change of design** below.

## Change of design (2026-10-05)

On 2026-10-05 the project owner replaced the Milestone 2 design that rounds 1 to 16 reviewed. The signed, hash-chained access log per space had grown too complicated to build and explain: owner vouching, endorsed prefixes, high-water marks, read-only spaces, re-homing and `close`, node placements and owner requests each answered a real finding, and together they made a protocol few people could follow. He asked for something "secure, but not overly complicated, like Joplin".

The new design (protocol.md, sections 6 to 11):

- **Folder keys.** Each shared space or folder has its own versioned key set: a draft key and a published key. Each revision's content key is wrapped with one of them. Readers get only published keys. Content outside every share stays sealed to the space's owners, as in Milestone 1.
- **Spaces and folders only.** No page grants, and shares don't nest.
- **Only owners seal folder keys**, during Share, Remove or adding a co-owner, to the people shown in the dialog. Saves never choose recipients. A person's own app seals keys to their own agents.
- **No safety codes.** Each app pins the first key it sees for each person, and warns and stops sharing when that key changes.
- **Removal rotates the keys.** The new key version goes only to the people who held the previous one, minus the removed person, and the server refuses saves under the old version.
- **Groups are deferred** to a later milestone (#30).
- **Accepted risks** (protocol.md §2.4). A malicious or breached server can ignore role rules, hide or roll back changes undetected, and keep serving a removed person data they could already decrypt. It can't read content, add a reader by itself, or decrypt anything saved after a removal. Keys are trusted on first sight, so a server can substitute a key before any app has seen the real one.

**What still applies from rounds 1 to 16.** Findings and outcomes about parts this design keeps still stand: the primitives and encodings (protocol.md §3), records and their key commitment (§5), the contacts record format (§8.1), agent enrollment and credentials, and the CLI's output rules (§9).

**What no longer applies.** Findings and outcomes about the access log, its replay and redaction, vouched keys and the endorsed prefix, high-water marks, read-only spaces, re-homing and `close`, node placements and the legacy manifests, audiences and server subtraction, pending access and rewrap operations, the author authority check, safety codes, owner requests and the agent revocation flow no longer apply. Those mechanisms are gone, so their findings are closed as **superseded**, not fixed. The Known items below were raised for that design; items 6, 7, 8, 10 and 11 still apply.

## Round 17: the folder-key design

Round 17 is the first review of the folder-key design: one security and implementability review of the `m2-share-design` branch (protocol.md, `wikistow.md`, CONTEXT.md, this log and the plan) against the #29 code, taking the project owner's decisions and the accepted risks A1–A11 as given. It found no Critical findings, 3 High, 5 Medium and 3 Low, plus 11 places where two implementations would differ (D1–D11) and 5 cuts (C1–C5). The core cryptography held: wraps, envelopes, the signed class, key commitment, readers never holding draft keys, agents never holding the owner region, and ancestor names staying in the owner region. The High findings were all about who counts as an accepted owner and which region a write lands in.

the project owner's rule for the fixes: "secure, but not overly complicated, like Joplin". Each fix is the smallest one that closes the finding. The only additions are the removed-owners list (M2) and the rule about which version a client writes under (H2). A first draft of that rule also required the sealing owner to be listed by the server, which stopped owners removing themselves. It was dropped: a malicious server can list a removed owner again anyway (A5), so it protected little and made a transfer take two people. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Severity | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | High | Accepted owners had no starting point for a co-owner, a non-creator owner or a Milestone 1 space. The obvious guess, taking the server's owner list, let a server add itself as an owner. | Fixed: §6.5 is one numbered rule. An app accepts itself if it authored the space's name record (new and Milestone 1 spaces), otherwise the signer of the first statement or folder key envelope that named it (first sight, A4), then anyone named in a statement by an owner it accepts. §14.2 points to it. |
| H2 | High | The "currently listed" filter also gated opening, so removing or transferring an owner made their v1 records, their key versions and their v2 envelopes unreadable. | Fixed: §6.5 opens with every owner ever accepted, and writes under a version sealed by any accepted owner the app hasn't removed (§6.8); only sealing uses the **sealing owners** (accepted, listed, not removed). Any owner can remove an owner, themself included, unless none would remain (`last-owner`), so a transfer is the old owner adding the new one and removing themself (§7.6). |
| H3 | High | Relabelling the tree could land an owner's new page or folder in a share, because `region-mismatch` compared only with `basedOn`; move previews took gainers from the server's member list. | Fixed: §6.1 defines a record's region by the key material that opened it, and a new node's region by its parent's own name record. §6.8 refuses when the tree disagrees. §10 takes regions the same way and gainers from verified envelopes of the destination's current version. |
| M1 | Medium | Statements signed automatically at every unlock spread a first-sight acceptance to every app in the space. | Fixed: §6.5 signs statements only when adding a co-owner: the adder for the new owner, and the new owner once for the adder. Nothing is signed at unlock. A4 says what a substitution at a co-owner's first contact costs. |
| M2 | Medium | The rotation set after removing an owner was ambiguous, and the remover's own app kept accepting the removed owner. | Fixed: §7.5 gives `remaining = ((E ∩ M) ∪ O) − X`. §8.2 adds `spaceOwners[].removedOwners`, written in a confirmed write (§7.6) and merged by union, except that the person's own "add a co-owner" takes an entry out, as a pin takes out a `removed` entry (§8.6); the remover's app doesn't accept them again unless its person adds them back. A5 covers other apps. |
| M3 | Medium | Rotations trusted the server's `currentKeyVersion`, so hiding one owner's rotation let another owner undo a removal. | Fixed: §6.8 **No going back** refuses (`key-version`) to write, rotate or move under a version below the highest one opened. §7.5 step 1 and §10 apply it. |
| M4 | Medium | Folder keys and `shareId` weren't scoped to the space. | Fixed: §6.2 names keys by `(spaceId, shareId, keyVersion, class)` everywhere; §6.1 makes share IDs unique across the installation; agents' `shares` items are `{spaceId, shareId}` (§8.2). |
| M5 | Medium | An agent's draft-key class at unlock came from the server's grant. | Fixed: §9.4 takes it from the agent's previous verified envelope; only a first grant uses the dialog's role. |
| L1 | Low | A browser contacts rollback re-enables sealing to a revoked agent. | Accepted, stated in A6. |
| L2 | Low | Retrying after `key-version` reuses the content key. | Accepted, stated in A2. |
| L3 | Low | Owner statements didn't require a human owner. | Fixed: §6.5. |
| D1–D6 | High–Medium | Divergences behind H1–H3, M2, M4 and M5, plus owners fetching all envelopes of a version (D3) and a record's region (D4). | Fixed with those findings; §6.4 **Serving** defines the owner route for D3. |
| D7 | Low | Which owner is accepted first when a member is in several shares. | Fixed: §6.5 step 2 decides once per space, statements first, then envelopes, in the server's order. |
| D8 | Low | A reader's `draftKey`: `null` or absent. | Fixed: §6.4, present and `null`; absent is `malformed`. |
| D9 | Low | Contacts lists had no stated uniqueness keys. | Fixed: §8.2 **Uniqueness** and **Sets**. |
| D10 | Low | Rotation request shape, and the server's exact-set check against members the client left out. | Fixed: §7.5 step 4. `removed` lists every listed member the app leaves out; owners are never left out, so no owner is stranded without keys. |
| D11 | Low | Demoting an editor took their role from their own envelope. | Fixed: §7.4 and §7.5. The dialog sets the demoted person's role, envelopes set everyone else's, and the request names them in `demoted`. |
| C1 | Low | §4.6 identity rotation format. | Cut: §4.6 defers it to a later milestone; `identity-rotation/v1` left the registry. |
| C2 | Low | §7.8 Stopping a share. | Cut: §6.1 says a folder holding a share can't be shared in Milestone 2. |
| C3 | Low | The `key-conflict` check. | Cut, with its error. |
| C4 | Low | Q6 (rotation after unpublish) was dropped but still appeared. | Done: §11, §7.5, §15 (`key-rotation-needed` gone), Q6, CONTEXT.md **Unpublish** and **Key rotation**, and the plan's Unpublish bullet. |
| C5 | — | Automatic statements at unlock. | Removed with M1. |

Protocol.md §16 now lists, for PR 2 and PR 3, what each changes in the #29 code and the route shapes PR 3 needs, from the review's implementability section.

**Outcome.** Every finding is addressed by this revision. Round 18 repeats the review against it.

## Round 18: the folder-key design, second review

Round 18 reviewed the round 17 revision (73a9d0b) the same way, taking the project owner's decisions and A1–A11 as given, including dropping the "currently listed" condition for writing. It found no Critical finding, 1 High, 6 Medium and 9 Low, plus gaps in the PR 2 and PR 3 notes. Every round 17 High was confirmed fixed. the project owner's rule held for the fixes: "secure, but not overly complicated, like Joplin". The only new field is the agent's `drafts` flag (N6); everything else is wording, a reorder of an existing dialog, or pointers. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Severity | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | High | Only new saves used the writing set. Rotating from a version, moves, publications, sealing to agents and the **No going back** mark could still use a version a removed owner sealed, so the remover's own app could seal to a person the removed owner and the server chose. | Fixed: §6.5 **Writing** now covers every use of a key version other than reading through it, and §6.8, §7.5 step 1, §9.4, §10 and §13 point to it. The accepted-owner module returns separate opening and writing predicates (§16). One addition beyond wording: the rotations that remove an owner, and the new owner's rotations after a transfer, still count that owner's envelopes when verifying version `v`, or a share that owner rotated last couldn't be rotated away from them (§7.6). |
| N2 | Medium | An owner who removes themself, or transfers the space, made the new versions and knows them, which §2.3 and A5 didn't say; the new owner could never exclude the old one. | Fixed: §2.3 and A5 say so. §7.6 **Transfer**: the new owner's app offers Rotate keys for every share, and doing so adds the old owner to its `removedOwners`. The self-removal rotation stays, and §7.6 says its purpose is leaving out the leaver's agents, whose grants the server also ends. |
| N3 | Medium | The **No going back** mark had no stated home, and its claim held only within one session. | Fixed: §6.8 keeps it per `(spaceId, shareId)`, in the local file on desktop and in the CLI (§9.3), and in memory for one browser session; the claim is narrowed to match, and A2 covers the browser case. |
| N4 | Medium | A write with `basedOn: null` on an existing page (publications, detach) had no region. | Fixed: §6.1 takes the region of that page's current draft or published record as opened; a new destination page takes its parent's region. §6.8 points to it. |
| N5 | Medium | At the Milestone 1 upgrade, a server withholding the owner's own name record got first sight to accept its own identity. | Fixed: §6.5 step 2. When the server lists the app's identity as an owner, first sight takes only statements, and accepts their signer only after the person confirms the co-owner dialog; declining accepts nobody. §14.2 notes the case. |
| N6 | Medium | An agent's class came from its latest envelope, which the server picks, and demoting an agent had no flow. | Fixed: agent `shares` items carry `drafts: boolean` (§8.2), which sets the class at unlock (§9.4). **Changing an agent's role** sets `drafts` in a confirmed write, re-seals and replaces the agent's envelopes, and on a demotion offers to ask owners to rotate. The `missing` route drops `latest` (§16). |
| N7 | Medium | Browser contacts rollback makes pins and accepted owners first-sight again, and drops `removedOwners`; A4, A5 and §2.5 read as unconditional. | Fixed: A4, A5 and the §2.5 row say so, citing A9. |
| L1 | Low | Agents' envelopes in a rotation's version `v` had no rule. | Fixed: §7.5 step 1 verifies envelopes to humans and skips agents' ones, which add nobody to `E`. |
| L2 | Low | Sealing to a new co-owner, who isn't listed yet, contradicted the sealing-owner rule. | Fixed: §6.5 **Sealing** names the co-owner being added. |
| L3 | Low | "The space's name record" was ambiguous. | Fixed: §6.5 step 1 takes the space's first name record, with `basedOn: null`, authored by the app's identity. |
| L4 | Low | A4 understated how a substituted adder spreads. | Fixed: A4 says every app in the space accepts it through the co-owner's statement, and that the co-owner dialog is where it can be noticed. |
| L5 | Low | A5 didn't say a removed owner's statements still count elsewhere, or that a later co-owner doesn't inherit `removedOwners`. | Fixed: A5. |
| L6 | Low | First sight's order across shares was the implementation's. | Fixed: §6.5 step 2 orders envelopes by `shareId` as bytes, then `keyVersion`; §16 adds `GET /api/spaces/:spaceId/my-envelopes`. |
| L7 | Low | A removal that failed after the contacts write left the app stuck. | Fixed: §7.6 refuses other owner actions and offers only a retry, which repeats the rotation and request; the contacts change is idempotent. |
| L8 | Low | "The adder" was undefined, and declining the dialog had no outcome. | Fixed: §6.5 defines it as the signer of the first acceptable statement naming this identity at its current key version; declining signs nothing and takes no owner action. |
| L9 | Low | Move preview gainers are a lower bound. | Fixed: §10 says so, and the preview reads "at least these people gain access". |
| PR 2, PR 3 | — | Missing module outputs and routes. | Done: §16 adds the two predicates, the region case and the mark's storage, and routes for agent approval with grants and envelopes, granting, regrading and removing a pick, agent grants when making a share, a person's envelopes in a space, and the owner-removal retry. |

**Outcome.** Every finding is addressed by this revision, with one rule beyond wording (the N1 exception for removal rotations) and one new field (N6). Round 19 repeats the review against it.

## Round 19: the folder-key design, confirmation review

Round 19 reviewed the round 18 revision (60ed7d6) the same way, taking the project owner's decisions, A1–A11 and the removal exception of §7.6 as given. It found no Critical or High finding, 1 Medium and 4 Low, and confirmed every round 18 finding fixed, including the PR 2 and PR 3 gaps. Nothing blocked PR 2. The fixes remove a special case rather than add one: the transfer exception is gone. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Severity | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | Medium | The transfer text didn't say whether the old owner went on `removedOwners` before or after the new owner's rotations, or which rotations the exception covered. If the list was written first and one rotation then failed, the new owner's app couldn't save in that share, and a later Rotate keys refused its current version. | Fixed: §7.6 **Transfer**. The new owner's app rotates every share first, while the old owner isn't on its list, so their envelopes count as usual. Only after every rotation succeeds does it add the old owner to `removedOwners`, in a confirmed write. A failure or a stop part-way leaves the offer pending; the app shows it again, and a retry rotates every share whose current version the new owner didn't seal. The transfer exception is dropped; A5 says the list is written after the rotations. |
| T2 | Low | §7.5 step 1 refuses a `v` that doesn't count for writing, and the §7.6 exception spoke only of verifying `v`, so a literal reading refused every share the removed owner rotated last. | Fixed: §7.6 step 2 says `R`'s current version counts for step 1's writing check, and `R`'s envelopes count when verifying it, in the remover's rotations only, without moving the **No going back** mark. §7.5 step 1 points to it as the only exception. |
| T3 | Low | The `my-envelopes` route's "at its current key version" could mean the caller's identity key version or each share's folder key version. | Fixed: §16 says "at the caller's current identity key version, for every folder key version". |
| T4 | Low | The region of a `basedOn: null` write had no rule when the page's draft and published records were opened in different regions, or when the app opened neither. | Fixed: §6.1 takes the current draft if the app opened it, otherwise the current published record, and refuses (`region-mismatch`) if the two were opened in different regions or neither was opened. |
| T5 | Low | §6.7's server check for making a share didn't cover agents in `agentGrants`, and the approval route's "versions 1 to current" could refuse when the client seals only up to the highest version that counts for its writing. | Fixed: §6.7 step 5 checks each agent in `agentGrants`. The §16 approval route says the client seals only up to that version, so a served version it can't write under makes approval refuse; the server could refuse anyway, so this is accepted. |

**Outcome.** Round 19 found nothing high or critical, so it meets the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed in this revision, which leaves only the removal exception of §7.6. protocol.md's status is now reviewed and frozen for Milestone 2: the identifiers in §3.5, and its byte layouts and rules, change only through a new domain version and a new entry here.

Clarifications only: no domain string changed, and no format or rule except where a row says so.

| 2026-10-06 | No spec change. §16's agent rows move from PR 3 to PR 5, which brings agent identities: `GET /api/me/agents/:agentId/missing` and `POST …/envelopes`, approving an enrollment with grants, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/me/agents/:agentId/grants/:shareId`, `agentGrants` in Make a share (PR 3 accepts only an empty list), and ending agents' grants when a member is removed (§7.5, step 4) or an owner is removed (§7.6). | PR 3's review, round 1 (code M2), approved by the lead: no agent exists to hold a grant before PR 5. PR 5's reviewers check these rows against §16. |
| 2026-10-06 | No format change. §7.7's "the owner the app accepted first for that space" holds within the session that accepted them: there, Shared with me names that first-sight owner for every share in the space. In a later session it names, for each share, the owner who sealed the person's earliest key version of that share. | PR 3's review, round 2, accepted by the lead: the contacts record keeps a space's accepted owners as a set (§8.2), so the order they were first seen in isn't stored, and a later session can't recover which came first. The two can name different owners for a share a co-owner made. |
| 2026-10-06 | No format change. §9.7: a password change ends every agent credential, as a reset does, and each account-wide revocation (reset, change, administrator, suspension) also revokes the account's agent identities and ends their grants; the person's app records them in `revokedAgents` at its next unlock. | PR 5's review, round 1 (security M1 and L1, code M1), approved by the lead: the revocation runs in a database trigger on the password write, so a reset can't half-apply, and it can't tell a reset from a change. A revoked-but-live-looking agent kept receiving sealed keys and kept its name. |
| 2026-10-06 | No spec change. §9.5's "create pages and folders, edit drafts" doesn't include renaming or moving a folder or page: those stay with people, as do deleting and every sharing action, so a CLI shouldn't offer them to an agent. | PR 5's review, round 1 (code review, the implementer's readings), approved by the lead: a rename is neither creating nor editing a draft, and the server keeps the metadata route for people only. |
| 2026-10-06 | No format change. The CLI's own files, which never leave its machine, use domains of their own, outside §3.5's registry of what crosses the wire: `cli-key-file/v1` (AEAD AD of the key file's secrets: format, server, agent ID), `cli-key-passphrase-wrap/v1` (server, agent ID, salt, opsLimit, memLimit), `cli-key-store-wrap/v1` (server, agent ID, tool, service, account), `cli-key-store-kek/v1` (hash input: the secret store's 32-byte secret), `cli-state/v1` (agent ID) and `cli-journal/v1` (hash input naming a journal file: agent ID, idempotency key). §9.3's local file is encrypted and authenticated under a key the key file holds, and a missing or changed one is refused rather than started afresh, since starting over would forget every No going back mark. | PR 6 (CLI): each local use gets its own domain, as §3.5 requires of every domain, so none can be taken for another. A copy of an older state file still opens: rollback of the local file by someone who can write the key-file directory is accepted, as they could replace the key file too. |
| 2026-10-07 | No format change. §17 gains Q10: owners aren't told when an agent is changed to reader, though it keeps the draft keys it had. The agent's person is offered Rotate keys when they own the space, and told to ask the owners otherwise. | PR 7's review, round 1 (code M5): §9.4's offer to ask the owners has no owner-side signal yet, since the server doesn't track which keys an envelope held. |
| 2026-10-07 | No format change. §17 gains Q11: the page's 🤖 pill and the activity card show the server's claim of which agent saved a draft. Checking it against the draft's signed author is deferred. | PR 7's review, round 2 (security questions): neither drives a key decision, and the check would need the draft revision, which the published view doesn't load. |
| 2026-10-07 | No format change. §10: a move across a region boundary also lists what moves, by decrypted name, and the owner's app wraps only the records of the folders and pages it listed, as §6.7 does for a new share. A version saved while the move is on its way is missing from it, so the server answers `stale-head` and the app wraps that version too and sends the move again. ## Round 20: comments

Round 20 is the first review of the comments design (ADR 0014, #320, accepted 2026-11-09), against `adr-comments` (6a82048). It ran the three briefs (server, member, encoding) as three independent reviewers in parallel. Each got only its brief, protocol.md, the ADR, and the code the design reuses: `contracts/src/records`, the client's record, envelope, key-wrap and encoding code, and the server's checks in `server/src/content`. They took the project owner's nine decisions and the accepted risks A1 to A13 and Q13 as given, and attacked how the decisions are specified. Users are trusted family and friends, so contrived cases were left out. It found no Critical finding, 1 High, 3 Medium and 11 Low. The core held: the wrap AD authenticates a comment's audience, so no server label gets a comment for editors wrapped under a published key; `pageId`, `thread` and `audience` are bound by `payloadHash` and `keyCommitment`; a comment can't open as a page version or a name; and the owner operations take the audience from what they opened. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S20-1 | 20 | High | 2.4 A2, 5.9 **Region**, 6.8 | With a member who holds a version's content key and another share's key, such as a reader removed from one share and still in another, a server can show the page in that other share: the member wraps the version under its key and the tree agrees. A reader's comment there goes under that share's keys, which the removed member holds. The same already worked for an editor's draft save; comments widen it to readers. | Accepted: A2 names it, for comments and saves alike, and §5.9 **Region** points to it. §6.8's claim that a relabelled tree can't put an owner's edit in a share now says "unless a member wraps the record it takes its region from". Closing it needs owner-signed wraps, which is more than this case is worth for family spaces. |
| S20-2 | 20 | Medium | 5.9 **The payload**, **Opening** | A reply bound only its thread's ID. With a member who signs a new version under a first comment's ID, a server ignoring the author check (A1) could put that member's question at the head of the thread, and others' signed answers then read as answers to it. | Fixed: §5.9. The payload gains `threadAuthor`, the first comment's signed `authorId` (`null` for a first comment); while the first comment is there and opens, a reply whose `threadAuthor` differs is `binding-mismatch`. |
| S20-3 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **Opening** | "Edited" came from the server's times, though the record signs it. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 6. A comment shows "edited" when its signed `basedOn` isn't `null`. |
| M20-1 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **The payload** (the page's own text) | The list of parts a quote takes as source didn't name embeds. The reviewer rated it High if the reader shows an embedded page's text; it doesn't (page embeds stay inert text), so it is Low. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 5. Embeds join the list, and the rule is stated in general: everything the app or a plugin draws from outside the version is taken as its source. |
| M20-2 | 20 | Medium | 5.9 **The payload**; ADR decision 13 | Nothing kept link or property completion out of the comment boxes. An editor picking from `[[` completion in a reply to readers would write a draft title, or an owner-region title, to every reader. | Fixed: §5.9 (**Typed text stays the writer's own**) and ADR decision 13. No comment, reply or edit box offers link, title or property completion. |
| M20-3 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **The payload**; ADR decision 5 | The writer checked a readers' quote against the latest unpublish only when the comment was first sent. A retry after an unpublish's `permission-version` would send a withdrawn passage. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 5. The check runs before every send, retries included, at the permission version it sends. |
| M20-4 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **Writing**; ADR decision 6 | Deleting one's own comment and resolving a thread one started didn't need current access, so a removed or demoted person could still act on threads they can't read. | Fixed: §5.9 **Writing** and ADR decisions 4 and 6. An edit, an author's delete and resolving need the same access to the comment's audience now; only an owner's delete doesn't. |
| M20-5 | 20 | Low | ADR decision 10 | `--for` was required only on first comments, by an agent that "may write both audiences", which nothing defined. The ADR's own case, a reader asking an agent to answer, is a reply. | Fixed: ADR decision 10 and its threat model. `page comment` always takes `--for`; on a reply the CLI refuses one that isn't the thread's audience as it opened it. |
| M20-6 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **For the build** | The built `metadataWrapSubject` always says `"metadata"` and the published key, and the v2 key unseal skips the payload, so reusing them for comments would wrap a comment for editors under the published key. | Fixed: §5.9 **For the build**. Comments get their own wrap subject with their audience as the class; owners open a comment in full before rewrapping it. Same as E20-L2. |
| M20-7 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **The payload**; ADR decision 13 | Nothing said where an unsent comment lives. Kept in `localStorage`, it would sit in plain text outside A13's sealed storage. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 13. It is kept in memory only, and goes when the app locks or signs out. |
| E20-F1 | 20 | Medium | 5.9 **Writing**; ADR decision 12 | Version IDs were unique only one way: a folder's new name could take a comment version's ID, which every reader sees. Metadata envelopes are stored by version ID alone, so a later move of that folder deleted the comment's envelopes, and the comment then stopped every owner operation on its page. | Fixed: §5.9 **Writing** and ADR decisions 4 and 12. Comment and metadata version IDs are one set, checked both ways, and a comment's wraps and envelopes are stored under a kind of their own. |
| E20-L1 | 20 | Low | 5.4, 5.7, 5.9 **For the build** | §5.7 typed `subjectKind` as space or folder only; the built v1 and v2 schemas share one enum, so adding `"comment"` there would let a v1 comment parse; the share scan took the expected kind from the record, and the rename route refused only groups. | Fixed: §5.7 (group v2 only, comment as accepted) and §5.9 **For the build** (separate v1 and v2 kinds, scan entries fix their kind, the metadata route by a list of what it takes). |
| E20-L2 | 20 | Low | 6.3, 5.9 **For the build** | Same as M20-6, from the wrap AD. | Fixed with M20-6. A client also refuses a listed audience other than `"draft"` or `"published"`. |
| E20-L3 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **The payload**, **Writing** | `thread` absent or `null`, and `quote: null`, had no stated meaning; readers' `malformed` list read as excluding shape checks; and the server didn't tie the request's comment ID to the record. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 4. `thread` and `threadAuthor` are present as `null` or IDs, a `quote` is absent or an object, shape failures are `malformed`, and the server refuses a request whose record isn't a comment's v2 record for the ID it names, `basedOn: null` for a first version (`invalid-request`). |
| E20-L4 | 20 | Low | 5.9 **A retained record**; ADR decision 7 | A comment in a newer major format stopped an owner operation as unopenable, and the app offered to delete it. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 7. It stops the operation with "Update the app", and no audience is taken from a payload of a newer major format. *Round 21 correction (M21-2):* the stop offers to delete it too, since any member can write one. |

**Outcome.** Round 20 found 1 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. The High is accepted as a residual risk in A2; every other finding is fixed. Round 21 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 21: comments, confirmation review

Round 21 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 20 revision (1fdef59). It found no Critical or High finding, 2 Medium and 6 Low. Round 20's fixes held: `threadAuthor` stops a server putting another person's question at the head of a thread, the one set of version IDs keeps an envelope to one record, quotes take embeds as source, the boxes offer no completion, `--for` is always required, and the build notes match the code they name. The member-wrap placement stays in A2. Following the stopping rule, the fixes are plain rules in wording, with no new field or mechanism. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M21-1 | 21 | Medium | 5.9 **The payload**; ADR decisions 5 and 13 | An unsent comment was kept per view but not per page, and the writer didn't require a quote's version to be of the comment's page. A quote taken from one page's draft, left in the panel while the person went to a page in another share, could be sent to that share's editors. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decisions 5 and 13. An unsent comment belongs to one page and one view, and a quote's `revisionId` must be a version of `pageId` that the writer's app opened. |
| M21-2 | 21 | Medium | 5.9 **A retained record of its page**; ADR decision 7 | Round 20's E20-L4 fix offered no delete for a comment in a newer major format, but any member can write one on purpose. Every share, cross-region move, ending or co-owner over its page then stopped with "Update the app", which no update fixes. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 7. The stop says to update, names the page and the comment's signed author, and offers to delete it too; owners' **Delete** in the panel works on every comment, opened or not. The audience is still never taken from a newer major payload. Same as E21-L3. |
| S21-1 | 21 | Low | 5.9 **The payload**; ADR decision 1 | In the owner region, a reply under a first comment in a newer major format could take its audience from the server's listing alone, so a reply to an editors' thread could be sealed as for readers and later wrapped under the published key. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 1. A writer takes a reply's audience only from a first comment it parsed, or in a share from its wrap's class, and offers no reply under one it can't open or read. |
| S21-2 | 21 | Low | 5.9 **The payload** | "Before every send" could make an edit drop a quote that "an edit keeps". | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 5. The check runs on a first version and its retries; an edit carries its quote unchanged. |
| M21-3 | 21 | Low | 7.4; ADR decision 7 | Only §7.3's preview said a person gets the comments for their role. Making a reader an editor, directly or through a group, gives every comment for editors without a word. | Fixed: §7.4 and ADR decision 7. Every dialog that gives a reader draft keys says they also get the retained drafts and comments for editors. |
| E21-L1 | 21 | Low | 5.9 **Writing**; ADR decision 4 | The 65,536-byte limit didn't say whether it counts decoded bytes or base64url characters, as the page limit does; the largest valid payload is about 64,100 bytes sealed. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 4. The limit counts decoded bytes, and later minor versions must keep within it. |
| E21-L2 | 21 | Low | 5.9 **The payload** | `extensions: { }` read as always empty, `quote` wasn't said to be loose, and the text's character rule could be read as refusing more than lone surrogates. | Fixed: §5.9 and ADR decision 3. `extensions: { [namespace]: JSON }`, the quote is loose like the payload, and the text fields take every well-formed scalar value. |
| E21-L3 | 21 | Low | 5.9 **A retained record of its page** | Same as M21-2. | Fixed with M21-2. |

**Outcome.** Round 21 found nothing High or Critical, so the comments design meets the stopping rule. Its two Medium findings and every Low are fixed in wording, with no new mechanism. The one High of round 20 stays accepted in A2. The comments design (protocol.md §5.9 and its notes) is reviewed and may be built.

## Round 22: live sessions

Round 22 is the first review of the live-sessions design (ADR 0015, #321, accepted 2026-11-12), against `live-protocol-review` at the ADR's merge (04d25c4). It ran the three briefs (server, member, encoding) as three independent reviewers in parallel. Each got only its brief, protocol.md (§5.10 and its notes), the ADR, and the code the design reuses or describes: `contracts/src/records`, the client's record, key-wrap, region and encoding code, the server's checks in `server/src/content`, the NDJSON streams, and the draft session. They took the project owner's nine decisions and the accepted risks A1 to A13 as given, and attacked how the decisions are specified. Users are trusted family and friends, so contrived cases were left out. The reviewers probed Yjs 13.6 in scratch directories. It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 6 Medium, 17 Low and 2 Info. The core held: the three domains bind installation, space, page, session, kind, sender, key version, join and `seq`, so no message replays across sessions, pages, kinds or joins; the wrap AD is built as `"draft"` whatever the server says, so readers never open a session; client IDs derived from the join stop anyone writing under another's client; a deletion past what its join signed is refused; snapshots are checked against a signed revision; and the region, key-version and owner checks on joining bring a removed member colluding with the server down to A2 or A5. Every claim about today's code checked out. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S22-1 | 22 | High | 5.10 **Writing the record**; 6.8; ADR decision 4 | A session start is a record write, so it inherited §6.8's retry: on `key-version`, the same payload, with the session key, wrapped under the new version. A server that refused a start under a version a removed member holds, then handed them the refused wrap, gave them the key of a session every app that knew of the rotation then joined: every keystroke, deletion and cursor, for the session's life. A2 accepts the retry leaking what is saved; here it leaked everything typed after. The same held for `recipients-mismatch`, `region-mismatch` and `permission-version`. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. A start refused for its key material, region or permission version is made afresh, with a new session ID, key, record version ID, `joinId` and first snapshot; only a resend after a lost answer goes unchanged. §6.8 and A2 point to it. |
| M22-1 | 22 | High | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decisions 1, 5 and 10 | The saver check runs only when an app's document has exactly a draft's state. A saver that sent one tiny change of its own before each save, and saved with a state leaving it out, kept every other document a step ahead, so no draft was ever compared, while each draft left out someone's paragraph. On leaving, the writer's save met `stale-head`, and "a revision of the session that holds its changes means it is done" took the unchecked draft as saving it; the offline queue withdrew entries on the same unchecked "holds". The paragraph was lost without the out-of-step rule the ADR promises. | Fixed: §5.10 **Saving** and ADR decisions 1 and 5. A `stale-head` while leaving, locking or publishing counts as done only for a session draft the app has compared; otherwise it catches up and saves on top, and if no such save lands before it goes, keeps its document on the device as an offline change waiting for the page, beside its acknowledged copy's text. The queue withdraws an entry only for a compared draft. The dodge now only keeps everyone at "Saving…", as the threat model says. |
| S22-2 | 22 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**, **Ending**, **Messages**; ADR decisions 8 and 12 | The fold's base came from the server's inputs. A head served from before the session descends from no session draft, so its base was undefined, and an old one brings back text the session deleted. A server that stopped listing a member made that member's drafts outsiders', folded from an older base. An ended session's catch-up base, "that draft", wasn't defined. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 4, 8 and 12. A draft whose signed `basedOn` chain reaches no draft of the session isn't merged: the app leaves, reports a possible rollback and saves as an outsider. A join an app has been told of stays listed for it. An ended session's catch-up merges from the latest draft of that session in the head's signed chain, and doesn't run when there is none. |
| S22-3 | 22 | Medium | 5.10 **Serving and relaying** | Whether a join has gone is the server's word per stream. Told only the saver that a join went, and relaying that join's last changes to the others, a server gets them folded in again by the saver, so they go in twice. | Accepted: A2 and §5.10 **Serving and relaying** name it. The passage shows twice for everyone, who can delete it; nothing is disclosed, and stopping it needs a signed fold of the gone join's clock, more than this case is worth. |
| M22-2 | 22 | Medium | 5.10 **Ending**, **Serving and relaying**; ADR decision 12 | Nothing made the end of a session atomic with the permission version moving; the streams it copies end about a second late. Typing in that second was stored and relayed to the removed person's open stream, with an honest server. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 12. The server ends the session in the same transaction that moves the permission version, takes each message under a lock that transaction takes, and relays a message only once stored, and only to joins whose callers can still edit; each message's caller must be able to edit the page now. |
| M22-3 | 22 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 8 | One member could take a fold grant, let it lapse and take it again, so no app saved while people typed, then name a false `saved` position so the server deleted the session after everyone left. The accepted loss "since its last save" assumed about 10 s. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. Once a grant lapses, the server gives it next to another person's join that asks. With M22-1's fix, a leaving app whose typing no compared draft holds keeps it on the device. |
| E22-F1 | 22 | Medium | 5.10 **Messages** | `clock` wasn't defined (next free clock or last one used) and wasn't checked against the update it came with. A member signing `clock = 0` made every later deletion of their text refused, and every later snapshot refused. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. `clock` is the client's Yjs state, the clock its next struct takes; a range `k`, `n` is within `c` when `k + n ≤ c`. An `update` or first snapshot whose `clock` isn't the end of its own structs (or the last taken, for none) is refused, as is any clock above 2^53 − 1 or going down. Only stored kinds count, and a client no join derives has none. |
| E22-F2 | 22 | Medium | 5.10 **States**, **Saving**, **`snapshot`**; ADR decision 2 | "Equal payloads" wasn't defined. Comparing attachments by ID alone let a saver swap a file's key and hash unseen; comparing serialized JSON flagged honest savers, since `Y.Map` key order differs between apps. | Fixed: §5.10 **States** and ADR decision 2. Payloads compare as JSON values, members as a set and numbers by value; attachments as a set of whole refs matched by `id`. |
| S22-4 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Opening the record**; 6.8 | Tabs take in each other's **No going back** marks as they write, and a tab that only types in a session never writes, so it never learned of a rotation or an ending in another tab. | Fixed: §5.10, §6.8 and ADR decision 4. A tab in a session takes in the marks when it joins and before each message it sends. |
| S22-5 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**, **Ending** | Nothing said a fold, or a catch-up from an ended session, checks the draft's or record's region first, so an owner-region draft could be merged into a share's session. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 8 and 12. A draft whose key material isn't in the session's region isn't merged, and the ended session's record must pass every check of **Opening the record**. |
| S22-6 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Retention**; 7.3, 7.4 | Yjs keeps deleted text for undo, so updates and snapshots carry text typed and deleted, kept while the session is; an editor added while a session with unsaved changes is kept reads it. | Accepted and stated: §5.10 **Retention** and the ADR's threat model. The text stays with the draft's audience; the §7.3 and §7.4 dialogs say a new editor gets what kept sessions hold. |
| S22-7 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | The `links` map could carry a choice of a page the link table would leave out, such as an owner-region page, showing its ID to the session. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 2. A writer puts in `links` only a page the table could name for this page. |
| S22-8 | 22 | Low | 2.3, 2.4, 2.5 | A2 and A6 spoke only of saves; a session draft's signature names only its saver; §2.5 had no session row. | Fixed: §2.3 (a session draft's signature and `with`), A2 (typing under an old version, sessions in another share, gone joins), A6 (a revoked agent reads a session's keystrokes, can't write), A7 (the size bucket; what an older app's publication shows), and a §2.5 row. Same as M22-4 and E22-L7. |
| S22-9 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | A dropped delete-only update went unnoticed, since `seq` gaps weren't detected, and presence could be replayed. | Partly fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. Stored kinds are numbered without gaps, a receiver holds a message that skips one, and a gap open 30 s while its join is present is out of step. Accepted: presence replay, which decides only who saves, as the server's order of joins already does (A1). Same as E22-L2. |
| M22-4 | 22 | Low | 2.3, 2.4, 7.3, 7.4 | Same as S22-8, and new editors get the typing in kept sessions without being told. | Fixed with S22-8 and S22-6. |
| M22-5 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | Map keys weren't checked: `fields` could name `markdown` or `format`, `extensions` could hold `wikistow.live`, an attachment key needn't be its `id`, and **Review** would open any revision `overlaps` named. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 2. Each of these takes the session out of step, and **Review** opens only a draft of the page in its draft region. Same as E22-L4. |
| M22-6 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`** | `saveFailing` was free text, shown to everyone, so a member could put any words in others' toolbars. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. It is a reason code, shown only in the app's own words. |
| M22-7 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **For the build** | The built `metadataWrapSubject` and `sealMetadataV2` say `"metadata"` and use the published key, so reusing them would seal a session to readers; the v2 schema has no `"live"`. | Fixed: §5.10 **For the build**, as §5.9 has. Same as E22-L5. |
| E22-L1 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **`update`** | The update rule mixed "refuses" and "out of step", and Yjs integrates structs before it reads the delete set, so checking after applying lets another client's struct in. | Fixed: §5.10. An update is decoded in full and refused before any of it applies for decoding, trailing bytes, another client's struct or skip, and deletions past the signed clock; content kinds and roots are checked as placed and take the session out of step. The content allowed is named exactly, and cursors resolve only inside `body`. |
| E22-L2 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | Same as S22-9. | Fixed and accepted with S22-9. |
| E22-L3 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Messages**; A7 | "The next multiple of 128" was ambiguous at a boundary, unpadding wasn't strict, and the 128-byte bucket still shows a little, which A7 overstated. | Fixed: §5.10 gives the length, `128 × ⌈(9 + length) / 128⌉`, and strict unpadding with content never empty; A7 says the bucket shows. |
| E22-L4 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **The document**; 5.5 | Same as M22-5, and map values' JSON rules and `wikistow.overlaps` part strings had no grammar. | Fixed: §5.10 (map values by §3.2's rules) and §5.5 (decimal integers, no leading zeros, `s ≤ e`, other parts ignored). |
| E22-L5 | 22 | Low | 5.10 **Opening the record**, **`snapshot`**, **For the build** | Same as M22-7; also a record signed by an agent wasn't refused, and "the first snapshot" was whatever the server labelled so. | Fixed: §5.10. An agent's record is refused, and the first snapshot is the message with `seq` 1 from the join the start named. |
| E22-L6 | 22 | Low | 3.5 | Messages don't name their record, and XChaCha20-Poly1305 doesn't commit to its key, so a member and the server could give two apps two records with one session ID and one ciphertext opening differently under each. | Rejected: a member with the server can already show two apps different text by signing two messages with one `seq`, which binding the record wouldn't stop. §2.3's one plaintext per ciphertext is about records, which still commit to their key. |
| E22-L7 | 22 | Low | 2.4; 5.10 | Same as S22-8; also "repaired as a save repairs it" pointed to §5.5, which doesn't define it. | Fixed with S22-8; §5.10 defines repair (`repairText`). |
| E22-I1 | 22 | Info | 5.10 **`snapshot`**, **States** | `state` names a Yjs update in a snapshot and an encoded snapshot elsewhere, and "has exactly" must compare what states hold, not bytes. | Fixed in wording: §5.10. |
| E22-I2 | 22 | Info | 5.10 **`update`** | The client ID's BLAKE2b has an 8-byte output, below `crypto_generichash_BYTES_MIN` and refused by some libraries, and isn't a cut of a longer hash. | Fixed in wording: §5.10 says so; step 5's test vectors fix it. |

**Outcome.** Round 22 found 2 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Both are fixed: a refused start is made afresh, and only a compared draft counts as saving an app's typing. S22-3 and part of S22-6 and S22-9 are accepted; E22-L6 is rejected; everything else is fixed. Round 23 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 23: live sessions, second review

Round 23 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 22 revision (efb166e). It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 7 Medium, 21 Low and 3 Info. Most of round 22's fixes held: a refused start made afresh leaks no session key, a fold whose chain reaches no session draft is refused, the end is atomic with the permission version, `clock` bounds deletions, and payloads compare as JSON values. Both Highs are in round 22's own wording: a tab that took in a higher **No going back** mark didn't have to leave, and "Saved" didn't go back to "Saving…" when the head moved on. Every claim about today's code checked out again. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S23-1 | 23 | High | 5.10 **Opening the record**; ADR decision 4 | Round 22's S22-4 fix made a typing tab take in other tabs' marks, but the leave rule fired only on *holding* a newer key version, so a tab that saw mark v+1 from its own person's rotation, with the server hiding v+1's envelopes, kept typing into the old session for the removed member. The owner region had no cross-tab signal for an owner's removal. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. An app leaves when its **No going back** mark, its own or another tab's, is above the record's key version; a tab also takes in the other tabs' contacts mark and reads the contacts record again on a higher one, so it leaves on an owner's removal too. |
| M23-1 | 23 | High | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | §5.10 showed "Saved" once an app had compared a draft holding its changes, and nothing took it back when the head moved on. A saver could save a faithful draft, then one dropping a paragraph that nobody's document ever matched, and every app kept showing "Saved"; leaving saved nothing. The ADR's wording (the head checked) didn't have the gap. | Fixed: §5.10, as the ADR has it. "Saved" shows only while the head is a session draft holding the app's changes that it has compared; when the head moves on to one it hasn't, the page shows "Saving…" again, and leaving or locking saves on top. |
| S23-2 | 23 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**, **Ending**; ADR decision 12 | At an end, an app saved only "what the head doesn't hold", by state, so a saver that saved a draft dropping a paragraph with the document's exact state and then ended the session at once got the drop through unchecked. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 5 and 12. An end is treated as leaving: an app whose head is a session draft it hasn't compared saves its own document on top, or compares it at once when its document has exactly the head's state. |
| S23-3 | 23 | Medium | 5.10 **Retention**; 7.3, 7.4; A2 | Deleting a session is the server's to do. An editor added later gets every key version, so a server that kept old sessions can show them what was typed and deleted in the share's past sessions. | Accepted: A2 and §5.10 **Retention** name it, since only forward secrecy would close it; the dialogs say what draft keys bring. |
| M23-2 | 23 | Medium | ADR decision 5 (**Publishing**) | A publish whose head moved publishes a newer session draft the publisher's document holds, so an editor without publish rights could slip text in between the publisher's save and publish, and readers get it signed by the publisher. | Rejected: decision 5 already publishes the shared draft as everyone sees it, others' typing included, and **Publish** says when others are editing. Text typed a moment before the press would go out the same way; the newer head is published only once the publisher's document holds it and has checked it. |
| M23-3 | 23 | Medium | 5.10 **Serving and relaying**; ADR decision 13 | The limits allowed about 1.5 GiB of stored messages a minute per person, none of it counted against the storage quota, and only the saver posts snapshots, so a member who starts a session could fill the server's disk and keep it filled for 30 days. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 13. At most 16 MiB of stored messages a minute per person (`rate-limited`); stored messages count against the sender's quota until the session is deleted (`storage-full`); and any participant may save and post a snapshot that is overdue by twice its size. |
| E23-F1 | 23 | Medium | 5.10 **Messages** | Round 22's `clock` rule left open what a message adding nothing carries, whether presence clocks count, and whether a message's own clock counts for its deletions, so honest apps could refuse each other's ordinary backspacing, or a stream giving presence first. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. In a stored kind, `clock` is the client's state over what the join has sent in stored messages, this one included; a message adding none carries the previous stored message's (0 for the first); a presence carries that of the stored message its `seq` names and is never checked against or counted; "signed" includes the message checked. |
| E23-F2 | 23 | Medium | 5.10 **`update`**, **For the build** | Struct origins weren't bounded by signed clocks, so one update anchored to typing nobody had signed stayed pending in Yjs for good; `encodeStateAsUpdate` then put it in every snapshot, which every newcomer refused, and in honest apps' updates. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. An update with a struct whose origin, right origin or parent is beyond what that struct's join has signed is refused before applying; **For the build** says updates come from the app's own transactions and snapshots are posted only with nothing pending. Same as S23-6. |
| E23-F3 | 23 | Medium | A7; 5.5 `wikistow.live`; ADR decisions 1 and 5 | The delete set in `wikistow.live.state` gives exact ranges under the starter's client, which counts the starting draft's characters, so a publication an older app made shows readers where and how much of that unpublished draft was deleted. A7 said "never text" and listed less. | Fixed in wording: A7, §5.5 and ADR decisions 1 and 5 say so, and every client, the CLI included, is to be updated before live sessions are turned on, as for the link table. |
| S23-4 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Ending** | A catch-up from an ended session had no check that the ended document holds its merge base, so a server serving it cut back reverted the session's later work. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 12. It catches up only when the ended document holds that draft's `wikistow.live.state`. |
| S23-5 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**, **Messages** | A session draft's author needed only a join the server lists, and needn't be under the session's key version; a save's own join could be listed under another person's join. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 4 and 5. A session draft's author must have a join the app took a message from, in a share it must be under the record's key version, and a further join under another person's join is refused. |
| S23-6 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **`update`** | Same as E23-F2. | Fixed with E23-F2. |
| S23-7 | 23 | Low | 2.3, A6 | A6 named only revoked agents, though a compromised live agent host with the server opens every session under its draft keys; §2.3's removal guarantee spoke only of saves. | Fixed: §2.3 (typing after a removal; the self-removing owner opens sessions under the version they made) and A6 (a live agent's host). Same as M23-9 and E23-L9. |
| M23-4 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **The document**, **Saving**; ADR decisions 3 and 6 | A member could put a ref to an uncommitted file in `attachments`, so every save failed `missing-blob`; and a fragmented delete set could push `wikistow.live` past the payload limit for good. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 3 and 6. The saver leaves out a ref no draft it opened lists and it didn't upload, and says so; a draft that only `wikistow.live` takes past the limit ends the session, as past 2 MiB. |
| M23-5 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**, **The document**; ADR decision 8 | The fold didn't say what it does with an outsider's `wikistow.live`, `wikistow.links`, `wikistow.change` and `wikistow.overlaps`, and **Review** took a note naming any old draft of the page. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. The merge ignores those four on both sides, and **Review** takes a note only for an outsider's draft that descends from a session draft and isn't one. |
| M23-6 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 8 | Nobody checks a fold took in all the outsider's changes. | Accepted and stated: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. A fold is the saver's word, as a writer's merge is today, and one that leaves something out is as if the saver deleted it after. |
| M23-7 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **`snapshot`**; ADR decision 6 and threat model | A later snapshot's revision could be any draft of the page, and which person wrote which struct of it isn't checked, so the ADR's "can't show them other text" overstated, and a notice could name the wrong person. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 6. A later snapshot's revision must be a session draft; the threat model says what is checked; a person is named only from their own signed changes. Same as E23-L5's second part. |
| M23-8 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | Two different signed messages with one `seq` were dropped as a repeat, hiding equivocation. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. Such a pair takes the session out of step and reports the sender. Same as E23-L8's third part. |
| M23-9 | 23 | Low | 2.3, A5; ADR threat model | Same as S23-7, for an owner who removes themself or transfers the space. | Fixed with S23-7. |
| M23-10 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Writing the record**; ADR decision 13 | No route said to answer a reader `not-found` before a check that shows a session exists (`duplicate`). | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 13. |
| M23-11 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Retention**; 7.6, 7.8, 7.9 | Only §7.3 and §7.4 said draft keys bring kept sessions; a co-owner and a group that edits get them too. | Fixed: §5.10 names every such dialog. |
| E23-L1 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | Two joins deriving one client ID weren't refused; a ground collision made Yjs change the victim's ID. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. |
| E23-L2 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **States** | A snapshot's state could be read off its bytes or from a fresh document after applying it, which a crafted one makes disagree; a `state` with trailing bytes decoded. | Fixed: §5.10. Its state is a fresh document's once applied, and `state` decodes strictly. |
| E23-L3 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **The document**; 5.5 | Map values lacked §3.2's after-parsing rules, how a save writes them, and the `links` and `overlaps` value shapes; part integers were unbounded. | Fixed: §5.10 and §5.5. |
| E23-L4 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **States** | A draft with two attachments of one `id` made payload equality undefined. | Fixed: §5.10. Such a draft equals no document. |
| E23-L5 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **The session record**, **`snapshot`** | Which snapshot is first rested on the start request's unsigned `joinId`. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. The session payload names the starter's `joinId`; and see M23-7. |
| E23-L6 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **For the build** | Yjs's change events don't say a split pair became U+FFFD, so an editor fed by them shows other text. | Fixed: §5.10 **For the build**. |
| E23-L7 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | The front matter's join to the body was given only by the app's `joinPage`. | Fixed: §5.10 spells out the rule. |
| E23-L8 | 23 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | The wire form named only rule 8, and a refused message's resend wasn't said to be unchanged. | Fixed: §5.10 (rules 1, 6, 7 and 8; resent unchanged); equivocation with M23-8. |
| E23-L9 | 23 | Low | A5 | Same as S23-7, for apps that still seal to a removed owner. | Fixed with S23-7. |
| M23-12 | 23 | Info | 5.10 **Ending** | A `saved` sent to an ended session could carry a later session's state, showing holders of the old key how much was typed since. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 12. |
| M23-13 | 23 | Info | 5.10 **`update`** | Some libsodium bindings refuse an 8-byte BLAKE2b output. | Fixed in wording: §5.10. |
| E23-L10 | 23 | Info | 5.10 **For the build** | Implementing the checks before applying needs a decoder of the app's own, since Yjs takes trailing bytes and makes roots as it reads. | Fixed: §5.10 **For the build**. |

**Outcome.** Round 23 found 2 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Both are fixed in round 22's own rules: the leave rule counts a raised mark, and "Saved" holds only for a compared head. S23-3 and M23-6 are accepted, M23-2 is rejected, and everything else is fixed. Round 24 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 24: live sessions, third review

Round 24 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 23 revision (edf9c0c). It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 5 Medium, 14 Low and 2 Info. Round 23's fixes held: a tab leaves on a raised mark, "Saved" holds only for a compared head, an end counts as leaving, struct origins and deletions are bounded by signed clocks, and the per-kind `clock` lets honest apps take each other's messages. The Highs are in what those rules lean on: the app that rotates never raised its own mark, and putting typing back trusted the saver's unsigned `wikistow.live.state`. Every claim about today's code checked out again. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
