| S24-1 | 24 | High | 6.8 **No going back**; 5.10 **Opening the record** | The mark counts versions an app has *opened*, and the app that rotates never opens the version it makes: `sealPlanned` wipes the new keys and `applyShareRotation` only forgets cached keys. So a server that took the rotation, then kept serving the rotator envelopes without it and kept the session open, kept every tab of the rotating owner in the old session, sealed under a key the removed member holds; §5.10 said such an app leaves. | Fixed: §6.8 and the ADR's threat model. The mark counts a version the app opened *or made*: an app that rotates raises it to the new version once the server accepts the rotation, before it reports it done, as an ending raises it to ended. A rule change to a built part, for the build of step 5. |
| M24-1 | 24 | High | 5.10 **Putting typing back**; ADR decisions 1, 6 and 10 | Putting back typing a gone join never sent took how far the old client got from the head's `wikistow.live.state`, the saver's word. A saver claiming the old client at a high clock made the app add the person's unsent paragraph to its merge base, so the merge read it as deleted by the session, dropped it without an overlap, and the queue withdrew it. | Fixed: §5.10 **Putting typing back** (new) and ADR decisions 1 and 6. How far the old client got comes only from that join's latest message the session serves, signed by the app itself, never past the last it sent; with none, nothing is added, since a duplicate shows and a loss doesn't. |
| S24-2 | 24 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 8 | A server serving an already folded outsider's draft as the head again, and granting the fold, got it folded twice, bringing back what the session deleted since, with no overlap note. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. A head that is an ancestor of a session draft the app has seen as head or as a snapshot's revision is a rollback, reported and not folded; and no app asks for, or places, a second grant for a revision it knows was folded. |
| S24-3 | 24 | Medium | 5.10 **Ending**; ADR decision 12 | An ended session already caught up could be served again, and its unsaved text merged in a second time. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 12. An app doesn't catch up from it when the head's chain after its latest draft holds a draft of another session, whose starter would have caught it up. |
| M24-2 | 24 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 (**Publishing**) | A publish meeting `stale-head` published a newer, checked head, which could hold a line someone typed after the publisher pressed **Publish**, then out under the publisher's name. Round 23 rejected the same case (M23-2); the reviewer showed it contradicts "as everyone sees it at that moment". | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5. The newer head goes out only when it holds nothing beyond what the publisher's document held at the press; otherwise the app says the page changed meanwhile. M23-2's rejection is withdrawn. |
| M24-3 | 24 | Medium | ADR decisions 5 and 11 | A session's drafts are charged to the saver, so a member who keeps changing a large hidden value fills the saver's storage, and then the next saver's. | Accepted: the ADR's threat model says so under **Flooding**. It needs a member acting against the others, shows as `storage-full`, and is for the owners to remove them; charging drafts by sender is more than this case is worth. |
| E24-F1 | 24 | Medium | 5.10 **Messages**, **`update`** | `clock` was defined as the end of a join's last struct, which differs from its state when the structs have a gap: an update starting past the join's last clock passed every check and stayed pending in Yjs for good, so no snapshot could be posted; a struct could also name an unknown root as its parent. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. A stored message's own structs start exactly at the join's last signed `clock`, with no gap, and its `clock` adds their lengths; skips, and a parent named as any root but the eight, are refused before applying. |
| S24-4 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **Ending** | A `saved` sent after an end for a removal carried the state of the sender's own typing, the refused part included, under the old key. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 12. It holds the state of what the session placed. |
| S24-5 | 24 | Low | 7.6, 7.8 | §5.10 said the co-owner and group dialogs mention kept sessions; those sections didn't. | Fixed: §7.6 and §7.8 say it. |
| S24-6 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **Messages**, **`presence`** | A presence fell in the 128-byte bucket without a cursor or failing save and in the 256 one with either, so its size showed both. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. A presence always pads to 512 bytes. Same as E24-L4. |
| M24-4 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 12 | Round 23's claim that a saver can't drop text and then end the session doesn't hold: a draft claiming a change it never sent, from a saver that then goes, is folded as an outsider's. | Fixed in wording: §5.10 and ADR decision 12 say so; it is what decision 8 already lets any outsider do. |
| M24-5 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decisions 5 and 13 | Nothing said how an app learns the draft head that "Saved", leaving and an end go by. | Fixed: the stream carries the page's draft head with each line and with `{ended}`. |
| M24-6 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | A tab closed while "Saving…" could lose everything since its last compared save. | Fixed: once "Saving…" has lasted 10 s, the app keeps its document sealed on the device as an offline change. |
| M24-7 | 24 | Low | 5.5 `wikistow.live` | `with` left out changes from further joins, so typing sent from one wasn't credited. | Fixed: §5.5 and ADR decision 5. Every join's changes count, another app's further joins included; only the saver's own app's changes for a save don't. |
| M24-8 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **`snapshot`** | The bounds on struct and delete ranges, and nothing pending, read as applying to later snapshots only. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 6. They apply to the first snapshot too. |
| M24-9 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **Serving and relaying**; ADR decision 13 | Presence and `saved` had no size limit, and further joins no number. | Fixed: presence 512 bytes, `saved` at most 512 KiB, at most two further joins per join, which keep no presence. |
| E24-L1 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **`update`**, **States** | The app's own decoder needs lib0's string rules, a snapshot's update its trailing-bytes rule, and "numbers by value" a number model. | Fixed: §5.10 (fatal UTF-8, UTF-16 lengths; no trailing bytes; IEEE 754 doubles, a map value's integers by its field's rules). |
| E24-L2 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **The document**; 5.5 | `wikistow.overlaps` had no single form, so an honest first snapshot could fail its comparison. | Fixed: §5.10. Written only when `overlaps` isn't empty, in code-unit order, compared as a set, and one that counts as none compares as absent. |
| E24-L3 | 24 | Low | 3.5; 5.10 **Messages** | A message doesn't name its record, so a starter who signs two records for one session ID can send one ciphertext opening differently under each key. Round 22 rejected this (E22-L6); here no equivocation check catches it, since the `payloadHash` is one. | Fixed: §3.5, §5.10 and ADR decision 4. Both message domains bind `recordHash`, the record's `payloadHash`. E22-L6's rejection is withdrawn. |
| E24-L4 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`**; A7 | Same as S24-6. | Fixed with S24-6. |
| E24-L5 | 24 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`**; A7 | A presence of a newer minor could fail to parse in an older tab, splitting the choice of saver; A7 listed only block numbers among the parts. | Fixed: a presence that doesn't parse counts as not `visible`, and A7 lists the parts. |
| M24-10 | 24 | Info | 5.10 **Ending** | Presence, never stored, wasn't said to go under the end's lock. | Fixed in wording. |
| M24-11 | 24 | Info | 5.10 **`snapshot`** | `repairText` refuses member names that collide once repaired. | Fixed in wording: such a draft starts no session. |

**Outcome.** Round 24 found 2 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Both are fixed: the rotating app raises its mark to the version it made, and putting typing back counts only the app's own signed messages. M24-3 is accepted; two earlier rejections, M23-2 and E22-L6, are withdrawn and fixed; everything else is fixed. Round 25 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 25: live sessions, fourth review

Round 25 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 24 revision (f030074). It found no Critical finding, 3 High, 4 Medium, 14 Low and 3 Info. Round 24's fixes held: the rotating app's mark, `recordHash`, the contiguous `clock`, the rollback and second-fold rules, the replayed ended session, and the press-time publish. The Highs are each the narrow form of a rule that needed its general one: the leave rule listed ways a key version stops counting instead of saying it, the put-back base corrected the server's word in one direction only, and a withheld deletion broke the claim that dropping is availability only. Every claim about today's code checked out again. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S25-1 | 25 | High | 5.10 **Serving and relaying**; A2; ADR decision 5 | The spec said a server that drops messages costs availability only. A server that relays someone's paste, withholds their deletion of it from the others and tells them it was placed leaves the others holding the deleted text, which a publish made meanwhile carries to readers. | Partly accepted: A2 names it, as hiding a change, and §5.10 and the ADR's threat model drop "availability only". It needs a malicious server and a publish in that window, and the sender's app saves its own document on leaving; detecting it after the fact is more than this case is worth. |
| S25-2 | 25 | High | 5.10 **Opening the record**; ADR decision 4 | Joining refuses a version that doesn't count for writing, but staying ended only on a newer version or a raised mark. An owner who removed the owner that sealed the session's version, with the server failing the rotation, kept typing into the session, readable by the removed owner. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. The joining checks hold for as long as the app is in the session: it leaves as soon as the record's key version stops being the highest that counts for writing, however that comes about, and in the owner region when any owner in `sealedTo`, the signer or a granter stops being a sealing owner. |
| S25-3 | 25 | High | 5.10 **Putting typing back**; ADR decision 6 | Round 24 made the base take the app's own changes the session holds from its own signed evidence, but the base also held own changes the server *said* it placed. A server that acknowledged a paragraph, relayed it to nobody and let the join lapse got it dropped by the merge, silently. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 6. Of its own changes the base holds exactly those the session serves, up to its old join's latest signed message it serves, never past what it sent; neither the server's acknowledgement nor a draft's state moves that line, in either direction. |
| S25-4 | 25 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving** (a fold without a save) | Which update was a fold without a save was the server's word, so it could label any update as the fold of an outsider's draft, and nobody would fold it in. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. It counts only once the update came from a further join of the grant's holder and that join's signed `saved` names the outsider's revision with that state. |
| M25-1 | 25 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | An app that caught a dishonest draft saved as an outsider, meeting the conflict, but every base it could merge from already held the dropped paragraph, so the merge took the drop as the other side's clean change and the review didn't show it. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5. It saves its own document on top of that draft, undoing the drop in the open, and offers History's comparison; if the head moved on, **Merge…** takes that draft as its base. A rolled-back head is met the same way. |
| E25-F1 | 25 | Medium | 5.10 **`update`**, **Serving and relaying**; ADR threat model | Additions commute only when Yjs made them: a member can craft items, within every check, whose origins make Yjs place them differently by arrival order, so apps diverge. | Partly fixed: §5.10 refuses an item whose origin and right origin aren't distinct, in one parent and key, in order. Accepted: the rest, which shows only as confusion until a comparison takes the session out of step, as a crafted snapshot's does; §5.10 and the ADR no longer say additions commute without that. |
| E25-F2 | 25 | Medium | 5.10 **`update`** | The checks before applying didn't fix one form of a v1 update: a client listed twice (Yjs keeps the last), own-client origins at or past the struct's own clock (Yjs throws midway), the parent-info bit beside an origin, content kinds deleted in the same update, non-minimal numbers, and "above" against "within". | Fixed: §5.10 names the accepted form: each client once, no skip, only GC, deleted, string and `ContentAny` content, the parent-info bit clear with an origin, own-client origins below the struct's own clock, minimal varuints, every bound as "not within". |
| S25-5 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | A fold's merge base needn't be held by the document, so a newcomer withheld later messages merged from a draft it didn't hold, undoing a span silently. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. The base's state must be held by the document, or the session is out of step. |
| S25-6 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | Leaving out an unlisted attachment depended on which drafts each app had opened, so honest apps' comparisons failed. | Fixed: the saver takes such a ref out of the document as a change of its save, and every app compares attachments as the document holds them. |
| S25-7 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Writing the record** | Only five refusal codes made a start afresh. | Fixed: a session key goes out under one set of key material; any refused start is made afresh unless sent again byte for byte. |
| S25-8 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | A fold checked only the outsider's region, not its key version. | Fixed: it must pass the record's checks, region and key version, and is reported otherwise. |
| M25-2 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | The press-time rule covered only the publish meeting `stale-head`, not its own save first, which could catch up and take in post-press typing. | Fixed: a publish's own save writes the document as at the press and never catches up and saves on top. |
| M25-3 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Ending**; 7.6 | Catch-up from an ended owner-region session was skipped for a later session whose starter couldn't open it; the co-owner dialog promised owner-region sessions. | Fixed: skipped only when that starter could open the record; §7.6 says a new co-owner gets the shares' sessions, not the owner region's. |
| M25-4 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | A save's copy could name the main join's unsent structs, which receivers then refuse for good. | Fixed: an app makes such a copy only when its main join has nothing unsent. |
| E25-L1 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | The equivocation rule could count a presence, flagging honest senders to every newcomer. | Fixed: stored messages only. |
| E25-L2 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | `recordHash` as the record's `payloadHash` doesn't commit to its content key, so two records could share it. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. `recordHash` is the BLAKE2b-256 of the record's signed bytes, with its `keyCommitment`. |
| E25-L3 | 25 | Low | 5.10; ADR decision 13 | Size limits mixed padded, sealed and decoded sizes. | Fixed: every limit counts decoded ciphertext; the ADR says a presence is padded to 512 bytes. |
| E25-L4 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **`update`** | lib0 keeps a leading U+FEFF that the repo's `decodeUtf8` refuses, and lengths of `ContentAny` weren't given. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| E25-L5 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | Writers had no well-formedness rule for map keys and values. | Fixed: writers `repairText` them. |
| E25-L6 | 25 | Low | 5.5; 5.10 | `property:` and `data:` didn't say where they count from or over what; an `overlaps` value's `revision` needn't be its key; empty and duplicate lists had no comparison rule. | Fixed: §5.5 and §5.10. |
| E25-L7 | 25 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | A clock after a refused message, and messages a snapshot already holds, had no rule. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| M25-5 | 25 | Info | A4 | A session adds a first sight A4 didn't list. | Fixed in wording: A4. |
| E25-L8 | 25 | Info | A7 | Client IDs come from public values, so the server can tie an older publication's per-client counts to people. | Fixed in wording: A7. |
| E25-L9 | 25 | Info | 5.10 **`presence`**; ADR decision 4 | A cursor at the end of `body` names its root, and "cut to 53 bits" didn't say which. | Fixed in wording. |

**Outcome.** Round 25 found 3 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Each is fixed by the general form of its rule: the joining checks hold throughout, the put-back base counts only the session's evidence, and S25-1 is accepted in A2 with the false claim taken out. Round 26 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 26: live sessions, fifth review

Round 26 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 25 revision (1ef87b7). It found no Critical finding, 1 High, 3 Medium, 14 Low and 1 Info, the fewest yet. Round 25's fixes held: the joining checks throughout a session, the out-of-step save on top, the signed fold without a save, `recordHash` over the record's signed bytes, and the press-time publish. The High is the put-back line again: drawn by `clock`, it couldn't see a message that only deletes. E26-F1 is a fault in round 25's own text, which would have refused an ordinary second `Y.Map.set`; this log's author confirmed it against Yjs 13.6.27 (the update's info byte is `0xa8`). Every claim about today's code checked out again. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S26-1 | 26 | High | 5.10 **Putting typing back**; ADR decisions 6 and 10 | The put-back base held the app's own changes up to a `clock`, which a message that only deletes doesn't move. A server that withheld a deletion and let the join lapse got the deleted sentence back into the session, and into the person's own document, with "Saved" shown; a kept message the snapshot claimed to cover counted as served without being placed. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 6. The base holds the insertions and deletions of the old join's messages up to the highest `seq` the document rebuilt from the session has placed; a message listed but not placed doesn't count, and a newcomer applies every kept message its snapshot doesn't hold. The queue records join and `seq`. |
| M26-1 | 26 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 and threat model | Round 25's recovery saved the app's document on top of a draft whenever the session was out of step. An app whose document was short of signed changes, because a false `covers` got the server to delete them, then signed a deletion of saved text. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5. An app saves its document over a draft only when the payloads differ at an exact state or the draft claims typing nobody signed; one that lacks signed changes says it can't catch up and edits as an outsider from the head, putting back only its own typing. The server refuses a snapshot covering less than the latest. |
| M26-2 | 26 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | "Compared" didn't say "and found equal", so read literally a draft that failed its comparison could be published, count as done, or withdraw a queue entry. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5 define it once. |
| E26-F1 | 26 | Medium | 5.10 **`update`** | Round 25's form required "the parent-info bit clear whenever an origin is present". Yjs sets that bit on every overwrite of a map key, and ignores it beside an origin, so honest changes to a cover, a link choice or an attachment would have been refused. | Fixed: §5.10. An item with an origin takes its parent and key from it whatever the flag says, as Yjs does; one with neither names its parent as a root by name (parent-info 1). |
| S26-2 | 26 | Low | A6; ADR threat model | "It can't write into one" is false under an identity the server invents (A4). | Fixed in wording: A6 and the ADR. |
| S26-3 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Opening the record**; 6.8 | Between sending a rotation and its answer, the rotating app's tabs stayed in the session. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. Until the answer, an app sends nothing in that share's sessions. |
| M26-3 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **The document**; 5.5 | **Review** could apply a part naming an extension the document never holds, and lacked the fold's key-version check. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. |
| M26-4 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | A no-save fold of a draft whose payload differs from what the document writes put every app out of step. | Fixed: the no-save path needs equal payloads; otherwise the fold is saved. |
| M26-5 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Writing the record**; ADR decision 13 | The server didn't check a start's `revisionId` is the draft head. | Fixed: `stale-head` otherwise. |
| M26-6 | 26 | Low | A5 | A5 named only owner-region sessions. | Fixed in wording: a share session under a version the removed owner sealed leaks typing too. |
| M26-7 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Serving and relaying** | Main joins per person had no cap. | Fixed: at most eight present per person, and stream opens count against the requests limit. |
| E26-L1 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **`update`** | Parent-info other than 1, zero-length structs and ranges, a `ContentAny` other than one string, and flag bits on GC passed the checks before applying. | Fixed: all are refused before applying. |
| E26-L2 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **States** | Strict decoding of a state wasn't defined, "equals" wasn't a defined comparison, nor -0. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| E26-L3 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`** | Which presence counts, what "doesn't parse" means, and a newer major's `visible` were undefined. | Fixed: the last relayed counts; any failed check makes it not `visible`; `visible` and `saveFailing` keep their meaning in every major; `saveFailing` is a short code. |
| E26-L4 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | "Held in full" was vacuous for a delete-only message, and a partly held first message after a snapshot was refused. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| E26-L5 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | Dedupe and equivocation weren't tied to verified messages. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. |
| E26-L6 | 26 | Low | 5.5 `wikistow.live` | A newer `format` counted as none, against decision 10. | Fixed: §5.5. |
| E26-L7 | 26 | Low | 5.10 **Writing the record** | The start's checks didn't list new IDs in the one set. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 13. |
| E26-L8 | 26 | Info | ADR decision 6; 3.4 | "Which Yjs tells apart by state" suggested an encoding §5.10 forbids; §3.4 didn't list the session key. | Fixed in wording. |

**Outcome.** Round 26 found 1 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. It is fixed: the put-back line is drawn by `seq` over what the document placed. Round 27 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 27: live sessions, sixth review

Round 27 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 26 revision (92ea2ad). It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 4 Medium, 13 Low and 5 Info. Round 26's fixes held: the put-back line by `seq`, the can't-catch-up rule, "compared" as found equal, and the parent-key flag read as Yjs reads it. The encoding reviewer fuzzed 4,000 ordinary edits through the accepted form and found only E27-F1. One High is a claim about today's code, the other a Yjs struct the form still let in. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S27-1 | 27 | High | 6.8 **No going back**; ADR threat model | Round 24's rule that a rotating app raises its mark to the version it made read as today's behaviour, but no rotation path does it (`applyShareRotation`, `applyOwnerRemoval` and the group rotations only call `forgetSpaces`), so a server hiding the new version keeps the rotator's drafts, and later its sessions, on the old one. | Fixed in wording: §6.8 marks the rule accepted with ADR 0015 and not built, and names every rotation path it covers; the client builds it with the live module. |
| M27-1 | 27 | High | 5.10 **`update`**, **`snapshot`**; ADR decisions 4 and 6 and threat model | The accepted form let a GC struct in. A later snapshot that writes deleted runs as GC has the same text and state and passes every check, but Yjs then turns any later item anchored in it into GC, so a newcomer silently drops others' typing, and its leave-time save signs the drop. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. No update or snapshot may hold a GC struct, which Yjs makes only for nested types the document doesn't have, and an item Yjs turns into GC as it places it takes the session out of step. |
| S27-2 | 27 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | The rollback rule caught only an ancestor of a seen draft, not a sibling session draft the server had refused and kept; the publish fallback took any compared head whose state the press-time state held, so a server could get an older sibling, holding deleted text, published. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 5 and 8. A session draft that doesn't descend from the last one seen as head is a rollback, and the fallback publishes only a head whose state is exactly what was saved at the press. |
| S27-3 | 27 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 8 | A newcomer that never saw an outsider's draft folded could be served it again as head, without its grant, and fold it a second time. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. A draft is folded only when its merge base's state holds that of the latest session draft the app has seen as head or as a snapshot's revision. |
| M27-2 | 27 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**, **Ending**; ADR decision 5 | One crafted message every app refuses or goes out of step on, kept in the session, kept every editor out of live editing on that page until a change of access, for up to 30 days. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5. An app that can't take part (a refused snapshot, a kept message it can't place, or out of step again after joining again) saves its document or keeps it on the device and ends the session. |
| E27-F1 | 27 | Medium | 5.10 **`update`** | Round 25's form required a `ContentAny` of exactly one string, but Yjs joins one client's deleted map values into one `ContentAny`, so an honest snapshot, or one transaction's set-and-delete, was refused. | Fixed: §5.10. A `ContentAny` holds one or more strings, and as placed a map entry's last string, the value Yjs reads, must be JSON text. |
| S27-4 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **Opening the record**; ADR decision 4 | Reading the sealed marks before every message isn't what today's code does and costs too much, and the hold during a pending rotation was per tab. | Fixed: tabs take in marks when they join and whenever another tab says on a `BroadcastChannel`, carrying no text, that it raised one; the hold covers every tab. |
| S27-5 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **Ending**; ADR decision 12 | Nothing kept an ended session from being caught up twice, by two editors at once or after a replay in the owner region. | Partly fixed: catching up takes a grant keyed by the ended session. Accepted: a server can still have an owner-region session caught up again when the later session's starter couldn't open it (A2), its changes put in twice where nothing overlaps. |
| S27-6 | 27 | Low | A7 | From who saves, the server learns whose tab is hidden and whose saves fail, which A7 implied stayed hidden. | Fixed in wording: A7, with E27-I4. |
| M27-3 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | A draft the app lacks only by deletions, with no `seq` gap, fit neither out-of-step branch; and an app that can't catch up puts its typing onto a head it hasn't compared. | Partly fixed: such a draft counts as claiming what never came. Accepted: the second, which needs the server and a dishonest saver and is said to the person as "can't catch up". |
| M27-4 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | A publish took the document, which the visual editor can show late, so text the publisher's screen never showed could go out. | Fixed: a publish saves what the editor last showed, and asks again when the document held more. |
| M27-5 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **The document**; ADR decision 8 | Any participant could add an `overlaps` entry for an outsider's draft already folded, offering an old revert under the outsider's name. | Fixed: an entry counts only from the further join that held that revision's fold grant, or a reviewer changing it. |
| M27-6 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | "Listed" attachments were matched by `id`, so a member could pair a listed `id` with an uncommitted blob. | Fixed: the whole ref is matched. |
| E27-L1 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **`snapshot`** | A snapshot from a join that had already sent structs failed the contiguity rule, and a fold without a save has no `wikistow.live` for a snapshot to name. | Fixed: a later snapshot comes from a further join with no structs of its own, its `clock` the join's previous one, naming a session draft with its own `wikistow.live`. |
| E27-L2 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`** | An empty `saveFailing`, a newer major's other fields, and an unresolved cursor could split the choice of saver. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| E27-L3 | 27 | Low | ADR decision 1 | Decision 1 still said the queue records client and clock. | Fixed: join and `seq`. |
| E27-L4 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | A refusal that only repeats had no rule, and equivocation depended on arrival order. | Fixed: such a refusal ends the join, and equivocation counts whichever came first. |
| E27-L5 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **States** | State decoding lacked minimal varuints, zero entries and byte order; "covers" named both a state comparison and a position. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| E27-L6 | 27 | Low | 5.10 **For the build**, **The document** | The as-placed checks ran after observers had seen the change, the origin-order walk's cost wasn't said, and "a message holding no change" had no form. | Fixed: the checks run in `beforeObserverCalls`, the walk costs what Yjs's own placing does, and the empty message is the update `00 00`. |
| M27-7 | 27 | Info | ADR threat model | The ADR said a paragraph is gone if the session ends before the check, which §5.10 no longer allows. | Fixed in wording. |
| E27-I1 | 27 | Info | 3.5 | `metadata-record/v2` is now hashed too. | Fixed in wording. |
| E27-I2 | 27 | Info | 5.5 | Code point order differs from JavaScript's default sort. | Fixed in wording. |
| E27-I3 | 27 | Info | 5.10 | `state` names two encodings, which `kind` keeps apart. | No change: the AD binds `kind`. |
| E27-I4 | 27 | Info | A7 | `kind` in the clear shows a cursor moving without typing. | Fixed in wording with S27-6. |

**Outcome.** Round 27 found 2 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. S27-1 is fixed in wording, M27-1 by refusing GC structs. S27-5 and M27-3 are partly accepted; the rest is fixed. Round 28 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 28: live sessions, seventh review

Round 28 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 27 revision (6382a45). It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 4 Medium, 9 Low and 2 Info. Round 27's fixes held: no GC structs, `ContentAny` of strings, the sibling rollback, the merge-base state rule, the press-time publish and the catch-up grant. The encoding reviewer found every update and snapshot Yjs 13.6 makes from ordinary editing, undo and redo over text and maps included, inside the accepted form, as this log's author had checked for GC. Both Highs are about marks across tabs: the pending hold lived only in memory, and §6.8 described tabs sharing marks as they write, which today's `kept-marks.ts` doesn't do. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S28-1 | 28 | High | 5.10 **Opening the record**; 6.8; ADR decision 4 | The hold while a rotation's answer is pending lived only in the sending tab and one channel message, so a tab opened or reloaded meanwhile, or a relaunched desktop app, joined the old session the server kept open, and the removed member read its typing. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. Before sending a rotation, an owner's removal, an ending or a move into or out of a share, the app records the pending change in its sealed marks and waits for that write; no tab or later launch joins or sends in that share's sessions while it is there. A definite answer clears it; a lost one keeps it until the app has fetched the share again. |
| S28-2 | 28 | High | 6.8 **No going back**; 5.10 | §6.8 said tabs take in each other's marks as they write; `KeptMarks` takes them in only when the tab raises a mark of its own, and reads the file at unlock, so another tab's raise didn't stop a tab writing under the old version. | Fixed in wording: §6.8 says what is built, and marks as accepted with ADR 0015, not built, that a tab reads the file again before it writes, rotates or moves, when it joins a session, and when told, and that a tab tells the others only once its write has landed. |
| M28-1 | 28 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 and threat model | Whether a draft "claims what never came" (save over it) or the app "can't catch up" (never save over) turned on which joins and messages the app was served, which is the server's. A server hiding one join from one app got it to save its document over everyone's work, blaming the honest saver. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5. An app saves its document over a draft only on a payload mismatch at an exact state, or when the draft claims ranges under its own signer's clients past what that join signed while a later message from it is held; everything else is "can't catch up", and an app with nothing of its own unsaved just leaves. The ADR's **Dropping** bullet is corrected. |
| E28-F1 | 28 | Medium | 5.10 **The document**; 5.5; ADR decision 8 | Round 27's rule that an `overlaps` entry counts only from the fold holder's join made every note die when its session ended, though notes are meant to last. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. A note the first snapshot holds counts when it equals an entry of the starting draft's `wikistow.overlaps`, and **Review** checks a carried note against the base's own chain. |
| E28-F2 | 28 | Medium | 5.10 **`update`**, **For the build** | Checking an origin comes before its right origin walks the list, so one crafted update cost each app seconds where Yjs took milliseconds, and the claim that it costs no more than Yjs's placing was false. | Fixed: §5.10. The ordering check is dropped, crafted placement already being accepted as confusion; the checks as placed look only at the struct and its two neighbours. |
| S28-3 | 28 | Medium | A7 | A save's own join marks which saves renamed a link, repaired text or added a file, so the server learns link edges A7 didn't list. | Accepted: A7 and the ADR's threat model name it. Sending a join with every save would hide it at the cost of a request per save. |
| S28-4 | 28 | Low | 5.10 **`update`** | A change placed next to another join's typing that arrived out of order was refused, not held, so honest reordering took the session out of step. | Fixed: it is held until that join's message signs the typing, for up to 30 s. |
| S28-5 | 28 | Low | 5.10; A2 | Moves had no pending hold or cross-tab signal. | Fixed with S28-1: the pending entry covers a move into or out of a share. |
| M28-2 | 28 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | Any join could widen a note's `parts` or put back a dismissed one. | Fixed: a later join may only take parts out or delete a note. |
| M28-3 | 28 | Low | 5.10 **Restoring** | A restore of an older app's publication carried `wikistow.live` or `wikistow.overlaps` into the document, putting every app out of step. | Fixed: a restore leaves the four namespaces out. |
| M28-4 | 28 | Low | 5.5 `wikistow.live` | `with` named an agent's person for the agent's folded draft. | Fixed: the agent by its own ID, which names its person. |
| M28-5 | 28 | Low | 2.3; 5.10 **Ending** | §2.3's note covered only drafts saved in a session, not those saved from its document after leaving; and changes lost after a rotation went unsaid. | Fixed: §2.3 covers both; the app says when a kept session's changes can't be recovered. |
| E28-L1 | 28 | Low | 5.10 **Messages** | The general bullet refused a newer major presence that the presence bullet reads. | Fixed: the general bullet excepts presence. |
| E28-L2 | 28 | Low | 5.10 **`snapshot`** | A later snapshot escaped the own-client origin rule, and Yjs throws halfway on a breach. | Fixed: per struct, for snapshots too. |
| E28-L3 | 28 | Low | 5.10 **Messages**, **`update`** | Empty client sections, a newcomer's first message past its structs, `seq` 0, and "deleted content" in the maps were open. | Fixed: §5.10. |
| M28-6 | 28 | Info | 2.5, 6.8, 7.3, 7.4, 7.6, 7.8 | Some notes didn't say live sessions aren't built. | Fixed in wording. |
| E28-L4 | 28 | Info | 5.10 **States**, **The document** | Delete sets compare as the clocks they cover; `joinPage('', body)` adds a line break. | Fixed in wording. |

**Outcome.** Round 28 found 2 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. S28-1 is fixed by a pending entry kept with the marks, S28-2 in wording. S28-3 is accepted in A7; the rest is fixed. Round 29 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 29: live sessions, eighth review

Round 29 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 28 revision (4cf94cb). It found no Critical finding, 1 High, 5 Medium, 9 Low and 5 Info. Round 28's fixes held: the pending entry in the sealed marks, §6.8's account of today's tabs, saving over a draft only on its signer's own evidence, notes outliving a session, and the dropped ordering walk. The encoding reviewer fuzzed 2,425 updates from three clients with undo and redo, all inside the accepted form. The High is the pending entry again: recording it told no tab already in a session. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S29-2 | 29 | High | 5.10 **Opening the record**; ADR decision 4 | Recording a pending rotation, removal, ending or move raised no mark and said nothing, so a tab already in that share's session, which doesn't save, kept typing into the old session while the server held the answer. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 4. Once the pending entry's write lands, the app says so on the `BroadcastChannel`, every tab in a session of that share reads the marks again and stops sending, and only then is the change sent. |
| S29-1 | 29 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 5 | The evidence that a draft "claims what never came", a message stored after it, rested on the server's positions, so a server could make an honest app save over an honest saver's draft and report it. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 5. A saver sends every change a draft holds before the `saved` naming it, and a draft claims what never came only when the app has taken, with no gap, that `saved` and every earlier message of the join, and the draft claims ranges past the `clock` that `saved` signed. |
| M29-1 | 29 | Medium | 5.10 **`snapshot`**, **Ending**; ADR decision 6 and threat model | A later snapshot's text under each client is checked only against a signed draft, not against what that client typed, so a member's forged draft posted as a snapshot made a newcomer save over an honest draft, blaming its saver, and an editor catching up a kept session save the forgery. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 6 and 12. An app built from a later snapshot is provisional until it has compared a session draft equal: a mismatch then means it can't take part and reports the snapshot's sender, and leaving puts back only its own typing. A catch-up first checks its document equal to the draft the latest `saved` names. An app in the session never applies a later snapshot. Same as E29-F1. |
| M29-2 | 29 | Medium | 5.10 **The document**; ADR decision 8 | A note carried from the starting draft was trusted whoever wrote that draft, so any writer could stage an old draft for **Review** as someone's change made "while you were editing". | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. A carried note counts only from a person-signed session draft, and shows as carried by that draft's signer, with its date. |
| E29-F1 | 29 | Medium | 5.10 **`snapshot`** | Same as M29-1. | Fixed with M29-1. |
| E29-F2 | 29 | Medium | 5.10 **Messages** | A newcomer's expected `clock` for a join's first message after the snapshot was undefined for a message adding no structs, so an honest backspace could make it refuse a kept message and end the session. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 6. A newcomer starts each join's `seq` and `clock` from that join's latest kept message at or before the snapshot's position; a batch is `Y.mergeUpdates` of its transactions' own updates. |
| S29-3 | 29 | Low | 10, 13 | §10 and §13 didn't drop `wikistow.live` and `wikistow.overlaps` from copies and publications, and §13's list of deliberate drops was no longer whole. | Fixed: §10 and §13. |
| S29-4 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | A session served as open while a later session's drafts were in the head's chain had no guard. | Fixed: the app refuses it, on joining and at every head, and reports it. |
| M29-3 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **Ending** | An ended session nobody's keys count for was still kept, and one still being saved was served to others. | Fixed: one ended by a rotation or a move away is deleted once its joins leave, and an ended session is served only after they have. |
| M29-4 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **Ending** | A person removed or made reader lost their last typing without a word. | Fixed: the app says so and offers it to copy. |
| M29-5 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | The publish hold and the check's cadence were only in the ADR. | Fixed: §5.10, the hold once each time a join starts publishing. |
| E29-L1 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | A newcomer couldn't count notes placed before its snapshot. | Fixed with M29-2: a note counts when the snapshot's draft carries it. |
| E29-L2 | 29 | Low | 3.2; 5.5 | `wikistow.live` and `wikistow.overlaps` integers weren't in §3.2's list, and the overlaps entries' shape was open. | Fixed: §3.2 and §5.5. |
| E29-L3 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **Messages**; ADR decision 10 | A `snapshot` or `saved` of a newer major made an older app end the session. | Fixed: it edits as an outsider and says to update, without ending it. |
| E29-L4 | 29 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`** | Which presence checks decide the saver was open. | Fixed: only `visible` and `saveFailing`, and a presence that fails to verify or decode. |
| S29-5 | 29 | Info | 5.10; 5.7 | A session draft's key material should name the record's share too; §5.7's schema line lacked `"comment"` and `"live"`. | Fixed in wording. |
| E29-L5 | 29 | Info | 5.10 | A join's identity wasn't stated. | Fixed: `(senderId, joinId)`. |
| E29-L6 | 29 | Info | 5.10 | `state` names two encodings. | No change: §5.10 says so, and `kind` keeps them apart. |
| E29-L7 | 29 | Info | 3.2 | The 256-deep nesting limit wasn't stated. | Fixed in wording. |
| E29-L8 | 29 | Info | 5.10 **For the build** | `Y.Map.prototype.toJSON` drops a `__proto__` key. | Fixed: a save reads entries one by one. |

**Outcome.** Round 29 found 1 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. It is fixed by announcing the pending entry. Every other finding is fixed. Round 30 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 30: live sessions, confirmation review

Round 30 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 29 revision (1e794a9). It found no Critical or High finding, 3 Medium, 12 Low and 2 Info. Round 29's fixes held: the announced pending entry, the signer's own `saved` as the evidence of a dishonest draft, provisional newcomers, the checked catch-up, and carried notes. The encoding reviewer ran 36 seeds of three apps through a strict decoder of §5.10's rules, gc on and off, undo and redo over all eight roots, merged batches and newcomers built from snapshots: every update and snapshot passed and the documents converged. Following the stopping rule, the fixes are plain rules in wording, with no new field, message or mechanism; where a reviewer suggested one (a sealed presence counter, a per-share Web Lock), the finding is met by a rule or accepted instead. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S30-1 | 30 | Medium | 5.10 **Putting typing back**, **Saving**; ADR decisions 6 and 10 | A change kept on the device kept the acknowledged copy's text as its base, which holds the person's typing the server placed but no draft saved, so once the session's keys stopped counting, the merge took that typing as already in and dropped it, with an honest server. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decisions 6 and 10. Put back onto a draft as a writer outside the session, the base holds, of the app's own changes, only those the latest session draft it has compared holds; that base's text is what it keeps beside such a change. Same as M30-1. |
| M30-1 | 30 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**, **Putting typing back**; ADR decision 5 | A saver's draft claiming a range it never sent, and never named by a `saved`, left the others unable to catch up, and their put-back onto that draft took typing the session served, but no draft held, as already in. | Fixed with S30-1. |
| M30-2 | 30 | Medium | 5.10 **Saving**; ADR decision 8 | When a draft counted as the session's through a fold without a save differed at its exact state, the rule saved over the outsider's honest revision and blamed it, when the grant holder's `update` was at fault. | Fixed: §5.10 and ADR decision 8. The app reports the grant holder, can't catch up, and never saves over the outsider's draft. |
| S30-2 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **Opening the record**; 6.8 | Other tabs may send what they had queued before they read the broadcast; how pending entries merge across tabs, and how the app tells its own change was accepted, were open. | Partly fixed: §5.10 says the change was accepted only when the share's version opens to keys this app made, and that entries merge so a clear is never undone (accepted, not built). Accepted: a message queued before the broadcast is read, which a server that commits the change can relay (A2). A per-share Web Lock would be a new mechanism. |
| S30-3 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **Messages**, **Saving** | A replayed `publishing` presence could hold saves without end, though the spec said replay decides only who saves. | Fixed: the hold comes at most once a minute for each person, the 20 s rule doesn't wait for it, and the claim is corrected. Same as M30-3. |
| S30-4 | 30 | Low | 5.10; ADR decision 7 | The server's unsigned list of joins could put anyone in the toolbar, and an invented join could be picked as saver. | Fixed: a join shows, and counts for the saver, only once a message from it has verified and decrypted. |
| S30-5 | 30 | Low | A4 | A4 didn't say a first-sight co-owner's sessions leak typing. | Fixed in wording. |
| S30-6 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **Messages**; ADR decision 13 | A batch of messages over the 1 MiB request limit was refused as if it would only repeat, ending the join. | Fixed: an app splits pending messages across requests in `seq` order. |
| S30-7 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **Saving**, **Ending** | "Can't take part" said to save the app's whole document, against "never saves its document over a draft"; a catch-up with no `saved` had no check. | Fixed: it puts back its own typing; with no `saved`, a catch-up builds from the first snapshot alone. Same as M30-4. |
| M30-3 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | Same as S30-3. | Fixed with S30-3. |
| M30-4 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **Saving** | Same as S30-7's first part. | Fixed with S30-7. |
| E30-L1 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **The document** | A map value within the depth limit alone could put the saved payload past it. | Fixed: depth counts as in the payload. |
| E30-L2 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **`update`**, **Putting typing back** | The hold for another join's typing read as an exception or as a refusal, and Yjs places per struct, not per message. | Fixed: the hold is stated as an exception, deletions beyond what was taken are refused, and a message counts as placed only in full. |
| E30-L3 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **`presence`**, **Messages** | Whether a failed presence counts as failing, and what a `saved` that doesn't decode is, were open. | Fixed in wording. |
| E30-L4 | 30 | Low | 5.10 **States**; 5.5 | A `wikistow.overlaps` of a newer format was dropped at the first session save. | Fixed: it is carried unchanged from the head, as `wikistow.links` is. |
| E30-L5 | 30 | Info | 5.10 | `state` names two encodings. | No change: §5.10 says so and `kind` keeps them apart; renaming the field would change the format. |
| E30-L6 | 30 | Info | 5.10; ADR decision 4 | The wire form didn't cite §3.3 rule 3, and the ADR's summaries left out `format`. | Fixed in wording. |

**Outcome.** Round 30 found nothing High or Critical, so the live-sessions design meets the stopping rule. Its three Medium findings and every Low are fixed in wording, as plain rules with no new mechanism; S30-2 is partly accepted. The live-sessions design (protocol.md §5.10 and its notes) is reviewed and may be built. Rounds 22 to 30 found 15 High findings in all: S25-1 is partly accepted in A2, and every other is fixed. What the rounds accepted is recorded in A1, A2 and A7, in §5.10, and in the ADR's threat model.

## Round 31: sharing in parts, first review

Round 31 is the first review of sending large shares, moves, endings and co-owners in parts (ADR 0019, #328, proposed 2026-11-23), against `t3/propose-sharing-in-parts` (9944db6b). The round number may change when this and another proposal's round are merged. It ran three briefs as three independent reviewers in parallel: server and member as usual, and a **concurrency** brief (prefix `C31`) in place of the encoding brief, since no record, wrap or envelope format and no domain changes. The server and member reviewers were Claude subagents; the concurrency reviewer was a different model (GPT), for an independent read. Each got only its brief, protocol.md (§6.7 **Sending in parts** and what it touches), the ADR, and the code the design reuses: `server/src/content` (sharing, moves, ending, owners, copies, quota), the content routes and body pools, and the client's sharing, move, ending, owner and share-change operations and `key-versions.ts`. They took A1 to A14 and the earlier decision that one mechanism serves all four actions as given. It found no Critical finding, 9 High (6 distinct), 8 Medium and 7 Low. The core held: staged wraps and envelopes depend only on their record, key version and recipient, so keeping them across a change of access is sound; members and agents can't see or reach a change; a new share's or an ending's parts give nobody a key; the scan's region check keeps `missing=` from mixing regions; and the space lock still serializes every commit. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S31-1 | 31 | High | 6.7 **Sending in parts**, step 4; ADR decision 6 | A new share's version 1 was reused across finishes. A finish refused with `permission-version`, after it sealed version 1 to a group member just taken out, was followed by one without them under the same keys, so a server that kept the first gave that person the whole share and every save under version 1. Today a retry makes new keys. | Fixed: §6.7 step 4 and ADR decision 6. The sealed audience part goes only after a `check: true` finish passes, the app counts everyone a sent finish sealed to as holding it, and reuses version 1 only for an audience that includes all of them; otherwise it starts a new share with new keys. Same as M31-1. |
| M31-1 | 31 | High | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | Same as S31-1, by a group change, a removal from every folder, or an agent revoked between finishes. | Fixed with S31-1. |
| S31-2 | 31 | High | ADR Threat model; 2.4 A10; ADR decisions 7 and 10 | "A cancelled share leaks nothing" and "a server can't finish a change by itself" held only until the first finish was sent, and refused finishes were routine. A kept `share` finish could be committed by the server after Cancel; a kept `co-owner` finish carries an owner statement, which every app follows. Cancel still said "Nothing changed". | Fixed in part: the check before the finish makes a refused finish rare; Cancel after one says what may remain; a cancelled co-owner whose statement went out is put on the app's removed-owners list (§6.7 step 4, ADR decisions 6 and 7, Threat model). Accepted in part: what a server keeps of a refused finish is what it keeps of a refused request today, now written in A10. |
| S31-3 | 31 | High | 6.7, 6.8 **No going back**; ADR decision 5 | The app checked its marks only before the finish, so parts and catch-up batches wrapped for minutes under a version another tab had rotated past, or sealed to an owner removed on another device. Today `moveNode` checks before every request. | Fixed: §6.7 (**The app's own checks run before every part**) and ADR decision 5. Marks, the contacts record and the sealing owners are read again before every part and catch-up; another tab's raise stops the sending. Same as M31-2 and C31-2. |
| M31-2 | 31 | High | 6.7; 6.8 | Same as S31-3, by a rotation hidden from the moving tab. | Fixed with S31-3. |
| C31-2 | 31 | High | 6.7, 6.8 | Same as S31-3. | Fixed with S31-3. |
| S31-4 | 31 | High | 7.6; ADR decisions 7 and 10 | The adder's app recorded the new co-owner as accepted before any upload, and a Cancel left it so, taking them off the removed-owners list when re-adding; the server could then list them as an owner and the app would seal to them. | Fixed: §6.7 step 4 and ADR decision 7. The contacts write comes just before the first finish, after the check, and is undone if the change ends before a finish was sent. |
| C31-1 | 31 | High | 10; ADR decision 5 | A page another owner moved into a folder being moved was wrapped for the destination share without asking, where today `rewrapper` stops for a new preview. | Fixed: §6.7 step 4 and ADR decision 5. A node the preview didn't list stops a `share` or `move` for a new preview and the owner's confirmation before anything is wrapped for it, from a fresh tree in which its parents lead to the subject. Same as M31-4 and S31-5. |
| C31-5 | 31 | High | 5.10, 6.8; ADR decision 10 | A finish whose answer was lost while it waited for the space lock was settled by fetching the shares, which still listed the share, so the pending change was cleared and live sessions resumed before the ending committed, and `ENDED` was never raised. | Fixed: §6.7 step 6 and ADR decision 10. Cancel settles it: it answers "finished" for a committed finish and otherwise deletes the change so no finish still waiting can commit. The pending change records the change's ID, and the marks are raised before it is cleared. |
| M31-3 | 31 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | Several access changes meanwhile (a group member removed, an invitee's key change, an agent revoked, an invitee made co-owner) give `recipients-mismatch` or `not-found`, not `permission-version`, so they deleted every part. | Fixed: §6.7 step 4 and ADR decision 6. Every refusal of the audience part keeps the change; only refusals of the subject delete it. |
| C31-3 | 31 | Medium | ADR decision 2 | Nothing serialized a finish with a part, a Cancel or the sweep on the same change, and no lock order was given. | Fixed: §6.7 step 5 and ADR decision 2. Each locks the change's row; the finish takes it after today's locks, and the others never take the space while holding it. |
| C31-4 | 31 | Medium | 6.7, 6.9 | An ending's finish sent again after a lost answer hit the route's early owner check, `not-found`, before the receipt; Cancel could delete the receipt. | Fixed: §6.7 steps 4 and 6. The change is found by `partsId` before any check that needs the subject; Cancel of a finished change answers as the finish did and keeps it. |
| C31-6 | 31 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decision 2 | Refusals of a part were undefined: whether anything was staged, and what the app does, when the same bytes can't succeed. | Fixed: §6.7 step 2 and ADR decision 2. A part is refused whole, names the entries at fault, and the app acts as for the same refusal at the finish; parts, `missing=` and the finish judge entries alike. Same as M31-7. |
| C31-7 | 31 | Medium | 6.7 step 3 | A comment listed alone by `missing=` failed the scanner's rule that a comment's page is in the same scan. | Fixed: ADR decision 5. For a change, that rule reads the change's records and the preview, not one batch. |
| C31-8 | 31 | Medium | ADR decision 10 | The cleanup at unlock cancelled every open change, a running tab's or another device's included. | Fixed: §6.7 step 6 and ADR decision 10. An app cancels by itself only changes it recorded as its own that no running tab holds; others are cancelled by hand. |
| C31-9 | 31 | Medium | ADR decision 8 | Staged rows for records gone, moved out or to former owners piled up past the scope bound, and two opens at once could both pass the two-change limit. | Fixed: §6.7 steps 1 and 2 and ADR decision 8. Rows go with their record, each part deletes those out of scope, a byte bound refuses the rest, and the limit is counted under an account lock. |
| C31-10 | 31 | Medium | ADR decision 5 | A page autosaving once a second could keep a finish from converging, and the count of missing records couldn't name it. | Fixed in part: `stale-head` names the pages, and the finish takes the catch-up inline, so the window is one short scan and one request. Accepted in part: holding writes for the last catch-up is left out (ADR, Considered options); after five tries the app names the page. |
| S31-5 | 31 | Low | ADR decision 5 | New nodes were taken on the server's word at the moment the owner was most likely to click through. | Fixed with C31-1, and the prompt shows each one's path with no default. |
| S31-6 | 31 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | The protocol dropped older-version wraps at the finish while the ADR refused; relying on `missing=` let a server commit a mix of versions. | Fixed: the finish answers `key-version` while any staged wrap is older, and the app rewraps from its own list. |
| M31-4 | 31 | Low | ADR decision 5 | Same as C31-1, from an editor moving a page into the folder being moved out of their share. | Fixed with C31-1. |
| M31-5 | 31 | Low | ADR decision 6 | A co-owner change finished after `permission-version` without asking could undo another owner's removal of that person. | Fixed: ADR decision 6. A `co-owner` change always asks again after `permission-version`; the others ask when who gains or keeps access changed. |
| M31-6 | 31 | Low | ADR decision 5 | The subject moved within its region gave `stale-head` with a new parent, which `missing=` can't fix. | Fixed: a row in decision 5's table: preview again, keep the rows, finish with the new parent. |
| M31-7 | 31 | Low | 6.7 step 2 | Same as C31-6. | Fixed with C31-6. |
| M31-8 | 31 | Low | 6.7 steps 2 and 6 | A removed owner could keep their change alive and staging for a day. | Fixed: §6.7 step 6. Every request on a change needs its opener to be an owner at the key version they opened at; removing them or a key change deletes their changes. |

**Outcome.** Round 31 found 9 High findings (6 distinct), so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed, S31-2 and C31-10 partly accepted (A10 and the ADR's Considered options). Round 32 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 32: sharing in parts, second review

Round 32 ran the three briefs again (server, member and concurrency), with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 31 revision (988275df). As in round 31, the server and member reviewers were Claude subagents and the concurrency reviewer a different model (GPT). It found no Critical finding, 4 High (3 distinct), 8 Medium and 8 Low. Round 31's fixes held: the check before the finish carries nothing secret, the app's checks before every part stop a hidden rotation or owner removal from reaching a part, new nodes stop a share or move for a new preview, the change's row lock orders a finish with parts, Cancel and the sweep, and Cancel settles a lost finish. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S32-1 | 32 | High | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | Reusing version 1 counted people, not roles: someone sealed the draft key as an editor through a group, and kept as a reader after leaving it, was given the same version 1, so a server that kept the first finish could give a reader every draft. | Fixed: §6.7 step 4 and ADR decision 6. The app keeps, for each recipient, their key version and the keys sent, and reuses version 1 only if each may still hold every key they were sent; otherwise a new share. Same as C32-1. |
| C32-1 | 32 | High | 6.7 step 4 | Same as S32-1. | Fixed with S32-1. |
| S32-2 | 32 | High | 2.4 A10; 6.8 **The region doesn't change on its own**; ADR decisions 6 and 7 | A share finish refused after it went out can be committed by the server later, after the owner gave up or started a new share for a smaller audience, and its wraps then put the owners' later saves in that share; A10 and §6.8 claimed less. | Fixed in part: a `share` change that ends without finishing after its sealed part left sets the app's ended **No going back** mark for that share ID, so that app never saves into it. Accepted in part, as for a refused request today: A10 and §6.8 now say a server can do this for other owners' apps, and for a co-owner's kept statement (A5). |
| C32-2 | 32 | High | 6.7 step 4; ADR decisions 7 and 10 | A tab that crashed after sending a co-owner finish left no record that the statement may have gone, so the cleanup said "Nothing changed"; and the contacts write undoing an add could undo one another tab finished. | Fixed: before any finish carrying the sealed part, the app records the change and that the part may leave in its kept marks, and the cleanup treats it as sent; the undo holds only if the change didn't finish. Same as S32-3. The statement itself stays accepted, as A5 (see M32-2). |
| M32-1 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decisions 2 and 10 | The check couldn't compare recipients, and nothing previewed again before it, so a removal from every folder or from a group during a day's wait offline went unnoticed, or was found only once the envelopes had left. | Fixed: the app previews again before every check, asking again if who gets keys changed, and the check carries the envelopes' headers without the sealed keys, so the server compares the recipients. |
| M32-2 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decisions 6 and 7 | Round 31's fix put a cancelled co-owner on the removed-owners list, which stops the adder's own saves once anyone else adds them, and strikes an existing owner re-added after a key change. | Fixed: round 31's removed-owners rule (S31-2) is dropped. A cancelled co-owner whose statement may have gone stays accepted, and the app says the add may still happen and offers **Remove owner**; an undo before any finish restores what the contacts record held. |
| C32-3 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 6; ADR decisions 7 and 10 | A finished change's receipt lasted a day, so a laptop offline over a weekend after an ending's finish found neither the receipt nor the share, and couldn't tell what happened. | Fixed: a finished change is kept, without its entries, until its opener's app has heard the outcome, and at most 30 days; past that the app settles from the shares. |
| C32-4 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 4 | The route's early check runs before the body is read, so an ending's finish sent again met `not-found` before it could name its change. | Fixed: the finish names its change in the query (`?parts=partsId`), and the early check finds it there, for its opener, kind and subject. |
| C32-5 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decision 8 | Staged rows cascading from their records could deadlock a part with the trash sweep's or retention's deletes. | Fixed: staged rows don't cascade; parts delete rows gone or out of scope, the finish ignores any left, and the sweep deletes the rest. |
| C32-6 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decisions 2 and 6 | Rewrapping after `key-version` from the app's own list included a page moved out meanwhile, whose entry refused the whole part. | Fixed: an entry for a record gone or out of scope is skipped and named, and the rest of the part is staged. |
| C32-7 | 32 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 2 | A large catch-up, such as a folder restored from the trash, couldn't fit in the finish's inline batch. | Fixed: the inline batch is at most 1 MiB; a larger catch-up goes as parts first. |
| C32-8 | 32 | Medium | ADR decision 2 | Deleting the subtree's old envelopes takes one SQL parameter a record today, past PostgreSQL's 65,535 at 256,000 records. | Fixed: ADR decision 2 requires every statement over the records to take them as one array parameter, as `replaceKeyMaterial` does. |
| M32-3 | 32 | Low | 6.7 steps 2, 4 and 6 | Envelopes staged to an owner removed after the last part were left for the finish's exact-recipients check, which then refused every time. | Fixed: removing an owner deletes every change's staged envelopes to them, and the finish sets aside entries out of scope or to former owners before it compares. |
| M32-4 | 32 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | A key change of the person being added as co-owner kept the change, which could never finish. | Fixed: it is a refusal of the subject, which deletes the change. |
| M32-5 | 32 | Low | 2.4 A10; ADR Threat model | Someone removed from, or made a reader in, the destination share while a move into it is open can open the parts sent before. | Fixed: A10 names it, and the app tells the owner when it learns of such a rotation during a move. |
| M32-6 | 32 | Low | 6.7 steps 2 to 6 | Only the open said "never an agent", and the move route takes agent credentials. | Fixed: every request on a change needs the opener's own identity with a person's session, and the move route refuses `parts` from an agent. |
| S32-3 | 32 | Low | 6.7 step 6; ADR decision 10 | Same as C32-2, for a share too. | Fixed with C32-2. |
| S32-4 | 32 | Low | 2.4 A10; ADR decision 5 | Parts for a node moved out of, or trashed from, a folder being shared stay on the server, and a server can give them to the new share's members. | Fixed in wording and accepted: A10 says so, and the finish names the nodes whose parts it dropped, which the app tells the owner. |
| S32-5 | 32 | Low | 2.4 A10; ADR decision 5 and Threat model | "Rare", "at most a day" and "reported below the mark" held only for an honest server. | Fixed in wording: A10 and the ADR say which are an honest server's, and the report is narrowed to the server's current key version against the mark. |
| S32-6 | 32 | Low | 6.7 step 4; 7.6; 8.3 | Moving the co-owner's contacts write also moved the pin of their key, so parts sealed to an unpinned key. | Fixed: the key is pinned before the first part, as today; only the accept moves. |

**Outcome.** Round 32 found 4 High findings (3 distinct), so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed; S32-2 and S32-4 are partly accepted in A10, as what a refused or kept request already gives a server today, and C32-2 leaves the statement accepted under A5. Round 31's S31-2 removed-owners rule is withdrawn (M32-2). Round 33 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 33: sharing in parts, third review

Round 33 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 32 revision (34daa0be). The reviewers were as in rounds 31 and 32. It found no Critical finding, 1 High, 8 Medium and 12 Low. Round 32's fixes held: version 1 is reused only for recipients still entitled to every key class they were sent, the check compares recipients by their envelopes' headers, the finish names its change before the body is read, staged rows don't cascade, and receipts last until the opener's app has heard. This was the third and last round the batch allowed, so the stopping rule isn't met yet: see the outcome. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M33-1 | 33 | High | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | The reuse test for version 1 ran only before the next finish. A catch-up too large for the finish went as parts first, wrapping versions saved after a group member's or an owner's removal under version 1, which they may hold from a refused finish a server kept. | Fixed: §6.7 step 4 and ADR decision 6. Once the sealed part has left, the app runs the test with a fresh preview before every part and `missing=` batch under version 1, and starts a new share before wrapping anything more if it fails. |
| S33-1 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 step 6; ADR decisions 6 and 10 | At unlock the app settled only the changes the server listed, so a crashed tab's share whose finish a server kept unlisted never got its ended mark. | Fixed: at unlock the app settles, by Cancel, every change its kept marks name as its own, listed or not, and acts on the answer. |
| C33-1 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 step 6; ADR decision 10 | A finish or cancel answered was taken as the app having heard, so a lost answer could lose the receipt before the marks were raised; other tabs still settled by fetching the shares. | Fixed: the receipt lasts until the app says it heard (`?heard=1`, after raising its marks), and a pending change carrying a change ID is settled only through it, in any tab, while the receipt lasts. Same as S33-6. |
| C33-2 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 7 | Undoing a cancelled co-owner's acceptance could undo an identical add another tab finished. | Fixed: the kept marks record which change made each provisional accept, and the undo holds only if no other change or add accepted them since and the server doesn't list them. |
| C33-3 | 33 | Medium | ADR decision 5 | The gap between the check and the finish can still be filled by autosaves every time on a slow link. | Fixed in part: only `share` and `co-owner` take the check, and their subjects are in the owner region, where only owners write. Accepted in part: holding writes for the last catch-up stays out (Considered options). |
| C33-4 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decision 8 | An owner whose key changed during an ending or a move out of a share kept staged envelopes at the old key, which every finish refused. | Fixed: envelopes to an owner at an older key version are deleted at the next part, set aside at the finish and listed by `missing=`. Same as M33-2. |
| M33-2 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 step 2 | Same as C33-4. | Fixed with C33-4. |
| C33-5 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; 10; ADR decision 5 | A move whose node another owner moved to another region first could pass the finish with new permission versions, since today's checks take the source region from the tree. | Fixed: the finish compares both regions the change recorded with the current ones under its locks, before new permission versions count (`region-mismatch`, deleting the change). |
| C33-7 | 33 | Medium | 6.7 steps 5 and 6; ADR decision 2 | Cancel held the change's row while deleting a large upload, so a finish, and every write behind its space lock, waited on it. | Fixed: Cancel and the sweep hold the row only to mark the change cancelled, and delete its rows afterwards, in batches; a part verifies signatures before taking the row. |
| S33-2 | 33 | Low | ADR decisions 7 and 10 | Decision 10 said "nothing changed" where decision 6 forbids it. | Fixed: decision 10 defers to decision 6 whenever the kept marks say the sealed part may have left. |
| S33-3 | 33 | Low | 2.4 A10 | The ended mark protects only the app that keeps it; A10 left out section 6.9's caveat. | Fixed: A10 names the person's other devices, their agents and apps whose marks are lost. Same as M33-5's second part. |
| S33-4 | 33 | Low | ADR decision 5; A10 | Nodes moved out of a folder being shared were named only after the sealed part had gone. | Fixed: the change keeps their IDs, the check names them, and the app asks before anything sealed is sent. |
| S33-5 | 33 | Low | ADR Threat model; A10 | After a cancelled move into a share, a server can place the pages in it with the staged wraps for the canceller's own app too. | Accepted: A10 already says so; a note per cancelled move would be a new mechanism for a case where the owner chose those gainers. |
| S33-6 | 33 | Low | 6.8; ADR decision 10 | Same as C33-1's second part. | Fixed with C33-1. |
| S33-7 | 33 | Low | 6.7 step 4; A10 | Whether the check stores its inline batch, that a kept co-owner finish gives every share's keys, and the own agents' contacts entries after a cancelled share were unstated. | Fixed: the check stores nothing, A10 names every share's keys, and the app unsets its agents' entries wherever it sets the ended mark. |
| C33-6 | 33 | Low | 6.7 step 2 | Pruning at each part lost which nodes were dropped. | Fixed with S33-4. |
| C33-8 | 33 | Low | 6.7 step 6; ADR decision 10 | An open sent again after a lost answer met its own reserved ID or the limit. | Fixed: an open sent again with the same ID, kind and subject answers as the first did. |
| M33-3 | 33 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | "Still in the audience" could be read to leave out the owners version 1 was sealed to. | Fixed: the sealing owners count; one removed or at a changed key fails the test. |
| M33-4 | 33 | Low | ADR decision 5 | Nodes moved out of a folder being moved into a share weren't named. | Fixed with S33-4, for moves into a share too. |
| M33-5 | 33 | Low | 6.7 step 4; A10 | The own agents' contacts entries, and agents' own marks, after a cancelled share. | Fixed with S33-7 and S33-3. |
| M33-6 | 33 | Low | 6.7 step 6 | `GET /api/parts` wasn't said to refuse agents. | Fixed: it is for people only. |

**Outcome.** Round 33 found 1 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed in wording, C33-3 partly accepted and S33-5 accepted in A10. The batch allowed three rounds, so the review stops here: sending in parts (protocol.md §6.7 and its notes) stays **proposed**, and needs at least one more round meeting the stopping rule before it may be built. Rounds 31 to 33 found 14 High findings in all (10 distinct); none is open, and what they accepted is in A10 and A5. The findings narrowed each round, from the mechanism to the cleanup after a crash.

## Round 34: sharing in parts, fourth review

Round 34 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 33 revision with Scott's decisions recorded (ffedc230). Scott accepted every recommendation of ADR 0019's Decide section on 2026-11-23, and chose this round for its question 8; the briefs took those eight decisions as given. The reviewers were as in rounds 31 to 33: Claude subagents for the server and member briefs, a different model (GPT) for the concurrency brief. It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 7 Medium, 8 Low and 2 Info. Round 33's fixes held: version 1 is tested before every part and `missing=` batch once the sealed part has left, the app settles its own changes at unlock whether or not they are listed, receipts last until the app has heard, and a cancel runs outside the change's row lock. Both Highs are in rules that leaned on someone else's word: the server's report of nodes that left a folder being shared, and the server's owner list for undoing a co-owner's accept. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S34-1 | 34 | High | 6.7 step 2; 2.4 A10; ADR decision 5 | Only the server named the nodes that left a folder being shared, so a server could stay silent about a page the owner moved out or trashed during an hour of parts, and give its staged wraps to the new members. Moving it out is what the interface invites, rather than cancelling the upload. | Fixed: the app keeps the set of nodes it staged material for and, before every check, compares it with its fresh tree and its own moves and deletions, naming any no longer under the subject. When its person moves or trashes a node under an open `share` or move into a share, it says then that only cancelling before the finish keeps it out. A move on another device that the server hides is A2, as A10 now says. |
| C34-1 | 34 | High | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 7 | The undo of a provisional co-owner accept held if no other accept came since and the server didn't list them, so a crashed tab's undo at unlock could restore an earlier accepted key over a removal another tab made meanwhile, and trust a removed owner again. | Fixed: the undo is decided from the app's own kept marks, never the server's owner list. It holds only if no finish left the device and the person's contacts entry is still exactly what that accept wrote; any later change to it, a removal among them, voids it. |
| C34-2 | 34 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decision 5 | A node another owner moves out after the check passed is only named by the finish, after the sealed part has left. | Fixed in part: the finish names it and the app tells the owner after it, and with S34-1 the app names what it can see itself. Accepted in part in A10: a second between a passing check and the finish, after another owner's move. |
| C34-3 | 34 | Medium | 6.7 "The app's own checks"; ADR decision 5 | Today's `unseen` check (every node of the preview had records in the scan) wasn't kept, so a server could leave a page out of the scan without a staged row to drop, and go undetected. | Fixed: before any check or finish, every node of the preview must have had its records listed across the whole scan and its `missing=` batches (`binding-mismatch`, unless a fresh tree shows it gone). |
| C34-4 | 34 | Medium | 6.7 step 6; ADR decision 10 | At unlock any answer to Cancel but "finished" counted as ending without finishing, so a crash after a share was made, with the receipt gone after 30 days, set `ENDED` on a share that exists. | Fixed: Cancel answers `cancelled` for an open change it deleted; `not-found` is an unknown outcome when a finish may have left, settled from the shares as today before any mark is set. |
| C34-5 | 34 | Medium | 6.7 steps 1 and 6 | An open sent again after a lost answer can open the change afresh if another device cancelled it meanwhile, taking a slot for a day. | Rejected, as Low: the retrying app holds the change and carries on with it or cancels it at its next unlock, so the cost is one slot, and only when another device's Cancel races the retry. Superseded in round 35 (C35-2): a cancelled change is now kept as a receipt. |
