| C34-6 | 34 | Medium | 6.7 steps 1 and 6; ADR decisions 2 and 8 | An owner's removal deleted staged envelopes to them without locking the changes, so a part already under way could stage one after it; the open's lock order against space deletion was unstated. | Fixed: a removal locks each open change in the space after the space's lock, as the finish does; an open takes the space's lock before the account's, the order deleting a space takes them in. |
| C34-7 | 34 | Medium | ADR decisions 2 and 5 | Autosaves can land between the check and the finish on all five tries; a finish holding the space lock can wait on a part holding the change's row. | Rejected: the first part repeats C33-3, accepted in round 33 (only `share` and `co-owner` take the check, and their subjects are in the owner region, where only owners write). The wait is bounded by one part's insert, after its signatures are checked outside the lock. |
| C34-8 | 34 | Medium | 6.7 steps 1 and 6; ADR decision 8 | A cancelled change's rows, deleted in batches afterwards, counted toward no account limit, and an interrupted deletion could leave them. | Fixed: a cancelled change counts toward the two-change limit until its rows are deleted, and the hourly sweep finishes any deletion that stopped. |
| S34-2 | 34 | Low | ADR decision 5; Decided 3 | A server can move a private page into the folder and present it as "appeared", with only its new path shown. | Fixed in part: the dialog shows, for a node the confirmed preview had elsewhere in the space, where it was. A third choice to move them out was left out, as a new flow; Cancel at that point still says nothing changed. |
| S34-3 | 34 | Low | ADR decisions 4, 7 and 10 | The app acted on a "finished" answer to Cancel when it knew no finish had left the device, and the co-owner undo leaned on the server's owner list. | Fixed: such an answer is reported as a possible attack and not acted on; the undo goes by the app's own records (C34-1). |
| S34-4 | 34 | Low | 6.8; ADR Threat model | §6.8 named only a kept refused finish among the ways a server can place pages in a share for later saves, and the Threat model cited A1 and A2 where A10 applies. | Fixed: §6.8 lists A10's three cases, and the Threat model cites A10 for a move into a share and a co-owner. |
| M34-1 | 34 | Low | ADR decision 5; Decided 3; 6.7 step 4 | A move's re-preview lists no nodes, so a page a member moved into the folder meanwhile went unnamed. | Fixed: a move names each node that appeared, by path, as a share does ("These appeared while moving"). |
| M34-2 | 34 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | Another owner's "Remove from every folder" on a direct invitee moves only the space's permission version, so the re-preview found nothing changed and the invitee still got the folder. | Fixed: for a `share` the app asks again whenever the space's permission version moved since the owner confirmed. |
| M34-3 | 34 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | The own agents' contacts entries were unset only when the sealed part had left, and when they were set wasn't said. | Fixed: they are set just before the first finish, recorded in the kept marks, and unset whenever the change ends without finishing. Same as S34's Info note. |
| M34-4 | 34 | Low | ADR decision 5 | "Close it where it is open" is wrong advice for an ending or a move out of a share, whose writers are members. | Fixed: the app names who saved the page last, and for those kinds says who is editing it. |
| M34-5 | 34 | Low | 6.7 "The app's own checks"; ADR decision 7 | The per-part rule forbade envelopes to the person being added; a re-add after a key change stalled the adder's owner-region saves for the whole of the parts. | Fixed: the person being added counts for the change's own envelopes, as `sealingOwners({ adding })` does; a re-add of someone the server lists as an owner at that key is accepted at the open, as today, and undone by C34-1's rule. |
| S34-I1 | 34 | Info | ADR decision 6 | Agents' entries after a change abandoned before the sealed part left. | Fixed with M34-3. |
| M34-I1 | 34 | Info | ADR decision 5 | "Give no one new a key" is wrong for a co-owner. | Fixed in wording. |

**Outcome.** Round 34 found 2 High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed in wording, C34-2 partly accepted in A10, and C34-5 and C34-7 rejected. Round 35 repeats the three briefs.

## Round 35: sharing in parts, fifth review

Round 35 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 34 revision (eefbff15). The reviewers were as in round 34. The first concurrency reviewer wouldn't act on a brief it was told to read from a file, and asked for confirmation; a new one was given the same brief inline, and it is that reviewer's report that is logged. It found no Critical finding, 2 High, 5 Medium, 9 Low. Round 34's fixes held: the app names nodes that left a folder being shared from its own records, the co-owner undo is decided from the app's kept marks, the scan's `unseen` check is kept across parts, and an owner's removal locks the open changes. The real High (S35-1) is in round 34's own fix for C34-4: settling a `not-found` from the shares let a server take back a share the app had given up. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S35-1 | 35 | High | 6.7 step 6; 2.4 A10; ADR decisions 6 and 10 | After a refused finish whose sealed part left, the app gave the share up but set its ended mark only once Cancel answered. A server that stalled Cancel until the app closed then answered `not-found` at unlock, which C34-4 settled from the shares, so the app took the share as made and saved into it, reaching the person it had decided to leave out. | Fixed: giving up a change whose sealed part may have left, by Cancel or for a new share, is recorded in the kept marks and the ended mark set before Cancel is sent. A change given up is never settled as finished from the shares; only one with no such decision is an unknown outcome. |
| C35-1 | 35 | High | 6.7 step 4; ADR decisions 4 and 6 | Another owner's **Remove from every folder** between a passing check and the finish moves the space's permission version but not the folder's, which is all `makeShare` checks, so the share is made with that invitee. | Rejected, as not worse than today: today's request has the same gap, from a preview taken before the whole scan, and checks the same root version. With parts the app previews again before the check (M34-2, M35-2), so the gap is the second between the check and the finish. |
| S35-2 | 35 | Medium | ADR decision 5 (Converging); 2.3 | Naming who keeps saving the page from the finish's answer let the server put the blame, and the owner's removal, on an innocent member. | Fixed: the app names the saver only from the signed version it fetched with `missing=` and verified, and otherwise says "someone". |
| C35-2 | 35 | Medium | 6.7 steps 1 and 6; ADR decisions 4 and 10 | An open sent again after a lost answer could open afresh a change another device had cancelled, and finish what the person had cancelled. Same as C34-5, now with a harm. | Fixed: a cancelled change is kept a day as a receipt without its rows, so a finish or an open sent again with its ID answers `cancelled`. C34-5's outcome is superseded. |
| C35-3 | 35 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 7 | Round 34's undo held while the person's entry was exactly what the accept wrote, so another tab's identical accept that finished could still be undone. | Fixed in part: the kept marks name the last writer of each person's entry, across tabs, and any later write, an identical accept among them, voids the undo. Accepted in part: an identical accept by the person's other device can't be told apart; the app then asks its person to accept them again, which costs availability only. |
| C35-4 | 35 | Medium | ADR decision 2 | The one-array rule covered record IDs only; `recordsUnder` and `bumpPermissions` pass one parameter a node, so a library of 66,000 pages and folders fails before the first part. | Fixed: every statement over the subtree's records or nodes, in the scan, parts, the check and the finish, takes one array parameter or works set-based. |
| C35-5 | 35 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decision 2 | A part refused a record "never stored" as `binding-mismatch` and skipped one "gone since", which the server can't tell apart, so a valid part could be refused whole. | Fixed: an entry for a record not stored now is skipped and named, either way; the finish still needs exact coverage. |
| S35-3 | 35 | Low | ADR decisions 9 and 10; A10 | "Nothing changed" after cancelling a move into a share or a co-owner whose parts went up. | Fixed: the app says the tree is as it was, but that a server which kept the parts could give the share's members, or the person being added, the versions they covered. |
| S35-4 | 35 | Low | 6.7 "The app's own checks"; ADR decision 9 | A part wrapped ahead while another was sent could go out after another tab raised a mark. | Fixed: the checks run again just before each part is sent, and a part they now refuse is thrown away. |
| S35-5 | 35 | Low | ADR decision 7 | The server could make a first-time co-owner look like a re-add, so they were accepted at the open. | Fixed: the accept at the open is only for someone this app's own contacts record accepted at an older key or holds as removed. |
| S35-6 | 35 | Low | 6.8; A10 | After a cancelled move into a share, the app could refuse the server placing those nodes in that share later. | Accepted in A10, as S33-5: the gainers are the people the owner chose, and a mark per cancelled move would be a new mechanism. |
| M35-1 | 35 | Low | ADR decision 5; 6.7 step 4 | A node restored from the trash into the subtree was listed with saved versions, caught up without asking, though the owner never saw it. | Fixed: a restored node counts as not shown unless the confirmed preview listed it, and is asked about for a `share` or a `move`. |
| M35-2 | 35 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 6 | Asking again whenever the space's permission version moved asks after every member's move, which teaches the owner to click through. | Fixed for `share`: the app asks when an invitee has since lost access elsewhere in the space; the version moving is the cue to look. Rejected for `co-owner`: asking every time is Decided 2. |
| M35-3 | 35 | Low | 6.7 step 4 | Refusals outside the list (`too-deep`, `rate-limited`, `binding-mismatch`, `invalid-request`) had no stated outcome. | Fixed: `rate-limited` and `too-deep` keep the change; `binding-mismatch` and `invalid-request` are faults, and the app cancels and reports. |
| M35-4 | 35 | Low | 6.7 step 5; ADR decision 2 | A staged table with a foreign key to the space would deadlock a part against a removal holding the space; "a removal never waits" was too strong. | Fixed: staged rows reference only the change's row, made at the open under the space's lock; a removal waits at most for one part's insert. |
| M35-5 | 35 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 8 | The finish set aside envelopes to people no longer owners, but not to an owner at an older key, so it could refuse until a part cleared them. | Fixed: the finish sets those aside too, and an inline entry replaces a staged one by record and recipient. |

**Outcome.** Round 35 found 1 High that holds (S35-1), so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every other finding is fixed in wording, but C35-1 rejected, C35-3 partly accepted, M35-2 partly rejected and S35-6 accepted in A10. Round 36 repeats the three briefs.

## Round 36: sharing in parts, sixth review

Round 36 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 35 revision (ab7122f4). The reviewers were as in round 35, the concurrency brief given inline. It found no Critical finding, 3 High, 5 Medium, 8 Low and 1 Info. Round 35's fixes held: giving up a change is recorded before Cancel, a cancelled change keeps a receipt, the one-array rule covers nodes, and staged rows take no lock on the space. The High that holds (S36-1) is again in round 34's fix for S34-1: the app's list of nodes it staged material for followed the server's skip answers. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S36-1 | 36 | High | 6.7 steps 2 and 4; 2.4 A10; ADR decision 5 | The app's own list of nodes it had staged material for dropped whatever the server answered as skipped. A server that answered a page moved out on another device as skipped, but kept its wraps, kept it off the list, so the app named nothing before the sealed part left. | Fixed: the app keeps the nodes it has **sent** material for, which no answer of the server's shrinks; a skipped entry counts as the node leaving, and is named before anything sealed is sent. |
| C36-1 | 36 | High | 6.7; ADR decision 5 | A page staged, trashed, left out of a refreshed preview and restored before the finish was covered by its old staged rows, and shared without being asked about. | Fixed: a dropped node's staged rows are never used again, so a node restored after it was dropped is `stale-head`, and not shown unless the latest preview the owner confirmed listed it. As a High, it doesn't hold: the page was in the preview the owner confirmed, another owner moves it in the second between a passing check and the finish, and today's request has the same gap for its whole scan. |
| C36-2 | 36 | High | ADR decisions 4 and 6 | C35-1 again: a **Remove from every folder** of an invitee between a passing check and the finish isn't caught, since `makeShare` checks only the folder's permission version. | Accepted, as not worse than today, now stated in ADR decision 6. Checking the space's permission version at the finish would refuse it after any move anywhere in the space, members' included, which works against converging. Two rounds raised it, so it was for Scott to confirm; he accepted it as a known limit on 2026-11-23. |
| C36-3 | 36 | Medium | ADR decision 10 | The kept marks named a change only around its finish, so a tab that crashed in the middle of a move's parts left it holding a slot all day, unknown to the next unlock. | Fixed: the app records the change's ID, kind and subject in its kept marks before it sends the open, for every kind. |
| C36-4 | 36 | Medium | ADR decision 7 | Two tabs re-adding the same co-owner: the second's provisional accept took the first's as what to restore, so cancelling both left an accept neither finished. | Fixed: an app holds at most one provisional accept for a person in a space; a second add waits until the first change has ended. |
| C36-5 | 36 | Medium | ADR decisions 2 and 8; 6.7 step 6 | Removing an owner deleted every staged envelope to them under the space lock, hundreds of thousands for a large ending. | Fixed: the removal records them as removed against each open change, under the lock; their envelopes are never used from then on, and are deleted afterwards in batches outside it. A part prunes in batches of at most a part's worth. |
| C36-6 | 36 | Medium | 6.7 step 6; ADR decisions 7 and 10 | A finished change's receipt went with its subject, or out of reach of an opener no longer an owner, so a lost finish couldn't be settled. | Fixed: a receipt stays until heard or 30 days even after its subject is deleted or its opener stops being an owner, readable by the opener's own identity and nothing else of the space; it goes only with the space. |
| C36-7 | 36 | Medium | 5.10; 6.8; ADR decisions 10 and 11 | A tab of an older app beside a newer one settles a pending change carrying a change ID from the shares. | Accepted, as Low: that tab settles it as today's app settles any, so between such tabs a lost finish is settled no better than today, and no worse. Stated in ADR decision 11. |
| C36-8 | 36 | Low | ADR decisions 5 and 8 | The dropped-node list wasn't bounded. | Fixed: at most as many IDs as the scope had nodes at the open, and a count beyond. |
| S36-2 | 36 | Low | ADR decisions 9 and 10; 6.7 step 6 | Expiry and the cleanup at unlock said "Nothing changed" after parts of a move into a share or a co-owner went up; "only cancelling keeps them out" is wrong for a move into a share. | Fixed: the kept marks record when the first such part is sent, every path that ends the change says what decision 9 says, and for a move into a share the app says the versions went up under its keys. |
| S36-3 | 36 | Low | ADR decisions 6 and 7; A10 | "As they were then" understates what a kept refused finish exposes after more catch-up parts. | Fixed: "as they were when the app last sent material for it". |
| S36-4 | 36 | Low | A10; 6.8; ADR Threat model | A cancelled move into a share later placed by the server reaches every writer's saves, not just the owner's; the app keeps no mark for it. | Fixed in wording ("every writer's next saves"). The mark is rejected, as S35-6. |
| S36-5 | 36 | Low | 6.7 "The app's own checks"; ADR decision 5 | A previewed node the server left out, with a fresh tree showing it gone, went unnamed and unshared. | Fixed: the app names it to its person as a node that left, whether or not it sent material for it. |
| M36-1 | 36 | Low | 6.7 step 2; ADR decisions 2 and 5 | A co-owner's parts kept going to the person's old key after they reset it. | Fixed: before each part after a resume the app fetches the person's identity again, and the server refuses a part to them once their key differs, as a refusal of the subject. |
| M36-2 | 36 | Low | A10; ADR decision 5 | Pipelined wraps under the old version still go up after a removal from the destination share; A10 said "sent before" the removal. | Fixed: each part's answer gives the destination share's current key version, so the app stops after at most the part in flight; A10 says "before the app learns of that rotation". |
| M36-3 | 36 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decisions 5 and 6 | A deleted group, a revoked agent or an agent's pick moved away is a bare `not-found` today, which reads as a refusal of the subject. | Fixed: the check and the finish answer them as `recipients-mismatch` with the ID, and decision 5's table lists them. |
| S36-I1 | 36 | Info | ADR Threat model; A7 | The open tells the server the change's kind and subject, and the check its audience, even for a change later cancelled. | Fixed in wording: what the one request tells it today, a little earlier. |

**Outcome.** Round 36 found 1 High that holds (S36-1), so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed in wording, but C36-2 and C36-7 accepted. This was the third of the three more rounds the batch allowed, so the review stops here: sending in parts stays **proposed**, and needs at least one more round meeting the stopping rule before it may be accepted. Rounds 34 to 36 found 2, 1 and 1 Highs that held, and each narrower than the last: a server staying silent about a node and an undo over a removal (round 34), a give-up decided too late (round 35, in round 34's fix), and a list that followed the server (round 36, in round 34's fix). C35-1 and C36-2, raised twice and accepted, are for Scott to confirm.

## Round 37: sharing in parts, seventh review

Round 37 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material, against the round 36 revision with Scott's acceptance of C35-1/C36-2 recorded (6f4f3586). The reviewers were as in round 36, the concurrency brief given inline. Each brief now listed what earlier rounds had accepted or rejected, C35-1/C36-2 among them as Scott's known limit, so the reviewers didn't spend the round on those. It found no Critical and no High, 5 Medium and 10 Low (the concurrency reviewer rated its seven findings Medium; three are logged as Low after triage). Round 36's fixes held: the app's list of nodes it sent material for doesn't shrink on the server's answers, a dropped node's rows are never used again, the change's ID is in the kept marks from the open, a removal no longer deletes envelopes under the space lock, and receipts outlive their subject. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C37-3 | 37 | Medium | 6.7 steps 2 and 4; ADR decision 5 | A check named a staged node as dropped but stored nothing, so a node another owner restored before the finish was covered by its old rows, and shared though the app had told its person it was left out. | Fixed: a check drops the rows of the nodes it finds dropped, the one thing it stores, and a node the app has named to its person as having left counts as not shown from then on, so it is asked about if it comes back. |
| C37-4 | 37 | Medium | 6.7 step 2; ADR decision 8 | After most of the scope moved out, a part could stay over the bound after pruning one part's worth, and if the refusal rolled the pruning back, every retry met the same bound. | Fixed: a part's pruning commits on its own first, and a part over the bound only because rows wait to be pruned is `rate-limited`, so the app sends it again and the next part prunes more. |
| C37-5 | 37 | Medium | 6.7 step 6; ADR decision 2 | An owner removed and made an owner again during an ending: "their staged envelopes are never used again" either refused the new ones or let the cleanup delete them. | Fixed: the removal covers envelopes staged until then; each staged row records when it was staged, so after a re-add `missing=` lists their envelopes, and those staged after it count and are never deleted by the removal's cleanup. |
| C37-6 | 37 | Medium | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 7 | A tab that crashed between the contacts write accepting a co-owner and the kept mark naming its writer left the undo without its evidence, so a re-add could leave an earlier removal cleared. | Fixed: the app records the writer, with what the entry held and what the write puts there, before it sends the contacts write; at the next unlock an entry holding exactly that counts as written by it. |
| M37-1 | 37 | Medium | 6.7 steps 2, 4 and 6; ADR decisions 6 and 8 | The rule dropping envelopes to people no longer owners had no exception for the person being added, so re-adding a former owner as co-owner could never finish; and another owner's removal of a re-added co-owner during the change had no stated outcome. | Fixed: for `co-owner`, envelopes to the person being added are judged by their identity and the subject's key version alone, in a part, `missing=` and the finish; their removal as an owner while the change is open is a refusal of the subject, which the app tells its person. |
| C37-1 | 37 | Low | 6.7 step 1; ADR decision 8 | "A lock on the account" could be read as the account row, which deleting a space locks before the spaces its unfinished copies go to, so an open there could deadlock with it. | Fixed in wording: the two-open limit is counted under an advisory lock of its own, which only an open takes, after the space's. Rated Low: the item limits it cites already use an advisory lock. |
| C37-2 | 37 | Low | 6.7 step 3; ADR decision 2 | The scan reads which records are in scope and their key material apart, so a page another owner moved out between the two came with owner envelopes in a share's scan, which the app takes as `region-mismatch`, costing the whole change. | Fixed: each page of a scan for a change reads both in one snapshot. Rated Low: a narrow race, which today's scan has too. |
| C37-7 | 37 | Low | ADR decision 10 | An 8 MiB part can't arrive within the 60-second deadline below about 1.1 Mbit/s, so a resume on a slow link never gets through. | Fixed: a part refused as `request-timeout` is sent again in halves, down to 256 KiB. Rated Low: such a link can't send today's one request either. |
| S37-1 | 37 | Low | ADR Threat model and decision 10; 6.7 step 6 | A server can hide another device's Cancel from the device sending the change, which then finishes it, so "availability only" was too strong. | Fixed: the Threat model says so, and Cancel on another device says the change stops once the device sending it hears, never "Nothing changed". No worse than today, where no device can stop another's request. |
| S37-2 | 37 | Low | 2.4 A10; 6.8 **The region doesn't change on its own**; ADR decision 5 | A10 and 6.8 named the parts of a cancelled or abandoned move into a share, but not a refused one, nor the wraps of a node that left a folder being moved into a share that then finished. | Fixed in wording: A10 and 6.8 say "a move into a share that didn't finish" and name nodes that left, and the app says such a node's later saves could go to that share too. |
| S37-3 | 37 | Low | 2.4 A10; ADR decision 5 | A comment edited or deleted while a `share` or move into a share is open keeps its staged wrap, which a server can give the share's members, for up to a day rather than today's seconds. | Fixed in wording and accepted: A10 names it, and the app says so when its own person edits or deletes a comment under an open one. |
| S37-4 | 37 | Low | ADR decision 5 | Row 1 of decision 5's table listed "a page or folder made" among what is caught up without asking, against row 2 and section 6.7. | Fixed: a new page or folder is row 2 only. Same as M37-2. |
| M37-2 | 37 | Low | ADR decision 5 | Same as S37-4. | Fixed with S37-4. |
| M37-3 | 37 | Low | ADR decision 5; 6.7 step 2 | For a node that left a folder being moved into a share, the app offered "start again with new keys", which a move doesn't have and which takes nothing back. | Fixed: that offer is for `share` only; for a move into a share the app says the node's versions went up under that share's keys and can't be taken back. |
| M37-4 | 37 | Low | 6.7 step 4; ADR decision 7 | A re-add accepted at the open seals the adder's owner-region saves to that person for as long as the change is open, up to a day, and a later Cancel said nothing about them. | Fixed: after such a change ends without finishing, the app says that its saves meanwhile were sealed to that person too. |

**Outcome.** Round 37 found nothing High or Critical, so sending in parts meets the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed in wording, as plain rules with no new mechanism, and S37-3 is partly accepted in A10. Before the outcome was recorded, each fix was read against the rest of section 6.7 and the ADR. The check now stores the nodes it finds dropped, so every place that said it stores or commits nothing says so. Sending in parts (protocol.md §6.7 and its notes, ADR 0019) is reviewed and accepted. It is to be built when a library passes half the limit, after the `bulk-request-large` log line (#735) (Decided 1). Rounds 31 to 36 found 21 High findings, 14 distinct that held, all fixed; what they accepted is in A10, A5, ADR decisions 6 and 11, and the known limit Scott accepted on 2026-11-23 (C35-1/C36-2).

## Round 38: identity key rotation, first review

Round 38 is the first review of the identity-key-rotation design (ADR 0018, #327, proposed 2026-11-23), against `t3/identity-key-rotation-adr` (060ad62). It ran the three briefs (server, member, encoding) as three independent reviewers in parallel: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, for a second model's look. Each got only its brief, protocol.md (§4.6 and the parts it touches), the ADR, and the code it describes: the client's trust, identity, vault, contacts, owner, rotation, folder-key, envelope and device-seal code, the server's vault, identity, owner and sharing code, the identities schema, the vault routes, the CLI's key file and the recovery page. They took the ADR's recommendations as given, since the project owner hasn't decided them, and attacked how the protocol specifies them. Users are trusted family and friends, so contrived cases were left out. It found no Critical finding, 11 High, 9 Medium, 8 Low and 1 Info (29 in all), several the same defect seen by two briefs. The statement held: the domain is new and distinct, both signatures cover one set of bytes under keys that must differ, `from.keyHash` binds the exact old keys, and a server alone can't make a chain, so it can't move a pin. Every claim the ADR makes about today's code checked out. The Highs were in what the statement leaves to the rest of the design: envelopes the old key signed choosing who gets new keys, contacts versions signed with the old key after the rotation, a rotation someone else made locking the person out before their app could notice, and agents a thief enrolled surviving it. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M38-1 | 31 | High | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 7.5 step 1; ADR decision 6 | In a rotation from a version sealed with an earlier key, the exception let envelopes signed with the leaked key put people into `E`, so whoever holds the old key, with the server listing a removed member, could get that member the next version, in the person's own rotations and every later one. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. Such envelopes let the version count for step 1 but add nobody to `E`; members whose keys came only from them are listed by label, unticked, and sealed to only if the owner ticks them. A15 says what a server can still put in that list. |
| M38-2 | 31 | High | 4.6 **Agents**; ADR decision 7 | Kept agents were ticked by default from a list the old key could write, and an agent the server knew of but the contacts record didn't list survived the rotation, so a thief's agent kept receiving new versions. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 7. Every live agent is listed with its label and `enrolledAt`, none ticked; the request names every live agent as kept or revoked, and the server refuses it otherwise; one the contacts record doesn't list is never kept. |
| M38-3 | 31 | High | 4.6, 8.5; ADR decision 1 | "Accepted only below the first version signed with a later key" meant nothing when the server chooses what a device sees: whoever holds the old key could add contacts versions after the rotation, signed with it, changing pins and accepted owners. | Fixed: §4.6, §8.5 and ADR decision 1. The statement signs `contacts`, the last old-key version and its hash; the rotate request carries the first new-key version; old-key versions count only up to the one named; the server refuses old-key writes after. Same as E38-F3. |
| M38-4 | 31 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; A15; ADR decisions 4 and 8 | After a thief's rotation the server's wrapper is the thief's, so the person's devices could never unlock to notice, and ADR 0009 made them drop the kept wrapper first. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 4. Before replacing a kept wrapper or asking for a passphrase because the vault version moved, a device checks the directory's key and chain, which are public; for a key it doesn't hold it asks whether the person did it, and **I didn't** keeps the old wrapper, opens what it can, warns, and signs nothing. Same as E38-F1. |
| M38-5 | 31 | Medium | 6.5 step 3; 4.6 | Owner statements the rotated owner signed before stopped counting, so an app reaching the space through that owner after the rotation never reached the owners they added; a co-owner who rotated before confirming the dialog found no statement naming them. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. Finishing signs each such statement again with the new key, the server takes it, and step 2 takes statements naming any key version the keyring holds. Same as E38-F4. |
| M38-6 | 31 | Medium | 8.2 `removed`, 8.4, 8.6 | Nothing said whether a key in `removed` stops a chain, or what declining a followed key does, so a thief rotating again after a decline was followed without a warning. | Fixed: §8.4 and §8.6. A chain through a removed key is never followed; **Not them?** removes the followed key and its later keys and moves the pin back; a new "decline a followed key" merge. Same as E38-F5 (a). |
| M38-7 | 31 | Low | ADR decision 4; A15 | "The keys alone: nothing new" missed that a device's kept contacts record, opened with the old key, holds the app-run agents' tokens. | Fixed in wording: A15 and ADR decision 4. |
| E38-F1 | 31 | High | 4.6; A15 | Same as M38-4. | Fixed with M38-4. |
| E38-F2 | 31 | Medium | 4.6 keyring v2; 5.6 | Format 2 kept the frozen `vault-keyring/v1` AD outside the wrapper's `format`, and today's keyring schema strips unknown keys, so a server relabelling the wrapper format 1 got a keyring read without its earlier keys. | Fixed: §4.6 and §3.5. A format 2 keyring is under a new AD, `vault-keyring/v2`, parses strictly, and its `format` must be the wrapper's. |
| E38-F3 | 31 | Medium | 8.5 | Same as M38-3, from the contacts chain: §8.5 checked every version under one key, and the mark had no key version. | Fixed with M38-3. The mark gains `ownerKeyVersion`, which never goes down along an accepted chain. |
| E38-F4 | 31 | Medium | 6.5; 4.6 | Same as M38-5. | Fixed with M38-5. |
| E38-F5 | 31 | Medium | 8.6 | Same as M38-6 (a), and (b): a device that followed further first made another's pending follow stop with `contacts-conflict`; what the change carries was unsaid. | Fixed with M38-6. The follow carries the chain's key hashes, and is a no-op when the newer pin is a later key on the chain. |
| E38-L1 | 31 | Low | 4.6, 8.4 | `after=n` was undefined, two statements for one version weren't refused, and a chain past the key in hand had two readings. | Fixed: the route is `?from=n`, by `from.keyVersion`; duplicates, gaps and disorder are refused and reported; a chain is followed to the directory's current key. |
| E38-L2 | 31 | Low | 4.6 | Only the key just before had to differ, so a chain could come back to a key it held; and that check was called `malformed`, though it needs resolved keys. | Fixed: no key appears twice on a valid chain; the check is a verification failure. |
| E38-L3 | 31 | Low | 3.2, 4.6 | Keyring v2's integers weren't listed, and `retired` entries weren't tied to the same ID or to versions 1 to `k − 1`. | Fixed in §4.6. |
| E38-L4 | 31 | Low | 3.3 rule 8 | Rule 8 didn't name rotation statements, so a later format would read as an attack. | Fixed: rule 8 and §4.6 name them. |
| E38-L5 | 31 | Low | 4.6; `device-seal.ts` | `openOnDevice` returns null for any failure, so a device trying only the current key would drop the browser's sealed marks after a rotation; `deviceTag` wasn't covered. | Fixed: §4.6 tries each retired key before counting an item absent, and works tags out again. |
| E38-L6 | 31 | Info | 4.6; ADR decision 8 | The protocol said the agent's local file and the ADR its key file. | Fixed: the local file, written with the contacts mark. |
| S38-1 | 31 | High | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 7.5 | The rotation exception let an envelope signed with a retired key set a recipient's role, so a reader could be shown as an editor and get the new draft key. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. With M38-1's fix such envelopes add nobody to `E`, and a member the owner ticks from them is a reader unless the owner raises them. |
| S38-2 | 31 | High | 4.6, 8.5, 8.6 | The first new-key contacts version had no signed cutoff, so a server could hand the app an old-key extension to build it on. | Fixed with M38-3, and §4.6: the request's first new-key version must extend exactly the version the statement names. |
| S38-3 | 31 | High | 4.6, 5.6 | Same as E38-F2. The reviewer rated it High; it is Medium by the scale, an availability break with no key reaching anyone. | Fixed with E38-F2. |
| S38-4 | 31 | High | 4.6 **Members**; 7.9 | `rekeyed` was defined by the server's rows only, so a contact who really rotated could be brought into a share they never held. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. A member goes in `rekeyed` only with a verified envelope of the current version, sealed by a writing-set owner to an earlier key on their chain; the role is that envelope's. |
| S38-5 | 31 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; A15 | Same as M38-4, and the device's anchor wasn't named, nor what a device without one can tell. | Fixed with M38-4: the device keeps its last identity's public key outside the wrapper. Accepted: a browser that keeps nothing takes the server's word, stated in A15. |
| S38-6 | 31 | High | 4.6, 5.10; A15 | The rotation ends only the live sessions open at that moment; new ones start under share keys the old key holds until an owner rotates, so typing, text deleted included, reaches whoever holds the old key, with the server. | Accepted: A15 and ADR decision 4 name live typing beside saves until the share rotates. The person's own shares rotate at once; refusing sessions in other shares would need apps to know which shares a rotated member holds keys in, which members can't see. |
| S38-7 | 31 | High | 8.4, 8.6 | An app could use the new key before its follow was stored, so a dropped write let a later launch follow a sibling branch unnoticed. | Fixed: §8.4 and §8.6. Following is a confirmed write; until the record holds it, the new key only opens. |
| S38-8 | 31 | Medium | 5.6; ADR decision 9 | `changeVaultPassphrase` fetches the wrapper again before saving (`packages/client/src/operations/vault.ts` line 153), so a recovery beside a rotation would write a passphrase wrap of the old vault key next to the new keyring. | Fixed: §4.6. A passphrase change replaces the wrapper the vault was opened from, at its version, and gets `stale-vault-version` otherwise. |
| S38-9 | 31 | Medium | 6.5; 4.6 | Same as M38-5. | Fixed with M38-5. |
| S38-10 | 31 | Low | 4.6 | The installation ID wasn't required to match the verifier's. | Fixed: `binding-mismatch`. |
| S38-11 | 31 | Low | 4.6; A15; ADR decision 1 | The signatures prove possession of signing keys only; the text said "the old key" and that the new keys are held. | Fixed in wording: authority to rotate follows the old Ed25519 secret key. |

**Outcome.** Round 38 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but S38-6, accepted in A15, and S38-5's case of a device with no anchor, accepted in A15. Round 39 repeats the three briefs against this revision. The formal rounds to freeze continue after the project owner decides ADR 0018.

## Round 39: identity key rotation, second review

Round 39 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and round 38's log, against the round 38 revision (00c5604). As in round 38, the member and encoding briefs ran as Claude subagents and the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent. It found no Critical finding, 11 High, 6 Medium, 9 Low and 1 Info (27 in all), most the same defect seen by two or three briefs. Round 38's fixes held where they reached: the contacts cutoff, the format 2 keyring's AD, `rekeyed`'s verified envelope, the confirmed follow and the passphrase change at the version it opened. The Highs were where they didn't reach: the person's own app, which the earlier-key rules didn't name; owner statements signed again from what the server served; the device's anchor, which sat where it couldn't be read before an unlock; a decline that nothing on other devices could see; owner-region live sessions; and agents named only by labels the old key could set. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S39-1 | 32 | High | 4.6 finishing; 6.5 | M38-5's fix took the owners to name again from served statements, so a statement the old key signed after the rotation came back signed with the new key, and named a thief's owner. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. The app signs again only for owners its own contacts record accepts in a space it owns, never from a served statement. Same as M39-1. |
| S39-2 | 32 | High | 8.5 | A client that knew of a rotation could still take a head below the cutoff, signed with the old key, as current. | Fixed: §8.5. Once a client knows of a rotation, the current record must be signed with the current key, and every version from the mark up chains through the cutoff; an old-key head is `contacts-rollback`. |
| S39-3 | 32 | High | 4.6; 5.10 | An owner-region session's `sealedTo` names IDs only, so a session sealed to an owner's old key passed the checks after the app followed their rotation. | Fixed: §4.6, **Live sessions in the owner region**. An app leaves such a session once it follows a rotation of an owner in it, and joins none sealed to an earlier key. |
| S39-4 | 32 | High | 8.4, 8.6 | After a decline, nothing kept a sibling branch from the restored pin from being followed. | Fixed: §8.4. No rotation of an identity is followed while `removed` holds an entry for its ID dated after its pin's `verifiedAt`. Same as E39-F1's first part. |
| S39-5 | 32 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated** | The S38-5 anchor sat in a sealed item, unreadable while locked, or with the `/api/me` answer, which every fetch overwrites. | Fixed: §4.6. The anchor is a public key kept unsealed, moved only by this device's own unlock or rotation. Same as M39-7 and E39-F2. |
| S39-6 | 32 | Medium | 4.6; 7.5; 7.9 | Members the owner left unticked went in `removed`, which deletes a direct member's row, so there was nothing to give access to later. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 11. The rotation names them in `waiting`; they keep their rows and grants, without the new version. |
| S39-7 | 32 | Medium | 4.6 | "No enrollment signed with an earlier key counts" made what dropped agents wrote unverifiable, though the directory serves revoked agents' enrollments for exactly that. | Fixed: §4.6. Such an enrollment counts to verify what the agent signed, never to seal to it. Same as M39-5. |
| S39-8 | 32 | Medium | 4.6 | A write that read the caller's old key before the rotation could land after it. | Fixed: §4.6. Signed writes check the current key under a lock the rotation takes too, held to commit. |
| M39-1 | 32 | High | 4.6 | Same as S39-1. | Fixed with S39-1. |
| M39-2 | 32 | High | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 6.5; 7.5 | The earlier-key rules were for "another app"; the person's own app records itself by key hash, so what its retired keys signed still counted fully there, in its own finishing rotations too. | Fixed: §4.6. The rules hold in every app, the person's own included for its retired keys. |
| M39-3 | 32 | High | 8.4, 8.6 | A decline wasn't a confirmed write, so a lost write left the thief's key pinned; and a decline merged over a device that followed further left that later pin. | Fixed: §8.4 and §8.6. Declining is confirmed, and its merge moves the pin back whenever the newer pin descends from the declined key, removing every later key. |
| M39-4 | 32 | High | 4.6 **Agents**; ADR decision 7 | The agent list showed only labels and dates, which the old key could set, so a thief's agent could pass as a real one. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 7. The dialog shows each agent's ID, and asks the person to check it with `wikistow whoami`. |
| M39-5 | 32 | Medium | 4.6 | Same as S39-7. | Fixed with S39-7. |
| M39-6 | 32 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | A share whose current version only an earlier key sealed stopped every app that followed, with nothing telling an owner to rotate it. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. Owners' apps list such shares and offer **Rotate keys**. |
| M39-7 | 32 | Medium | 4.6; A15 | Same as S39-5, and a server helping the thief can keep serving the person's devices the old wrapper and key. | Fixed with S39-5. Accepted: A15 says the server can hide the rotation from the person's own devices. |
| M39-8 | 32 | Low | 4.6 **Members** | Whether the rotated person's app may write under a version it holds only through a retired key was open. | Fixed: it only reads under such versions. |
| M39-9 | 32 | Low | 4.6 **Members**; 7.9 | A plain **Give access** for a rotated member would leave the share unrotated. | Fixed: owners are offered only the rotation that gives access. |
| E39-F1 | 32 | High | 8.4, 8.6, 8.2 | Same as S39-4, and the restored pin read as an ordinary pin on other devices, so they sealed to it when the server listed it as current. | Fixed with S39-4: while the entry is there the restored pin counts for opening only, on every device. |
| E39-F2 | 32 | High | 4.6; A15 | Same as S39-5. | Fixed with S39-5. |
| E39-L1 | 32 | Low | 8.4, 8.6 | Declines didn't move the label's key hash back, and a change carrying only hashes couldn't rebuild a pin. | Fixed: both move the label's key hash, and a follow carries the full keys at each end. |
| E39-L2 | 32 | Low | 8.6 | A follow over a record with no pin for the ID could undo an unpin. | Fixed: no-op then, and when the chain's first key is in `removed`. |
| E39-L3 | 32 | Low | 4.6; ADR decision 10 | Today's apps refuse a format 2 wrapper only as an unreadable answer, not by asking to update. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 10. A release before rotation teaches them to refuse an unknown format as such. |
| E39-L4 | 32 | Low | 4.6 | Several statement fields had no stated type or bound, and `contacts: null` was allowed past version 1. | Fixed in §4.6. |
| E39-L5 | 32 | Low | 8.4, 8.6 | Keeping `method: "confirmed"` across a follow showed a key nobody compared as checked. | Fixed: a follow writes `first-seen`. |
| E39-L6 | 32 | Low | 8.5 | "From the key the client holds" read backwards for the person's own app. | Fixed in wording. |
| E39-L7 | 32 | Low | 4.6; 6.8 | Another tab of the rotating browser, holding the old keyring, could read the re-sealed marks as nothing kept and write them back under the old key. | Fixed: the rotating tab locks the others first. |
| E39-L8 | 32 | Info | — | `identities.keyVersion` is a 32-bit integer while clients accept any safe integer. | No change: rotations are never that frequent. |

**Outcome.** Round 39 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M39-7's second part, accepted in A15, and E39-L8. Round 40 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 40: identity key rotation, third review

Round 40 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and the log of rounds 38 and 39, against the round 39 revision (110ea07). The member and encoding briefs ran as Claude subagents and the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent; the first Codex run was refused by its provider's content filter, and a second, given the same material as a review of the specification's consistency, ran. It found no Critical finding, 9 High, 6 Medium, 10 Low and 1 Info (26 in all). Round 39's fixes held where they reached: the head rule after the cutoff, the unsealed anchor, the agent dialog, the own-app rule for retired keys, `rekeyed`'s verified envelope and the shares left at an earlier key. The Highs were the decline block, which compared clocks and which the merge ignored; owners kept from contacts versions the old key could write before the rotation; owner-region sessions, whose recipients' keys nothing showed; and a member whose key was replaced, whom ordinary rotations and gives still sealed to or gave keys without rotating. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S40-1 | 33 | High | 4.6 finishing; 6.5; 8.5 | The contacts cutoff fixes where old-key versions end, but versions before it, signed with the old key, may hold an owner only the old key's holder added, which finishing then signed again with the new key. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. Finishing signs again only for owners the person confirms, by label, per space. |
| S40-2 | 33 | High | 4.6; 5.10 | `sealedTo` names IDs, and an app sees only its own envelope, so round 39's rule had no evidence of which key the other owners' envelopes went to. | Fixed: §4.6. The session payload gains `sealedToKeys` (a minor addition); an app that followed a rotation of an owner in `sealedTo` joins no session without it, or naming an earlier key. |
| S40-3 | 33 | High | 8.4, 8.6 | Same as E40-F1. | Fixed with E40-F1. |
| S40-4 | 33 | Medium | 4.6; 7.5; 7.9 | A member in `waiting` had no envelope of the new version, so the next rotation removed them, and after a rotation of their own `rekeyed` couldn't take them. | Fixed: §4.6. They stay in `waiting` through later rotations, and an owner gives them access as a rotation that ticks them. |
| S40-5 | 33 | Medium | 4.6; 8.5 | **I didn't** checked the kept contacts record at the current key, and the app forgot a remembered unlock when the server ended its session, before the check. | Fixed: the check runs before anything is forgotten on a session's end, and the kept record is checked against the device's own mark at the old key. |
| S40-6 | 33 | Medium | 4.6 | A device whose rotation landed but whose answer was lost took its own rotation for someone else's. | Fixed: the device keeps the statement's hash before sending, and recognises it. |
| S40-7 | 33 | Low | 4.6 | The rotate route's agent and wrapper checks weren't exact. | Fixed in §4.6. |
| S40-8 | 33 | Low | 4.6 | No-reuse depended on where a verifier started the chain. | Fixed: chains are checked from key version 1. |
| M40-1 | 33 | High | 7.5 step 4; 4.6 **Members** | A member who rotated keeps their row at the old key version, and nothing said what any other rotation does with them, so a rotation by an older app, or one built like today's `planRotation`, sealed the new version to the leaked key, as an honest server requires today (`packages/server/src/content/sharing.ts` lines 975 to 986). | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. Every rotation names such a member in `rekeyed`, `waiting` or `removed`; the server refuses an envelope to a member's earlier key version; the app resolves members against the directory and the chain. |
| M40-2 | 33 | High | 4.6 **Members**; 7.3, 7.8, 7.9 | Adding a rotated member to a group, sharing with them again, or **Give access** sealed the current version to their new key without rotating, so their app then wrote under a version the old key holds, and stopped waiting. | Fixed: §4.6. The server refuses envelopes for such a member that don't come with a rotation; their app writes only above the highest version it held through a retired key. |
| M40-3 | 33 | High | 8.4, 8.6 | Same as E40-F1, and the restored pin's `verifiedAt` was unstated. | Fixed with E40-F1. |
| M40-4 | 33 | High | 4.6 finishing; ADR decision 2 | Same as S40-1, and finishing signed at the key it accepted an owner at, an earlier one included. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. The dialog lists every accepted owner of each space the person owns by label and fingerprint, none ticked; it signs those kept at their latest followed key, and puts the rest on `removedOwners` before rotating. |
| M40-5 | 33 | Medium | 4.6 `waiting`; 7.9 | A member in `waiting` looked to the server as holding their keys, so nothing listed them or let anyone give them access, and a rotation can't carry a raised role. | Fixed: §4.6. The server marks them waiting from `v + 1` and lists them; **Give access**, or for a rotated member a rotation naming them in `rekeyed`, restores them; `waiting` and `rekeyed` entries carry the role. Same as S40-4. |
| M40-6 | 33 | Medium | ADR decisions 2 and 4; 4.6 | A rotation doesn't change the account password, so a thief who knows it signs in again and fetches what is sealed to the old key, with no server help. | Partly fixed: the dialog offers a password change (ADR decision 2), which ends agents' credentials (§9.7). Accepted: A15 states the case. What they can fetch is what the old key already opened; after M40-1 no new version is sealed to it. |
| M40-7 | 33 | Low | 4.6; A15 | An enrollment signed with an earlier key verified any agent's records, so the old key could invent one. | Fixed: only for agents the contacts record named at or below the cutoff. |
| M40-8 | 33 | Low | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 step 1 | Self-acceptance under a retired key was an unlisted use of an earlier key, and which key the app records itself at was open. | Fixed: §4.6 names it and records the current key. |
| M40-9 | 33 | Low | ADR "What the code supports today" | The kept wrapper is replaced on every online read, and a moved version forgets the remembered unlock at once, both before the anchor check could run. | Fixed: the ADR names both. |
| E40-F1 | 33 | High | 8.4, 8.6 | The "follow a rotation" merge never checked round 39's decline block, so a follow re-applied over a record holding a decline went through, and its new `verifiedAt` lifted the block. | Fixed: §8.4 and §8.6. A decline is a `wikistow.declined` entry, `{id, keyHash}` of the restored pin, in the shared payload; a follow is a no-op while the newer record has one for the ID, and only confirming a key takes it out. |
| E40-F2 | 33 | High | 8.4; 3.2; 8.2 | The block compared timestamps from different clocks, which nothing else in the record does, with no stated `verifiedAt` for a follow or a restored pin, and string and instant comparisons disagree. | Fixed with E40-F1: the block is structural, and §8.4 says neither timestamp orders anything. |
| E40-F3 | 33 | Medium | 8.4 | The block caught every `removed` entry dated after the pin, so an ordinary decline of a substituted key stopped sealing to the real pin for good, with no way out. | Fixed with E40-F1: only a declined rotation adds an entry; an ordinary decline keeps sealing to the pin as today. |
| E40-L1 | 33 | Low | 8.5 | "Any other version at that key" could refuse the versions below the cutoff. | Fixed in wording. |
| E40-L2 | 33 | Low | 8.4, 8.6 | Over a follow of several steps, which keys a decline removes, and which it restores, was ambiguous, and a device that hadn't followed couldn't build the change. | Fixed: every key above the restored one goes into `removed`, and the change carries the restored key in full. |
| E40-L3 | 33 | Low | 7.5, 7.6, 7.9; 4.6 | The exact-set checks didn't subtract `waiting`, the four lists weren't disjoint, and the other rotation shapes didn't carry the new fields. | Fixed in §4.6. |
| E40-L4 | 33 | Low | 8.5; 9.3 | Marks kept before rotation have no `ownerKeyVersion`, and the CLI's local file had nowhere to keep the followed key. | Fixed: an absent one reads as 1, and the local file gains the followed key. |
| E40-L5 | 33 | Low | 4.6 | `retired`'s order was unstated. | Fixed: ascending key version. |
| E40-L6 | 33 | Info | 17 Q4; 18; ADR | They still said one round. | Fixed. |

**Outcome.** Round 40 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M40-6's second part, accepted in A15. Its fixes haven't been reviewed: as agreed for this ADR, the rounds stopped at three, and the formal rounds to freeze continue once the project owner decides ADR 0018, starting with a review of round 40's fixes. Rounds 38 to 40 found 31 High findings, falling from 11 to 9 a round, each in what the last round's fixes reached into; none was in the statement's bytes.

## Round 41: identity key rotation, fourth review

Round 41 is the first review after the project owner decided ADR 0018's questions on 2026-11-23, against the round 40 revision with that recorded (49c0635). It ran the three briefs again with fresh reviewers, each given the same material and the log of rounds 38 to 40, and asked to check round 40's fixes first: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, worded from the start as a read-only review of the specification's consistency with its threat model. It found no Critical finding, 7 High, 9 Medium, 10 Low and no Info (26 in all), several the same defect seen by two or three briefs. Round 40's fixes held where they reached: the statement's bytes, `sealedToKeys` against replay, the structural decline block, the lost-answer hash, chains from key version 1, and every rotation naming a rotated member. The Highs were again one step past them: owners and pins the old key wrote into the contacts record outside the spaces the person owns, a co-owner add that gave keys without rotating, a device rolled back below the key it made, a race between a share rotation and a member's rotation, and a server hiding the rotation from contacts. Three were accepted in A15 rather than met with a new mechanism. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S41-1 | 34 | High | 4.6 **Members**; A15 | A server helping the thief can keep showing other people's apps the person's old key, so an owner's rotation seals its new version to the leaked key, and later saves reach the thief. | Accepted: A15 says a server can hide the rotation from contacts, and §4.6 says the member rule holds on a server that lists the new key. Only an independent source of key freshness would prevent it, which is a new mechanism. |
| S41-2 | 34 | High | 4.6 route; 7.5 step 4 | On an honest server, a share rotation that checked a member's key before the member's identity rotation could commit after it, sealing the new version to the replaced key: the signed-write lock covered only the caller. | Fixed: §4.6. Every write that seals envelopes checks each recipient's current key under that recipient's lock too, held to commit; a stale one gets `stale-head` and is planned again with fresh folder keys. |
| S41-3 | 34 | High | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 8.1; A15 | Other people's apps can't read the contacts record, so they can't check M40-7's condition on an earlier-key enrollment: trusting the server undoes it, refusing undoes S39-7. | Fixed in wording and accepted: §4.6 says only the person's app and agents check it, and others take such an enrollment for opening only, as the person's earlier writing; A15 names agents' records among what the old key can show as earlier writing. Rated High by the reviewer; by the scale it is Medium, an integrity break with no key reaching anyone. Same as M41-8. |
| S41-4 | 34 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 7.5; 7.6 | Owners left unticked went on `removedOwners`, but the server still listed them, so the plain rotations that followed stopped at `unaccepted-owner`. | Fixed with M41-3. |
| S41-5 | 34 | Medium | 4.6 **Members**; 7.9 | A waiting member who then rotates has no envelope of the current version, which `rekeyed` required, so nothing could restore them. | Fixed with M41-4. |
| S41-6 | 34 | Medium | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; 5.6 | A server could serve the old wrapper under the current version beside the genuine key, and the kept wrapper was replaced before it failed to open. | Fixed with E41-F1: a served wrapper replaces the kept one only once it opens to a keyring at the anchor or after it. |
| M41-1 | 34 | High | 4.6 finishing; 6.5; A15 | S40-1's review covered only spaces the person owns: an owner the old key added in a space the person is a member of carried into the new key's record, and its versions counted for the person's writing. | Accepted with M41-2: A15 names accepted owners in spaces the person doesn't own among what carries over. Reviewing them there would need a way back from a mistaken untick that a member can't take, which is a new mechanism. |
| M41-2 | 34 | High | 4.6; 8.2; 8.4; A15 | Everything else the old key wrote into the contacts record before the rotation, pins, `removed`, `wikistow.declined` and plugin approvals, carried into the new key's record unreviewed, so a pin moved to a server key kept being sealed to. | Accepted: A15 and ADR decision 4 say what carries over. Reviewing every pin would be a new mechanism, for what is already the case after any pre-rotation compromise of the old key. |
| M41-3 | 34 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 7.6 | Finishing never said it sends §7.6's removal; with two unticked owners each removal had to seal to the other; and none ticked by default removed every co-owner on a click. | Fixed: §4.6, §7.6 step 3 and ADR decision 2. Each owner is kept or removed with no default, the dialog says what removal means, and one removal per space leaves all those not kept out, checked with each of them in place of `R`. |
| M41-4 | 34 | Medium | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**, **Members**; 7.9 | `rekeyed` needed a verified envelope of `v` by a writing-set owner, which neither a waiting member nor one whose envelope came from an owner's earlier key has. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. `rekeyed` also takes those members once the owner ticks them, as readers unless raised; the server's check is unchanged. Same as S41-5 and E41-F2. |
| M41-5 | 34 | High | 4.6 **Members**; 7.6 | Adding a rotated member as a co-owner sealed every version to their new key without rotating, and the write rule covered only spaces others own, so their saves went under a version the old key holds. | Fixed: §4.6. The server refuses the co-owner add until every share where they hold keys through an earlier key is rotated with them in `rekeyed`, and the write rule holds in every space. |
| M41-6 | 34 | Medium | 6.5 step 3; 4.6 **Owners** | An app that first met a rotated owner at the new key found only statements naming the earlier key, which read as a key change, so it never accepted them. | Fixed: §4.6 and §6.5. A statement naming an earlier key counts as naming the latest key of a valid chain the app follows past it, with nothing removed or declined after it. |
| M41-7 | 34 | Low | ADR decision 10; 4.6 | Older owners' apps can't rotate shares holding a rotated member, or give them access, and don't know `waiting`. | Fixed in wording: ADR decision 10 and §4.6. |
| M41-8 | 34 | Low | 4.6 | Same as S41-3. | Fixed with S41-3. |
| M41-9 | 34 | Low | 4.6; 7.9 | **Give access** sealed versions from `v + 1` up, where §7.9 takes every version or none. | Fixed: every key version. |
| M41-10 | 34 | Low | 4.6 | `waiting` and `rekeyed` had two shapes. | Fixed with E41-L4. |
| E41-F1 | 34 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; 8.5 | Only a newer served key was checked, and the anchor could move down, so a server serving the old wrapper and key could get the device that made the rotation to unlock with the old passphrase and seal to the leaked key. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 4. The anchor never moves down; a key or keyring below it, or off a valid chain after it, is a rollback, reported, and replaces nothing kept; a served wrapper replaces the kept one only once it opens at the anchor or after it. |
| E41-F2 | 34 | Medium | 4.6; 7.9 | Same as M41-4. | Fixed with M41-4. |
| E41-F3 | 34 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 | §8.4's block needed the entry to name the pin's key hash, §8.6's merge only the ID, so a stale entry after an unpin and a new first sight blocked every later follow for good. | Fixed: §8.4 and §8.6. An entry that doesn't name the pin's key hash does nothing, the merge checks the same, and every write that replaces the pin otherwise drops the ID's entries. |
| E41-F4 | 34 | Medium | 8.5; 6.8 | Today's apps parse the kept mark strictly and read a failed parse as nothing kept, so a mark written with `ownerKeyVersion` would wipe every mark in a browser with an old tab open. | Fixed: §8.5. Writers leave `ownerKeyVersion` out at key version 1, and the release before rotation teaches parsers to accept it. |
| E41-L1 | 34 | Low | 8.4; 5.5 | `wikistow.declined` had no format or parse rule, so a copy of the other extensions' "none if unparseable" would fail open. | Fixed: `format: 1`, strict entries, and a payload with one that doesn't parse is `malformed`. |
| E41-L2 | 34 | Low | 8.4; 8.6 | Confirming a declined key left it in `removed`, and "passes through a key in `removed`" read over the whole history. | Fixed: confirming drops that key's `removed` entry and the ID's declines, and the chain check is after the pin. |
| E41-L3 | 34 | Low | 4.6 **Live sessions in the owner region** | `sealedToKeys` had no shape, was checked as a blocklist, and "once it has followed" was recorded nowhere. | Fixed: a list of `Hash32` matching `sealedTo`, each the owner's key the app accepts, and a session without it only when every owner is at key version 1. |
| E41-L4 | 34 | Low | 4.6; 7.5; 7.9 | The `waiting` and `rekeyed` shapes, roles and absent fields were unstated, §7.5 step 2 still put waiting members in `X`, and nothing limited older versions' envelopes to `rekeyed`. | Fixed in §4.6. |
| E41-L5 | 34 | Low | 4.6 | The kept statement hash named no bytes, and the anchor sentence could be read as keeping the keyring unsealed. | Fixed: BLAKE2b-256 of its signed bytes; the sentence reworded. |
| E41-L6 | 34 | Low | 4.6 **Agents**; 8.1 | Nothing said the first new-key contacts version lists the kept agents with their new enrollments, and the others in `revokedAgents`. | Fixed in §4.6. |

**Outcome.** Round 41 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but S41-1, M41-1 and M41-2, accepted in A15, and S41-3's part about other people's apps, accepted in A15. Round 42 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 42: identity key rotation, fifth review

Round 42 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and the log of rounds 38 to 41, against the round 41 revision (50c1565): the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, worded as a review of the specification's consistency with its threat model. It found no Critical finding, 5 High, 6 Medium, 10 Low and 2 Info (23 in all). The encoding brief found no High for the first time. Round 41's fixes held where they reached: the recipient lock, the anchor that never moves down, `rekeyed` by the owner's tick, the co-owner add for a rotated member, the decline entry's format and the mark without `ownerKeyVersion`; and the reviewers found A15's text for round 41's accepted findings correct. The Highs were in finishing's owner review, again one step on: owners not yet chosen were still used, and the owners being removed still chose who got the new versions; and in two cases where a server holding back or keeping what it was sent did what A15 already lets it do by hiding a rotation. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S42-1 | 35 | High | 4.6 route; 6.8; 7.5 | A server can keep a share rotation it refused because a member rotated meanwhile, sealed to the member's old key, and serve it to writers as the current version. | Accepted with S41-1: a server helping the thief can as well commit that rotation, or hide the member's rotation from the owner, with the same result. A15 now names it. An owner-signed activation of each version would be a new mechanism. |
| S42-2 | 35 | High | 4.6 finishing; 6.5; 6.6 | Owners the finishing dialog hadn't reviewed yet still counted for sealing and writing, so an owner-region save, or a space the server listed only later, went to an owner only the old key added. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. Until a space's owners are all chosen, those listed count for opening only, and the app saves, seals, sends queued changes and acts as an owner there only after; a space listed later is reviewed first. Same as M42-2. |
| S42-3 | 35 | High | 4.6 **Members**; 6.8 | The rotated person's write floor came from the envelopes the server serves, which it can withhold, so after a co-owner add it hid the member row for, their app wrote under a version the old key held. | Partly fixed: §4.6. The floor is also taken from the **No going back** marks the device kept from before the rotation. Accepted: A15 says a server can withhold that evidence from a device that kept no mark, as it can hide the rotation (S41-1). |
| S42-4 | 35 | Medium | 4.6 **A chain**; 8.4 | Two of the person's devices rotating at once each sign a statement; a server can show contacts the one it refused, so they seal to keys the person's vault doesn't hold, and the app called this two parties holding the key. | Accepted: A15, as a loss of access with no key reaching anyone. Fixed in wording: the diagnostic names this case. |
| S42-5 | 35 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 7.6; 16 | The removal of several owners at once had no request: the route names one owner in its path. | Fixed: `POST /api/spaces/:spaceId/owners/remove {owners, rotations}` in §4.6 and the route table. Same as E42-L7's first part. |
| S42-6 | 35 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**; 8.4 | §4.6 blocked a statement naming an earlier key on any `wikistow.declined` entry for the ID, though §8.4 makes an entry that doesn't name the pin's key hash inert. | Fixed: §4.6 uses §8.4's condition, and §6.5's step 3 bullet names the earlier-key case. Same as M42-6 and E42-L2. |
| M42-1 | 35 | High | 4.6 finishing; 7.6 step 2; 7.5 step 1 | In finishing's removal, §7.6 let the envelopes of the owners being removed count when verifying `v`, so an owner only the old key added could put an identity of their own among the remaining people, while every real member showed unticked. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. In that removal such envelopes count for step 1's writing check only, and add nobody to `E`. |
| M42-2 | 35 | High | 4.6 finishing; 6.5; 5.10 | Same as S42-2, and an owner not yet reviewed could rotate a share to the person's new key and be written under. | Fixed with S42-2. |
| M42-3 | 35 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 6.5; 7.6 | An owner the server lists but the contacts record doesn't accept wasn't in the dialog, so every rotation stopped at `unaccepted-owner` and finishing couldn't remove them. | Fixed: §4.6. The dialog lists every owner the server lists; one the record doesn't accept, or has on `removedOwners`, can only be removed. |
| M42-4 | 35 | Medium | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 7.9 | A member in `waiting` kept their role and publish right on the server, and their agents their grants, with nothing in the dialog to leave them out. | Fixed: §4.6. Until given access they act as readers without a publish right, their agents too, and the dialog offers to leave a member out. |
| M42-5 | 35 | Low | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; A15 | After someone else's rotation, the person's agents follow it like any contact. | Fixed in wording: **I didn't** says so and offers a password change, which ends every agent's credential; A15 names it. |
| M42-6 | 35 | Low | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 | Same as S42-6. | Fixed with S42-6. |
| E42-F1 | 35 | Medium | 4.6 **Live sessions in the owner region** | "The key the app accepts them at" also read as an earlier key, since an app keeps accepting an owner at the keys it followed from, so an app could join a session sealed to an owner's old key. | Fixed: the key the app would seal to them at, the latest on a valid chain, never an earlier one. |
| E42-L1 | 35 | Low | 4.6; 5.10 | `sealedToKeys`' presence and length weren't tied to `sealedTo`. | Fixed: absent exactly when `sealedTo` is `null`, otherwise the same length; else `binding-mismatch`. |
| E42-L2 | 35 | Low | 4.6 **Owners**; 8.4 | Same as S42-6. | Fixed with S42-6. |
| E42-L3 | 35 | Low | 8.4; 8.5; 8.2 | A fresh record after `contacts-invalid` could drop `wikistow.declined`, and duplicates were undefined. | Fixed: the MUST NOT list names it, an unknown extension format offers only an update, and duplicates are ignored as in other sets. |
| E42-L4 | 35 | Low | 4.6 **When someone else rotated** | When a rotation the device made moves the anchor was unstated, so moving it on send would make a lost race read as a rollback. | Fixed: it moves when the rotation lands; a refusal drops the kept hash and runs the check. |
| E42-L5 | 35 | Low | 4.6 finishing; A15 | `removedOwners` entries the old key added for owners the server still lists were never reviewed, and A15 didn't say so. | Fixed with M42-3: such owners are in the dialog; A15 names entries for owners the server no longer lists. |
| E42-L6 | 35 | Low | 4.6; 7.9 | Raising a ticked member above their access, and which role **Give access** uses for a waiting member, were unstated. | Fixed: a role is at most what their access needs, a higher one refused, and **Give access** uses the kept one. |
| E42-L7 | 35 | Low | 4.6 route; 7.6; 16 | Same as S42-5, and `keptAgents` and `revokedAgents` weren't disjoint. | Fixed with S42-5; the agent lists are disjoint sets. |
| E42-L8 | 35 | Low | 4.6 **Opening with earlier keys** | The tab lock had no message that older tabs obey, and retired keys were tried on device items with no end, so a later old-key item was accepted. | Fixed: a `lock` message every tab obeys from the release before rotation, and retired keys tried only until the device has sealed everything again. |
| E42-I1 | 35 | Info | 8.5 | Whether an explicit `ownerKeyVersion: 1` is accepted. | Fixed: it is. |
| E42-I2 | 35 | Info | 8.6 | The decline change didn't carry the pin's `method` and `verifiedAt`, and a removed-only merge's "holds" was undefined. | Fixed: it carries them, and such a merge counts as held. |

**Outcome.** Round 42 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but S42-1 and S42-4, accepted in A15, and S42-3's part about a device that kept no mark, accepted in A15. Round 43 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 43: identity key rotation, sixth review

Round 43 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and the log of rounds 38 to 42, against the round 42 revision (3c9284f): the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent. It found no Critical finding, 4 High, 8 Medium, 12 Low and 1 Info (25 in all). The statement's bytes, the keyring and the chain held again. Round 42's fixes held where they reached: removed owners' envelopes adding nobody to `E`, the multi-owner request, the kept-mark floor, `sealedToKeys`' key, and the anchor moving only when a rotation lands; the reviewers found A15's text for the accepted findings correct, with two cases it understated. Two Highs were one finding, finishing's owner review once more: an owner the server lists only after the review was never reviewed. The others were an older owner's app joining a session sealed to the old key after confirming the new one, and a decline over a record where its follow never landed adding no block. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S43-1 | 36 | High | 4.6 finishing; 6.5 | The review covered the owners the server listed then; one it left out, or who left and came back, kept their acceptance and was sealed to once listed again. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 2. The review covers every owner the record accepted by the cutoff, listed or not; an owner is chosen once on `removedOwners` or named by a statement the new key signed; one not chosen counts for opening only, and the app asks before acting wherever the server lists them. Same as M43-1. |
| S43-2 | 36 | High | 4.6 **Live sessions in the owner region**; 5.10; ADR decision 10 | An owner's older app that confirms the person's new key after the warning still joins an owner-region session sealed to the old key. | Fixed and accepted: the release before rotation ships carries the `sealedToKeys` check, for confirmed keys too; A15 names apps older than that release. |
| S43-3 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 6.4 | When an owner the person doesn't accept sealed their only envelope of `v`, the app couldn't open `v`, so finishing couldn't remove that owner. | Fixed: §4.6. In that removal such a `v` counts as the version to rotate from, every member then shown unticked. |
| S43-4 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 7.5 step 2 | Members whose only envelope came from a removed owner went in `X`, and so in `removed`. | Fixed with M43-2. |
| S43-5 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 step 2 | A member who rotated before opening a share had no first-sight item at their current key, so couldn't read its history. | Fixed: §4.6. Step 2 takes folder key envelopes to a retired key too, for opening. |
| S43-6 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 8.5 | The earlier-key enrollment rule needed the cutoff version's payload, which the server doesn't keep. | Fixed: §4.6. The check uses the current record's `agents` and `revokedAgents`, which carry the cutoff's, for agents enrolled by then. |
| S43-7 | 36 | Low | A15 | "Kept no mark" understated the withheld-envelope case: a mark below the withheld version is the same. | Fixed in A15. |
| S43-8 | 36 | Low | A15 | Any statement the server received and refused, not only two devices', can be shown to contacts. | Fixed in A15 and the §4.6 diagnostic. |
| M43-1 | 36 | High | 4.6 finishing | Same as S43-1, and "chosen" had no definition. | Fixed with S43-1. |
| M43-2 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 7.5; 7.6 | Same as S43-4: a removed owner who last rotated every share could strand every member. | Fixed: §4.6. Such members are shown unticked, as for an earlier key's envelope, and otherwise wait. |
| M43-3 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 finishing; 6.5 step 2 | A space whose co-owner dialog was pending or declined had no accepted owner to keep, and one unfinished space held up the rest. | Fixed: §4.6. A pending dialog runs first, a declined space is left out (A15 names it), and finishing goes on space by space. |
| M43-4 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 **Members**; 9.4; 16 | The write floor and the agent routes disagreed: sealing only above it was refused, sealing every version let an agent write under one the old key held. | Fixed: §4.6. The app seals an agent only the versions above the floor, the server's checks take only those for such a person, and the dialog says so. |
| M43-5 | 36 | Low | 4.6 **Members**; 6.8 | The live marks rise after the rotation, so "marks kept from before" needed a copy. | Fixed: the device copies them beside the anchor when its rotation lands. |
| M43-6 | 36 | Low | 4.6; 7.9 | Whether `waiting`'s reader cap was their access made coverage checks loop. | Fixed: it caps what they may do; for coverage they count as left out. |
| M43-7 | 36 | Low | 4.6; ADR decision 10 | An older owner's app puts waiting members in `removed`. | Fixed: the server refuses that from a request without a `waiting` list and asks to update. |
| M43-8 | 36 | Low | 4.6 **Agents** | The agents the app runs could be ticked to keep. | Fixed: they are always revoked. |
| M43-9 | 36 | Low | 4.6 **Members** | The co-owner add waited on shares the person had been removed from. | Fixed: only shares whose current version they hold through an earlier key. |
| E43-F1 | 36 | High | 8.6; 8.4 | A decline merged over a record whose pin was still the restored key, as when its follow never landed, added no `wikistow.declined` entry, so a sibling chain was followed. | Fixed: §8.6. The merge adds the entry when the newer pin is that key too, and a decline is applied after any pending follow, never in its place. |
| E43-F2 | 36 | Medium | 4.6 **When someone else rotated** | After a lost answer, a resend was refused, and the refusal dropped the kept hash, so the device took its own rotation for someone else's. | Fixed: §4.6. The server answers a resend of the same statement as accepted; the device resends the same request, and drops the hash only once the stored statement is shown not to be its own. |
| E43-L1 | 36 | Low | 4.6; 5.10 | E42-L1's `sealedToKeys` rule refused a session without it, which the next sentence allowed. | Fixed in wording. |
| E43-L2 | 36 | Low | 8.6 **Confirmed writes** | When a follow is "held" was undefined. | Fixed: when the served pin is the followed key or a later one on its chain. |
| E43-L3 | 36 | Low | 4.6; 6.8 | Tabs older than the release ignore `lock`. | Fixed: the rotating tab also sends the signed-out message they already obey. |
| E43-L4 | 36 | Low | 4.6 | The re-seal record named no key, so a second rotation cut the retired keys off early. | Fixed: it records the key's hash. |
