| E43-L5 | 36 | Low | 4.6; 7.9 | The `waiting` role had no source and could disagree with §7.9's sealing check. | Fixed: it is the role their access needs. |
| E43-I1 | 36 | Info | 8.6 | A device that never did the follow has no stored `method` and `verifiedAt` for the restored pin. | Fixed: such a device writes `first-seen` and its own clock, which order nothing (section 8.4). |

**Outcome.** Round 43 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but S43-2's part about apps older than the release before rotation, accepted in A15. Round 44 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 44: identity key rotation, seventh review

Round 44 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and the log of rounds 38 to 43, against the round 43 revision (f43c3f4): the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent. It found no Critical finding, 4 High, 5 Medium, 10 Low and 1 Info (20 in all). The statement's bytes, the chain, the keyring and the contacts cutoff held again. Round 43's fixes held where they reached: chosen owners in owned spaces, members stranded by a removed owner, the removal from a version the app can't open, first sight through a retired key's envelope, the resend after a lost answer, agents sealed above the write floor and the decline after a pending follow. The Highs were an owner confirming a key inside a session already open, a device whose **I didn't** the server could undo by serving the old state again, the agents an app runs, which only the old key's payload names, and a decline that a withheld statement turned into a silent no-op. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S44-1 | 37 | High | 4.6 **Live sessions in the owner region**; 5.10 | The `sealedToKeys` check ran on joining and on following; an owner already in a session who confirmed the rotated owner's new key, with the server not ending it, kept typing to the old key. | Fixed: §4.6. An app leaves on a confirmed key change too, and the release before rotation carries that; A15 says older apps can stay in such a session. |
| S44-2 | 37 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; 5.6 | After **I didn't**, a server serving the old key, wrapper and record again matched the device's anchor, so the device signed and sealed again. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 4. **I didn't** records the successor key beside the anchor, and the device signs and seals nothing until it unlocks at that key or after it. |
| S44-3 | 37 | Medium | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 8.2 | "Enrolled at or before the cutoff" can't be read from the current record. | Fixed with E44-L3. |
| S44-4 | 37 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 step 2 | A co-owner who rotated before opening the space had only a statement naming their old key, so the pending co-owner dialog couldn't name its adder. | Fixed: §4.6. Step 2 takes statements naming a held key version for the co-owner dialog as for any adder; envelopes to one count for opening only. Same as M44-3. |
| S44-5 | 37 | Low | A15 | A15 still described the review before S43-1. | Fixed. Same as M44-8 and E44-L2. |
| M44-1 | 37 | High | 4.6 route, **Agents**; 9.8 | The server can't tell the agents an app runs from others, and the app knows them only from the old key's `wikistow.appAgents`, so a thief who removed an entry got that agent offered, with no machine to check, and kept if ticked. | Accepted: A15 names it, and §4.6 makes it the app's rule, not the server's check. A server-side mark from approval would be a new mechanism, and agents approved before it would still lack it. |
| M44-2 | 37 | Medium | 4.6 finishing | "Chosen" wasn't limited to spaces the person owns, so in spaces they're a member of every owner counted for opening only. | Fixed: §4.6. It applies in spaces the server lists them as an owner of. |
| M44-3 | 37 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners** | Same as S44-4: "for opening" read as covering statements too. | Fixed with S44-4. |
| M44-4 | 37 | Medium | 4.6; 7.9 | A member left unticked waited like anyone else, so a plain **Give access** brought back an account only the old key vouched for. | Fixed: §4.6. The server keeps why and by whom they were left out, `GET /api/me/waiting` reports it, and **Give access** shows it. |
| M44-5 | 37 | Low | 4.6 finishing | The dialog marked an owner remove only before working acceptance out through the owners kept in it. | Fixed: it works it out again first. |
| M44-6 | 37 | Low | 4.6 | The first new-key version put agents only the server knew of into `revokedAgents`, so their old-key enrollments verified. | Fixed with E44-L3. |
| M44-7 | 37 | Low | 4.6 | "Without a `waiting` list" couldn't tell an older app's request from an empty list. | Fixed: a body with no `waiting` property; current apps always send it. |
| M44-8 | 37 | Low | A15 | Same as S44-5. | Fixed with S44-5. |
| E44-F1 | 37 | High | 8.6; 8.4 | Whether the newer pin descends from the restored key was undefined; a server withholding a statement made the decline fall to `removed` only, counted as held, and the declining device kept sealing to the thief's key. | Fixed: §8.6. Descent is decided on a valid chain from key version 1; when it can't be fetched, the merge stops and asks, and never falls back to `removed` only. |
| E44-L1 | 37 | Low | 4.6 | After a refusal that stored nothing, the kept hash could never be dropped. | Fixed: a definite refusal drops it, and a retry is a new statement with new keys. |
| E44-L2 | 37 | Low | A15 | Same as S44-5. | Fixed with S44-5. |
| E44-L3 | 37 | Low | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**, **Agents**, finishing | The agent and owner rules read the cutoff version's contents, which the server doesn't keep. | Fixed: `revokedAgents` gains only the cutoff version's agents not kept, server-only agents are never named, and "enrolled at or before" is dropped; the owners accepted by the cutoff read as the current record's `owners`, which only grows. |
| E44-L4 | 37 | Low | 4.6 route | The route listed a check on app-run agents the server can't make. | Fixed with M44-1. |
| E44-L5 | 37 | Low | 4.6; 8.5 | After a restore that lost the rotation, the device had no way forward, and sign-out's effect on the anchor was unstated. | Fixed: it offers to rotate again to the same keys from its kept keyring; the anchor stays until the device forgets what it kept. |
| E44-L6 | 37 | Info | 8.6 | Which key a decline restores on a device that didn't follow was unnamed. | Fixed: the pin in its kept copy of the record, or else the key just below the current pin. |

**Outcome.** Round 44 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M44-1, accepted in A15. Round 45 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 45: identity key rotation, eighth review

Round 45 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and the log of rounds 38 to 44, against the round 44 revision (9275589): the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent. It found no Critical finding, 4 High, 1 Medium, 12 Low and 1 Info (18 in all). The member and encoding briefs found no High; all four came from the server brief. The statement, the chain, the keyring and the contacts cutoff held again, and round 44's fixes held where they reached. The Highs were in round 44's own additions: the retry after a restore signed whatever head the restored server served, the waiting reason was the server's word, the block on an unexpected successor came only after the person chose, and a tab learned a new pin from another tab without checking its open session again. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S45-1 | 38 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated**; 8.5 | The rotate-again offer after a reported restore used the restored contacts head as the cutoff, so a head the old key forged after the real rotation was signed into a new statement and carried into the new key's record. | Fixed: §4.6. The device keeps the version its statement named, and rotates again only from that version or one it chains down to; otherwise it stays read-only. |
| S45-2 | 38 | High | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 7.9 | The waiting reason was the server's to report, so it could list a member left unconfirmed as an ordinary one, and another owner's **Give access** sealed to them. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 6. An owner's app that can't verify a member through a writing-set owner's envelope of the current version says no owner it accepts vouched for them, whatever the server reports, and seals only on a confirmed new grant, on every path that seals. Same as M45-1. |
| S45-3 | 38 | High | 4.6 **When someone else rotated** | Only **I didn't** recorded the successor, so closing the prompt and a replay of the old state let the device sign at the old key again. | Fixed: §4.6 and ADR decision 4. The device records the successor before it shows the prompt. |
| S45-4 | 38 | High | 4.6 **Live sessions in the owner region**; 5.10 | A tab that learned an owner's new key from another tab's contacts write kept typing in a session sealed to the old key. | Fixed: §4.6. The `sealedToKeys` check holds for staying in a session too, checked again before each message once the record has changed. |
| M45-1 | 38 | Medium | 4.6; 7.8; 7.9 | Same as S45-2, on the paths other than **Give access**, and across the member's own rotation. | Fixed with S45-2; the reason stays through the member's rotation. |
| M45-2 | 38 | Low | 4.6; ADR decision 10 | An older owner's app that confirmed a rotated member's key removed them in its next rotation, and "a request" didn't fit bodies with several rotations. | Fixed: the server also refuses `removed` for such a member from a rotation entry with no `rekeyed`, and both checks are per rotation entry. |
| M45-3 | 38 | Low | 4.6 finishing; A15 | A server that stops listing the person as an owner of a space they own leaves it unreviewed. | Accepted: A15. |
| M45-4 | 38 | Low | 4.6 finishing | "Chosen" didn't exempt the person. | Fixed: they are always chosen, and not listed. |
| M45-5 | 38 | Low | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 step 2 | "For opening only" read as limiting the sealer's acceptance, which the record can't hold and which stranded the member. | Fixed: such an envelope accepts its sealer as first sight does; the envelope counts for opening only. |
| M45-6 | 38 | Low | 9.6; 4.6 | Agents' routes didn't include the rotation and earlier-row routes the CLI needs to follow its person. | Fixed: §9.6 names them. |
| E45-L1 | 38 | Low | 8.6 | "Its kept copy" of the record was the synced one after a follow. | Fixed: the copy from before it first took a pin on this chain above the restored key. |
| E45-L2 | 38 | Low | 4.6; 8.5 | What survives a reported restore, and what the rotate-again request sends, were unstated. | Fixed with S45-1. |
| E45-L3 | 38 | Low | A15; 8.5 | A reported restore rolls contacts' pins back after they met the new key. | Fixed in A15: it says so, with a restore notice on every device. |
| E45-L4 | 38 | Low | 4.6 route | The resend rule wasn't ordered before the route's other checks, which fail on a resend. | Fixed: it compares with the stored statement first. |
| E45-L5 | 38 | Low | 8.4; 4.6 | Statements served past the directory's current key had two readings. | Fixed: a chain ends at the directory's key, and more is a key change. |
| E45-L6 | 38 | Low | 8.6 | A follow that no-ops for want of a pin read as a key change. | Fixed: it is first sight then. |
| E45-L7 | 38 | Low | 4.6 **Owners** | Statements were matched by key version only. | Fixed: by `owner.keyHash`. |
| E45-I1 | 38 | Info | 8.4; 8.5 | An unknown-format `wikistow.declined` the old key wrote blocks the rotation with an update that doesn't exist. | Fixed in wording: the message says the record may have been written with a leaked key. Rotating first, which A15 accepts, is worse. |

**Outcome.** Round 45 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M45-3, accepted in A15. Round 46 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 46: identity key rotation, ninth review

Round 46 ran the three briefs again, with fresh reviewers given the same material and the log of rounds 38 to 45, against the round 45 revision (d77d597): the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent. The first Codex run was refused by its provider's content filter, as in round 40, though its brief was worded as a consistency review; a second, worded as a review of whether the specification's rules agree, ran. It found no Critical finding, 4 High, 3 Medium, 9 Low and 2 Info (18 in all). The encoding brief found no High or Medium for the second round running. Round 45's fixes held where they reached. The Highs were a session the app kept sending in after verifying a successor whose follow wasn't stored yet, two things only the release before rotation can teach older apps (the decline block, and the vouching check for members left unconfirmed), and an owner the server no longer lists, whose removal in finishing left every share they sealed unrotatable. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes, which are not yet reviewed.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S46-1 | 39 | High | 4.6 **Live sessions in the owner region**; 8.4 | An app that verified an owner's successor kept sending in a session sealed to the old key while the server stalled its follow, since its record hadn't changed; a tab could also send before another tab's broadcast arrived. | Fixed: §4.6. The app stops sending once it verifies the chain, a follow still pending included, and a browser tab reads the shared mark under the lock before it sends. |
| S46-2 | 39 | High | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 7.9; ADR decision 10 | An older owner's app gives access to a member left unconfirmed with no vouching check. | Fixed: the release before rotation carries the check, and the server refuses envelopes for such a member from a request without `confirmedNew`. Accepted: A15 names older apps against a server that doesn't refuse. Same as M46-2. |
| S46-3 | 39 | High | 8.4; 4.6; A15 | An older app carries `wikistow.declined` forward but doesn't enforce it, and seals to the restored pin when the server lists it. | Fixed and accepted: the release before rotation enforces it; A15 names apps older than that. |
| S46-4 | 39 | Medium | 4.6; 8.5 | The rotate-again check needed versions the restore took away, and §8.5 had two readings of the restored head. | Fixed: only the exact version named, as the rotation's input, not as the current record; otherwise read-only. Same as E46-L1. |
| S46-5 | 39 | Medium | 4.6 **When someone else rotated** | Another tab, still unlocked, could sign at the old key after one tab recorded the successor. | Fixed: recording it sends `lock` to the other tabs, and every tab checks the record before signing or sealing. |
| S46-6 | 39 | Low | 4.6 **Members**; 6.4 | An envelope names its recipient by ID and key version, so with two statements for one version the owner can't tell which key received it. | Fixed in wording: in that case no envelope of the member's counts as vouching. |
| M46-1 | 39 | High | 4.6 finishing; 7.5 step 1; 7.6 | Removing an owner the server no longer lists was only a `removedOwners` entry, after which every share whose current version they sealed couldn't be rotated, so finishing never ended and members kept saving under keys the old key holds. | Fixed: §4.6. In finishing's rotations of that space such an owner's envelopes count for step 1 only and add nobody to `E`, as in a removal. |
| M46-2 | 39 | Medium | 4.6; 7.9 | Same as S46-2, and the waiting reason as a new property breaks older apps' strict parse of the whole list. | Fixed with S46-2: the reason is served only with `?reasons=1`. |
| M46-3 | 39 | Low | 4.6 finishing | Entries the old key wrote for the person's own ID could make them remove themself. | Fixed: the review ignores them, and the first new-key version drops them. |
| M46-4 | 39 | Low | 4.6 **Members**; 6.5 | "Only reads" forbade rotating from the version every share the person owns sits at. | Fixed: it only reads, and rotates from. |
| M46-5 | 39 | Low | ADR decisions 2 and 6 | The log said ADR decision 6 carried S45-2, and it didn't; M45-4 wasn't in decision 2. | Fixed in the ADR. |
| E46-L1 | 39 | Low | 4.6; 8.5 | Same as S46-4. | Fixed with S46-4. |
| E46-L2 | 39 | Low | 8.6; 8.5 | The pin a decline restores came from a copy no section keeps. | Fixed: the browser and desktop app keep, per ID, the pin before a synced move along a chain; the CLI restores one step down. |
| E46-L3 | 39 | Low | 8.5; 9.3 | Whether the CLI's followed key survives a reported restore was unstated. | Fixed: it does, as the anchor does. |
| E46-L4 | 39 | Low | 8.6 | An unpin merged over a pin a follow moved had three readings. | Fixed: it removes the pin whatever key it holds, and the keys on the chain go into `removed`. |
| E46-L5 | 39 | Low | 4.6 route | "No other row holds" could be read as the pair of keys. | Fixed: key by key. |
| E46-I1 | 39 | Info | 8.4; 4.6 | A rotation landing between the directory read and the chain read made an honest server look like an attack. | Fixed: the rotations route serves the current row from the same read. |
| E46-I2 | 39 | Info | 4.6 | A successor recorded before a restore that took it away had no stated way out. | Fixed: forgetting what the device kept is the only one. |

**Outcome.** Round 46 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but parts of S46-2 and S46-3, accepted in A15 for apps older than the release before rotation. Its fixes are unreviewed.

**After rounds 41 to 46.** Six rounds ran after the project owner decided ADR 0018 (2026-11-23), with 7, 5, 4, 4, 4 and 4 High findings, and none Critical. The statement's bytes, the chain, the keyring and the contacts cutoff held from round 38 on, and the encoding brief found no High in rounds 42, 45 and 46. The Highs kept coming in the same few places, each one step past the last round's fix: finishing's owner review (which owners it covers, when they count, how they're removed), the person's own devices across a lost answer, a restore or an unexpected successor, owner-region live sessions while a key changes, and what apps older than the release before rotation still do. Seven Highs were accepted into A15 rather than met with a new mechanism, and three more in part. The rounds stop here unmet, as agreed for this run, for the project owner to decide how to go on.

## Round 47: identity key rotation, narrowed, first review

On 2026-11-24 the project owner narrowed ADR 0018's scope (its question 9): a rotation no longer carries ownership, and a rotated owner is added again as a co-owner, as after any key change (protocol.md §7.6). The rotating person's app no longer signs owner statements again, finishing has no owners review, and the multi-owner removal route is gone. Where no owner a member's app trusts is left to add them again, as in a space the person alone owns, apps accept the new key as the owner through the chain, a choice made without the project owner and recorded in the ADR.

**Moot after narrowing (2026-11-24).** These findings were about the owner re-signing, the owners review, the removal of several owners at once, or owners being followed through the chain in spaces with other owners, all now gone, so they no longer apply: S39-1, S40-1, M40-4, S41-4, M41-3, M41-6, S42-2, M42-1, M42-2, M42-3, S42-5, S42-6, M42-6, E42-L2, E42-L5, S43-1, M43-1, S43-3, S43-4, M43-2, S44-4, M44-2, M44-3, M44-5, S44-5, M44-8, E44-L2, M45-3, M45-4, M45-5 (its part about owner statements), M46-1, M46-3 and M46-5, the owners part of E44-L3, and the part of S43-2 and S44-1 about leaving a session on a confirmed key change, which today's apps now do through the sealing-owner check. M38-5's fix, the re-signing, is withdrawn: the gap it found, an app working out a space's owners afresh not reaching through the rotated owner the owners they added, is accepted as an availability cost of the narrowing. M41-1 and M41-2 still stand, widened to every space by the review's removal (A15).

Round 47 reviewed the narrowed revision (11a6fe7) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers each given the narrowed text first, the log of rounds 38 to 46 and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, worded as a read-only design review of the specification against its own threat model, which its provider ran without refusing. It found no Critical finding, 5 High, 9 Medium, 8 Low and no Info (22 in all). Every reviewer found the narrowing applied consistently, with nothing left of the removed review, re-signing or removal route. The statement, the chain, the keyring and the contacts cutoff held again. The Highs were in the paths that bring a rotated owner back: a thief removing the co-owners to make the person the only owner, the person's own entry stopping a co-owner's first sight, a decline that left the declined key an owner, and a member writing under a version a newer one it couldn't open had replaced; and one in the frozen rotation of section 7.5, an envelope of a refused rotation served again. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S47-1 | 40 | High | 7.5; 4.6 **Members**; 6.4 | An envelope binds its version's number, not its keys, so a server can keep an envelope from a rotation it refused (as when a member rotated meanwhile) and serve it later as that member's envelope of the version committed in its place, and the owner's next rotation lists the member the retry left out. | Accepted: A2, and §7.5's claim that a server can't slip a person into a rotation now names the exception. It is in the frozen design, for any refused rotation; binding a version's keys would need a new envelope format or the owner-signed activation S42-1 declined. |
| S47-2 | 40 | High | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.8; 5.10 | A member who holds the rotated owner's new-key version, which it can't open before a re-add, still wrote under the version below it, which the old key holds, and stayed in its sessions. | Fixed: §4.6 **A version it can't use yet** and §6.8. The app raises its **No going back** mark to that version, so it writes, rotates and moves content under nothing below it, and leaves the sessions. |
| S47-3 | 40 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**; 7.6 | A co-owner adding a rotated owner again couldn't open the versions that owner's finishing sealed with the new key, which the re-add must seal on. | Fixed: §4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again** and §7.6. The adder's app records the new key in a confirmed write before it gathers keys. Same as E47-F1. |
| S47-4 | 40 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 | When two owners both rotate, their re-adds of each other are signed by keys no member accepts, so members never open anything new. | Fixed: §4.6 **When no owner the app trusts can add them again**. Where no listed owner is one the app accepts at their current key, it accepts the owners it accepted at the latest key of their chains. Same as E47-F3 and M47-5. |
| S47-5 | 40 | Medium | 4.6 **The person's own app**; 6.5 | The first new-key contacts version recorded the person as an owner wherever the server listed them, so a false listing became an acceptance in their record. | Fixed: §4.6. It replaces only its own entries at retired keys, and adds none where it had none. |
| S47-6 | 40 | Low | 4.6; 8.6 | Agents can't write the record that keeps the sole-owner acceptance. | Fixed: agents take it from their person's record and never work it out. Same as E47-L2 and M47-6. |
| M47-1 | 40 | High | 4.6 **Owners**; 7.6; A15 | Someone who rotated first, with the session, could remove the co-owner who would have to add them again, leaving the person the only owner, whom members then followed. | Fixed: §4.6. The server keeps the key version it last added each owner at, and refuses an owner above it removing another owner or adding a co-owner while an owner at theirs is listed. A15 says that before the rotation the old key's holder can act as the person anywhere, and that a co-owner who leaves first leaves the space to the new key. |
| M47-2 | 40 | High | 4.6 **The person's own app**, finishing; 6.5 step 2 | A co-owner who rotated before first opening the space got an entry for themself, so step 2's co-owner dialog never ran, finishing never rotated the space, and members kept saving under a version sealed to the old key; nothing prompted a rotation after the re-add either. | Fixed: §4.6. The person's own entry doesn't count in step 2 and none is added; the re-adding app rotates every share whose current version was sealed to the owner's earlier key; the dialog and A15 say such a space stays at versions the old key holds until then. |
| M47-3 | 40 | High | 8.4; 6.5; 4.6 | Declining a followed key moved the pin back but left the declined key accepted as an owner, so the member kept writing under versions it sealed. | Fixed: §4.6 and §8.6. An `owners` entry at a key in `removed` counts for opening only, and accepting one is a no-op. |
| M47-4 | 40 | Medium | 4.6 **Using an earlier key**; 7.3; 7.6; 7.9 | Removing a rotated owner instead of adding them again left versions they sealed that the remover couldn't open, which every later grant and co-owner add must cover. | Fixed: §4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead** and §8.6. The remover records the new key for opening only, as for any removed owner. |
| M47-5 | 40 | Medium | 4.6; 6.5 | Same as S47-4. | Fixed with S47-4. |
| M47-6 | 40 | Low | 4.6 | Same as S47-6. | Fixed with S47-6. |
| M47-7 | 40 | Low | 7.6; 6.5 | What declining the adder dialog after a re-add means was unstated. | Fixed: §7.6. As for a new co-owner: it signs nothing and takes no owner action there. Same as E47-L5. |
| E47-F1 | 40 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | Same as S47-3. | Fixed with S47-3. |
| E47-F2 | 40 | Medium | 4.6 **A space with no other owner** | The sole-owner exception depended on when an app looked: a member offline while the person rotated and then transferred the space never applied it. | Fixed: §4.6. The exception is decided from the owners the app accepts, at any time, and accepts unlisted owners too, so the transfer's statement is followed. |
| E47-F3 | 40 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | Same as S47-4. | Fixed with S47-4. |
| E47-F4 | 40 | Medium | 6.5 step 2; 4.6 | Whether first sight takes an item signed with an earlier key, and at which key, was undefined. | Fixed: §4.6 **Owners**. It skips such items and accepts a signer at the key they signed with; A15 names a new key taken by first sight. |
| E47-L1 | 40 | Low | 8.6 | The merge table didn't say the first new-key version takes the person's own ID out of `removedOwners`. | Fixed in §8.6. |
| E47-L2 | 40 | Low | 4.6 | Same as S47-6. | Fixed with S47-6. |
| E47-L3 | 40 | Low | 4.6; 8.4; 8.6 | The sole-owner acceptance didn't wait for the follow to be stored, and its merge ignored `removed`. | Fixed: it waits, and the merge is a no-op for a key in `removed`, which a decline fills. |
| E47-L4 | 40 | Low | 4.6 **Using an earlier key** | "A later key the app never accepted" didn't require a valid chain. | Fixed: on a valid chain from one it accepted. |
| E47-L5 | 40 | Low | 7.6; 6.5 | Same as M47-7, and when the re-added owner's dialog runs. | Fixed with M47-7: when it meets a statement naming it at its current key. |

**Outcome.** Round 47 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but S47-1, accepted in A2 as a property of the frozen rotation. Round 48 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 48: identity key rotation, narrowed, second review

Round 48 reviewed round 47's revision (983a86c), with fresh reviewers given the narrowed text, the log of rounds 38 to 47 and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief didn't run.** Its Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent was refused by its provider's content filter partway through, as in rounds 40 and 46, and again on the one retry, worded as a review of whether the specification's outcomes match its own goals and accepted limits. So round 48 had two of the three briefs, and can't meet the stopping rule whatever it found. The two found no Critical finding, 2 High, 7 Medium, 8 Low and no Info (17 in all). Both found the narrowing applied consistently, and round 47's fixes holding where they reached; the Highs were one step past them: an honest co-owner's own rotation turning the exception for spaces with no trusted owner on, and a retried removal letting the removed owner's new key choose who gets the new versions. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M48-1 | 41 | High | 4.6 **When no owner the app trusts can add them again**; finishing | A co-owner's own rotation, while a rotated owner nobody added again was listed, left the members no trusted owner, so their apps accepted that owner's key through the chain, with no server help, and the server's refusal of their owner actions lapsed too. | Fixed: §4.6 **Before it sends the rotation** and ADR decision 2. The rotating app first asks the person to add such an owner again or remove them, and sends its rotation only then. A15 says so. |
| E48-F1 | 41 | High | 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**; 7.6 **If the request fails** | Once the removal's contacts write recorded the new key, a retry couldn't tell it from a key the app had accepted, so the new key's envelopes put people into `E` again. | Fixed: §4.6. | M48-2 | 41 | Medium | 4.6 **When no owner the app trusts**; ADR decision 6 | The exception accepted owners whether or not listed, so it brought back, in members' apps, an owner a co-owner had removed. | Fixed: §4.6. Unlisted owners count only as signers of statements reaching the listed owners, never for writing or opening what they sealed. |
| M48-3 | 41 | Medium | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again**; 7.6 | A re-add that recorded the new key and then failed left the space stuck with nothing offering to finish it. | Fixed: §4.6. The re-add stays pending until the server serves its statement, the app offering only to send it again, as a failed removal does. Same as E48-F5. |
| M48-4 | 41 | Medium | 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**; 7.9 | After the removal, members could never open what was wrapped under the versions the removed owner's new key sealed. | Fixed: §4.6. The remover wraps those records again under the new version. |
| E48-F2 | 41 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**, **Using an earlier key** | Only one key of a chain was recorded, so versions sealed with keys between couldn't be opened or sealed on. | Fixed: §4.6. Every key of the chain between is recorded too, for opening only. |

| E48-F4 | 41 | Medium | 4.6 **The person's own app**; 6.5 **Opening** | Replacing the person's own retired-key `owners` entries dropped what those keys sealed from opening, for their agents among others. | Fixed: the current-key entry goes beside them. |

| M48-5 | 41 | Low | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again** | An owner could add themself again, moving their added-at version with nobody vouching. | Fixed: the server refuses a co-owner add naming the caller. |
| M48-6 | 41 | Low | 4.6 **Shares left at an earlier key** | **Rotate keys** was offered where it can only stop at `unaccepted-owner`. | Fixed: it offers to add that owner again or remove them. |

## Round 49: identity key rotation, narrowed, third review

Round 49 reviewed round 48's revision (e11e9ca) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the narrowed text, the log of rounds 38 to 48 and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent. The first Codex run was refused by its provider's content filter partway through, as in round 48; the one retry, worded as a review of whether the specification's outcomes match its own goals and accepted limits, ran. It found no Critical finding, 4 High, 13 Medium, 10 Low and no Info (27 in all). Every reviewer found the narrowing itself applied consistently. The Highs were all in what rounds 47 and 48 added to hold the exception for spaces with no trusted owner: the check before rotating ran only in the client and could be raced, it could block a person's rotation for good, and a server's owner list could make an owner's own app take the exception. So the fixes replace that machinery rather than extend it: the exception now fires only where the person is the only owner the member's app accepts, read from its own record, never from the server's list; the server's added-at rule and the check before rotating are gone, the latter kept as advice; removals record the removed owner's later keys in a set of their own, for opening only, and carry the remover's envelopes in place of re-wrapping. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S49-1 | 42 | High | 4.6 **Before it sends the rotation**; route | Two owners' apps both passed the check before rotating while both were at their first keys, and both rotations landed, so members accepted both new keys through the exception. | Fixed by removing what it relied on: the exception reads only the member's own record, and a member that accepts two owners never takes it (§4.6 **A space whose only accepted owner is the person**). Two owners who both rotate leave members waiting, an availability cost the ADR names. Same as E49-F1. |
| E49-F1 | 42 | High | 4.6 **Before it sends the rotation**; A15 | The check before rotating ran only in the client, so someone rotating a co-owner second skipped it, and members accepted both keys. | Fixed with S49-1. |
| E49-F2 | 42 | High | 4.6 **When no owner the app trusts**; 6.5 | The exception's gate was the server's owner list, so a server leaving an owner out of their own app's list made that app accept a rotated co-owner, for good. | Fixed: the gate is the app's own state (it has accepted itself there neither in step 1 nor through the co-owner dialog), and the test never reads the server's list. |
| M49-1 | 42 | High | 4.6 **Before it sends the rotation**; 6.5 step 2 | In a space where the person's app can take no owner action, the check before rotating could never be met, so the person could never rotate. | Fixed: the check is now advice in the dialog and holds nothing back. |
| S49-3 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 6.5; 7.6 | A re-added owner whose adder rotated since had no signer it could accept for its co-owner dialog. | Fixed: §4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again**. It takes that statement only to run the dialog, which offers to add the adder again or remove them. |
| S49-4 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | After a transfer, a member that took the person's new key only as a statement signer couldn't open what they published under it. | Fixed: the member now accepts the person at the new key, so it opens it. |
| S49-5 | 42 | Medium | 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**; 7.5 | The lowest-key rule shut out the envelopes of a key a co-owner had vouched for, with no way to carry those members forward. | Fixed: removed keys go in `openOnly`, and only keys the remover doesn't hold in `owners` add nobody to `E`; members whose only envelope came from one are shown unticked and wait if left so. Same as E49-F4. |
| S49-6 | 42 | Medium | 4.6 | A pending re-add couldn't move to the owner's newer key, or turn into a removal. | Fixed: it offers both. Same as M49-3 and part of E49-F5. |
| S49-7 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 6.8; 7.6 | A refused envelope above the committed version raised the mark, which then stopped the owner rotating from the version below. | Fixed: that mark doesn't stop a rotation or a removal from below. |
| S49-8 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | A re-wrap after a removal, interrupted, was never resumed. | Fixed: the removal carries the remover's envelopes of those versions in the same request, and nothing is re-wrapped. |
| E49-F3 | 42 | Medium | 4.6 | Which of a removed owner's keys add nobody to `E` had three definitions, and none for an owner never accepted. | Fixed: one rule, any key the remover doesn't hold for that owner in `owners`. Same as M49-5. |
| E49-F4 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 8.2 | Same as S49-5. | Fixed with S49-5. |
| E49-F5 | 42 | Medium | 4.6 | A pending re-add was visible only on the device that started it, and had no exit. | Fixed: it is derived from the record and the served statements on every device, with exits as in S49-6. |
| E49-F6 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 8.2; 8.4 | Declining a co-owner's new key and then removing them either couldn't proceed or left versions nobody could open. | Fixed: an `openOnly` key opens for reading and sealing on even when in `removed`, and the removal's writing check takes it as an earlier key's. |
| E49-F7 | 42 | Medium | 4.6 **Before it sends the rotation** | Whom the check covered was ambiguous, and the broad reading blocked rotation. | Fixed with M49-1. |
| M49-2 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 7.5 step 1 | A rotated owner nobody added again who removed themself left every share at a version no one could rotate from. | Fixed: **Shares left at an earlier key** lists those shares and offers **Rotate keys**, that owner's envelopes counting as in a removal. |
| M49-3 | 42 | Medium | 4.6; 8.4 | Same as S49-6, with a declined key. | Fixed with S49-6. |
| S49-2 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | Re-wrapping a removed owner's records under the new version gave a member's new agent history the dialog said it wouldn't read. Rated High by the reviewer; the agent reads only what its person reads, so by the scale it is Low. | Fixed with S49-8: nothing is re-wrapped. |
| E49-L1 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | `sealedToKeys`' errors overlapped, and a repeated `sealedTo` ID was refused only beside it. | Fixed: it parses first, then binds; a repeated ID is refused either way. |
| E49-L2 | 42 | Low | 8.6 | The merge rows carried one key, not a chain's. | Fixed: "accept an owner" carries every key it records, none in `removed`; "remove a rotated owner" writes `openOnly`. |
| E49-L3 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | Which owner actions moved the added-at version was unstated. | Moot: the added-at rule is gone. |
| E49-L4 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | "Current key" was undefined without a valid chain. | Fixed: such an owner is a key change, never an earlier key for the exception. |
| E49-L5 | 42 | Low | 4.6; 6.8 | Whether a follow not yet stored raises the mark was unstated. | Fixed: it does. |
| E49-L6 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | An unlisted owner as a statement signer was unrecorded, and could sign in a stranger. | Moot: the exception no longer reads the server's list. |
| M49-4 | 42 | Low | ADR | The ADR's claim that settling first meant members never follow both was false for an app working out the owners afresh. | Moot with M49-1; the ADR rewritten. |
| M49-5 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | Same as E49-F3. | Fixed with E49-F3. |
| M49-6 | 42 | Low | 4.6 | The re-wrap carried a doubted key's content to members unannounced. | Moot with S49-8. |

**Moot after this revision.** Round 47's server rule and its finding M47-1's fix, E48-F3 and M48-5 (the added-at version), M48-1's fix (the check before rotating, now advice), M48-4's re-wrap and E48-F1's lowest-key rule, replaced as above.

**Outcome.** Round 49 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed or moot. Round 50 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 50: identity key rotation, narrowed, fourth review

Round 50 reviewed round 49's revision (bf1adaa), with fresh reviewers given the narrowed text, the log of rounds 38 to 49 and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief didn't run.** Its Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent was refused by its provider's content filter partway through, and again on the one retry, worded more plainly still, as a consistency check of the ownership rules. So round 50, like round 48, had two of the three briefs, and can't meet the stopping rule whatever it found. The two found no Critical finding, 4 High, 6 Medium, 8 Low and no Info (18 in all). Both found the narrowing itself consistent, and round 49's fixes holding as far as they claimed. The Highs were in what round 49 added: a decline that left owners reached through the declined key, the removal's replacing envelopes going where the server said, "remove instead" from a pending re-add letting the unvouched key choose recipients, and the exception firing on an empty set of owners. The fixes again take mechanisms out: the replacing envelopes are gone, the exception is worked out each time and never recorded, and the raised mark is a check, not a stored mark. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M50-1 | 43 | High | 4.6 **A space whose only accepted owner is the person**; 8.4 | Declining the followed key left the owners accepted through its statements, so a member kept writing under versions a stranger sealed. | Fixed: what the exception accepts, and what step 3 reaches through it, is worked out each time and never recorded, so a decline takes it all back. |
| M50-2 | 43 | High | 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead** | The removal's replacing envelopes went to whatever members the server named, unverified, with no class bound. | Fixed by removing them: what the removed owner's new key wrapped stays closed to members, the dialog says so, and an owner may save it again. Round 48's M48-4 is accepted as that availability cost. |
| M50-3 | 43 | High | 4.6; 8.6 | "Remove instead" from a pending re-add kept the unvouched key in `owners`, so its envelopes chose `E`. | Fixed: the removal moves the owner's later keys from `owners` to `openOnly`. |
| E50-F1 | 43 | High | 4.6 **A space whose only accepted owner is the person** | An empty set of accepted owners passed the test, so a member who had declined a space's only owner accepted any contact who rotated next as its owner. | Fixed: the test needs at least one accepted key, and a key in `removed` still counts, so it blocks. |
| M50-4 | 43 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | Two listed owners a co-owner accepts at no current key can't be removed one at a time, as each removal must seal to the other. | Accepted: an availability cost of the narrowing, added to the ADR's; adding either again, after checking with them, is the way out. |
| M50-5 | 43 | Medium | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again** | The pending test caught the person's own app, and every creator, whom no statement names. | Fixed: it applies only to another owner, recorded at a later key than one held for them before, and counts statements this person signed at any key. |
| M50-6 | 43 | Medium | 4.6 | The replacing envelopes stranded members who accepted only the new key. | Moot with M50-2. |
| M50-7 | 43 | Medium | 4.6 **Shares left at an earlier key** | After a rotated owner removed themself, what they saved under the new key was lost to everyone else. | Accepted, as M50-2's cost, and stated in §4.6. |
| E50-F2 | 43 | Medium | 4.6; 6.8 | The raised mark had two definitions, and the stored one undid S49-7's fix. | Fixed: it is a check worked out each time from the envelopes held, raising no mark. A server that stops serving the envelope brings the app back below it (A2). |
| E50-F3 | 43 | Medium | 4.6; 8.2; 8.6 | `openOnly` and `owners` could hold the same key, with no rule, and `openOnly` had no parse rules. | Fixed: a key in both counts as `openOnly`, writers keep them apart, "add a co-owner" moves a key back, and §8.2 gives `openOnly`'s format. |
| M50-8 | 43 | Low | 4.6 | Same as E50-F1. | Fixed with E50-F1. |
| M50-9 | 43 | Low | 4.6; 7.6 | The "owner by its own state" gate missed co-owners promoted from members. | Fixed: an app that becomes an owner records its own ID, and the gate is that entry. Same as E50-L1. |
| E50-L1 | 43 | Low | 4.6; 6.5 step 1 | The gate depended on a name record the server can withhold. | Fixed with M50-9. |
| E50-L2 | 43 | Low | 4.6 | The pending test contradicted its own exit, and an adder who rotated made it pending again. | Fixed with M50-5. |
| E50-L3 | 43 | Low | 8.6 | The re-add's merge didn't carry the chain or skip `removed`, and wasn't a confirmed write. | Fixed: "add a co-owner" carries the chain, skips `removed`, moves keys out of `openOnly`, and is confirmed. |
| E50-L4 | 43 | Low | 6.4; 6.5 | A signer's key wasn't bound to its key version now that `owners` can hold several. | Fixed: §4.6 takes the chain's key at `signerKeyVersion` or `sealerKeyVersion`. |
| E50-L5 | 43 | Low | 4.6 | The replacing envelopes didn't name the recipient's key version. | Moot with M50-2. |
| E50-L6 | 43 | Low | 4.6; A15 | Filtering `removed` keys out of the test widened it past A15. | Fixed with E50-F1. |

**Outcome.** Round 50 found Highs, and ran without the server brief, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed, moot or accepted as availability. Round 51 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 51: identity key rotation, narrowed, fifth review

Round 51 reviewed round 50's revision (05c486b) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the narrowed text, the log of rounds 38 to 50 and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, worded as a review of whether the specification's outcomes match its own goals and accepted limits, which ran the first time. It found no Critical finding, 6 High, 8 Medium, 8 Low and no Info (22 in all). Every reviewer found the narrowing itself consistent, and nothing left of the removed review, re-signing or removal route. All six Highs were in the exception where the person is a member's only accepted owner and in the re-add and removal rules around it: not recording what the exception reached let a second rotation fire it again past a co-owner; a decline didn't reach the member's agents; co-owners from before this text had no own entry for the exception's gate; and a removal merged across devices kept a key a concurrent re-add recorded. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S51-1 | 44 | High | 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**; 8.6 | A removal moved only the keys it carried, so a key another device's retried re-add recorded meanwhile stayed in `owners` and chose `E`. | Fixed: the change names the latest key held before any re-add, and the merge moves every key above it. |
| S51-2 | 44 | High | 4.6 **A space whose only accepted owner is the person**; A15 | The exception recorded nothing, so after a member reached a co-owner through it, a second rotation of the person, by someone holding the second key, fired it again and dropped the co-owner. | Fixed: what the exception accepts, the keys between and the owners reached, is recorded with `via`, so a recorded co-owner stops it firing again; a decline drops the `via` entries. Same as M51-1. |
| E51-F1 | 44 | High | 4.6 | Co-owners from before this text have no own entry, so the exception could fire in an owner's app. | Fixed: the gate also excludes an app the server lists, or a served statement names, as an owner, and the release before rotation records the own entry where the app confirmed a co-owner dialog or accepted a statement naming it. |
| E51-F2 | 44 | High | 4.6; 8.4; 9.4 | Declining a key didn't reach the member's agents, which kept writing under versions it sealed. | Fixed: a decline ends the person's agents' picks in every share whose current version a declined key sealed, until the person picks it again. Same as M51-2. |
| M51-1 | 44 | High | 4.6; A15 | Same as S51-2. | Fixed with S51-2. |
| M51-2 | 44 | High | 8.4; 4.6; 9.4 | Same as E51-F2. | Fixed with E51-F2. |
| S51-3 | 44 | Medium | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again** | A finished re-add stayed pending on a key between. | Fixed: the test reads only the latest key held for that owner. |
| S51-4 | 44 | Medium | 4.6; 6.5 | After a second rotation, members lost history the key between sealed. | Fixed with S51-2: the keys between are recorded. Same as M51-4. |
| S51-5 | 44 | Medium | 4.6 | First sight skipped the only envelope that gave a rotated member their history. | Fixed: an envelope to the app's own retired key isn't skipped. |
| E51-F3 | 44 | Medium | 4.6; 7.6 | A former owner's own entry stayed for good, so the exception never fired for it. | Fixed: removing oneself adds one's ID to `removedOwners`, and such an entry doesn't make the app an owner. |
| E51-F4 | 44 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners** | "A key hash the app holds" had two readings for the exception's acceptances. | Fixed: one the app accepts now, from its record; the exception's are now recorded. |
| E51-F5 | 44 | Medium | 6.5 step 2 | Whether an entry at a removed key counts as having accepted an owner was unsettled. | Fixed: every entry counts. |
| M51-3 | 44 | Medium | 4.6 | Without round 48's refusal, a rotated owner could add themself and end a pending re-add. | Fixed: the server again refuses a co-owner add naming the caller, and the pending test ignores the named owner's own statements. |
| M51-4 | 44 | Medium | 4.6; 6.5 | Same as S51-4. | Fixed with S51-4. |
| S51-6 | 44 | Low | 6.8; 4.6 | §6.8 applied the pause to rotating from the version below. | Fixed: it may rotate from it. |
| E51-L1 | 44 | Low | 8.2; 8.6 | An older app's re-add left a key in both `owners` and `openOnly`, which silently didn't take. | Fixed in wording: such a re-add doesn't take and the app asks again; a current app takes the key out of `openOnly`. |
| E51-L2 | 44 | Low | 4.6 | The pending test's signer and its reading of `openOnly` were undefined. | Fixed: an owner the app accepts at their current key, not on `removedOwners`, and `owners` only. |
| E51-L3 | 44 | Low | 4.6 | Whether a declined key, or a removed owner's, pauses a share was undefined. | Fixed: neither does. |
| E51-L4 | 44 | Low | 4.6 | Which key the exception takes while a newer follow is pending was ambiguous. | Fixed: the latest stored, an earlier key until the newer one is stored. |
| M51-5 | 44 | Low | ADR decision 6 | The ADR said agents take the exception from the record, the protocol that nothing was recorded. | Fixed: it is recorded now, and both say so. |
| M51-6 | 44 | Low | 4.6; 7.6 | A member promoted through a statement only the exception made acceptable recorded its own entry before its dialog. | Fixed: it records its own entry after any dialog. |
| M51-7 | 44 | Low | 4.6; 6.8 | The pause didn't cover sealing to agents. | Fixed. |

**Outcome.** Round 51 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed. Round 52, the sixth after the narrowing and the last this run allows, repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 52: identity key rotation, narrowed, sixth review

Round 52 reviewed round 51's revision (df44918) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the narrowed text, the log of rounds 38 to 51 and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, whose first run was refused by its provider's content filter and whose one retry, worded as a plain consistency check, ran. It found no Critical finding, 9 High, 8 Medium, 8 Low and no Info (25 in all). Every reviewer again found the narrowing itself consistent. Every High was in the exception where the person is a member's only accepted owner, or in the re-add and removal records around it: `via` lost in a contacts merge or on owners reached in a later run, a stale sole-owner acceptance merged over a co-owner another device recorded, agents that a decline doesn't stop, and a removal that can't tell which keys a re-add wrote. **These findings are open, not fixed**: this is the sixth round after the narrowing, the last this run allows, and the outcome column gives the fix each points to, for the project owner's decision below.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The "accept an owner" change carries `via`, is a no-op when its `via` key is in `removed`, and `via` passes to owners reached through a `via` entry. Same as E52-F1, E52-F2 and M52-2. |

**Outcome.** Round 52 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule, and its findings are open.

**After rounds 47 to 52.** Six rounds ran on the narrowed scope (2026-11-24), with 5, 2, 4, 4, 6 and 9 High findings, and none Critical; rounds 48 and 50 ran without the server brief, which its provider refused twice each. In every round, all three reviewers found the narrowing itself sound: nothing was left of the owner re-signing, the owners review or the removal route, "a rotation doesn't carry ownership" held across opening, writing, sealing, live sessions and the person's own app, and the statement, the chain, the keyring and the contacts cutoff held throughout. After round 47, every High was in one place: the exception that lets a member follow a rotated owner who is the only owner it accepts, and the re-add and removal records that rounds 47 to 51 built around it to keep it from firing where a co-owner could still vouch. Rounds 49 to 51 each replaced or removed part of that machinery (a server rule, a check before rotating, re-wrapping, replacing envelopes), and each round's new Highs came from what replaced it. The rounds stop here unmet, for the project owner to decide whether to keep that exception, or to drop it and accept that members of a space the person alone owns open nothing new there after the person rotates.

## Round 53: identity key rotation, narrowed, seventh review

On 2026-11-24 the project owner answered ADR 0018's question 10: drop the rule that a member's app follows a rotated owner who is the only owner it accepts. A rotated owner is an owner again only when a co-owner adds them, in every space. The cost he accepts: in a space the person owns alone, members open nothing new after the person rotates, and the person shares again from a new space. The revision (b5dd73a) removes the rule, `via` and the gates built for it; the rotation dialog now warns about spaces the person owns alone before they confirm, offers to add a co-owner first, and after the rotation offers to share each such space again from a new one (protocol.md §4.6 **Before it sends the rotation**, **A space the person owns alone**). The re-add and removal rules stay, as each is needed in a space with co-owners too; the ADR says which, and why.

**Moot after dropping the sole-owner rule (2026-11-24).** These findings were about the exception where the person is a member's only accepted owner, its gate, or the records that held it (`via`, own entries as its gate), so they no longer apply: S47-4, M47-5 and E47-F3 (two owners who both rotate now leave members waiting, an availability cost the ADR already names), S47-6, M47-6, E47-L2, E47-F2, the acceptance half of E47-L3, M48-2, M48-7, E48-L2, S49-1, E49-F1, E49-F2, E49-F7, S49-4, E49-L4, E49-L6, M49-4, M50-1, E50-F1, M50-8, E50-L6, M50-9, E50-L1, S51-2, M51-1, S51-4 and M51-4 (as exception findings; the keys between are still recorded on a re-add), E51-F1, E51-F3, E51-F4, E51-L4, M51-5, M51-6, S52-1, E52-F1, E52-F2, M52-2, S52-2, the part of S52-3, E52-F3 and M52-1 about owners reached through a declined key, the gate's part of S52-6, E52-F5 and M52-3, E52-F7, E52-L1 and E52-L3.

**Round 52's other findings**, open until now, are settled in the same revision: S52-3, E52-F3 and M52-1, a decline now ends the person's agents' picks and approved groups in every share where it holds a version a declined key sealed, and picking it again seals nothing at or below it (§8.4); E52-F4 and M52-5, a removal of a rotated owner moves every key above the earliest one the record holds for them to `openOnly`, which every device works out the same, at the cost that a later key a co-owner vouched for counts as the new key does (§4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**, §8.6); S52-4, accepted as an availability cost of the kind the ADR names for two owners who both rotate; S52-5 and E52-L4, a statement the adder signed at a key now earlier ends the pending state; S52-6's other half, E52-F6, the person's own rotation clears its own `removedOwners` entry only where the server lists it as an owner, and becoming an owner again clears it; M52-6, the release before rotation records the own entry only where the app accepted itself in step 1 or confirmed a co-owner dialog; S52-7, **Shares left at an earlier key** moves that owner's keys to `openOnly` before it rotates; S52-8, §6.8 names §4.6's conditions; E52-L2, a key in `openOnly` counts as not held for `E`; M52-4, "remove instead" says a re-add another device sent may land first.

Round 53 reviewed that revision (b5dd73a) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the log of rounds 38 to 52, the dropped rule and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, worded as a consistency check of the specification's own rules against its §2.3 guarantees, which its provider ran the first time. It found no Critical finding, 6 High, 7 Medium, 11 Low and no Info (24 in all; 4 distinct Highs). Every reviewer found the exception fully gone, with nothing left of `via`, the only-owner test or a member accepting the new key without a co-owner. The Highs were in what this revision added and one step past round 52's fixes: sharing again from a new space shared the whole copy, owner region included, with anyone the server listed; a decline still left the person's agents writing under the declined key's version; and a pending re-add's own key could vouch for the owner that ended it. Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M53-1 | 53 | High | 4.6 **A space the person owns alone**; 10; 6.1 | Sharing again copied the space into the new space's owner region and shared the whole space with everyone ticked, so a member of one share got every other share, the owner region and drafts. | Fixed: each old share's copy becomes a share of its own, with that share's people at their roles; nothing in the owner region is shared. Same as S53-2. |
| M53-2 | 53 | High | 4.6 **A space the person owns alone**; 7.5 | The share-again list was every member the server listed, unchecked, so a removed member the server listed again could be ticked in. | Fixed with M53-1: only the members finishing sealed each share's new version to, never anyone else the server lists. |
| M53-3 | 53 | High | 8.4; 8.6; 9.4; 6.8 | A decline took agents' groups out of the record only; the server kept their grants, and the agent, which sees only its person's envelope, kept writing under the declined key's version, or did so again after a new pick. | Fixed: a decline records a floor per share in `wikistow.declined`, kept for good; every device and agent reads it, and none writes under, moves into or seals to an agent a version at or below it. Same as E53-F1 and S53-4. |
| S53-1 | 53 | High | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again**; 6.5 step 3 | The key a pending re-add recorded counted as an accepted owner on other devices, so a statement it signed accepted another owner, whose statement back ended the pending state, and owner-region saves sealed to them. | Fixed: until its statement is served, a re-add's key counts for opening only on every device, and signs in nobody. |
| E53-F1 | 53 | High | 8.4; 9.4 | Same as M53-3, groups and picks again. | Fixed with M53-3. |
| S53-2 | 53 | High | 4.6; 10; 6.1 | Same as M53-1. | Fixed with M53-1. |
| E53-F2 | 53 | Medium | 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**; 8.6 | "Above the earliest key" needs key versions the record doesn't hold, so devices could disagree. | Fixed: the removal moves every key but the earliest, which the change names. |
| E53-F3 | 53 | Medium | 4.6 **Owners**; 6.5 step 4; 8.6 | Becoming an owner again had no merge row, and an own ID on `removedOwners` blocked the statement chain from ever bringing the app back. | Fixed: a "become an owner" change, confirmed, which only the co-owner dialog writes; the dialog runs for an app whose own ID is listed, and step 4 says the own ID blocks step 1 too. Same as S53-5 and M53-7. |
| S53-3 | 53 | Medium | 4.6 **Shares left at an earlier key**; 8.6 | A removal, or rotating a share a rotated owner left, recorded in `openOnly` only keys the app had recorded, so a key it never recorded still couldn't be opened. | Fixed: the change also adds the keys of their chain the app verified, to `openOnly`. |
| S53-4 | 53 | Medium | 8.4; 9.4 | Same as M53-3. | Fixed with M53-3. |
| S53-5 | 53 | Medium | 4.6; 6.5 | Same as E53-F3. | Fixed with E53-F3. |
| S53-6 | 53 | Medium | 4.6 | The warning and the offer to share again used different tests for owning a space alone, so a space a co-owner had left got the warning but not the offer. | Fixed: one test, the server listing no other owner or the record accepting none not on `removedOwners`, named in **Before it sends the rotation** and used everywhere. Same as M53-4 and E53-L5. |
| S53-7 | 53 | Medium | 4.6 **When someone else rotated** | A reload before a definite answer left the device with only the statement's hash, unable to send the same request again. | Fixed: the device keeps the whole request, sealed for itself, until a definite answer; a browser that keeps nothing may rotate afresh, the first to land winning (A15). |
| M53-4 | 53 | Low | 4.6 | Same as S53-6. | Fixed with S53-6. |
| M53-5 | 53 | Low | 4.6 **Before it sends the rotation** | "Add a co-owner first, so members carry on" isn't true for members who meet the new key before the co-owner. | Fixed: the dialog says it helps only those. |
| M53-6 | 53 | Low | 4.6 **Shares left at an earlier key** | That rotation's `openOnly` move had no change of its own, and a later co-owner's add of that owner isn't taken. | Fixed in part: it writes "remove a rotated owner", which adds them to `removedOwners`; not taking another owner's add of someone this person removed is today's rule (§7.6), and stays. |
| M53-7 | 53 | Low | 4.6; 6.5 step 4 | Same as E53-F3. | Fixed with E53-F3. |
| M53-8 | 53 | Low | 4.6; 10 | The copy re-signs edits the old key made before the rotation as the person's current writing. | Fixed in wording: the dialog says the copy carries the space as it stood, those edits included. |
| E53-L1 | 53 | Low | 4.6; 8.6 | **Shares left at an earlier key** didn't say which change it writes. | Fixed with M53-6. |
| E53-L2 | 53 | Low | 8.2; 4.6 | An `openOnly` key in `removed` contradicted "nothing a removed key signed is accepted". | Fixed: §8.2 names `openOnly` as the one exception, for opening and sealing on. |
| E53-L3 | 53 | Low | 8.6 | "Add a co-owner" took the ID off `removedOwners` even when its key was in `removed` and nothing was added. | Fixed: the whole change is then a no-op. |

The rest is accepted: the false pending state only offers to send the re-add again or remove the owner, an availability cost. |
| E53-L5 | 53 | Low | 4.6 | Same as S53-6. | Fixed with S53-6. |
| E53-L6 | 53 | Low | 4.6 | **A version it can't use yet** read "verifies under a later key", inviting a try of each key; and clearing the own ID from `removedOwners` where the server lists the person let a server undo a self-removal. | Fixed: the version is judged by its `sealerKeyVersion`; the rotation no longer clears `removedOwners` at all, and an own ID there comes off only through the co-owner dialog (A15 already names entries the old key wrote). This replaces S52-6's fix and E47-L1's. |

**Outcome.** Round 53 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed, or fixed in part with the rest accepted as availability. Round 54 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 54: identity key rotation, narrowed, eighth review

Round 54 reviewed round 53's revision (27bbd42) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the log of rounds 38 to 53, the dropped rule and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief's Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent was refused** by its provider's content filter partway through, worded as in round 53, which it had run; its last message said it was checking devices with only part of a share's history. As the run's instructions say, the server brief was then given once, unchanged, to a Claude subagent, so this round's server findings come from a second Claude reviewer, not a second model. The round found no Critical finding, 6 High, 4 Medium, 15 Low and no Info (25 in all; 4 distinct Highs). Every reviewer found the exception fully gone and the "owns alone" test consistent. The Highs were again in what the last revision added: sharing again from a new space took its members from finishing, not the current version, and its shares' roots from the server's unsigned tree; floors were worked out once, from one device's view; and an own ID on `removedOwners` the old key wrote, which the rotation no longer clears, blocked finishing for good in a space the person owns alone. Sharing again carried a choice the person makes better in the ordinary share dialog, so it is now a copy shared with nobody.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S54-1 | 54 | High | 4.6 **A space the person owns alone**; 6.1; 10 | Sharing again took each share's root and contents from the server's unsigned share row and tree, so a server could put owner-region pages or another share under a root and have them sealed to its members. | Fixed by removing the carry-over: the new space is a copy shared with nobody, and the person shares it with the usual dialog and its preview (§6.7). |
| S54-2 | 54 | High | 4.6 **A space the person owns alone**; 7.5 | Sharing again used finishing's members and roles, so someone the person removed or demoted since got the share back, drafts included. | Fixed with S54-1. Same as M54-1. |
| S54-3 | 54 | High | 8.4; 8.6; 9.4 | Floors were worked out once, by the declining device, so a share version withheld from it, or one another device sealed to an agent, never got a floor. | Fixed: a floor is a standing rule; the declining device fetches every share's envelopes first, and every device adds the floor, in a confirmed write, at each unlock and whenever it meets such an envelope, before sealing to an agent there; agents read the record before each write. Same as E54-F1. |
| M54-1 | 54 | High | 4.6 | Same as S54-2. | Fixed with S54-1. |
| M54-2 | 54 | High | 4.6; 6.5 step 4 | Since the rotation no longer clears `removedOwners`, an own ID the old key wrote there left a space the person owns alone with no way back: finishing couldn't rotate it, members kept saving under keys the old key holds, and the dialog said otherwise. | Fixed: the rotation dialog names spaces whose first name record the person signed and whose `removedOwners` holds their own ID, and the person may confirm "become an owner" there before rotating, the one way back without a co-owner. |
| E54-F1 | 54 | High | 8.4; 9.4 | Same as S54-3. | Fixed with S54-3. |
| S54-4 | 54 | Medium | 4.6 **Shares left at an earlier key** | A server that stopped listing a rotated owner led the co-owner's app to write a lasting removal when they pressed **Rotate keys**. | Fixed in wording: the dialog says it removes them as an owner, as **Remove owner** does, and that the server may only be hiding them. |
| M54-3 | 54 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 | A floor was permanent, so one the old key wrote, at a huge version, stopped the person writing in a share for good. | Fixed: a floor above the share's current key version counts for nothing, and a rotation above a floor ends its effect. |

## Round 55: identity key rotation, narrowed, ninth review

Round 55 reviewed round 54's revision (2962fbf) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the log of rounds 38 to 54, the dropped rule and the list of what earlier rounds accepted: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, worded as a consistency check against §2.3 with the accepted list headed "settled", which its provider ran the first time. It found no Critical finding, 1 High, 5 Medium, 12 Low and no Info (18 in all). Every reviewer found the exception gone and the copy into a new space, shared with nobody, consistent with §6.7 and §10; the server brief found no failure of a §2.3 guarantee beyond the accepted risks. The one High was in round 54's way back for an own ID on `removedOwners`, offered only where the person created the space. The Mediums were all in how floors merge and when they are added, and in a live publication blocking the copy.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M55-1 | 55 | High | 4.6 **Before it sends the rotation**; **Owners**; 6.5 step 4 | "Become an owner" in the rotation dialog was offered only where the person signed the first name record, so in a space they were given and own alone an own ID the old key wrote kept finishing from rotating, and members kept saving under keys the old key holds, while the dialog said they would stop. | Fixed: it is offered in every space the server lists the person as owning, and the dialog says that until they confirm, finishing can't rotate it and its members keep saving under keys the old key holds. |
| S55-1 | 55 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 | Floors kept one value per share, the higher, so a floor above the current version, which counts for nothing, hid a lower floor a decline needed. | Fixed: `floors` is a set by share and version, and the highest at or below the reader's current version counts. Same as E55-F1. |
| S55-2 | 55 | Medium | 4.6 **A space the person owns alone**; 10 | A live publication from another space made §10 refuse the whole copy. | Fixed: live publications are left out of the copy and listed, for the person to publish again. |
| E55-F1 | 55 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 | Same as S55-1, with an old-key floor planted before a later decline. | Fixed with S55-1. |
| E55-F2 | 55 | Medium | 8.6 | Adding a floor had no merge row and wasn't on the confirmed list, so a re-applied write could drop another device's decline entry. | Fixed: an "add a floor" row that touches only `floors`, confirmed, and listed in **Confirmed writes** and **When to write**, with "become an owner" and "remove a rotated owner". Same as S55-3. |
| E55-F3 | 55 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 | A decline merged onto a newer record put more keys in `removed` than its floors covered, and nothing added the missing floors. | Fixed: the record's `removed` or `wikistow.declined` growing is a trigger, and a merged decline isn't done until its floors are added. |
| S55-3 | 55 | Low | 8.6 | Same as E55-F2. | Fixed with E55-F2. |
| S55-4 | 55 | Low | 8.4; 6.4 | Whether an envelope first met after its sealer was declined is verified for a floor was undefined. | Fixed: for a floor, its signature is verified under the sealer's key on the chain, and one that can't be resolved or verified gets a floor, failing closed. Same as E55-L2. |
| S55-5 | 55 | Low | 8.5; 8.6 | Two devices could restore different pins for one decline. | Fixed: every device restores the pin from the record's version history before the declined follow; a kept pin is used only when it is that key. |
| S55-6 | 55 | Low | 4.6 finishing; 7.5 | Finishing had no rule for when a share is done after a lost reply. | Fixed: done once the app verifies a current version above the one it rotated from, sealed with its own current key or by an owner it writes with. |
| S55-7 | 55 | Low | 4.6; 7.5 step 1 | §7.5 named the owner's removal as its only exception, against §4.6. | Fixed: §7.5 names both. |
| M55-2 | 55 | Low | 4.6 | "Become an owner" in the rotation dialog can undo a self-removal the server ignored. | Accepted: it needs the person's confirmation, the dialog says to confirm only if they didn't leave, and the cost is the members' pause until a co-owner acts. |
| M55-3 | 55 | Low | 4.6 | The "owns alone" test didn't require the person to be a listed owner. | Fixed. |
| M55-4 | 55 | Low | 7.6 | §7.6 still said adding someone again is the only way off `removedOwners`. | Fixed. |
| E55-L1 | 55 | Low | 4.6 | The "owns alone" test left open entries at removed keys, `openOnly`, pending follows and what shows nobody added them again. | Fixed: each is defined, and the test only chooses what the app says and offers. |
| E55-L2 | 55 | Low | 8.4 | Same as S55-4. | Fixed with S55-4. |
| E55-L3 | 55 | Low | 8.4; 6.8 | "The share's current key version, as the app verified it" had no meaning for an agent. | Fixed: for an agent, the highest version its person sealed to it. |
| E55-L4 | 55 | Low | 4.6; route | The statement's `contacts` could predate the dialog's own writes, or a floor write landing meanwhile. | Fixed: it is read after the dialog's writes; a write landing meanwhile makes the route refuse, and the app builds a new statement. |

**Outcome.** Round 55 found a High, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M55-2, accepted as an availability cost the person confirms. Round 56 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 56: identity key rotation, narrowed, tenth review

Round 56 reviewed round 55's revision (08718e0) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the log of rounds 38 to 55, the dropped rule and the list of what earlier rounds settled: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief's Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent was refused** by its provider's content filter at its start, worded as in round 55, which it had run. The server brief was then given once, unchanged, to a Claude subagent, as in round 54. The round found no Critical finding, 1 High, 4 Medium, 16 Low and no Info (21 in all). Every reviewer found the exception gone and round 55's fixes holding, and the member brief found no way for a removed member, an agent or the old key's holder to gain owner authority or new keys beyond the accepted risks. The one High was in round 55's finishing rule: a share counted as done once anyone the app writes with had rotated it, so a co-owner's rotation that landed first dropped the person's removals without a word.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S56-1 | 56 | High | 4.6 finishing; 7.5 | A share counted as done once a co-owner's rotation landed above the version the person rotated from, so a member the person left out, whom the co-owner kept, read everything saved after, with finishing shown complete. | Fixed: a share is done only once the app holds its own envelope, sealed with its current key, of a version above every version its retired keys sealed; when someone else's rotation landed first, it plans again and shows the dialog again. Same as E56-L5 and M56-1. |
| S56-2 | 56 | Medium | 8.6 decline row; 8.5 | The decline's restored pin was read from version history the server doesn't keep: replaced versions keep only their signed header. | Fixed: a device declines from the pin it kept from before the follow its notice reports, and the change carries that key; a device that keeps none offers only to compare fingerprints. Same as E56-L1. |
| S56-3 | 56 | Medium | 4.6 **A space the person owns alone**; 10 | Leaving live publications out of the copy needs a copy mode §10 doesn't have. | Fixed: the app lists those pages and offers to detach them first, which the person may do as the space's owner (§13); the copy runs once none is attached. |
| E56-F1 | 56 | Medium | 8.4 | Which envelopes get a floor was ambiguous, and an unresolvable envelope from an ordinary co-owner could get a permanent one. | Fixed: only envelopes sealed by an ID with a `wikistow.declined` entry are tested, and each floor names that ID. |
| E56-F2 | 56 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 | Floors outlived the distrust behind them: confirming the key again left the person and their agents unable to write there. | Fixed: a write that drops an ID's declined entries (a pin, a confirmed key, a follow) drops its floors too, and "add a floor" skips an ID with no entry. |
| S56-4 | 56 | Low | 8.4 | The reader's current version could be raised by envelopes that count for opening only, bringing back M54-3 while the server serves them. | Fixed: for a device, the highest version that counts for writing; for an agent, the highest its person sealed to it with their current key. |
| S56-5 | 56 | Low | 4.6 | "Become an owner" could make an own owner entry where the person was never an owner. | Fixed: offered only where the record holds an own entry at a key the keyring holds, or the person created the space. |
| S56-6 | 56 | Low | 4.6 | A space whose own `removedOwners` entry the person didn't confirm was both closed to owner actions and offered the copy. | Fixed: the "owns alone" test needs the app to be an owner by its own record. Same as M56-4. |
| E56-L1 | 56 | Low | 8.6 | Which follow a decline undoes was circular. | Fixed with S56-2. |
| E56-L2 | 56 | Low | 4.6 | The "owns alone" test's third branch didn't leave out `removedOwners`, and a pending re-add's key read as added again. | Fixed. |
| E56-L3 | 56 | Low | 4.6 **The person's own app** | A sentence still said only a co-owner's add brings the own ID back. | Fixed. |
| E56-L4 | 56 | Low | 8.4; 8.6 | `floors` parsing, an absent extension and the "grows" trigger were loose. | Fixed. |
| E56-L5 | 56 | Low | 4.6 finishing | Same as S56-1. | Fixed with S56-1. |
| E56-L6 | 56 | Low | 8.5 | A known-format `wikistow.declined` that doesn't parse has no way out but an update. | Accepted: it fails closed, as an unknown format does, and a fresh start keeps the extension. |
| E56-L7 | 56 | Low | ADR | The ADR's summary of "owns alone" was incomplete. | Fixed. |
| M56-1 | 56 | Low | 4.6 finishing | Same as S56-1, a co-owner's version sealed to the old key. | Fixed with S56-1. |
| M56-2 | 56 | Low | 6.5 step 2; 4.6 | In a space the person created, an own ID the old key put on `removedOwners` could lead to first sight's co-owner dialog naming a stranger. | Fixed: the person's app never runs that dialog in a space it created; only "become an owner" applies there. |
| M56-3 | 56 | Low | 4.6 | The warning didn't say that leaving "become an owner" unconfirmed is for good in a space the person owns alone. | Fixed. |
| M56-4 | 56 | Low | 4.6; 10 | Same as S56-6. | Fixed with S56-6. |
| M56-5 | 56 | Low | 4.6 | The statement's `contacts.version` tells anyone who may look the person up how many versions their record has had. | Accepted as metadata of the kind A7 accepts, and stated. |
| M56-6 | 56 | Low | ADR | Decision 2 still said "share again", and didn't mention "become an owner" or detaching. | Fixed. |
