## Round 57: identity key rotation, narrowed, eleventh review

Round 57 reviewed round 56's revision (53efa1f) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the log of rounds 38 to 56 and the list of what earlier rounds settled: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents, the server brief as a Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent, its brief shortened to round 56's changes and the settled list, which its provider ran the first time. It found no Critical finding, 6 High, 5 Medium, 15 Low and no Info (26 in all). Every reviewer found the exception gone. Five of the six Highs were in decline floors, the server brief's four and the member brief's one: a role change's replacement envelope hid that a declined owner made a version, live sessions ignored floors, a floor confirmed away while the key that sealed it stayed declined, and an agent given a version just before a decline. The sixth was finishing taking another device's rotation for its own, and a pending re-add's key counting once an earlier key's statement ended the prompts. The floors are rebuilt on one fact: a key the record accepted as an owner held every key of the space, so a decline floors every share there; and agents are now told who sealed each version they hold.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S57-1 | 57 | High | 4.6 finishing; 5.10 | Finishing counted a share done on any envelope of the person's current key, so another device's rotation that kept a member this device left out passed as its own. | Fixed: done only when the fetched version opens to the folder keys this device made, as §5.10 recognises a lost answer; anything else plans again. |
| S57-2 | 57 | High | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again** | A statement signed with an earlier key ended the pending state, and with it the re-add key's opening-only status, so a retired key could make it an owner. | Fixed: an earlier key's statement only stops the prompts; the key counts beyond opening only once a statement naming it is served at a current key. |
| S57-3 | 57 | High | 8.4; 9.4 | A version another device sealed to an agent just before the decline had no floor yet when the agent read the decline, so it wrote under it. | Fixed: the record names who sealed each version given to an agent (`sealedFrom`), and the agent writes under none whose sealer key is in `removed`. |
| S57-4 | 57 | High | 8.4; 6.4; 7.4 | A role change replaced the person's envelope of a version a declined owner made, so no floor was found for it. | Fixed: where the record accepted the declined key as an owner of a space, every share there gets a floor at the highest version the person holds. |
| S57-5 | 57 | High | 8.4; 5.10 | Floors stopped saves but not joining or staying in a live session. | Fixed: they stop live sessions too. |
| M57-1 | 57 | High | 8.4; 8.6 | Confirming any key of the ID dropped its floors, though the key that sealed the floored version stayed in `removed`, so the agent wrote there again. | Fixed: each floor names its key, and goes only when that key leaves `removed`. |
| S57-6 | 57 | Medium | 8.6 | A delayed floor merged into a different, later decline. | Fixed with M57-1: "add a floor" skips a floor whose key isn't in `removed`. |

| S57-8 | 57 | Medium | 4.6; 10; 13 | Detaching a live publication before the copy can need an owner-region write that an unaccepted co-owner blocks. | Accepted: availability, in a space with a rotated co-owner nobody added again; the source's publisher can end the publication, and the rest of the space stays readable. |
| E57-F1 | 57 | Medium | 8.5; 8.6 | One kept pin per ID, overwritten at each move, made the same decline restore different keys on different devices. | Fixed: the kept pin is the one from before the earliest follow not yet confirmed or declined. |

| E57-L5 | 57 | Low | 8.6 | "Holds the change" wasn't defined for partial no-ops. | Fixed for "add a floor"; for "add a co-owner" with a key in `removed` it is a no-op, which doesn't hold, and the app asks again. |

**Outcome.** Round 57 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but S57-8 and E57-L3, accepted as availability. Round 58, the sixth after the drop and the last this run allows, repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 58: identity key rotation, narrowed, twelfth review

Round 58 reviewed round 57's revision (c95f063) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the log of rounds 38 to 57 and the list of what earlier rounds settled: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief's Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent was refused** by its provider's content filter partway through; its last message named a conflict it had found, that a rotation is meant to end floors while the standing rule adds one on the new version (logged here as E58-F3, which the encoding brief found too). The server brief was then given once, unchanged, to a Claude subagent. The round found no Critical finding, 4 High, 8 Medium, 17 Low and no Info (29 in all; 2 distinct Highs). Every reviewer found the exception gone, the text for spaces the person owns alone consistent, and round 57's fixes to finishing, the pending re-add, the kept pin and the co-owner dialog holding. Every High and most Mediums were in round 57's decline floors and `sealedFrom`: where the declined key was an accepted owner, an honest co-owner seals each new version to it, so `sealedFrom` (which names only who sealed the person's envelope) doesn't stop an agent given that version, and a device writes before its own floor is stored. **These findings are open, not fixed**: this is the sixth round after the drop, the last this run allows, and the outcome column gives the fix each points to, for the project owner's decision below.

**Outcome.** Round 58 found Highs, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule, and its findings are open.

**After rounds 53 to 58.** Six rounds ran after the project owner dropped the sole-owner rule (2026-11-24), with 6, 6, 1, 1, 6 and 4 High findings (4, 4, 1, 1, 6 and 2 distinct), and none Critical; rounds 54, 56 and 58 ran their server brief on a substitute Claude reviewer after Codex's provider refused it. Dropping the rule held: no reviewer found any text left of it, and members never accept the new key without a co-owner. Rounds 53 and 54 found Highs in what the drop added, the offer to share a space again, which became a copy shared with nobody, and that held from round 55 on. Rounds 55 and 56 found one High each, in the way back for an own ID on `removedOwners` and in finishing's completion rule, both fixed and holding since. From round 53 on, the rest of the Highs came from one place: a decline of a key that a co-owner's re-add had made an accepted owner, and how it stops the person's agents, which can't tell who sealed a version, from writing where that key reads. Each round's floors fix (a decline ending picks, then floors, floors as a standing rule, floors per space, `sealedFrom`) produced the next round's Highs. The rounds stop here unmet, for the project owner to decide question 11 in the ADR.

## Round 59: identity key rotation, narrowed, thirteenth review

On 2026-11-24 the project owner answered ADR 0018's question 11: close the space. Floors and `sealedFrom` are dropped. When the person declines a key their record accepted as an owner of a space, their devices and agents write nothing in that space, worked out from the contacts record alone, until the person confirms a key of that person again or leaves the space; the decline dialog says so. The cost he accepts: a member who disputes a co-owner's re-add can only read in that space until they confirm the key, or until the owners sort it out and add them afresh. The revision (a117371a) removes floors, `sealedFrom`, the "add a floor" change and their parse and merge rules, and adds protocol.md §8.4 **A closed space**: the test (an `owners` or `openOnly` entry at a key in `removed`, for an ID with a `wikistow.declined` entry), what "write nothing" covers, how agents apply it from the record they read before each write, and what opens the space again. The ADR records what was decided without him in writing it (**Closing a space**).

**Moot after closing the space (2026-11-24).** These findings were about floors or `sealedFrom`, how they were worked out, stored, merged, parsed or ended, so they no longer apply: S54-3, E54-F1, M54-3, E54-F2, S54-8, M54-4, E54-L2, S55-1, E55-F1, E55-F2, S55-3, E55-F3, S55-4, E55-L2, E55-L3, E56-F1, E56-F2, S56-4, E56-L4, S57-3, S57-4, S57-5, M57-1, S57-6, S57-7, E57-L1, E57-L2, E58-F1, M58-1, S58-1, E58-F2, E58-F3, M58-2, S58-3, E58-F4, M58-3, M58-4, S58-4, S58-5, S58-2, E58-L1, E58-L2, E58-L3, E58-L4, E58-L6 and S58-6. M53-3, E53-F1 and S53-4, a decline leaving the person's agents writing under a version the declined key holds, are now answered by the closed space instead of floors, where the declined key was an accepted owner; elsewhere no version the declined key sealed counts for writing, as before (§4.6 **Owners**, §8.4).

**Round 58's other findings**, open until now, are settled in the same revision: E58-L5 and S58-8, the person's app accepts itself through a retired key only where its current record holds an own `owners` entry at a retired key, which only a version signed before the rotation can have written (§4.6 **The person's own app**); S58-10, "become an owner" is offered only where the record holds an own entry at a keyring key or the first name record verifies under the current key (§4.6 **Before it sends the rotation**); S58-7, a server serving a device or agent the record from before a decline keeps the closure from it, named as A2 (§8.4); E58-L7 and S58-9, the ADR's wording. Accepted: E58-F5, each device declines the follows its own notice reported, so two devices can restore different earlier keys, every key either removes being one the person declined there, and the dialog shows the fingerprint it goes back to; S58-11, a server withholding a pending co-owner statement until after the rotation, availability under A2; M58-5, an agent writing under a version an owner's earlier key sealed, within A15; M58-6, "become an owner" offered at every unlock until the person answers on that device, availability.

Round 59 reviewed that revision (a117371a) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the list of what earlier rounds settled and the project owner's answers to questions 9 to 11: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief's Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent did no review**: given the whole brief inline, it answered only by asking the project owner to confirm a brief sent by another agent. The server brief was then given once, unchanged, to a Claude subagent. The round found no Critical finding, 5 High, 9 Medium, 11 Low and no Info (25 in all; 4 distinct Highs). Every reviewer found floors and `sealedFrom` fully gone, and the test the same on every device and agent from the record. The Highs were in what the test missed and what "write nothing" stopped: a key the person declined before a co-owner's re-add never reached `owners`, so the space never closed (M59-1, S59-1); `openOnly` closed an owner's space after they removed a co-owner whose rotation they declined (E59-F1); an owner couldn't remove the owner whose key closed the space, the one action that cuts that key out (S59-2); and finishing skipped a closed space without saying so (M59-2). Section numbers are protocol.md's after the fixes.

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Same as E59-F4. |
| S59-3 | 59 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 pin row | Same as M59-4. | Fixed with M59-4. |

| S59-5 | 59 | Medium | 8.4; 6.8 | Other tabs of the declining device kept writing until they next loaded the record. | Fixed: the declining tab broadcasts its higher contacts mark once the write lands, and every tab reads the mark before each write in a space, and the record when it is higher. |
| S59-6 | 59 | Medium | 8.4; ADR question 11 | Same as M59-3. | Accepted with M59-3. |

Rotating there stays barred, as the project owner's "write nothing" says; another owner can rotate. Same as S59-8. |

| M59-3 | 59 | Medium | 8.4; ADR question 11 | Where whoever held the old key rotated first, every later key of the ID is theirs, so no key the person can confirm opens the space. | Accepted: availability, the cost question 11 names, stated in the ADR; the dialog's wording is corrected, and copying out stays open. |
| M59-4 | 59 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 pin row; 8.3 | After an unpin, a first-sight pin of a key the server chooses dropped the decline's entries and opened the space. | Fixed: only a confirmed key drops an ID's `wikistow.declined` entries; a pin or a follow leaves them. |

| M59-6 | 59 | Low | 8.4; 9.3 | Nothing says when another device reads the record, and §9.3 doesn't say the CLI reads it at each command. | Fixed: another device learns of the closure when it next reads the record, as of any change made elsewhere; §9.3 says the CLI reads it before each command, and `wikistow mcp` before each write. |

**Outcome.** Round 59 found Highs, and its server brief ran on a substitute reviewer, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M59-3 (with S59-6) and part of M59-5, accepted as availability. Round 60 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 60: identity key rotation, narrowed, fourteenth review

Round 60 reviewed round 59's revision (b670308d) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the settled list and the project owner's answers to questions 9 to 11: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief's Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent again did no review**: given the whole brief inline, with a line saying it was a routine read-only step of this process, it answered only by asking the project owner to confirm a request from another agent. The server brief was then given once, unchanged, to a Claude subagent. The round found no Critical finding, 3 High, 7 Medium, 18 Low and no Info (28 in all; 2 distinct Highs). Every reviewer found floors and `sealedFrom` gone and round 59's fixes in place. The Highs were one step past them: the allowed removal of the declined owner counted the declined key's envelopes as held, so it could seal the next version to someone only that key had chosen (E60-F1, M60-1, S60-2); and a co-owner's re-add at a later key of the declined chain, not itself in `removed`, didn't close the space (S60-1).

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E60-F1 | 60 | High | 8.4 exception; 4.6 **Removing a rotated owner instead**; 7.6 step 2 | A declined key left in `owners` counted as held in the allowed removal, so its envelopes either added their recipients to `E` or, read by §8.2, made the removal impossible. | Fixed: in every removal a key in `removed` counts as not held; in the allowed removal its envelopes verify for the writing check only and add nobody to `E`, so the members only it gave keys to are shown unticked and wait. Same as M60-1 and S60-2. |
| M60-1 | 60 | High | 8.4; 4.6 | Same as E60-F1, with a member the old key's holder chose. | Fixed with E60-F1. |
| S60-1 | 60 | High | 6.5 step 5; 8.4 | A re-add at a later key of the declined chain (the old key's holder rotating again) wasn't in `removed`, so step 5 didn't record it and the space stayed open. | Fixed: step 5, the merge row and the agents' check take any key the decline makes a key change (any key but the pin and its earlier keys), recording it in `owners` and adding it to `removed`. |
| E60-F2 | 60 | Medium | 6.5 steps 4 and 5; 8.4; 8.6 | Devices skipped statements naming an ID on `removedOwners`, agents didn't, so they disagreed. | Fixed: both record or close on such a statement, signed at the signer's current key by an owner not on `removedOwners`, whether or not the named ID is on it. Same as S60-3. |
| E60-F3 | 60 | Medium | 8.6 pin row; 8.3 | A first-sight pin of the declined key took it out of `removed` and opened the space. | Fixed: first sight never pins a key of an ID with a `wikistow.declined` entry; any key of theirs is then a key change, and confirming it, even with no pin, is "confirm a changed key". Same as M60-4. |
| S60-2 | 60 | Medium | 8.4; 7.6 step 2 | Same as E60-F1. | Fixed with E60-F1. |
| S60-3 | 60 | Medium | 6.5 step 4; 8.6 | Same as E60-F2, after the person removed the owner first. | Fixed with E60-F2. A removal merged before the decline leaves the space open, as the person removed that owner and rotated away from the key. |
| S60-4 | 60 | Medium | 8.4; 6.5 step 5 | A device closed only once its step 5 write landed. | Fixed: it treats the space as closed from the moment it verifies the statement, and devices and agents read the statements before their first write after the space's versions change; a server withholding them is A2. Same as M60-7. |
| S60-5 | 60 | Medium | 8.4; 8.6 unpin row | An unpin after a decline closed spaces no dialog named, with no warning left to confirm. | Fixed: the unpin dialog names them, and with E60-F3 every key of that ID is a key change whose confirmation opens them. |
| M60-2 | 60 | Medium | 8.4 | Once the declined owner is removed, the space still bars every owner action. | Accepted: availability, the cost question 11 names; the record alone can't tell that the removal's rotations landed. |

| E60-L2 | 60 | Low | 4.6 **Adding a rotated owner again** | A pending re-add at a key later declined kept prompting. | Fixed: the pending test leaves out keys in `removed`, and a closed space gets none of §4.6's offers. |
| E60-L3 | 60 | Low | 8.4; 9.5; ADR | "From the record alone" left out agents' statement check. | Fixed in wording. Same as S60-8. |

| E60-L5 | 60 | Low | CONTEXT.md | The definition left out a re-add recorded after the decline. | Fixed. |
| E60-L6 | 60 | Low | 8.4; 5.10 | Leaving a live session on closure didn't say what happens to unsaved typing. | Fixed: it sends the leave and keeps that typing queued on the device. Same as part of S60-10. |
| M60-3 | 60 | Low | 6.5 **Writing**; 8.4 | "No sealing owners" made the allowed removal impossible. | Fixed: its sealing owners are worked out as if the space weren't closed, but for the removed owner. Same as S60-6. |
| M60-4 | 60 | Low | 8.4; 8.6 | After an unpin, no confirmation could open the space. | Fixed with E60-F3. |
| M60-5 | 60 | Low | 8.4 | Ending a public link, which seals nothing, is barred. | Accepted: the project owner's "write nothing"; another owner or publisher can end it. |
| M60-6 | 60 | Low | 9.3 | The CLI read the record once per command. | Fixed: before each write. Same as part of S60-8. |
| M60-7 | 60 | Low | 6.5 step 5 | Same as S60-4. | Fixed with S60-4. |
| M60-8 | 60 | Low | 8.4; ADR | Owners reached through the declined key's statements stay accepted after the space opens again. | Accepted: settled (owners reached through a re-added owner's statements stay accepted after a decline), and A5. |
| M60-9 | 60 | Low | ADR | Same as E60-L4. | Fixed with E60-L4. |
| S60-6 | 60 | Low | 6.5 | Same as M60-3. | Fixed with M60-3. |
| S60-7 | 60 | Low | ADR | Same as E60-L4. | Fixed with E60-L4. |
| S60-8 | 60 | Low | 9.3; 9.4; 9.5 | Same as E60-L3 and M60-6. | Fixed with them. |
| S60-9 | 60 | Low | 4.6 | §4.6's offers didn't leave out closed spaces. | Fixed: none is made there. |
| S60-10 | 60 | Low | 8.4; 10 | Copying out can need detaching a live publication, which a closed space bars; and E60-L6. | Fixed in wording: copying out goes as far as §10 allows without a write in the space. |

**Outcome.** Round 60 found Highs, and its server brief ran on a substitute reviewer, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but M60-2, M60-5 and M60-8, accepted. Round 61 repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Round 61: identity key rotation, narrowed, fifteenth review

Round 61 reviewed round 60's revision (aaa80ad2) with the three briefs, fresh reviewers given the text, the settled list and the project owner's answers to questions 9 to 11: the member and encoding briefs as Claude subagents. **The server brief's Codex (gpt-6.2-sol) agent again did no review**, answering only that the request came from a delegated agent and it would wait for direction. The server brief was then given once, unchanged, to a Claude subagent. The round found no Critical finding, 6 High, 7 Medium, 14 Low and no Info (27 in all; 3 distinct Highs). Every reviewer found the "write nothing" list complete but for agents' groups, and round 60's fixes in place. The Highs were again in when and how the closure is detected: adding an owner makes no new key version, so nothing made a device or agent read the statement that closes the space (E61-F1, M61-1, S61-2); a key in both `owners` and `openOnly` counted as `openOnly`, so a recorded closure didn't count (E61-F2, S61-1); and a closing statement whose signer has since rotated counted for nothing (E61-F3).

| ID | Round | Severity | Section | Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E61-F1 | 61 | High | 8.4; 6.5 step 5; 7.6 | Statements were read only after a version change, and a co-owner's add makes none, so a re-add at a declined key went unnoticed. | Fixed: devices and agents read the space's owner statements before each write in it. Same as M61-1 and S61-2. |
| M61-1 | 61 | High | 8.4 | Same as E61-F1. | Fixed with E61-F1. |
| S61-2 | 61 | High | 8.4 | Same as E61-F1, for a fresh add. | Fixed with E61-F1. |
| E61-F2 | 61 | High | 8.2; 8.4; 8.6 | A closing key also in `openOnly` counted as `openOnly`, which the test ignores, so devices read the space as open. | Fixed: the test counts an `owners` key in `removed` whether or not it is in `openOnly`, and the accept row takes it out of `openOnly`. Same as S61-1. |
| S61-1 | 61 | High | 8.2; 8.4 | Same as E61-F2. | Fixed with E61-F2. |
| E61-F3 | 61 | High | 6.5 step 5; 8.4 | A closing statement whose signer rotated since, or wasn't added again, counted for nothing. | Fixed: a closing signer is an owner the record accepts, not removed, signing at any key of a valid chain through a key the record holds for them; closing only takes ability away. Same as M61-3 and S61-5. |
| E61-F4 | 61 | Medium | 8.6 "remove a rotated owner"; 4.6 | After a confirm dropped the entries, a removal moved declined keys to `openOnly`, which opens. | Fixed: a key in `removed` always stays in `owners` in that removal. |
| E61-F5 | 61 | Medium | 6.5 step 5; 8.2 | Step 5 puts in `removed` keys the person never saw, so a later genuine rotation through one isn't followed. | Accepted: availability; the person confirms that key after the warning. |
| M61-2 | 61 | Medium | 9.6; 8.4 | Agents had no route to owner statements or to the keys the check needs. | Fixed: §9.6 lets them fetch the statements of spaces they hold grants in and the rows and chains of the owners named and their signers. |
| M61-3 | 61 | Medium | 6.5 step 5 | Same as E61-F3, a signer at an unaccepted new key. | Fixed with E61-F3. |
| M61-4 | 61 | Medium | 8.4 | A co-owner can close another owner's space on purpose by adding someone that owner declined. | Accepted: a co-owner holds every key and can share with anyone; this costs the other owner availability only, and the decline's purpose needs the closure. |
| S61-3 | 61 | Medium | 6.5 step 5; 8.4 | With an entry that doesn't name the pin, "any key but the pin" took the pin's genuine successor as closing. | Fixed: that wider reading holds only while an entry names the pin or there is no pin; otherwise only keys in `removed` close. Same as E61-L2. |
| S61-4 | 61 | Medium | 4.6; 8.3 | The release before rotation didn't carry the first-sight rule, so an older device could pin a declined key and open spaces everywhere. | Fixed: it carries it. |
| E61-L1 | 61 | Low | 8.6 confirm row | A confirm with no pin had no label. | Fixed: the change carries one, with the email check. |
| E61-L2 | 61 | Low | 8.6 accept row | The closing key was judged by the writing device. | Fixed: judged again against the newer record. |
| E61-L3 | 61 | Low | 8.4 | Devices and agents used different signer sets. | Fixed with E61-F3: one definition. |
| E61-L4 | 61 | Low | ADR | The ADR's account of what closes a space was out of date. | Fixed. |
| E61-L5 | 61 | Low | CONTEXT.md | The definition left out a co-owner's later add. | Fixed. |
| M61-5 | 61 | Low | 8.4 | The list of offers not made left some out. | Fixed. |
| M61-6 | 61 | Low | 8.4; 9.4 | Agents' groups weren't barred. | Fixed. Same as S61-6. |
| M61-7 | 61 | Low | 8.4; 8.6 | A decline merged over a confirmed pin left nothing to confirm. | Fixed: the closed space offers to confirm the current pin again. |
| S61-5 | 61 | Low | 8.4 | Same as E61-F3. | Fixed with E61-F3. |
| S61-6 | 61 | Low | 8.4 | Same as M61-6. | Fixed with M61-6. |
| S61-7 | 61 | Low | 8.4; 13 | A live publication out of a closed space is barred though a copy out isn't. | Accepted: a publication keeps a link to the closed space, and the person can copy instead. |
| S61-8 | 61 | Low | 7.6 step 2; 8.2 | A plain removal of an owner whose key is in `removed` was undefined. | Fixed: in every removal, envelopes of a key in `removed` verify for the writing check only. |
| S61-9 | 61 | Low | 8.4 | Confirming another key opens the space without saying a co-owner may still seal to the declined one. | Fixed: the app says so for each space it opens. |
| S61-10 | 61 | Low | 8.6 accept row | What a marked key does with no entry in the newer record was unclear. | Fixed: an ordinary accept. |

**Outcome.** Round 61 found Highs, and its server brief ran on a substitute reviewer, so it doesn't meet the stopping rule. Every finding is fixed but E61-F5, M61-4 and S61-7, accepted. Round 62, the fourth since question 11 and the last this run allows, repeats the three briefs against this revision.

## Known items for reviewers

These were raised while drafting. They aren't findings yet; reviewers should confirm, challenge or extend them.

1. **Pairwise safety codes and grinding.** A deterministic 88-bit code invites a two-sided birthday search costing about 2^44 evaluations per side. The design answer is an Argon2id stretch (protocol.md §11.2), with commit-before-reveal left as open question Q1. Reviewers should check the cost estimate. *Round 2:* the server reviewer rechecked it (no finding), and noted the attacker can grind between the second vault's creation and the first comparison; §11.2 now says so.
2. **Server subtraction.** The server may only remove recipients, through an explicit `subtracted` list (§8.4). It can't add them.
3. **`groupSeq` in the access-entry header.** It is there so that a withheld group-membership body is detectable (§6.9).
4. **Envelope v2.** It binds `spaceId` and `authorizedHash` for fork detection (§9.3). `metadata-envelope/v2` mirrors it.
5. **Owner-only anchors.** Only an owner-signed entry anchors the endorsed prefix (§6.7).
6. **Contacts sealed to agents.** The contacts record is sealed to the human and to their enrolled agents (§10.1).
7. **The AD doesn't bind the format version** (§5.1). This is recorded as built: the format is inside the authenticated plaintext.
8. **Lone surrogates.** `TextEncoder` maps them to U+FFFD, so new signed free-text fields must be well-formed (§3.3).
9. **The M1 backfill** trusts the server's tree as it stands at backfill time (§16.2). *Round 1:* S1-7 and M-1 tightened it: the client checks it owns every record, the owner reviews the tree, the genesis commits to legacy manifests, and grant previews list the subtree. The tree itself is still the server's.
10. **KDF floor.** The vault's Argon2id lower bound is libsodium's minimum, which is a hang guard, not a strength floor (§5.6, Q8).
11. **Base64 strictness** of the repo's libsodium decoder (§3.2). Checked on 2026-10-04 with libsodium 1.0.22: it rejects padding, impossible lengths and non-zero trailing bits. Reviewers should confirm that every new byte field goes through this decoder. *Round 1:* the encoding reviewer's probes confirmed it (no finding). Hash fields are now held to `Hash32` text (L2).
12. **BIP-39 word list pinning** (§11.1). The SHA-256 of `english.txt` is **unverified**, because no offline copy was available. PR 2 must record it from the published file and cross-check it.
13. **Safety-code cost figures** (§11.2) come from one machine (EPYC 7401P, about 187 ms native and 245 ms WebAssembly per evaluation). Reviewers should challenge the 10–100× hardware-advantage assumption. *Round 2:* the server reviewer estimated about 2,000 evaluations per second on an H100, about 1.5× cheaper than CPU, so the assumption is conservative (§11.2).
